Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 401: Sorry, but This Is Just the Beginning
--------------------------------------------------
At the words that came from outside, the expressions of the three hostages all stiffened at once.

The right answer?

If they were capable of thinking at all, there was no way a rescue party that had come to save them would have knocked so politely on the door, would they? They would have broken it down and burst in by surprise.

Most decisively, the woman’s face filled with joy.

‘Damn it! They’re on her side!’

Yang Jung let out a deep sigh.

‘But just who is it, for her to be so happy?’

Creeeak.

As the door opened, Ian threw herself toward the person standing in front of it.

“Young Master!”

Without a thought for anything else, she flung herself into Geom Mugeuk’s arms.

“I missed you!”

She had hugged Geom Mugeuk several times before, but never had she been so glad, so happy to see him as she was today.

Yes, the right answer had arrived!

No, what did it matter if he was the wrong answer? It was Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk, who appeared at just the right moment in such a difficult situation, had come.

The trio watching the scene felt fear at the appearance of these new arrivals, but at the same time, they envied the man.

‘Who on earth is he, for this beautiful woman to be so delighted?’

Looking at his face, he was a young and handsome man.

Only belatedly did Ian notice Chwima standing behind Geom Mugeuk and looking at her.

“Ah! Lord Chwima!”

Startled, Ian pulled away from Geom Mugeuk, her face turning red.

To think that the woman who had been debating whether to cut off their arms or their necks just moments ago could look so shy!

But right now, that was not the issue for the three of them.

‘Chwima?’

The three who had been captured looked at one another with puzzled expressions. They stared at each other as if to say, ‘I misheard that, right?’ After all, there was only one Chwima they knew of.

Their gazes, desperately hoping they had heard wrong, turned back toward Chwima.

The handsome middle-aged man did not reveal any particular aura, yet for some reason he possessed a presence that drew the eye. As their gazes lingered on his vaguely decadent air and empty eyes, they eventually fell to the wine bottle at his waist.

They might not know anything else, but they knew that much for certain. Chwima’s signature weapon was a wine bottle.

‘We didn’t mishear!’

A chill ran through them.

‘A Demon Venerable has come? Here? Why on earth?’

The fear a Demon Venerable inspired in the martial world was truly tremendous. When Chwima glanced at them, the three lowered their heads in unison and avoided his eyes.

Chwima looked at Geom Mugeuk. His gaze seemed to say:

I may get bullied by you, but when I’m outside, this is the kind of person I am.

Geom Mugeuk smiled at him. He knew that even if Chwima acted calm on the outside, his heart was not at ease, so he tried to smile at him often.

Geom Mugeuk asked Ian,

“Who exactly were you trying to save?”

“I knew it! You understand even without me telling you.”

Ian explained how she had ended up coming all the way here. It had begun when she happened to meet a woman on the way to the place where Seo Jin, the younger sibling of the Ghost Spirit, was.

“……I came all the way here because I accepted a cup of water and listened to her story. It was an extremely expensive cup of water, really.”

Geom Mugeuk knew. This incident explained, in the simplest way, what kind of person Ian was.

She had not thrown herself in because she was merely his guard, or simply to save him. She had thrown herself in willingly because she was this kind of person.

“Was handling this kind of thing always this difficult? Whenever I watch you, Young Master, you take care of things so easily that I thought it would be simple.”

Ian looked at the three people she had kidnapped and let out a sigh. Up until she brought them here, she had felt good about it. She had thought she would make a splendid hostage exchange, then leave with the people she had rescued.

“How was I supposed to know? That he would even abandon his own child. So I was just debating whether to kill these people outright and leave.”

Those words, which she did not mean, were said for the benefit of the three captives. However she handled them, she had to create an atmosphere of fear.

Now Geom Mugeuk took over the situation.

“May I handle the rest from here?”

“Please do!”

It was Geom Mugeuk, so what was there to worry about? Though it would never happen, even if he ruined everything, what did it matter? To her, Geom Mugeuk was that kind of existence.

Geom Mugeuk approached the three and whispered quietly.

“You really would have been in serious trouble if I hadn’t come. That pretty woman over there—snip, snip—she can be terrifying.”

Ian spoke from behind him.

“I can hear you, you know.”

At that moment, the three were thinking similar thoughts.

‘Who is this young man? Is he Chwima’s subordinate?’

Somehow, that did not seem to be the case. Considering the woman had called him Young Master, he seemed to be of high status….

But no matter how they looked at him, he did not feel like someone who had learned martial arts at all. His appearance and the impression he gave off were exactly that of a scion of a prestigious family. But a scion of a prestigious family was associating with a Demon Venerable and committing such acts?

In any case, what he had to do was clear. Yang Jung resolved that he had to use this variable somehow and survive.

‘I’ll use this bastard to survive.’

Yang Jung asked politely,

“May I ask who you are, Young Hero?”

Forcing down the urge to snap, “And who are you supposed to be?” he behaved as courteously as possible.

“I am the lifesaving benefactor who will save you.”

It was an incomprehensible statement, but in any case, since he said he would save them, Yang Jung seized the opportunity.

“If you spare me, I will grant you whatever you desire.”

“Whatever?”

“That is so. If you want money, I will give you money. If you want martial arts, I will teach you our sect’s martial arts. If you want fame, I will help you gain fame as well.”

For now, he recklessly threw out promises without knowing whether he could keep them or not. His life was on the line, so what could he not say?

Ian, standing behind, smiled faintly. Just who was he offering money to, and who did he think he could teach martial arts? On top of that, even if everyone in his sect were combined, they would not measure up in fame.

“Can’t you give me all three?”

“I will give you all three!”

But soon Geom Mugeuk’s eyes turned suspicious.

“But didn’t you say your father refused the hostage exchange? Would someone like that really give up money and pass on martial arts?”

“That is……!”

Truly, this was the most humiliating and infuriating part of Yang Jung’s life.

Then Geom Mugeuk casually proposed,

“How about this instead? Tell us where the people we need to save are imprisoned. Then we’ll save them ourselves. After that, we can release you. Our goal is to save those people anyway.”

The moment she heard those words, Ian understood.

‘Ah! So that was the right answer.’

Coax him into revealing where they were being held, then go rescue them directly.

‘Why didn’t I think of something so simple?’

It was a plan that made her feel she should obviously have thought of it first. Yet astonishingly, she had not.

Exchange this side for that side. Once her thoughts had fixated on that, nothing else had occurred to her. Her opponent was a cold-blooded man who had even abandoned his child, yet she had been caught up only in the idea of making a fair exchange. Of course the situation would not have gone as she wanted.

If Geom Mugeuk had not come, she would surely have kicked off her blanket far in the future, thinking, ‘Ah, why didn’t I think of that?’

‘As expected, being with the Young Cult Leader puts me at ease.’

At that moment, Ian’s eyes met Chwima’s. Chwima’s expression seemed to say, ‘I know that feeling too.’

Right now, he was simply watching comfortably like this, but what if the Young Cult Leader had been an immature troublemaker? What if he had to follow that troublemaker around and clean up after every accident he caused? It was truly something he did not even want to imagine.

“You have to survive first, don’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk’s persuasion was also for Ian’s sake. She would surely want to save the captured woman’s husband, and the others as well.

The problem was that even if they released Yang Jung, the North Blood Sect would absolutely not release those people. If they had been willing to release them, they would have accepted the hostage exchange in the first place.

According to Ian, they had gathered people with unusual constitutions, so it seemed they were conducting some kind of experiment.

They were undoubtedly plotting something that could not be revealed to the outside.

After agonizing over it, Yang Jung spoke cautiously.

“They’ve probably already moved those people.”

By then, he was halfway persuaded.

“You know where they might have moved them, don’t you?”

Of course, Yang Jung had a rough idea of several possible locations.

“Even if I tell you, the security will be so tight that you won’t be able to save them.”

Geom Mugeuk looked back at Chwima and said,

“Didn’t you hear who he was earlier?”

Yang Jung’s heart began to pound. Chwima frightened him, and he was also afraid because he felt he would end up confessing everything.

“Besides, if we go there and get captured, that’s even better for you, isn’t it? What? Are you afraid you’ll be treated as a traitor? You can just say this later: that you told us the location in order to catch us.”

Geom Mugeuk even provided him with a way out.

“If I really tell you, will you release us?”

“I’ll release you.”

“How can I believe that?”

“Because I’m promising you that I will.”

Looking at those clear eyes and that kind, handsome face, it felt as if he would certainly keep his promise. But how could one trust someone based on appearance alone? Come to think of it, he still did not even know who this person was.

As his hesitation dragged on, Geom Mugeuk turned away coldly.

“Then just die. What are you doing? Kill all three of them!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s command, Ian drew her sword and approached the three. As a thick, unwavering killing intent surged forth, Yang Jung cried out urgently,

“I’ll tell you!”

In the end, Yang Jung revealed the locations. Not just one place, but several where they might be.

“If the place you told us is right, then when you open your eyes again, you’ll wake up in your room at the North Blood Sect.”

Whoosh! Whoosh! Whoosh!

Three streams of finger wind flew out simultaneously and sealed their acupoints. They fell asleep on the spot.

Of course, even if the location was correct, he had no intention of simply releasing them. He needed to make a deal with the Ice Palace and obtain what could be obtained from that side first.

And that was not the end of it. There was still punishment to mete out for whatever the North Blood Sect had been plotting.

Ian let out a sigh and said,

“Why couldn’t I think of this from the start?”

“Even if you had thought of it then, it wouldn’t have worked.”

“What? What do you mean?”

“He would never have revealed it. He would have denied everything to the very end. And you’re not the type to torture someone.”

“Ah! So he confessed because he learned that his father had abandoned him!”

“That’s right.”

“I didn’t think of that part either! Why does the right answer keep avoiding me?”

Ian gazed at Geom Mugeuk with fresh admiration.

Geom Mugeuk did not forget to show off.

“That look in your eyes, so full of respect, always feels good no matter how many times I see it.”

After joking like that, the three of them went outside.

Outside, the snow that had briefly stopped was falling heavily again.

“We’re certainly getting more than enough chances to look at snow.”

Ian looked up at the sky with a bright face. Geom Mugeuk once again felt that Ian suited winter well.

Ian ran out into the snow-covered yard.

Watching Ian run around like an excited puppy, Geom Mugeuk said to Chwima,

“Hyung, take care of rescuing those people. I’ll go meet the Young Palace Lord.”

Chwima silently nodded, then walked wordlessly into the snow.

Ian, who had been running around, stopped for a moment and watched Chwima. Was it because she had never properly seen his back before? Or was it because of the snow falling in great flurries? For some reason, Ian thought Chwima’s back as he walked alone through the snow looked lonely.

“Is this really a problem that can be solved with just, ‘take care of it’?”

“Why do you think a Demon Venerable is called a Demon Venerable?”

“Thanks to him, I’m free now!”

Ian flopped down on her back in the snowfield.

Looking up at the snow-filled sky, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Sorry, but this is only the beginning.”

* * *

At Geom Mugeuk’s message asking to meet, Han Seol arrived at the appointed place with Chan.

Two people were waiting there.

The moment Han Seol saw the woman standing beside Geom Mugeuk, she could tell. That woman, wearing a bamboo hat and face veil, was the subordinate who had kidnapped the North Blood Sect Leader’s child this time.

‘So his subordinate was a woman.’

The two were conversing comfortably. Even from a distance, it was clear that their relationship was an easy one. They seemed almost like friends, or perhaps like siblings.

‘As expected, he has no authority.’

It had been that way in front of Chwima, and it was the same in this situation now.

Then, naturally, the thought occurred to her that the rumors and achievements concerning the Young Cult Leader might have been exaggerated. If it was the Demonic Cult, they could fabricate as much as they wanted.

When Han Seol approached them, Ian respectfully cupped her fist in greeting.

“I greet the Young Palace Lord.”

Han Seol accepted the greeting with only the slightest movement of her head. Her expression said that a subordinate was, in the end, nothing more than a subordinate.

In her manner, Geom Mugeuk sensed neither brusqueness nor a consciousness of authority. What he felt now was emptiness.

“Why did you ask to meet?”

At Han Seol’s question, Geom Mugeuk went straight to the point.

“I will return the child of the Northern Blood Sect Master.”

Harboring the hope that things might unexpectedly go well, Han Seol asked calmly.

“Of course, there must be conditions?”

“Naturally.”

“State them.”

“First, you must not hold my subordinate responsible for abducting them.”

Han Seol’s gaze flicked briefly toward Ian before returning to Geom Mugeuk.

“I wonder whether the Northern Blood Sect will accept that.”

Though she said as much, the Northern Blood Sect would have no choice but to accept. One could blame and curse a typhoon that had blown in, but one could not charge headlong into it simply because one was angry.

“Very well. We accept. Where is Young Master Yang?”

“There is one more condition.”

“What is it?”

“Allow us to stay in the Ice Palace. We wish to remain for a short while and investigate the death of the lord of Baekju Snowfield.”

Han Seol refused firmly.

“This palace does not allow outsiders to stay.”

“Those are my only two conditions.”

“That is not for me to decide. I will discuss it with the Palace Mistress and inform you.”

Just as Han Seol was about to turn away, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“This much seems like something you could decide yourself. Why not make the decision and persuade the Palace Mistress? As the Young Palace Mistress, surely you possess at least that much authority.”

If it was a provocation, then it was a provocation, but Han Seol did not let her pride rise to the bait.

“I have no such authority.”

“Then take this opportunity to gain it.”

Han Seol’s eyes turned cold.

“Isn’t your intention far too obvious?”

“I consider it an opportunity for the Young Palace Mistress.”

“What kind of opportunity?”

“An opportunity to formally negotiate with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. The Palace Mistress will be watching the process and the result as well. She may even be expecting it—expecting you to take the lead in handling this matter.”

For a moment, Han Seol imagined herself making that report.

I decided this matter on my own. Please trust and support me, Palace Mistress.

If she truly said that, how would her mother respond?

That was the problem. She could not predict it at all. She still did not truly know what kind of person her mother was.

“I will contact you again.”

She was about to turn away then.

“May I say something?”

Ian, who had remained silent until now, stepped forward. She slowly removed her bamboo hat and veil.

For a moment, Han Seol could not take her eyes off Ian’s face. It was not because she was admiring her beauty.

Han Seol was thinking one thing.

She is someone of a different texture from me.

When had she ever thought such a thing before? Had it been long ago, about her mother?

At the same time, a question arose. Why was she thinking this while looking at a mere subordinate of the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader? Why was she feeling such a deep resonance? Even to herself, she could not understand it.

“What is it you wish to say?”

It was the first moment the gazes of the two women met—one cold, the other warm.

Chapter 402: What Bastard Drinks in Broad Daylight.
---------------------------------------------------
“What I want to say is…”

Han Seol, of course, and even Geom Mugeuk were curious as to what Ian’s answer would be. Her stepping forward had not been planned in advance.

Ian calmly conveyed her sincerity.

“Please try believing in our Young Cult Master just once.”

A flicker of surprise crossed Han Seol’s face, a face that rarely showed emotion. She truly had not expected a subordinate of the Young Cult Master to say such a thing.

“I know this is a situation where it’s difficult for us to trust one another.”

“Then why say such a thing?”

At Han Seol’s cold question, Ian answered gently.

“Because in this world, there are things that transcend all of that.”

From beginning to end, it was an answer that defied Han Seol’s expectations.

Han Seol’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you saying that transcendent being is the Young Cult Master?”

“Yes. Though to you, Young Palace Mistress, it must sound like the words of a madwoman.”

Han Seol, who had been looking at Ian, looked at Geom Mugeuk. Her eyes seemed to say, Now you explain this madwoman.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not one to obediently follow her intent.

“This is how much respect I receive from my subordinates.”

Han Seol glared at Geom Mugeuk as if to pierce through him.

Even from the start, she had not believed the myths within the rumors surrounding Geom Mugeuk. But after hearing Ian’s words, that suspicion had now become certainty.

‘Perhaps it really was fabricated.’

Otherwise, there was no way a subordinate would tell someone to trust their leader because he was a “transcendent being.” She had either been brainwashed, ordered to say it, or was truly mad. Whatever the case, it did not suit a great hero who was changing the landscape of the martial world.

“The beginning is always difficult, but once you get used to it, doesn’t anything become easier? So, Young Palace Mistress, just go ahead and do it!”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward Ian.

“Like that child just now.”

Ian lowered her head deeply. Because he was someone who would say that no matter what she did, perhaps that was why she could cause such trouble.

Ian mustered her courage and spoke once more.

“Please believe in the Young Cult Master. You will never regret it.”

Han Seol gave a cold snort, then bid Geom Mugeuk farewell and turned away.

“I will contact you again.”

After she left, Ian sighed.

“I ruined everything, didn’t I?”

“Transcendent was a bit much.”

“The words just came out without me realizing it. Though I don’t know why I wanted to help such a cold person.”

Were you not the one who came all this way after being given a single glass of water?

Geom Mugeuk already knew the answer.

Ian found her answer in the scenery around her.

While dealing with the Northern Blood Sect matter, she had not seen anything around her at all. But after meeting Geom Mugeuk, the landscape had begun to come into view.

“I think it was because the snowy scenery was beautiful after so long.”

* * *

“The Young Cult Master has made a new proposal.”

Han Seol acted differently from what she had told Geom Mugeuk.

“In exchange for handing over the young disciples of the Northern Blood Sect, he requested to stay at our palace for a short while. And I accepted that proposal.”

She had told Geom Mugeuk that she would discuss it with the Ice Palace Lord, but she spoke to her mother as though informing her that she had already accepted it. It was not because of Ian’s words telling her to try believing in Geom Mugeuk.

She was curious about her mother’s reaction. How would she respond when Han Seol made a decision on her own?

She had expected anger or a cold response, but unexpectedly, the Ice Palace Lord was calm.

At the Ice Palace Lord’s questioning gaze, asking for the reason, Han Seol answered.

“Because saving the young disciples of the Northern Blood Sect comes first.”

“No. Tell me the true reason you accepted. That is not the foremost reason, is it?”

She was right. It was her mother who had made a secret agreement with the Northern Blood Sect Master. There was a reason that came before the life and death of those who had been taken.

“I wanted to confirm something.”

“What?”

How you would react to my acting on my own.

But she gave another reason instead.

“The Young Cult Master as I saw him was different from the rumors. So I wanted to observe him from up close and confirm whether he truly is such an extraordinary person. And…”

Besides the reason that false rumors would not work on him, there was another reason as well.

“If the Institute Master really was murdered as they think, are you not curious? Who killed that person?”

The Ice Palace Lord looked at her daughter in silence. She might have said something about how different Han Seol seemed from usual, but instead—

“Handle this matter as you see fit.”

“Thank you, Palace Lord.”

Han Seol bowed respectfully and left the Palace Lord’s Hall.

The Ice Palace Lord walked to the window and looked outside. The snow-covered Ice Palace looked the same as it had yesterday, yet today she gazed upon it with a different heart.

* * *

Yang Yi, who had been pressing his ear to the iron door, let out a sigh.

The person in the cell across from him had been dragged away and had not returned.

Mr. Lin, who had been with him, had also been dragged away several days ago and had not come back. Nor did Yang Yi think they were resting somewhere clean and comfortable. If that were so, they would not have been imprisoned in this damp dungeon-like place to begin with.

“Hyeon, Min.”

Tears welled in Yang Yi’s eyes. He was a man who had never shed tears in his life.

He missed his children terribly.

He had not known it when they were together, but now that he thought he might never see them again, only their faces kept coming to mind.

His wife, who was already full of worries, must not be sleeping at all by now.

When those people had brought him here, they had done so with smiles, yet they would not even allow him to send a single letter saying he had arrived safely.

The reason he had come here was that they had said a renowned physician was testing a newly developed medicine. At the largest clinic in the village, they gathered people and conducted various examinations. Since they paid several days’ wages just for being examined, many people had gathered.

And in the village, only he and Mr. Lin possessed the constitution they wanted.

They said that if he agreed to be experimented on for just two or three months, they would pay him a large sum. It was an amount he would have had to work for years to earn with his current income.

His wife had tried to stop him, saying it was dangerous. His body might be harmed, and it was suspicious that they were offering such a huge sum. On top of that, they were outsiders.

But after much thought, Yang Yi decided to go.

There were even people who wanted to go but could not because their constitution did not match.

Besides, those who had examined his body had been extremely courteous and refined. They had honestly told him that, depending on the case, his health might be harmed. Because of that, he trusted them even more.

No matter how hard he and his wife worked, they could not save much money. Just when they thought they had saved a little, something came up that required spending it. Just when they thought they had scraped together a bit more, something else came up.

If he received that lump sum, he planned to open even a small shop. He had lived his life doing every kind of rough and dirty work, but he did not want his children to live that way.

Would his children even be able to endure the hardships he had endured? His wife told him to have faith in them, but Yang Yi could not. No, he did not want to make them suffer like that. Just one small shop that could feed their family of four! Please!

But after leaving his hometown, he sensed that something had gone wrong.

It was not people from the clinic who took them away, but martial artists carrying swords who escorted them.

They had come all the way to the North Sea instead of the place they were originally supposed to go, and they had been confined not in a medical hall but in a secluded villa. They were not allowed to go outside, nor even step beyond the door. Then, not long ago, they had been moved to this place, which had been made to resemble a dungeon outright.

‘I should have listened to my wife.’

At that moment, a martial artist opened the iron door and came in.

“Let’s go.”

Yang Yi’s heart dropped. His turn had finally come.

His heart began to pound madly.

‘Am I going to die now? I can’t die like this.’

Should he ambush the martial artist before him and run? Even headbutt him?

But that would not have been easy even against an ordinary person, and his opponent was a martial artist. No matter how much his body had been tempered by construction work, he could not face a martial artist.

Yang Yi was dragged out.

As he walked down the corridor, he felt no gazes at all. Normally, there had been people in the cell in front of his, in the cell beside his, and in the one beside that as well. But those who had been dragged away had not returned.

‘I’m the last one!’

The place he was dragged to was filled with all manner of objects whose purpose he could not identify. There was a strange medicinal smell he had never smelled in his life.

And in the middle of the room was a bed made of jade.

The martial artist who had brought him said to Yang Yi,

“Take off your clothes and lie on the bed. Strip everything off. Leave nothing on.”

When Yang Yi hesitated, the martial artist glared at him coldly, as if to say he would not repeat himself.

At the fierce, chilling pressure, Yang Yi took off his clothes. Should he charge him even now, subdue the bastard, and flee? His instincts screamed that at him, but his body, which had never fought anyone in its life, could not obey those instincts.

Building houses, paving roads, piling embankments. Though he had spent his whole life doing rough and difficult labor, he was gentle by nature and had never once hit another person.

Yang Yi lay on the bed. It was far colder than he had expected. The martial artist bound Yang Yi’s arms and legs with leather straps connected to the bed so that he could not move.

Once he was ready, the door opened and four people entered.

A middle-aged man in a blue long robe and three martial artists guarding him. One of the martial artists wore a mask, and his gaze was even more intense than that of the middle-aged man in the blue robe.

The middle-aged man approached and looked down at Yang Yi. His gaze was colder than the chill of the bed Yang Yi felt against his back.

Yang Yi prayed desperately. He did not know what kind of test they were conducting, but please, let him be someone who matched their requirements.

‘Please, just let me live!’

He no longer needed money. If only he could return alive. If only he could see his wife and children again.

The middle-aged man began the test in earnest.

When he manipulated something beneath the bed, the bed began to grow even colder.

“Uuuuugh!”

It was so cold that a groan escaped him on its own. At this rate, he felt he would freeze to death.

“Please spare me!”

At Yang Yi’s plea, the man said coldly,

“Among those born with the Ice-Cold Physique, there is said to be one in a thousand who possesses the Extreme Cold Physique. If you are one of them, you will be able to survive.”

Yang Yi despaired. In other words, did that not mean that if he was not one in a thousand, he would die?

It was at that very moment. The middle-aged man in the room flared his nostrils.

“But what is that smell?”

A smell was entering through a small hole in the iron door.

“It smells like liquor.”

The middle-aged man’s expression immediately hardened.

“What bastard is swilling liquor in broad daylight?”

One of the three martial artists standing there went outside.

“I will check.”

The martial artist opened the door and went out. After checking the corridor, he was startled. The far end of the corridor looked as though it were covered in fog.

‘What is this?’

There was no way such fog could form indoors.

Sensing an unknown danger, the martial artist drew his sword.

In an instant, the fog surged in. The smell of liquor was coming from within this fog.

‘The smell of liquor in fog? Then this is!’

It was liquor qi.

Those weak to alcohol—no, even those who could hold their liquor well—would become so drunk on this energy that they could barely control their bodies. It was such intense liquor qi that it made one think this was what it might feel like if a dam holding back liquor collapsed and flooded up to one’s knees.

At that moment, before he could even sense anything approaching, an arm with a clenched fist suddenly shot out from within the fog.

The fist merely tapped him lightly, yet the martial artist flew back, slammed into the wall, and lost consciousness.

The person who emerged from the fog was none other than the Drunken Demon.

The liquor qi coiled around his body like protective qi. At the moment, it looked like ordinary fog, but in truth, it was the Drunken Demon’s liquor qi, capable of transforming into any form.

He opened the door to the place the martial artist had come from and entered.

Shiiik.

Another martial artist, sensing that the situation outside was unusual, sent his sword flying toward the Drunken Demon.

The Drunken Demon lightly avoided the incoming sword, then this time struck the martial artist’s neck lightly with the edge of his hand. He merely tapped him, yet the martial artist who collapsed could not get back up.

Whirrrr.

The fog-like liquor qi that had filled the corridor outside gathered entirely toward the Drunken Demon, then wrapped around his body and shimmered like a haze.

The middle-aged man in the blue long robe looked at the Drunken Demon with a startled expression. His two subordinates were not so weak as to be defeated this easily. That meant his opponent’s martial arts were truly powerful.

But he did not despair yet. Among the three martial artists, the real expert was the masked man.

The Drunken Demon glanced at the two of them, then immediately examined Yang Yi lying on the bed. Frozen cold, he looked as though his breath would cease at any moment.

It was a situation in which the masked man could have launched a surprise attack while he was paying attention to Yang Il, but the Drunken Demon did not care in the slightest. Even though the Drunken Demon had turned his back to look at Yang Il, the masked man did not dare attack. The liquor qi wavering around his body felt as though it were watching him.

After untying the leather binding Yang Il, the Drunken Demon took hold of his wrist. As a hot energy flowed into him, Yang Il let out a calm breath and came to his senses.

Even after confirming his opponent’s skill, the middle-aged man warned in a low voice.

“Do you know who we are, acting like this? If you withdraw now, you may be able to keep your life.”

At that, the Drunken Demon readily revealed his identity. Since the Northern Sea Ice Palace already knew that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had intervened in this matter, there was no particular reason to hide it.

“I am the Drunken Demon.”

“!”

The middle-aged man froze, his face turning ashen. The masked man’s expression also hardened stiffly.

“Why are you here?”

If not for the move he had just shown against his subordinate, if not for the realm in which no opening could be found even when he turned his back, he would never have believed it.

But it was certainly the Drunken Demon. This deep liquor qi and faint aroma of alcohol drifting from him were energies that no one but the Drunken Demon could even imitate.

No, leaving all of that aside. The man before him was exuding a presence that no one dared approach.

Yang Il was just as astonished as they were.

This man was the famous Drunken Demon of the Demonic Cult’s Eight Demon Venerables? Who in the world did not know of them? There was even a saying that if children cried, a Demon Venerable would come and take them away.

“Is there perhaps someone named Yang Il among those who were brought here?”

When the Drunken Demon looked for him, Yang Il was startled.

“I am Yang Il. It’s me.”

He had never even been personally involved with a third-rate martial artist in his entire life, yet a Demon Venerable had been looking for him. How did he know my name?

“That’s a relief. Saving you was my purpose.”

Yang Il could not understand this situation at all.

‘A Demon Venerable came to save me?’

It felt as though he were dreaming. He looked down at the bed.

‘Ah, am I dreaming right before I die?’

He thought that perhaps his real self was freezing to death on the bed, and in his final moments, he was having a dream like this.

But it was far too vivid to be called a dream or illusion. Even the bed he was sitting on now was cold enough to chill his backside.

“You came to save me? Why would someone as honored as you come for me?”

Yang Il’s voice trembled violently. He had thought the man had come for some other matter and happened to be saving him.

Then an astonishing answer flowed from the Drunken Demon’s mouth. It was probably the most shocking thing Yang Il had ever heard in his life.

“Your wife asked me. She asked me to bring her husband home.”

“!”

Yang Il’s eyes widened in sheer shock. How could his wife possibly have made such a request of a Demon Venerable?

“My wife did? My wife is someone who hasn’t even left the village since marrying me……”

Soon, Yang Il shook his head. There was no way such a thing could happen.

‘Ah, I’m already dead. Wife, thank you for saving me, even if it’s in a dream. Raise the children well, and live well. I’m sorry I couldn’t stay with you to the end.’

Even amid that, the dreamlike reality continued.

“I haven’t had any liquor, so my nerves are a bit on edge.”

After placing the ice liquor Wonju had poured into the Blood Tear, the Drunken Demon had not yet drunk it. When he killed the one who had killed her, he intended to drink that ice liquor and kill him. So that she could be with him at the moment of revenge.

“Do you think I’m more dangerous when I’ve been drinking? Or when I haven’t?”

Chapter 403: Do You Think Selling Noodles Is Easy?
--------------------------------------------------
The middle-aged man in blue wanted to shout:

‘You ask because you don’t know? It’s the damned Drunken Demon. Of course both are dangerous.’

Of course, he could not say that, so he spoke as politely as possible.

“I did not know the Demonic Cult was involved in this matter. Had I known, I would not have helped.”

He meant it. Nothing good could come from getting entangled with the damned Demonic Cult.

The Drunken Demon asked him,

“Who are you?”

At that, the man glanced at the masked man. It was not the look one gave a subordinate; he was watching the man’s reaction. When the masked man showed no response, the middle-aged man revealed himself.

“My name is Hwang Su. I am a scholar who studies the human body.”

“A scholar, my foot! A butcher, more like. How many have you killed so far?”

Hwang Su could not make excuses. Too many people had died. He did not even know the number.

“What tests did you perform on them?”

Hwang Su hesitated to answer.

Then Yang Il, who had been on the bed, mustered his courage and spoke.

“He said he was looking for some kind of physique. What was it again? Ah, right. The Extreme Cold Physique. He said if I had the Extreme Cold Physique, I could survive.”

The Extreme Cold Physique.

A physique naturally born with resistance to cold was the Ice-Cold Physique. Those born with it could live their entire lives without catching so much as a cold.

And among those with the Ice-Cold Physique, the one especially specialized for cold was the Extreme Cold Physique.

A person born with the Extreme Cold Physique would not die from cold, at the very least. Even if they stripped naked and entered icy water in the dead of winter, they might die of hunger, but they would not freeze to death.

“This bed became terrifyingly cold.”

At Yang Il’s words, the Drunken Demon examined the bed. It was no ordinary bed.

“So this must be the bed used to find the Extreme Cold Physique.”

The Drunken Demon turned back to Hwang Su and asked,

“Why are you searching for the Extreme Cold Physique?”

Seeing Hwang Su refuse to answer, the Drunken Demon wore a contemptuous expression.

“I believe I told you. Since I have not had a drink, my nerves are on edge.”

Hwang Su looked again at the masked man beside him.

The Drunken Demon, too, had realized from the beginning that he was no subordinate. His eyes had never been those of a subordinate in the first place.

“He was sent to watch you.”

At last, the masked man stepped forward.

“If you thought we would be afraid because you are a Demon Supreme, you were mistaken.”

The “we” did not refer to Hwang Su.

Two masked men entered the room.

Their presence was different from the martial artists who had fallen to the Drunken Demon earlier. Their gazes were as fierce as that of the masked man who had been speaking.

From the start, three of them had come, taking turns monitoring Hwang Su while assisting with this mission. Their skills were equal, and their temperaments similar.

At their appearance, Hwang Su’s face brightened. The reason the masked man had stayed silent without stepping forward was because he had been waiting for them to arrive.

‘If these three are together!’

Just as he thought that even against the Drunken Demon, they might have a chance—

Wooooooosh.

In the next instant, the liquor qi rippling around the Drunken Demon’s body scattered in all directions. In the blink of an eye, a thick mist filled the room.

Hwang Su could see nothing.

‘Please! Kill the Drunken Demon!’

A moment of silence passed, and then Hwang Su saw a blade glimmer. It was the blade of one of the masked men.

The next instant—

Crack.

The sound of bone breaking rang out. The sword visible in midair twitched violently, then vanished into the mist as if being sucked in.

‘He got him!’

Silence fell over the place again.

Whoooosh.

When another masked man infused his sword with internal energy and swung it through the air, the liquor qi spread like mist coiled around his blade.

His intention was to draw in the thing blocking his sight with his internal energy and expel it, but the Drunken Demon’s liquor qi was not ordinary fog or smoke.

For a moment, it seemed to wrap around his sword, but soon scattered in every direction and grew even thicker.

At that moment, Hwang Su saw it: the Drunken Demon’s figure flashing through the mist.

He was moving leisurely, hands clasped behind his back, and even grinned at him.

‘Watch out!’

Before the words could leave his mouth—

Crunch.

The masked man who had tried to disperse the liquor qi had his neck snapped by the Drunken Demon’s hand-blade and collapsed on the spot.

Before he even hit the floor, the Drunken Demon vanished into the mist.

The last sword Hwang Su saw was flying toward him.

‘Killing the witness to silence him!’

The lone remaining masked man had realized he could not face the Drunken Demon and was trying to kill Hwang Su first. It seemed he had been given such an order from the beginning: if a moment like this came, kill him.

The incoming sword stopped right before Hwang Su’s heart.

Craaaack.

The sound of bones being crushed came from directly in front of him.

As the dense mist vanished, the masked man’s figure came into view. The man who had thrust his sword at Hwang Su had his neck twisted at a bizarre angle, and the light had already gone out of his eyes.

As he crumpled to the floor, the Drunken Demon stood behind him. It was, in every sense, an overwhelming difference in skill. He had not even touched Hyeollu, the signature weapon at his waist.

They had a chance if there were three? There had never been a greater delusion.

‘So this is the strength of the Demon Supremes I have only heard about!’

Hwang Su’s heart pounded as if it would burst.

The liquor qi flickered around the Drunken Demon like flame, then vanished completely into his body.

“What is the reason you are trying to obtain the Extreme Cold Physique?”

Now Hwang Su had no reason to watch anyone’s reaction.

“I don’t know that either. I was only hired and paid to procure someone with the Extreme Cold Physique.”

“By whom?”

As Hwang Su hesitated to answer, the Drunken Demon informed him of reality.

“You already died a moment ago.”

Hwang Su looked at the corpses of the fallen masked men, then decided to reveal who had requested it of him.

“The person who paid me was—”

It was then.

Whoosh.

Both sides of Hwang Su’s forehead swelled in an instant.

Pop! Pop!

They burst open, spraying blood.

Hwang Su died instantly. Astonishingly, a restriction had been placed on him that would kill him if he attempted to speak about the one behind it.

The Drunken Demon looked down at the corpse with a grave expression.

Making someone die when they tried to give a specific answer was an extremely difficult suggestion and restriction. It meant a formidable bastard was behind this.

The Drunken Demon said to Yang Il, who was sitting there terrified,

“Get dressed.”

Yang Il, who had remained naked until then, hurriedly put on his clothes.

‘So this wasn’t a dream.’

Once he was dressed, Yang Il followed the Drunken Demon out.

Only then did Yang Il fully realize how reckless his idea had been—to ambush the martial artist who had brought him here and escape.

When they turned the corner of the corridor, more than ten martial artists lay collapsed.

It was the same outside the building. Dozens of martial artists lay fallen everywhere.

Seeing that so many martial artists had been guarding this place, Yang Il realized that this matter had been no ordinary important affair.

‘If I had possessed the Extreme Cold Physique, what on earth were they planning to make me do?’

No matter how much he thought about it, he could not know.

A carriage was waiting a short distance away. It was a carriage from the North Sea branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult that would take Yang Il back to his hometown.

Those taking him would coordinate with the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult branch near Yang Il’s hometown to protect his family, so there was no longer any need to worry.

“You need not worry about retaliation. Live well with your family.”

Even if not for that, by the time Yang Il arrived home, everything on this side would likely be over.

Yang Il could not believe he had survived.

“It still feels like a dream. To think that none other than a Demon Supreme would save me!”

“I was not the one who saved you.”

“What do you mean?”

“If not for your wife’s earnest desire to save her husband, who could have sent someone all the way to this distant North Sea? Hear the details from your wife when you return.”

At the thought that he could go home, Yang Il was so overjoyed he felt as though he might fly. To think he could see his wife and children again.

While imprisoned, he had resolved countless times. He would no longer be greedy for happiness all at once. Instead of the greed of earning money to make them happy, he would be there with his children for each moment as they grew.

‘Thank you. I will surely live that way.’

He bowed deeply.

“Thank you so much for saving my life.”

Yang Il intended to think of the Drunken Demon every year on this day, over a cup of wine.

“I will make sure this bow reaches the person who ought to receive it.”

Then, in the direction opposite the departing carriage, the Drunken Demon began to walk.

Crunch, crunch.

Leaving the first traces in the pure white snow, the Drunken Demon’s steps gradually quickened. No more footprints remained in the snow.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Ian rented a carriage and returned to Eunwol’s safe house.

The trio whose acupoints had been sealed were still asleep.

The two loaded them into the carriage.

“Once Senior Drunken Demon returns, let’s head straight to the Ice Palace.”

Ian could tell. Geom Mugeuk believed without a doubt that the Drunken Demon would handle the matter well and return. Usually, he treated him like an easygoing older brother, but at least now, he regarded him as a Demon Supreme.

Ian was not worried about the Drunken Demon either. If anything, the one she was worried about was the Young Palace Lord they had met.

“But will the Ice Palace accept your proposal, young master?”

Even if the Drunken Demon rescued the captured man, it would be a problem if the Ice Palace refused.

“They will accept it.”

“How can you be so certain?”

“You said so to the Young Palace Lord, didn’t you? That you were the Transcendent One, so he should believe you!”

“Don’t joke. Why?”

Geom Mugeuk explained the reason.

“We had two conditions. That they do not punish you, and that they allow the Ice Palace to investigate the death of the Hall Master.”

“Yes.”

“First of all, they can’t lay a hand on you anyway. Not only can they not carelessly touch you, my own limb, but you also had justification for kidnapping their young master. Most decisively, the Northern Blood Sect is trying to hide this matter, so they cannot blow it up just to punish you.”

“What about the second condition? The Ice Palace might not accept us, might it?”

“The Ice Palace Lord must already be well aware of the relationship between that Hall Master and Brother Drunken Demon. It was a bond formed over many long years of exchanging letters and wine. If such a person takes interest in this death and they refuse? If that happened, Brother Drunken Demon would become even more suspicious of the death and start digging in earnest. In the process, he might uncover information the Ice Palace does not want revealed. The Ice Palace Lord will anticipate that as well, so he cannot refuse easily.”

Ian was impressed. She had not thought of a single one of the reasons Geom Mugeuk had mentioned.

“I’m going to retire. I don’t have the confidence to survive in this kind of murim. It’s a wonder I’m still alive. I’ll retire and go to some countryside place where martial artists don’t come, and make a living selling noodles.”

“You think selling noodles is easy? After being worn down by people all day, you’ll think, ‘Ah! It was nice when I just swung my sword beside the young master without thinking,’ and you’ll want to run back immediately.”

Ian sighed.

“Since we’re talking about it, would you like some noodles? There were ingredients in the kitchen.”

“Do you know how to make them?”

“Noodles, at least, I can make.”

“Let’s eat. I was just getting hungry anyway.”

“I’ll boil some up quickly.”

When Ian disappeared into the kitchen, Geom Mugeuk leaped lightly onto the roof of the carriage. Even though he landed on the roof, there was not so much as the sound of a feather falling.

Geom Mugeuk lay there and looked up at the sky.

Suddenly, he missed his father.

He missed the Demon Supremes as well, but even so, it was always his father he missed first.

He must be sitting sternly in the grand chair in the Heavenly Demon Hall, handling business.

‘Father, your son misses you like this. Are you thinking of me?’

While he was thinking of his father, Ian swiftly made noodles and brought them over.

“Where would you like to eat?”

“Let’s eat here.”

Holding the noodles, Ian leaped lightly onto the roof of the carriage. With her smooth lightness skill, not a single drop of broth spilled. Her martial realm was rising with each passing day.

“Please eat.”

For something made so quickly, it tasted excellent.

“It’s delicious. With this much skill in your hands, you won’t starve even if you retire. Let me invest in that noodle shop too!”

Ian felt pleased. Inwardly, she had been worried Geom Mugeuk might say it tasted bad.

Just as the two were slurping down the noodles—

The Drunken Demon entered the place.

Geom Mugeuk and Ian looked at him. The Drunken Demon nodded once toward the two of them. The moment Ian saw that nod, she felt relieved. She could tell he had rescued him safely.

Of course, there was also unfortunate news.

“I only saved that man.”

Ian found it terribly regrettable, but it could not be helped.

He would not reproach himself, wondering whether he might have saved more people if only he had done better. He intended to add that anger to the punishment he would deal to the one who had plotted this.

“Is there any noodle soup left?”

“I can make some for you right away. But will noodles be enough? I’ll buy you something delicious.”

After all, he had stepped forward for her sake.

“No need. Noodles are enough.”

“Understood. I’ll boil some right away.”

“One more bowl for me too!”

“Yes!”

As Ian ran off to the kitchen, Chwima lightly climbed up onto the roof of the carriage.

“You worked hard, hyung.”

“What do you mean, worked hard?”

“But why were they abducting people?”

“They were looking for someone with the Extreme Cold Physique.”

“Extreme Cold Physique?”

Among the many constitutions a person could possess, the Extreme Cold Physique was an exceedingly rare one.

“The moment he tried to answer who had given him the order, his head burst and he died. It was an extremely powerful restriction.”

The instant he heard that, Geom Mugeuk thought of an organization he had known before his regression.

The Blood Death Alliance.

Bound together by blood, they did not fear death. For the sake of the organization, they threw away their lives without hesitation.

Everyone feared the Blood Death Alliance.

But later, the truth had been revealed. They had not sacrificed their lives out of sincere loyalty. They had been placed under a powerful restriction that caused their bodies to burst apart if they betrayed the alliance.

The one who led that Blood Death Alliance.

Among the Twelve Zodiac Kings, the Ninth King.

The Blood King.

One of the Twelve Zodiac Kings had appeared once again.

Just because a king ranked lower did not necessarily mean he was weaker than those above him.

The one among the Twelve Zodiac Kings who best fit that saying was none other than the Blood King. It was said that he had made himself the Ninth King because he liked the number nine.

The Blood King was a formidable figure. First of all, he commanded countless subordinates, and those subordinates did not fear death. They could not betray him, and they did not hesitate to use self-destructive arts.

Because of that, it was unknown what martial arts the Blood King himself used, or how strong he truly was. All history related to the Blood King was the history of his subordinates, and the history of their blood.

‘That Blood King is searching for the Extreme Cold Physique? Why?’

Geom Mugeuk looked around at the snow-covered surroundings.

‘How much blood will have to be spilled upon this pure white snow?’

Ian, who had brought the noodles, saw the grave look on Geom Mugeuk’s face.

From that expression, she could tell. This matter with the Ice Palace would be anything but easy. Her heart began to boil with fighting spirit.

Just then, her eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s.

“I’ll just work hard boiling noodles.”

At her playful words, Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly.

Then Chwima suddenly spoke.

“Don’t worry.”

When Geom Mugeuk turned to look, Chwima was lying at an angle, gazing up at the sky as if he were drinking at Chwimongru.

His posture was loose, but the gaze visible between the strands of hair that had fallen over his face was deeper and more intense than ever.

“This hyung will take care of everything.”

Chapter 404: What Made Her Freeze?
----------------------------------
“Of all the versions of you I’ve seen, you look the coolest right now.”

Chwima was the sort to take such words with a flourish.

“We simply haven’t met enough yet. Considering all the cool sides of me you’ve yet to see, we’re practically meeting for the first time. A pleasure to make your acquaintance, Young Cult Leader.”

He liked joking around like this, and he was good at laying his feelings bare. That was why, at times, Chwima seemed like a weak person.

But he was not. Chwima was stronger than anyone. I thought he was stronger precisely because he was someone who wavered. His wavering was the kind that came from trying to find his proper place.

And he was lonelier than anyone. Whenever I saw him, the first image that came to mind was always of him sitting alone in a small boat, drinking.

I looked down at Ian, who was standing beside the carriage, and said,

“Brother will take care of everything for us. This time, let’s just relax and enjoy the snow. Find us a place that cooks well. Since we came all this way, we should try everything before we leave.”

Ian smiled and said,

“First, let’s eat these noodles.”

She sprang lightly upward with the noodles in hand.

We sat side by side on the carriage roof and ate the noodles.

“Oh? These are good. Why are they so good?”

I added my own fuss to Chwima’s admiration.

“Right? I was shocked too. I thought the chef from the Heavenly Demon Hall had secretly stopped by.”

Ian laughed, pleased.

“Next time, I’ll make you something even better.”

And so, sitting on the carriage roof, we emptied even the broth completely.

“Now then, since our bellies are full, shall we go?”

“I’ll drive the carriage.”

Ian hopped down and sat in the driver’s seat.

“We’re departing.”

Chwima and I simply remained on the carriage roof. If we rode inside, those unpleasant fellows would be sleeping there anyway. This was cooler and better.

As the carriage sped on, Chwima said,

“Perhaps this matter with the North Blood Sect and the lord’s death are connected.”

I nodded. They might be separate incidents that happened by chance, or they might not be. One thing was certain: the Blood King was behind this affair. That very Blood King, an opponent exceedingly troublesome and perilous to face.

“In the end, we’ll come to know the whole truth. The North Blood Sect and the Northern Sea Ice Palace—we’ll resolve both matters before we return.”

Seeing Chwima nod at my words, I added with a flourish,

“Ah, my mistake. Not we. Brother will resolve them before we return.”

The carriage swiftly crossed the snowfield, where endless snow stretched out toward the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Chwima ascended to the Palace Lord’s Hall under Han Seol’s guidance.

Since the Northern Sea Ice Palace had accepted the deal, they were going to formally greet the Ice Palace Lord.

Ian, who had said she was extremely nervous about meeting the Ice Palace Lord for the first time, marveled at the circular staircase whose color changed with the light.

“It was built so beautifully.”

Unlike her, Geom Mugeuk sensed a faint killing intent from the staircase. It was not killing intent produced by a person.

‘A mechanism.’

Since everything was transparent and visible within, it seemed impossible for there to be mechanisms anywhere. He could not even imagine how these beautiful ice walls, stairs, and pillars would deal with intruders.

This was not a mechanism, but art.

That was precisely why he had a premonition that it would be more dangerous than any mechanism he had experienced before. Ian, for one, had reached the ninth stage of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, and yet she could not sense the existence of this mechanism at all.

Thus, the three followed Han Seol up to the Palace Lord’s Hall.

“I pay my respects to the Palace Lord.”

Geom Mugeuk was the first to greet her courteously.

The Ice Palace Lord, too, observed the courtesy due to the Young Cult Leader.

“Welcome, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk had met her once in his life before returning to the past. Though it had been much later than now.

At that time, this cold woman had been shedding hot tears.

Before her daughter’s death.

By contrast, the Ice Palace Lord was meeting Geom Mugeuk for the first time today. It was the moment she finally met the person who had endlessly surprised her through the reports of her spies.

The Ice Palace Lord slowly examined Geom Mugeuk’s gaze and bearing. He truly accomplished all those things? This young man?

Geom Mugeuk did not completely conceal himself, nor did he reveal himself excessively. He had reached a realm of returning to simplicity and truth that was more natural, more perfect than completely hiding oneself.

Her daughter had spoken of Geom Mugeuk as though she distrusted him. She had said she wanted to observe him longer and confirm things. Naturally, the Ice Palace Lord thought. Even she found him difficult to read.

“Is the Cult Leader well?”

When the Ice Palace Lord asked after his father, Geom Mugeuk smiled and asked in return,

“Have you met my father before?”

“I met him long ago.”

She had seen him only once, but he was not an easy person to forget. Perhaps it was because his gaze had been so intense.

“He is well.”

“I hear you handled a great affair recently. I heard you were the one who led it.”

The summit between the Orthodox, Unorthodox, and Demonic factions had not been held in strict secrecy; it had taken place in Maga Village. It was only natural that the Ice Palace Lord knew of it.

“Since I have met you today, Palace Lord, why not attend the next summit as well? I intend to invite Cult Leader Pungcheon then too.”

It was impossible to tell whether he was joking or serious, but the Ice Palace Lord calmly nodded.

“Be sure to invite me.”

She had answered as a joke, but Geom Mugeuk confirmed it once more.

“If a five-party summit truly opens, will you attend?”

The leaders of the Orthodox, Unorthodox, and Demonic factions, who had gathered once, would gather again? And Cult Leader Pungcheon as well? Would such a thing even be possible?

“Send word.”

“I will look forward to that day.”

After exchanging greetings with Geom Mugeuk in that way, she greeted Chwima next.

“You have come a long way, Demon Venerable.”

“It has been a long time since I last saw you.”

“You remember.”

“Of course.”

Chwima had met the Ice Palace Lord a very long time ago. It was before he had become a Demon Venerable, and before she had become Palace Lord.

Back then, she had not felt like this. Now she felt like an ice statue whose depths could not be known at all, but the Ice Palace Lord of that time had been a pure woman who suited pure white clothes.

What had frozen her like this? The cold winds of the Northern Sea? The difficult life she had lived, striving to protect the Ice Palace as a woman?

“Time flows so swiftly. Life truly is fleeting.”

At Chwima’s words, the Ice Palace Lord nodded.

Lastly, Geom Mugeuk introduced Ian.

“This is my subordinate.”

The Ice Palace Lord had already received reports that Geom Mugeuk had brought the subordinate who had caused this incident. At first, she could not understand it. Even hiding her would not have been enough, yet he deliberately revealed her like this?

“I pay my respects to the Palace Lord.”

At Ian’s greeting, the Ice Palace Lord once again slowly studied her.

Was it because her beauty was excessive? Ian drew the Ice Palace Lord’s gaze more than necessary.

“Why would someone so beautiful cause such an incident?”

“I apologize. I still lack experience, so my handling of matters in the martial world was immature.”

She had much she could say, but Ian apologized courteously. After all, this was not something for which she could hold the Ice Palace Lord responsible.

The one who spoke on Ian’s behalf was Geom Mugeuk.

“She was cautious precisely because she was immature. She brought back those she kidnapped unharmed.”

Geom Mugeuk stared straight at the Ice Palace Lord and added,

“Conversely, the North Blood Sect, with all its experience and skill, did not handle matters that way. I wonder why they all regard human lives so lightly.”

As he said so, Geom Mugeuk observed the Ice Palace Lord’s expression. Did the Ice Palace Lord know what the North Blood Sect had been doing? He had brought it up deliberately to find out.

“I am not sure what you mean.”

From her expression alone, she truly seemed to know nothing.

“Something like that happened. I will tell you in detail next time.”

Geom Mugeuk did not press further. Now was not the time to provoke the Ice Palace Lord, but to investigate the lord’s death.

As important as dealing with the Blood King was, easing Chwima’s heart was important as well. No, that was even more important.

He would handle Chwima’s matter, and deal with the Blood King as a side issue. Even if the Northern Sea sank into blood, Geom Mugeuk resolved to face this matter with that mindset. Because evil was trivial, and life was important.

The Ice Palace Lord asked Han Seol,

“Is Young Master Yang safe?”

“Yes.”

“Bring Young Master Yang here, and guide the three of them to their lodgings.”

“Understood.”

Lastly, the Ice Palace Lord said to Chwima,

“You said you wished to look into the death of the lord of Baekju Snowfield?”

“That is correct.”

“His death is regrettable, but I cannot give you much time for that investigation.”

It meant an order for them to leave could be issued at any time.

“I will hurry as much as possible.”

“Thank you for understanding.”

Thus ended their first meeting, a silent probing match that moved between past and present.

* * *

Yang Jung, the second son of the North Blood Sect Lord, woke from sleep feeling a chill.

He was sitting in a chair. Looking around, he saw that the walls, pillars, everything was made of ice.

“Are you awake?”

A woman stood ahead of him with her back turned.

“Who are you?”

The woman turned and walked toward him. She was none other than the Palace Lord of the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

“Palace Lord?”

Yang Jung was startled when he recognized her. Astonishingly, this place was the Palace Lord’s Hall of the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

“How is your body?”

“I am all right. But why am I here?”

Wonderful news was delivered to him.

“We made a deal with the Demonic Cult and brought you out, Young Master Yang.”

Realizing that he had survived, Yang Jung’s expression brightened.

“Thank you, Palace Lord! I thought I would be killed by that Demonic Cult bastard.”

He was blurting out careless words that did not need to be spoken in this place, but the Ice Palace Lord’s expression did not change.

“I am glad you are safe.”

Yang Jung could not understand it. Why had the Ice Palace saved him?

Just then, a middle-aged man entered. With sharp eyes like a beast’s, a square jaw, and a powerful build, he was a martial artist who looked strong at a glance.

Yang Jung’s face stiffened when he confirmed who it was. The one who had entered was his father, the North Blood Sect Lord Yang Sin (楊愼).

The reason he had been able to raise the North Blood Sect into one of the three great powers of the Northern Sea was not only because of his martial prowess in mastering the Twelve Northern Fist Forms. He looked like a bear, but he possessed a mind sharper than anyone’s, and he was a man of great ambition.

“Father!”

Even though Yang Jung knew full well that his father had abandoned him, he was still glad to see him. Had the Ice Palace moved because his father had requested it? Had his father actually had some grand purpose all along? That hope lasted only a moment before Yang Sin said bluntly,

“I will be leaving shortly, so go down first.”

Yang Jung’s chest tightened. Not even a word asking if his body was all right to the son who had returned after being kidnapped? It was the moment when the sliver of hope that perhaps his father had saved him vanished like foam.

‘Yes, Father abandoned me. The moment he refused the hostage exchange, that woman could have killed me on the spot.’

Resentment and hatred mingled in Yang Jung’s heart. Both the North Blood Sect Lord and the Ice Palace Lord sensed the emotions he felt, but they pretended not to notice.

Leaving his father and the Ice Palace Lord behind, Yang Jung turned and descended the ice stairs. He glanced upward, but all he could see was the faint sight of the two standing side by side and talking; he could not hear what they were saying.

‘What in the world are you plotting, to the point of abandoning even your son? What kind of deal did you make that made the Ice Palace save me from the Demonic Cult?’

When Yang Jung descended to the entrance of the Palace Lord’s Hall, a man stood there with his back turned. Naturally assuming him to be a subordinate, he said to him,

“Where is the carriage?”

The man did not answer and merely looked up at the sky.

“I asked where the carriage is!”

The sparks of the fierce emotions that had surged up because of his father were flying toward an innocent person.

“Even if you return alive, it isn’t the same as before, is it?”

The moment he heard the voice, Yang Jung flinched in surprise. The man standing with his back turned was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“I promised that when you opened your eyes, you’d be in your room, but I couldn’t keep that. Still, you saw your father, so that should be enough, right?”

Yang Jung’s head told him this: You were barely released. There is no need to provoke this man again.

But he was a man who lived more by his heart than his head. Especially when his father was mentioned, his anger flared even more.

“I will not forget the humiliation I suffered this time!”

Behind his bold words lay a certain belief. Since they had made a deal with the Ice Palace, there was no way this man would seize him here.

“If you are a martial artist, then of course you should not. Be sure to come for revenge next time.”

As if I wouldn’t come just because you told me to!

Because he could not shout that aloud, Yang Jung was infuriated. The only thing that came to mind was this.

“Those who have been captured shall never be released.”

He was referring to the ones your subordinates had attempted to rescue by kidnapping even him.

“It seems you haven’t heard yet? You killed all of them. Barely one was saved.”

In that moment, Yangjung felt the air around him grow as cold as Geommugeuk’s gaze.

“If only one of you survives as well, that would be fair, wouldn’t it?”

Would it be you? Or your father?

He did not speak, but Geommugeuk was asking that very question with his eyes.

He had spent his whole life threatening others, never once being threatened himself. And now, he was on the receiving end of a truly proper threat for the very first time.

Just then, the Lord of the Northern Blood Gate came down after finishing his conversation with the Ice Palace Lord.

He recognized Geommugeuk and greeted him respectfully.

“It is an honor to meet you.”

Yangjung was startled by how respectful the greeting was. No matter that he was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, wasn’t Father one of the top three figures in the North Sea?

Geommugeuk returned the greeting with cupped fists.

“I have long heard of the Lord’s illustrious reputation.”

“It is nothing compared to yours, Young Cult Leader.”

While seeming to treat him with special regard, he was as calm and composed as if he were dealing with just anyone else. Geommugeuk knew well how difficult such a balance was.

Unlike his son, he was not a man swayed by emotion. The moment Geommugeuk laid eyes on him, he could tell. This was a man who would never pass his position to this second son.

“We shall formally welcome the Young Cult Leader at our main sect before long.”

“I shall await it.”

The Lord of the Northern Blood Gate walked away first, and Yangjung followed after him.

A transmission from Geommugeuk flew to him.

—If only one person survives, I daresay it will be your father.

It was a remark that poured oil on the flames of hatred for his father. These words would remain in his heart, making things uncomfortable between the two and driving a wedge between them. Geommugeuk had no intention of letting them be at ease.

Yangjung glared at Geommugeuk with an angry face, then strode away from that place.

Geommugeuk, watching that retreating figure coldly, abruptly spoke.

“Why are you not curious?”

Then Hanseol walked out from behind the building in the rear. She had been there since earlier. She hadn’t deliberately hidden, but had failed to find an opportunity to step forward.

“What do you mean?”

“About what schemes they are plotting.”

“Why are you so certain I am not curious?”

Geommugeuk turned to look at her and asked.

“Are you curious?”

“Of course I am curious.”

It was a lie. In truth, she was not curious. She thought their ulterior motives were obvious. They wanted to become stronger. And thereby maintain and expand the hegemony of the North Sea.

Because she thought it was something her mother would resolve anyway. Her mother would know everything. In this North Sea, it was impossible to hatch a plot while evading her mother’s eyes.

But Geommugeuk’s thoughts were different.

“This time, I feel your mother will be in dire need of her daughter’s help.”

Chapter 405: Evidence That It Wasn't Suicide.
---------------------------------------------
“What do you mean by that, all of a sudden?”

When Geom Mugeuk spoke as if he had read her mind, saying she ought to help her mother, Hanseol was inwardly startled.

The reason Geom Mugeuk had said it was because he had the force behind this matter in mind. If the Blood King was targeting the Ice Palace, they would have to join hands to stop him.

Of course, he could not tell her that.

“The reason the Ice Palace Mistress accepted the condition I put forward was probably because she had made some sort of deal with the Northern Blood Sect. She must have saved the Northern Blood Sect Master’s second child and received compensation for it.”

Hanseol could not deny his words.

“And in the midst of that, we entered the Ice Palace. Your mother will be preoccupied with the Northern Blood Sect matter, so I meant that you would have to deal with us.”

Was that really why he had said it? It had felt a little more important than that.

“Young Cult Leader, there is no need for you to involve yourself in the affairs of this palace.”

“I said it for your sake.”

Aside from her mother, Hanseol never held long conversations with anyone.

For one thing, she kept her business brief, and who would dare draw out a conversation with the Young Palace Mistress?

Yet this Young Cult Leader kept making her speak. No—he made it impossible not to.

“Why do you say it’s for my sake?”

“Because I simply want to help.”

“There is no such thing as goodwill without reason in this world.”

After saying so firmly, she began to walk away.

She disliked receiving goodwill, but she disliked even more the situation in which she had to return it because she had received it. The emotions exchanged between people, that give-and-take, felt to her like passing around hidden weapons smeared with poison.

Geom Mugeuk followed after her.

“Why are you following me?”

“That’s hardly something someone who hid and eavesdropped on me should say.”

For a moment, Hanseol flared up.

“Eavesdropped? I merely…”

“Merely?”

“There was no opportunity for me to step forward.”

“Just as there is no goodwill without reason in this world, there is no grave without an excuse.”

Hanseol gave him a look of disbelief and began walking again. Come to think of it, she realized it had been a very long time since she had flared up like this. She truly disliked people like him, people who provoked others and stirred up their emotions however they pleased.

“Do not fall under the delusion that everyone in the world will be captivated by you. They simply… pretend to like you because you are the Young Cult Leader. If you were a low-ranking martial artist, do you think they would like you?”

“I would probably have been beaten half to death for being a mere low-ranking martial artist who talked too much.”

Hanseol started to burst out that she had not been joking, then stopped herself. If she did, he would only respond with some other absurd remark.

She said nothing more and continued on.

The martial artists passing by greeted her respectfully.

Hanseol accepted their greetings with the same expressionless face as always.

Then Geom Mugeuk interfered even with that.

“Are you always this stiff with your subordinates?”

“We are martial artists. Why expect kindness?”

In the end, she stopped walking again. This man’s values were far too different from hers.

“Did those people come here to play? Can I laugh and joke with them, then order them to go out on a dangerous mission where their lives are at stake? If I give that order, will they go willingly?”

Geom Mugeuk said nothing.

“Why are you silent?”

“You’re right. What am I supposed to say?”

Hanseol stared at Geom Mugeuk quietly, then left him with one final remark before turning away.

“Even if you are not a low-ranking martial artist but the Young Cult Leader, someday you are going to get beaten to a pulp.”

Hanseol strode off as if telling him not to follow. Geom Mugeuk’s voice came from behind her.

“Let’s have a drink together sometime! The Drunken Demon is abstaining from alcohol, so I haven’t been able to drink! Ian won’t drink with me either, saying she has to stay loyal to him.”

That was how he was until the very end. The Drunken Demon abstaining from alcohol? And what? His subordinate would not drink because she was staying loyal? Then was he saying she looked disloyal enough to drink with him? Just how much did he intend to mock people?

And so she left.

The playfulness vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face as he watched her go.

There was no way to approach her by ordinary means when she had built an invisible wall around herself, so he was digging in like this.

The reason he was doing so was not because he wanted to brighten her personality or fill the hole in her heart. It was for her safety and that of the North Sea Ice Palace.

If the Blood King was involved with the Ice Palace, he would surely have stretched his hidden hand toward Hanseol as well. Or he would try to. That was how they had acted until now.

Geom Mugeuk looked around. In this barren land, the towering buildings and the enormous warrior statues made of ice gleamed as they reflected the sunlight.

“Blood King, where are you?”

Where have you hidden that cruel red blood of yours?

* * *

The North Sea Ice Palace had provided them with three rooms in total.

Geom Mugeuk said to Ian,

“You stay in the middle room.”

“So the two of you can protect me from either side?”

“We have to protect you if we want you to make us delicious noodles.”

They had no idea what was happening inside the North Sea Ice Palace, so they had to be as cautious as possible. The Drunken Demon would take care of himself, and if Ian was in the next room, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art he had mastered to great completion would react.

“Before coming here, I sent word to Eunwol and the Tongcheon Pavilion. I asked them to concentrate every bit of information-gathering power they can on the North Sea Ice Palace and the Northern Blood Sect.”

If the two intelligence organizations joined forces, they would exert tremendous power.

“I think the force behind this is the same people who plotted within the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance.”

The Drunken Demon and Ian nodded in agreement. They were the same ones who had been operating in secret even in the Pungcheon Cult beyond the borders. If they were not in the Ice Palace, that would be the stranger situation.

“Since they’ve been struck once already, this is even more dangerous. Please be careful, Young Master!”

“It’s fine. Hyung will protect you. Right? Why aren’t you answering? ‘I’ll take care of it!’ Why won’t you say it again?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s urging, the Drunken Demon pretended not to hear.

“Ah! I want to drink.”

“You mustn’t go back to how you used to be!”

Watching the two converse, Ian smiled faintly.

She had truly never dreamed that the Drunken Demon was this kind of person. He had always been drunk, and she had feared whether she would even be able to communicate with him. She had thought that if he became angry, he would become intoxicated with emotion and his mad drunken habits would burst out.

More than anything, she had thought he would overcome his sorrow with drink.

But he knew. He knew that drinking and grieving was not true mourning.

Instead, he had quit drinking, was looking calmly at the world, and was governing his own heart. In that way, he was comforting himself. He knew that one had to know how to comfort oneself in order to truly mourn.

“Be especially wary of the Northern Blood Sect. We discovered what they tried to hide.”

At the Drunken Demon’s words, Ian asked worriedly,

“Then won’t the Northern Blood Sect try to erase all evidence and cut off the tail?”

“They will.”

The one who answered was Geom Mugeuk.

“Then is it all right for us to just stay like this?”

“It’s fine. The things they do in order to cut off the tail will become another tail.”

“I see.”

Though she answered as if she understood, Ian could not tell exactly how he intended to respond, and she let out a sigh.

She had never fought a battle like this before.

The force behind it, the Northern Blood Sect, the North Sea Ice Palace.

It truly felt as if she were standing in the middle of enemy territory, surrounded by terrifying foes.

“How does it feel?”

“I’m nervous.”

But after looking back and forth at the two people who would fight alongside her, she added one more word to that trembling.

“In a good way.”

* * *

The next morning, the investigation into the Head’s death began in earnest.

Hanseol personally came to fetch the three of them.

As if she had never flared up the day before, she treated Geom Mugeuk calmly.

“First, I will guide you to the late Head’s room. The Palace Mistress ordered that it not be cleared out yet, so it has been preserved as it was.”

That was fortunate for investigating this incident. But why had the Ice Palace Mistress ordered it not to be cleared? Because they had been close? Or because the Ice Palace Mistress herself had doubts about her death?

“What was her cause of death?”

At the Drunken Demon’s question, Hanseol answered,

“She drank poisoned wine.”

At the words poisoned wine, the corners of the Drunken Demon’s eyes twitched.

“Could she have been poisoned by someone?”

“That cannot be. She left a will.”
