As if she had expected the Drunken Demon to ask, she took the will from inside her robes and handed it to him.

It contained a confession that life had been difficult, along with apologies to the people of Baekju Snowfield who made the wine. It concluded with a request for the Ice Palace Mistress to forgive her disloyalty.

As the Drunken Demon read the will, something unusual passed through his sorrowful gaze.

“Our investigation confirmed that it was definitely the Head’s handwriting.”

“That’s right. It is certainly her handwriting.”

The Drunken Demon also acknowledged it. He had seen more of her writing than anyone, so at the very least, it meant the will had not been written by someone else.

“Where did she obtain the poison?”

“We did not investigate that.”

Since it seemed certain that she had taken her own life, apparently no investigation had been conducted.

“What happened to the Head’s body?”

“She was buried in the graveyard where the martial artists of this palace are interred.”

If she had already been buried, they could not determine what poison had been used from the corpse.

Hanseol guided the three of them to the Head’s room.

“I heard she had no family, so there is no one to come for her belongings.”

In that sense, the Drunken Demon was also the Head’s only family.

Together, they entered the Head’s room.

Upon stepping inside, the Drunken Demon mourned her for a moment.

“I have known you for so long, yet this is my first time entering this room.”

The room was neatly arranged. By the window stood a small bed just large enough for one person to lie on, and beside it were a small desk and chair.

“You must have sat at that desk and sent me your letters.”

The image of her sitting there and writing letters rose in the Drunken Demon’s mind.

Most of the books lined up on the desk were related to liquor.

Various parts and devices used in brewing were placed in a corner, and the clothes she wore while working were neatly folded.

Everyone felt the same thing.

She truly was someone who knew nothing but liquor.

On a display shelf against one wall stood several dozen bottles of liquor. They were bottles of all shapes.

“Since she kept them separately, they must be precious liquors, right?”

The Drunken Demon did not answer. No, he could not. Among the collected bottles were liquors he had sent her. Many of them were ordinary drinks that could easily be bought in the marketplace, yet she had kept them together with the rare liquors she had collected.

And that was not all she had kept.

The Drunken Demon found a box beneath her desk. Inside, she had carefully gathered the letters she had received from him.

“You kept all of these!”

The Drunken Demon did not know where the letters she had sent him were. Some were likely missing, and others were probably stuffed away somewhere.

Yet the Head had sorted and preserved the letters by date.

The Drunken Demon took one out and read it. It was worn with the marks of being read again and again.

“Ah, this happened too.”

A memory from a very long time ago returned to him. The letter contained things that made him wonder if he had really told her even such stories. His history itself was contained in those letters.

“May I take the letters she and I exchanged?”

At the Drunken Demon’s words, Hanseol readily answered,

“Please do.”

Even as he filled his bosom with the letters, the Drunken Demon showed no emotion.

“Now let us go to Baekju Snowfield.”

* * *

Baekju Snowfield was bustling with activity.

Behind the people loading ingredients onto carts and transporting them, they could see the place where the liquor was fermented, as well as the place where it was aged. There were also people bottling the finished liquor.

After they watched the workers for a moment, the man serving as the interim Head of Baekju Snowfield was called over. He was said to have been the person most trusted by the deceased Head.

“I have something to ask about the former Head.”

“Please ask.”

“Was there anything strange about her before she died? Did she behave differently, perhaps?”

“There was nothing of the sort.”

“Was there anyone she fought with?”

“The Head was not the sort of person to fight with anyone.”

They spoke with several people besides this man, but there did not seem to have been any internal conflict.

Before leaving Baekju Snowfield, the Drunken Demon investigated the liquor made there. The types of liquor produced, the production schedule, and where that liquor was sent.

After completing their investigation, the four of them left Baekju Snowfield.

“When it is this clear that she took her own life, is there any need to investigate further?”

If it was certain that she had taken her own life, the three of them would no longer have any justification to remain in the Ice Palace.

But in the face of Hanseol’s certainty, the Drunken Demon showed a certainty of his own.

“It wasn’t suicide, so we must investigate in earnest. Have the Ice Palace aid us more actively.”

When Han Seol looked puzzled, Chwima spoke with confidence.

“In today’s investigation, I found three indications and pieces of evidence that it was not suicide.”

“In what respect?”

Not only Han Seol, but Geom Mugeuk and Ian also looked curious.

Three of them?

“First, even if she had taken her own life, she would not have died after drinking poisoned liquor.”

“She loved liquor all her life, so couldn’t she have chosen to go on her final journey with it as well?”

Chwima firmly shook his head.

“A drunkard like me might have done that. But she was not a drunkard—she was someone who made liquor.”

Seeing that Han Seol still did not understand, Chwima added an explanation.

“I’m saying she was the sort of person who would sooner leap off a cliff than ever allow poison to be mixed into liquor she had made herself. Because she loved liquor in its purest form.”

Geom Mugeuk understood that sentiment like this.

A martial artist might be able to take his own life by driving his sword into his heart, but a swordsmith would not wish to stain a sword he had forged with blood.

“What are the other reasons?”

“I looked over the schedule for the Baekju Snowfield earlier. Five days after she died, the ice wine to be sent into the Palace Lord’s Hall was completed. That highest-grade ice wine can only be made during this period. It is also the most important day of the year at the Baekju Snowfield. And yet she took her own life without completing that final batch? Absolutely impossible.”

Geom Mugeuk and Ian nodded together. Han Seol did not react outwardly, but she too thought Chwima’s words made sense. It was a doubt that had been obscured by the fact that a suicide note had been found.

“What is the third and final reason?”

“That is the decisive one.”

Chwima spoke calmly, his gaze sunken.

“She left no suicide note for me.”

At the very least, he was certain she would never have left without saying anything to him.

“By leaving no note for me, she sent me her final message. She was asking me to reveal the truth of her unjust death.”

If she had written the note under threat? If she had foreseen her death, she might have left her final words for Chwima.

But by leaving nothing at all, she had told Chwima that this death was not suicide. It was something only possible with deep trust.

“Do you know what their mistake was? They killed the person who would have protected them.”

Chwima slowly stroked Blood Tears hanging at his waist as he spoke.

“The person who would have stopped me from getting drunk and going mad.”

Chapter 406: Try Being Fooled by the Young Cult Master
------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk could feel Chwima’s resolve.

He had made up his mind to kill everyone involved in this affair.

Once he had made that decision, it was not one Geom Mugeuk could intervene in, nor should he. This was the decision of the Demon Venerable.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Ian. Her face was flushed as she stared at Chwima. Ian, too, was sensing it instinctively—what Chwima had resolved to do. The resolve of a Chwima all the more terrifying because he was not drunk.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Han Seol.

‘Do you feel it?’

How could Han Seol not feel Chwima’s emotions? She simply could not understand them.

‘Why is he going this far?’

It did not seem like the sort of relationship that warranted this. If she had truly been a woman he loved, he would not have lived apart from her. They had merely exchanged letters and liquor once or twice a year. Was that not the full extent of their relationship?

Humans were creatures who deceived and fooled others even while staring straight into their eyes, so what could there possibly be in a relationship where they had not even seen each other’s faces?

Still, there was one thing she agreed with.

“It is true that there are questions surrounding the Plain Lord’s death.”

Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“Please help us so that the Ice Palace can actively resolve this matter.”

“What is it you want?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Chwima. He intended to leave this matter entirely to him.

But Chwima’s reaction was unexpected.

“You decide, Young Cult Leader. The smarter person should use his head.”

“You made such a splendid deduction just now, though.”

“That was only because I knew that person well.”

One could tell Chwima was intelligent simply from the fact that he could make such a judgment.

Chwima looked at Geom Mugeuk and spoke with his eyes.

—Take me to those bastards.

Geom Mugeuk answered with his eyes.

—I will. Without fail.

Geom Mugeuk said to Han Seol,

“Please grant us just one request. We need no other help. Only that one thing.”

Han Seol was curious. What kind of request was it that he would say only one?

“Please join our investigation team, Young Palace Mistress.”

At the completely unexpected request, Han Seol’s eyes widened.

“Let the four of us solve this!”

Chwima and Ian were deeply impressed.

If Han Seol joined them, there would be no need to make separate requests of her from now on. If they moved together with her, they would be able to handle most matters within the Ice Palace.

It was truly a moment where, if a demon asked, “What is your wish?” one answered, “To have you.”

And hidden within this request was one more intention of Geom Mugeuk’s that Ian and Chwima did not know. Since the Blood King might target her, he intended to keep her close.

“Are you saying that sincerely?”

“I am.”

The words of refusal rose all the way to Han Seol’s throat. Mingling with others was something her instincts rejected.

Even so, the reason she did not refuse immediately was that she, too, had begun to think the Plain Lord had not taken her own life. If that was the case, then for the sake of the Ice Palace as well, they had to find the culprit.

“I am already helping you, am I not?”

“You are not helping us as one of our own, are you?”

“!”

He was right. When she had accepted this proposal on her own authority, she had told her mother that she wanted to find out what kind of person the Young Cult Leader was. To be more precise, she had wanted to show that she could see through the exaggerations in the rumors about him.

‘Become one of them?’

Han Seol looked at the three of them with a strange, renewed feeling.

It was a truly bizarre combination. That excessively beautiful subordinate had come all the way to the Northern Sea to save the husband of a woman who had given him a single cup of water.

Chwima, who was abstaining from drink, said he would take revenge for someone with whom he had exchanged liquor and letters from time to time.

And that swindler-like Young Cult Leader, praised as a transcendent being, was asking her to join them.

Han Seol shook her head with an expression that said she could not understand. But her decision was the opposite of that gesture.

“Very well. I will join you. However, I have a condition.”

“Name it.”

“As we handle this matter, you must not hide anything from me.”

“Very well. I will tell you everything without omission. In return, promise me this. You must not avoid it because you do not wish to hear it. You must not complain that your ears hurt.”

And so Han Seol, too, became a member of the group investigating this incident. But the great mountain she truly had to cross began now.

“Then move your residence at once. I think you can stay with Ian.”

“Move my residence?”

“Why are you so surprised? From now on, we are one family, so of course we must sleep together, eat together, and think together.”

One family? Han Seol regretted her decision to join them. Merely allowing herself to become one of the same group had been a significant decision for her.

‘Just what do you want from me?’

Though he sensed that resistance, Geom Mugeuk pretended not to notice and said to her,

“There is a power that comes from the experience of eating and sleeping together. Take this opportunity to experience it once.”

Han Seol looked at Ian. He wanted her to sleep with that woman? She had never slept in the same room as another person before.

In this situation, Geom Mugeuk attended to Ian.

“I’m sorry for deciding without discussing it with you first.”

“No, I’m fine with it.”

Ian said to Han Seol,

“Please grant me the honor of being with you, Young Palace Mistress.”

Chwima also added a word.

“This is an important matter to me, so please yield this once, Young Palace Mistress.”

She was not inclined to, but in the end, Han Seol permitted it.

“Fine. Let us do that.”

It was not because of the two of them. Han Seol had only just begun her sparring match with Geom Mugeuk. She would prove, without fail, that Geom Mugeuk was someone who swayed and used people with words.

“What do we do now?”

Not only Han Seol, who had asked the question, but Chwima and Ian as well were at a loss. What were they to investigate now? Should they meet more people around her? Or should they examine battlefield records?

Fortunately, Geom Mugeuk was standing before the path they had to take.

“We must question it. Why the Plain Lord? Of all the many people in the Ice Palace, why did they have to kill the Plain Lord of Baekju Snowfield?”

And Geom Mugeuk found that answer in one person. Since Han Seol was present, he spoke respectfully.

“I believe the Demon Venerable already knows that answer.”

The gazes of the two women turned toward Chwima. Everyone was surprised, but Chwima was the most surprised of all.

“You say I know the reason?”

“Yes. You may not know who killed her, but you must know why she was killed.”

“I don’t know.”

Chwima was flustered, as though he had been accused of being the culprit, but Geom Mugeuk was firm.

“No. You do know.”

There was a reason Geom Mugeuk thought so.

“The Plain Lord had no problems within Baekju Snowfield, nor was she someone who had learned powerful martial arts. She had neither power, grudges, nor secrets. Then what reason could there be?”

The reason he had said Chwima knew was contained in these final words.

“Her death is surely connected to liquor.”

* * *

Only when the carriage entered the North Blood Sect did Yang Jung finally feel relief.

‘I’ve returned home.’

The carriage stopped only after reaching the great hall deep within the inner compound.

Yang Jung stepped down from the carriage. His father had not returned in the same carriage. It would have been natural for them to ride together and exchange various words.

He was angry. Yang Jung understood his father. To him, the North Blood Sect was more precious than his own child. Since this incident had caused great damage, it was only natural that he would be angry with him.

On the other hand, once Yang Jung actually returned home, his resentment toward his father faded.

When an exchange of hostages had been demanded, his father had abandoned him, but he had still made a deal with the Northern Sea Ice Palace and saved him. In any case, he had survived, and that was enough.

His father was alone in the great hall. He had dismissed all the warriors who usually guarded the place.

‘He means to reproach me for my mistake.’

He would not want to damage his son’s honor in front of his subordinates. Feeling his father’s consideration, his heart softened even more.

“Father.”

His father stood with his back turned in silence, looking out the window. Today, that back felt more unfamiliar and frightening than ever.

Yang Jung shifted all responsibility to the Demonic Cult without hesitation.

“I truly did not know the Demonic Cult bastards would intervene.”

At that, the North Blood Sect Leader asked coldly,

“Why did you tell them?”

“What do you mean?”

“The location of the laboratory.”

In that instant, Yang Jung’s heart sank. He had forgotten. He had told them every place where the people they had captured might be held.

‘I told them to survive. You refused the hostage exchange and abandoned me, so I did it to survive!’

But ordinarily, the two of them did not share such frank conversations.

Suppressing the emotions that surged within him, Yang Jung said what Geom Mugeuk had told him to say.

“I did it to lure them there and capture them. If the laboratory had been moved, I believed you would surely have prepared a secret measure against intrusion at the place it was moved to.”

But it did not work on his father.

“If he threatened to kill you and you had no choice but to speak, you should have told him a place that was not the laboratory. You should have insisted that you knew only that place, and no other.”

“It was a matter of life and death, Father!”

Yang Jung offered no excuses. It would have been good if the scolding had ended around this point, but the real reproach began now.

“Because of you, Hwang Su died.”

Hwang Su was the person who had been conducting the experiments there.

‘So what? Are you saying that bastard’s death is more important? More important than this son of yours surviving?’

It was something he had been storing up in his heart all this time. But he could not say it. It would surely only provoke his father’s anger further. His father would dismiss it as childish whining from someone who could not distinguish public affairs from private feelings.

“I was wrong. I will never make such a mistake again.”

But his father did not forgive him.

“Leave.”

“!”

“Go to Zhejiang and do not return until I tell you to.”

Zhejiang was far too distant from here. To Yang Jung, being told to leave for Zhejiang was no different from a declaration of death.

Yang Jung was shocked by the realization that his father intended to cast him out.

His father had made his two sons fight over the succession. His father had not placed all his trust in his eldest son. That was why Yang Jung had held hope. He, too, could become the successor.

And so he had truly done every kind of thing.

For the North Blood Sect, and for his father. He had done things that should never be known to the world.

But now he understood. He had not been dreaming together with his father. The dream had been his alone.

“I refuse. I will not leave!”

Yang Jung screamed. Until now, he had been too afraid of his father to reveal his true feelings properly, but he would no longer hold back.

“I did everything you ordered me to do. If you told me to kill, I killed. If you told me to kidnap, I kidnapped. And now you tell me to leave? I refuse!”

‘You used me as much as you pleased, like a hunting dog. If your child is a hunting dog, then what does that make you, Father?’

Those were words he could not bring himself to say aloud. But considering what was about to happen, it was a resentment he might as well have voiced.

Puuuk!

With the sound of flesh tearing, Yang Jung’s words and movements stopped.

His head lowered, and he stared at the bloodstained blade protruding through his chest. Was it because he was too shocked? It did not hurt.

Though he had seen so many times what it looked like when a sword pierced a person, the sight now was terribly unfamiliar.

Who could it be? If it was someone who could enter this place?

When he slowly turned his head to look behind him, the person who had stabbed him was the first person who had come to mind.

It was Yang Seok, the eldest son of the North Blood Sect and his elder brother.

His brother, who had always regarded him as a thorn in his eye during the battle for succession, was more than capable of doing something like this.

The person Yang Jung could not understand now was his father. That father, silently watching this scene. His brother would never have dared commit such an act without his father’s permission. He had been told to kill him if he said he would not leave.

“……If you were going to do this, why did you save me?”

To the question full of resentment came a cold reply.

“Because I could not allow the blood of the North Blood Sect to die by another’s hand. It can die only by our hands.”

It was nonsense. If so, he should have agreed to the hostage exchange. Instead of killing him, he should have sent him to Zhejiang by any means necessary. If he refused to go, he should have persuaded him. He should have sealed his blood points and sent him away. If he was his father, then that was what he should have done….

Yang Jung’s resentment ended there.

Puuuuk!

The sword was ripped out harshly, and Yang Jung collapsed as blood gushed from his chest, dying on the spot.

Ju Gyu and Hwang Chu, who had been kidnapped together with Yang Jung earlier, had already been eliminated as well.

The North Blood Sect Leader said to Yang Seok,

“The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult has begun investigating this matter while staying at the Ice Palace.”

“We will cut off every loose end that could lead back to us.”

“The life and death of our sect rests on this. Handle it with care.”

“Leave it to me.”

After Yang Seok left, the Lord of the North Blood Sect looked down at his dead son. His heart ached. But it was a pain he could endure. If the North Blood Sect were to collapse, that would be unbearable.

* * *

The Drunken Demon was walking about the courtyard.

‘I know the answer. The answer is inside me.’

Geom Mugeuk’s conjecture seemed plausible. She was not someone who would have earned another’s grudge. If she had been caught up in a plot, then surely it would be one related to liquor.

While pacing like that, the Drunken Demon took out the letters exchanged with her and read them. Then, after walking again, the Drunken Demon stopped and read another letter. Looking up at the sky, thinking this and that, then walking once more.

Ian was watching the Drunken Demon from the window inside the room.

“Lord Drunken Demon seems to have a great deal on his mind.”

Han Seol gave no answer and merely sat on the edge of the bed. She had not even unpacked the belongings she had brought.

“Do you regret it? Deciding to come with us?”

Han Seol only sent her a cold look, still saying nothing.

Ian stopped trying to speak to her and looked out the window again.

It was grave outside, and grave within. As for Geom Mugeuk, the one who had given both of them such heavy worries, he had stepped away, saying he had somewhere to go for a while.

Just then, Han Seol abruptly spoke from behind her.

“Does it not occur to you that you are being deceived?”

Ian turned her head and looked at Han Seol.

“By the Young Cult Leader?”

Han Seol nodded.

Ian could tell how Han Seol saw her. She thought Ian was being deceived and used by Geom Mugeuk.

Ian considered simply giving her a smile and leaving it at that, but decided she should not. It was fine if Han Seol thought that of her, but Ian could not stand for her to think that of Geom Mugeuk.

“Perhaps.”

Though she answered as if admitting it readily, that was the beginning of “teaching her who Geom Mugeuk truly was.”

“By being deceived like that, I cured the side effects of the martial art I had learned. I never said it aloud, but they were side effects that troubled me greatly.”

Ian did not reveal the specifics of those side effects.

“By being deceived like that, I learned swordsmanship I would never have dreamed of, and became the head of an organization I would never have dreamed of leading. By being deceived like that, I even became the adopted daughter of someone I would never have dreamed of calling father.”

Han Seol was not curious what swordsmanship it was, nor what organization it was, but she was curious who this father was—someone Ian said she could never have dreamed of in her lifetime.

“Who are you speaking of?”

Ian answered honestly. It was not a secret anyway.

“The Fist Demon, one of our cult’s Demon Venerables.”

At the words that Ian was the Fist Demon’s daughter, Han Seol was startled.

‘This woman is the Fist Demon’s daughter?’

Was that why the Young Cult Leader cherished this woman? No. Ian was saying that it was because she had met the Young Cult Leader that she had been able to become the Fist Demon’s daughter.

“By being deceived like that, I became drinking companions with the Hwangcheon Pavilion Master and the Demon Army Lord, and the person who was going to become the next Demon Venerable became a squad leader in the organization I lead.”

Not a single thing she continued to say was ordinary, so Han Seol began to suspect Ian might be lying. But would she dare lie so openly to her? About things that would be revealed if she looked into them?

“And now I am conversing with the Young Palace Mistress of the Northern Sea Ice Palace. If I had not been deceived by our Young Cult Leader, my life would have been spent standing guard in front of a door.”

Ian’s gaze was clearer and calmer than ever.

“If that is the life one lives while being deceived, isn’t it rather fine?”

It was not merely to clear up her misunderstanding about Geom Mugeuk. If it had been someone else, Ian might have let it pass.

Yet strangely, she wanted Han Seol to know properly. Even though this chilly Young Palace Mistress was not the sort of person Ian would normally like.

“It’s hard to believe, isn’t it? Sometimes I can’t believe it either. There are many times when I wonder if this is a dream. That’s why I told you before to try trusting him once. Ah, I suppose I should put it differently now.”

After a brief pause, Ian said,

“Try being deceived by the Young Cult Leader once.”

Their gazes became entangled in midair. Han Seol could not understand it, nor could she believe it. And yet, in Ian’s clear eyes as she looked at her, she could sense no falsehood.

At that very moment, the Drunken Demon hurried into the room, looking for Geom Mugeuk.

“The Young Cult Leader? Where is the Young Cult Leader?”

“He went out, saying he had something to attend to. What is it?”

The Drunken Demon was somewhat agitated, and with good reason.

“The answer was inside me!”

Chapter 407: If You Prepare, So Will I.
---------------------------------------
I was drinking tea in a tea house, watching the people passing by.

People walked along the snow-covered road without so much as slipping. Just as those who lived beneath the sandstorms beyond the frontier made their lives there, the people of this North Sea lived alongside the snow.

The person I was to meet here was someone I would be seeing for the first time today. So I had no idea which of the passersby might suddenly enter the tea house and sit before me.

The reason I had pulled my bamboo hat low so as not to stand out was not to hide myself, but because of the person I was meeting. For him, secrecy was life itself.

How long had I been there?

A middle-aged man naturally took the seat across from me.

“Goodness, it’s cold.”

The ordinary-looking man with a generous face was none other than the head of Eunwol’s North Sea branch.

All of Tongcheon Pavilion and Eunwol’s intelligence networks were now focused on the North Blood Gate and the North Sea Ice Palace, and Eunwol had sent word that there was something urgent to report. It was something that could have been conveyed from afar through voice transmission, but I had wanted to meet the branch head at least once.

“Have a cup of warm tea first. You’ve been working hard.”

I poured tea into his cup. Even if I could not offer liquor to a subordinate who had been toiling away, I at least wanted to give him a cup of warm tea.

Liquor would have been better, but the reason I had chosen a tea house rather than an inn as the meeting place was because of Chwima. I had told Hanseol that we would drink next time, but until Chwima drank, I intended not to drink either.

While I poured the tea, the man swiftly sent a voice transmission.

—Yang Jung, the second young master of the North Blood Gate, is dead.

It was unexpected news. I had anticipated that Yang Jung would be pushed out of the succession because of this incident, but still.

Dead? The courteous figure of the North Blood Gate Master, who had said he would invite me, came to mind.

‘He was far more ruthless than he appeared.’

Just what was he plotting, to the point of even killing his own child?

There would be any number of reasons they could attach to his death. It was the death of the North Blood Gate Master’s son, so there would be no one to step forward and investigate the cause.

This report allowed me to infer one thing with certainty.

That the North Blood Gate was connected to the Blood King.

Otherwise, he would not have dealt with this matter so hastily, even to the point of killing his own child.

—From now on, focus on the movements of the North Blood Gate’s eldest son.

—I will send word as soon as we detect any unusual movements.

—Thank you.

The man left first.

I remained a little longer, drank the rest of my tea, then left the tea house.

As I opened the door and stepped outside, a thought suddenly occurred to me.

The name North Blood Gate had been chosen by fate.

A gate that would guide me to the Blood King.

Ah, Blood King. Do you know this? The northern gate is the gate to the underworld.

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk returned, Chwima conveyed welcome news.

“I found out why that person was killed.”

He had not told Ian or Hanseol yet, intending to speak of it once Geom Mugeuk returned and everyone was present.

“I knew our Demon Venerable would uncover it.”

In front of Hanseol, Geom Mugeuk treated Chwima not as his older brother, but as the Demon Venerable.

Now that everyone had gathered, Chwima revealed how he had discovered it.

“First, believing that the matter was definitely related to liquor, I examined the letters she and I had exchanged recently. Most of our conversations were about liquor.”

Since they were letters Wonju had collected, naturally what he read were the letters he himself had sent. That had made it extremely difficult to find a clue.

“Then I discovered this passage.”

Chwima showed them a line.

—I, too, would like to taste Fragrant Snow Ice Wine.

Fragrant Snow Ice Wine was a liquor neither Geom Mugeuk nor Ian had ever heard of. Hanseol, on the other hand, knew it well.

“Fragrant Snow Ice Wine isn’t just ordinary liquor. It’s liquor with a special effect.”

“What effect?”

“It helps one endure the cold. Of course, drinking alcohol normally warms the body and makes the cold less noticeable, but with Fragrant Snow Ice Wine, that effect is remarkable.”

Chwima, too, knew well the effects of Fragrant Snow Ice Wine. And the moment he saw the words Fragrant Snow Ice Wine, one fact came to mind.

“The constitution those bastards were trying to find this time was the Extreme Cold Physique, wasn’t it?”

The Extreme Cold Physique and Fragrant Snow Ice Wine. Both shared the common point of resisting the cold.

“Isn’t that rather coincidental?”

Geom Mugeuk and Ian nodded.

At that moment, Hanseol raised a question.

“Isn’t that reason alone insufficient to say that Wonju died because of it?”

“You’re right. It is.”

Chwima readily acknowledged it, then added another decisive reason.

“As far as I know, Fragrant Snow Ice Wine can only be made with permission from the Ice Palace.”

“That’s right. Liquor with special effects must be made with the palace’s permission.”

“But earlier, when I looked into the recent liquor production at Baekju Seolwon, there was nothing about Fragrant Snow Ice Wine.”

“There wasn’t? That can’t be.”

If that was the case, it was certainly suspicious.

“She must have made it secretly for someone.”

Chwima agreed with Geom Mugeuk’s conjecture.

“The fact that she told me about it means she never imagined that liquor would be used for something wicked. Most likely, someone she knew well asked her to make it. Someone she trusted.”

It was a conjecture possible because he knew her well.

“Did she say who asked her to make it?”

Chwima shook his head, revealing his regret.

“She didn’t tell me that much. She said it was her first time making Fragrant Snow Ice Wine and that it had been a truly valuable experience, and I replied that I wanted to taste it.”

“To find the answer from just that passage. Impressive.”

Geom Mugeuk praised Chwima. It was not empty flattery, but sincere praise. Chwima was certainly more intelligent than he had expected.

“Since I’m not drinking, it seems my head is spinning nice and clear!”

Smiling at Chwima’s joke, Geom Mugeuk looked at Hanseol.

Hanseol understood what Geom Mugeuk wanted.

“I’ll look into who asked her to make Fragrant Snow Ice Wine.”

She answered, but could she truly find out? If it had been made without the palace’s permission, they would have thoroughly hidden the process. Had he read her thoughts?

“Move together with Ian.”

Hanseol refused Geom Mugeuk’s suggestion.

“It’s more convenient to investigate alone.”

Sleeping in the same room together was already enough of a burden. Now he wanted them to go around together during the day as well? She wanted no part of that.

“I know.”

“Then why are you doing this?”

“Because you may be in danger.”

It was an unexpected reason not only to Hanseol, but also to Ian and Chwima.

“You’re worried about my safety, the Young Palace Mistress, inside the Ice Palace?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk conveyed the news he had just learned.

“Yang Jung is dead.”

The most surprised person was Ian.

“He died? Why?”

“To the North Blood Gate Master, perhaps even he was a tail to be cut off.”

Those words contained everything.

In any case, Yang Jung was someone who had deceived and brought in so many people, then killed them through experiments, so he deserved to die. But the fact that the person who killed him was his father was shocking.

Hanseol was shocked as well, but not because of Yang Jung’s death. She was shocked because Geom Mugeuk knew of his death before she did.

‘Their intelligence is better than ours? Not anywhere else, but here in the North Sea? Does Mother know of this news?’

Geom Mugeuk warned Hanseol, who had briefly fallen into thought.

“You must never let your guard down. The essence of this fight is that a child of the North Blood Gate Master has met death within the North Blood Gate.”

To put it more broadly, it meant this was a fight in which the blood kin of the Ice Palace Master might die within the North Sea Ice Palace.

“So move together with Ian.”

From the look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he gazed at her, Hanseol felt an authority she could not defy. It was not an overbearing attitude. It was a compulsion born from a sincere desire to protect her.

“Very well.”

Hanseol did not refuse any further. After all, even as the daughter of the Ice Palace Master, this was the first time she had felt such tension.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Ian. There was no need for words, voice transmission, or even a nod. She would handle it well on her own. A single meeting of their eyes was enough.

Once the two women left the room, only Geom Mugeuk and Chwima remained.

“They’re searching for a way to endure extreme cold. Why? Are they trying to cultivate some evil art we don’t know about? Or are they plotting a conspiracy related to the North Sea Ice Palace?”

At Chwima’s question, Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat.

“There’s somewhere I need to go. Hyung, secretly protect Ian and the Young Palace Mistress.”

“Where are you suddenly going?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke with an inscrutable smile.

“I’m going to prepare for the cold as well.”

“What does that mean?”

Leaving behind the curious Chwima, Geom Mugeuk also left the room.

“Answer me before you go! Hurry! I told you, I’m sensitive because I haven’t been drinking!”

* * *

Even if I had wanted to answer him, it was something I absolutely could not tell him. I had come to handle something related to my life before the regression.

The place I had flown to in an instant with Swift Steps was a snow mountain.

Among those vast snow mountains, it was the place where the wind blew coldest and fiercest: Daepunggok. The Great Wind Valley, said to be where the strongest wind in the world blew.

Whoooooooooosh.

A terrifyingly powerful wind blew through the valley. It was a valley of wind known to be impassable even to experts who had mastered martial arts. A place where the wind tore apart human bone and flesh. On top of that, snow whipped through the wind, making it difficult to see.

The people of the North Sea also called the wind here the Mad Killing Wind. As the name suggested, it truly was a mad wind, a wind of death. It was so cold and fierce that even if one raised internal energy to protect the body, crossing the valley was no easy task. It was truly a mystery crafted by nature.

After hearing the conjecture that those bastards were searching for a way to prepare against cold energy, I finally realized that the moment had come for me to visit this place.

Before the regression, when I wandered in search of materials for the Great Art, I traveled everywhere. If I encountered a place like this, I would instead go in all the more tenaciously.

I had even come as far as this place.

Whew. I truly have no confidence I could live that life again.

Whoooooosh.

I pushed through the wind and continued walking into the valley.

The deeper I went, the stronger the wind became.

The wind blew so fiercely it seemed it would even shred my protective qi aura, and because I had to use internal energy just to keep from being blown away, advancing inward was truly difficult.

‘It was around here.’

As I went on, I entered a crevice in the right wall. It was a space where one could hide for a very brief moment from the wind outside.

Back then, I had hidden myself like this as well.

It was probably fate that I discovered there was a passage leading elsewhere at the far corner of this crevice. After all, that had been a time when I went around inspecting every nook and cranny.

There was another gap in a place utterly impossible to notice with the eye. It was a narrow crevice behind a place blocked by thick vines.

It looked like somewhere one would never want to enter. The thought naturally came to me that if one got stuck while going in, it would be the perfect place to die.

I went inside. I kept moving through that passage where a single person could barely squeeze through.

After passing all the way through, a slightly wider space emerged.

As though I had entered another world, sunlight streamed in there, and grass and trees grew. Outside, a knife-like wind was blowing, yet here, a stream trickled gently. It was a place where one could feel the mystery of primordial times.

“It’s been a while.”

At the center stood a single tree. Hanging from that tree was a white fruit. It looked as though a lump of ice had been suspended there. This was the legendary elixir.

The Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit.

It could grant far more internal energy than the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng, and on top of that, it had one astonishing effect.

Not only would one become resistant to cold, but one could also comfortably accept any energy of the cold attribute. That was not all. It could allow one to withstand attacks imbued with extremely cold internal energy.

For example, even if one were struck by the Ice Palm, it meant the cold energy would not damage one’s blood vessels and meridians. Conversely, one could master the Ice Palm more easily than anyone else.

Through the Heavenly Secret Pill I had obtained from the Poison King in the past, I had gained a body impervious to cold and heat, but this Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit was an elixir specialized in warding off cold. If the two elixirs overlapped, I would be able to overcome even extreme cold that surpassed the limits.

In my life before the regression, I had been unable to consume the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit.

The cold energy contained within the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit was so powerful that it could not be consumed as is. The moment one greedily swallowed it, one’s blood vessels and meridians would freeze solid. It was probably an elixir that only the Ice Palace Master, who had reached great completion in the Ice-Cold Divine Art, could consume.

I still remember it.

The words I said as I left the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit behind.

If we are fated, let us meet again next time.

Back then, I had believed that, after my regression, I might come back to this place again.

Now I could try. I had gained internal energy incomparable to what I had before, my meridians had grown sturdy, and above all, I had achieved great completion in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. I believed that the perfected Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art would shield my body from the cold energy emitted by this Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit.

If that belief were lacking even in the slightest, I must not consume the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit. It was certain I would suffer severe internal injuries.

But I felt that all of this was fate. Before my regression, I had discovered this place; I had achieved great completion in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art; and my enemies had found an Extreme Cold Constitution and were trying to use it.

Fate was telling me.

That now was the time to consume the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit. No—not merely that I could, but that I absolutely had to.

Along with fate, my instincts said the same.

If you are preparing for the utmost cold, then I too shall prepare.

“Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, I’m counting on you.”

The moment I grasped the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit in my hand, a chill spread through me as though my entire body would freeze solid. Merely touching it, the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit was warning me of its danger. If you eat me, you will die.

Without hesitation, I consumed the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit. The instant it entered my mouth, I felt a cold unlike anything I had ever experienced in my life. I had thought it would be hard and would not melt easily, but I was wrong. The moment it entered my mouth, it melted in an instant, and an extreme chill spread through my body.

The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art had already activated, resisting the frigid cold energy.

Sssssssssss.

It was a cold that made a scream rise of its own accord. Truly, it was hard to believe that something so cold could exist in this world. Even with the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art activated, I felt pain as if my entire body were freezing over. I wanted to expel this energy from my body at once. Fear seized me that I might actually die like this.

“Endure it. I have to endure.”

The entire time I melted the energy of the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit, I recited the formulas of the Nine Flames Demonic Art and called the evil spirits to mind. I thought of east, west, south, and north, and I thought of the Heavenly Demon Souls I had seen in the illusion.

I thought of everything related to the Nine Flames Demonic Art and the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. When that still was not enough to endure it, I thought of my father. I pictured him looking at me and sneering.

It worked. Even in that agonizing situation, astonishingly, a smile formed on my lips.

I endured, and endured again.

And the result was a tremendous success.

I had succeeded in melting all of the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit’s energy.

A great quantity of internal energy was added to my dantian, and above all, I achieved what I had wanted. The cold energy of the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit had settled permanently throughout my meridians.

Now, unless an energy even colder than the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit invaded my body, no cold energy would be able to do anything to me.

I was delighted at the thought that I had gained another weapon with which to oppose them. This was how I would grow stronger, step by step.

“I will be sure to use this power for a good purpose.”

After clasping both hands and bowing to the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit tree, I left that place.

Passing through the narrow passage, I emerged once more into the valley where the violent wind blew.

Whooooooooosh.

An incredible wind assaulted me.

Though I had not raised my internal energy to protect my body, the flesh-cutting wind did not feel cold at all. With a slight exaggeration, the murderous gale had now become a spring breeze to me, a warm wind.

Before my regression, I had turned back the way I came from here. I could not go any farther into the valley.

But today, I was curious. What lay at the end?

And so I began walking toward the source of the wind.

Chapter 408: Let’s Have a Drink When We Meet Again.
---------------------------------------------------
Hanseol and Ian were walking together through the inner court of the Ice Palace.

Hanseol had taken on the task of finding out to whom the former palace lord had brewed the Hyangseolbingju, but in truth, she was at a loss.

‘Who should I ask?’

This was not a matter of intelligence or stupidity. It was a matter of experience. She had never investigated an incident even once.

Even so, she had no intention of giving up. Asking her mother for help was also not an option she had considered.

“Let us start by returning to Baekju Seolwon.”

Her attitude toward Ian had grown somewhat more courteous. Until now, she had treated her as a subordinate of the Little Palace Lord, but now she had begun to treat her as the daughter of Gwonma. Even if she was an adopted daughter, one could not treat the daughter of the Demon Venerable carelessly.

“That is a good idea.”

“Why is that?”

“When I saw the late former palace lord’s room, I thought to myself, ah, this person truly loved brewing liquor. He likely spent more time at Baekju Seolwon than at home. So would not the answer we seek also be there?”

At Ian’s words, Hanseol nodded slightly.

‘Everyone is so sharp.’

This time, Geommugeuk, Chwima, and Ian had completely shattered her prejudices regarding demonic humans.

She had thought demonic humans were cruel, violent beings who did not hide their desires.

But these three were not the Little Palace Lord, the Demon Venerable, or the subordinate she had originally imagined.

‘Even so, I cannot lose to demonic humans.’

Since Chwima had investigated the missive and found a clue, she too had to show something. Not to the Little Palace Lord.

She wanted to show her mother.

The sight of a daughter overwhelming them. Though she was someone who had never wished to look good to others, toward her mother alone, such feelings still remained. Not that she liked her mother. No, perhaps her mother was the person she hated most.

“You have come again, Little Palace Lord.”

The acting lord of Baekju Seolwon welcomed the two, but Hanseol had not come to meet him.

There, Hanseol met Jo Myeong, the person in charge of the ingredients used in brewing.

Jo Myeong was extremely nervous at Hanseol’s visit.

“I have something to ask you.”

“Please speak.”

“As you must be busy, I will not speak in circles. Did the late former palace lord ever request ingredients to brew Hyangseolbingju?”

“No, never.”

Perhaps due to nerves, Jo Myeong’s voice trembled.

“This is important, so you must not deceive me.”

When Hanseol looked at him coldly, Jo Myeong bowed his head.

Then Ian stepped forward.

“I believe you must have promised the former palace lord. To tell no one.”

Jo Myeong, head still bowed, said nothing.

“We suspect that the former palace lord passed away because of that liquor.”

The moment she said that, Jo Myeong flinched.

“Would not finding those who caused the former palace lord’s death be more important than keeping a promise?”

Jo Myeong’s hesitation was palpable. He must have been deeply connected to the late former palace lord to deny it so adamantly. He was not the type to deceive people without batting an eye.

Jo Myeong raised his head and asked cautiously.

“Did he truly pass away because of that liquor?”

“I believe so.”

The Little Palace Lord took up Ian’s words.

“Opportunities do not always come. If you miss this chance, there will never be another to re-examine the former palace lord’s death.”

It was spoken in a rather cold tone, but the words had an effect.

Jo Myeong hid nothing more and revealed the truth.

“He quietly summoned only me and asked me to procure the ingredients to brew Hyangseolbingju. He said it was liquor covertly requested by a precious person. He asked me to keep it secret, so I did. I am sorry.”

“Did he say to whom it was being sent?”

“I do not know that either.”

“Was there anyone who was close to the former palace lord?”

“There is no one in particular to mention. He preferred to be alone.”

There was nothing more to ask, so Hanseol stopped her questioning.

“You tampered with the liquor ingredients and left no records, so you will be punished.”

“I am prepared.”

She could sense that he had always respected the former palace lord. Indeed, that must be why he had secretly provided the ingredients.

Ian warmly comforted him for speaking honestly.

“The former palace lord would undoubtedly be grateful as well.”

Thus the two came outside.

The words telling them not to punish him had risen to Ian’s throat, but she did not speak. Even if she felt forgiving, she might cause him to be punished by speaking up needlessly.

“You are kind to people in a way unbecoming of a demonic human.”

At that, Ian asked.

“In this martial world, between a ruthless person and a kind person, who survives longer?”

“Well, obviously.”

Hanseol did not finish. The answer was so clear that there was hardly any need to say it.

But Ian’s thoughts differed from hers.

“I am of the belief that kind people survive longer.”

Kindness? It was an emotion Hanseol had never experienced. She had never received kindness, nor had she ever treated anyone kindly.

In any case, Hanseol did not agree with her words. Preaching kindness in this ruthless martial world?

Just then, Jo Myeong ran up to them and said.

“I was too nervous earlier and could not recall, but I have seen the former palace lord drinking with Elder Seo several times. He was not one to keep company with others, so it stands out in my memory.”

Jo Myeong bowed and went back.

Ian said to Hanseol with a smile.

“This, too, is a result born of kindness.”

Even at her jest, Hanseol’s expression grew grave.

“Is something wrong?”

“It is nothing.”

But Ian could sense it. The moment she heard the name Elder Seo, something had weighed on Hanseol’s mind. Ian calmly said to her.

“Was it not the Little Palace Lord’s condition that we must hide nothing?”

“Don’t you hide anything, since I will hide” — you are not thinking this, are you?

When even Ian’s eyes asked this, Hanseol finally spoke the words she had been hesitant to say.

“Elder Seo is the person my mother trusts most.”

Elder Seo was a figure of great influence within the Ice Palace and could be called the Ice Palace Lord’s greatest supporter.

“Then the former palace lord may have felt at ease fulfilling his request.”

He was a fitting candidate for the condition that it would have been brewed for a trustworthy person. Elder Seo was also a person of excellent character.

But in a situation where they believed Hyangseolbingju was related to her death, that the recipient was Elder Seo?

Whether her mother knew was a problem, and whether she did not know was also a problem.

Surviving through kindness?

“Elder Seo is also the only person our mother treats kindly.”

* * *

Whooooooosh.

The wind grew ever stronger, but I kept moving my feet.

The reason it had originally been difficult to walk here was not merely the wind’s strength, but the terrifying cold carried on the wind.

It was a cold no human could endure, so blocking it consumed an enormous amount of internal energy.

Internal energy used to keep from being blown away, internal energy used to block the cold. One had to expend internal energy doubly.

Meanwhile, now I did not have to worry about the cold, only the wind’s strength.

Having already become impervious to cold and heat thanks to the Cheongidan, after consuming the Ten Thousand Year Snow Fruit as well, its efficacy was truly miraculous to an astonishing degree. This biting cold that could freeze blood felt like nothing at all.

So I had thought I could reach it easily.

Yet with every step forward, the wind grew fiercer. With each step, the sound of the wind changed.

I could swear to it. Unless one was a chosen person, one could never pass through this place.

I, too, am a chosen person.

After regressing, I had gained an enormous amount of internal energy and mastered the Nine Transformations Demonic Art and the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art. Moreover, I had consumed the Ten Thousand Year Snow Fruit. Even someone like me doubted whether I could reach the end—so who on earth could break through this place?

‘Why did they seek the extreme physique?’

The thought that perhaps it was to break through this place had briefly crossed my mind, and that was part of the reason I had tried coming.

But having actually tried it, that was definitely not the case. They had to be scheming something else entirely.

After advancing a bit more, the wind blew so fiercely that I could not think of anything else.

My internal energy and the distance to the end. The gradually strengthening wind. I calculated all of it. I could not recklessly push my internal energy to the limit, yet if I tried to conserve it, I would simply be blown away. Added to that, I calculated the minimum internal energy needed to protect my body when my strength gave out and I was sent flying.

Walking while using only that calculated amount of internal energy, pain began to set in. It hurt. It truly hurt. To think the wind could hurt this much.

But I could not raise my internal energy further to avoid the pain. Even greater pain awaited me later. I had to endure this pain to reach that place.

More internal energy was needed than I had anticipated. I had to muster internal energy to protect my eardrums. The wind was so loud it could damage my ears. The internal energy consumed in that way returned as pain.

Huuuuuuuuuung!

‘Why must I go there? At this rate, I might suffer internal injuries.’

The desire to avoid pain tempted me endlessly.

If this were not my life before the regression, I would never have been able to press forward like this.

Back then, I had suffered countless temptations. Give up. How could a human regress? Give up now and live my life.

Because I had overcome those endless temptations and walked that lonely path alone.

‘I can walk this path too.’

Walking and walking.

The abundant internal energy in my dantian was now nearly bottoming out.

‘Just a little, just a little more.’

At the last moment, I felt the sound of the wind vanish and my surroundings grow silent.

Could this be a hallucination? If I fell into qi deviation like this, it would be disastrous.

If it were such a precursor symptom, I had to give up and turn back immediately.

That was the final temptation.

I resolutely took another step. Because the way I had always overcome temptation was by silently taking that one step.

Guooooooo!

The moment I overcame the temptation, a sound of wind unlike anything I had heard before rang out again.

Thus I finally reached the source of the wind. The wind’s strength was at its peak. I used every last reserve of internal energy to bring my protective astral barrier and Heavenly Demon Guardian Art to their limits.

‘I’ve made it! Just here! Please!’

Enduring the pain of my whole body being torn apart, I took the final step.

The moment I passed through the wind valley and twisted my body aside, the pain vanished as if by a lie. I had escaped from that tremendous wind.

“I did it!”

A cry of ‘I survived’ escaped me.

Wind was blowing into the valley I had just escaped. How fiercely it must have been blowing for waves of wind to be visible to the eye.

The source of the wind was the sky. Wind descending diagonally from the far-off sky was rushing fiercely toward the valley. It looked as if a demon were sucking in all the world’s air through its mouth. It was truly a mystical, unfamiliar, and fearsome sight.

And I realized.

That reaching this place itself was a greater fortuitous encounter than any other.

Breaking through to this place, I had exerted all the internal power I possessed.

I had brought forth every ounce of potential a martial artist possessed. Having used that potential until not a single drop remained, I felt as though I could see the martial artist that was me transparently.

I felt as if my martial arts realm had risen. It seemed I, as a person, had grown stronger as well. It was a strange experience and sensation.

I was certain that this first-time experience would surely affect the Nine Transformations Demonic Art as well.

Leaving behind the demon’s mouth devouring the wind, I slowly moved my steps.

Far behind, a massive cliff blocked the way. It was so high that the top could not be seen. The ridgeline of the snow mountain blocked all directions behind. As if this were a place one could not enter without breaking through that wind valley.

Before it lay a spring landscape. Flowers bloomed and birds flew about. Rabbits and deer saw me but did not flee. They had the eyes of those seeing a human for the first time.

Mysterious energy overflowed. It was the primordial mystery of a place that had never admitted a human since the beginning.

And I saw.

A single person standing in the midst of it all.

He was an old man standing with his hands behind his back. An old man in such a place? He was clearly no ordinary person.

Someone came to mind in an instant. Surely not? It could not be him?

“Elder?”

I cautiously called out to the old man. Then he slowly turned toward me.

Astonishingly, he was the very old man who had sent me back in time.

“Elder!”

The old man grinned broadly and shook the liquor bottle he was holding.

“Did I not say we would share a drink next time we met?”

They were the words the old man had said right before sending me back.

So I had a premonition that the old man might appear before me again someday. I never thought it would be today.

“I am abstaining from alcohol, though. You came at this time on purpose, did you not? To tease me.”

The old man cackled loudly.

“You have brightened up a lot since then.”

“It is thanks to you.”

The old man, who had been looking at me with deep eyes, slowly approached and patted my shoulder.

“It has been hard, has it not? You are doing well.”

The moment I heard his words, my chest tightened.

To others, I tried so hard to express all sorts of things.

It seemed I had wanted to hear them too.

I must have wanted to be comforted by someone who knew my efforts from beginning to end. That I had lived this earnestly from before the regression until now. So please, just say those words—that I am doing well. That hearing just that one phrase would be enough.

“Since you are abstaining, let us drink next time.”

“Are you leaving already?”

“As you know, many people seek me, so I am very busy.”

Still, it sounded like he might come to meet me again, which made me feel glad.

Whooooooosh.

A sudden gust of wind blew.

The old man vanished before my eyes along with the wind. Along with him, the paradise-like scenery around me vanished as well. The trees, grass, and animals all disappeared with him.

Now the surroundings were nothing but snow and ice.

Because it came and went in an instant, it felt like a dream. Had I seen an illusion? I had exhausted both my mental strength and internal energy to the point of collapse.

But it was not. The hand that had patted my shoulder was all too vivid. I was so happy and glad to see him again.

Had I forgotten to look up at the sky these past few days, that you came to see me directly? I, too, shall look up often, so please watch over me until the very end. Stop pretending to be busy when we meet. And... I am truly grateful.

Chapter 409: The One Time He Got Angry.
---------------------------------------
I didn't simply leave Wind Valley.

How hard had I struggled to get here—as if I'd just up and leave?

First, I scoured the interior of the valley. Never before had I been to a place so thoroughly cut off from the outside world.

If I made this place my safe haven, it would be perfect. Never mind secrecy; even if an enemy knew I was here, this was a place they could never enter.

Of course, to bring people I needed to protect inside, I would need far more internal energy than I had now.

I resolved that once I reached a realm where I could enter this place with ease, I would build a villa here.

There was something I absolutely had to do before leaving.

I immediately unfolded the Spacetime Displacement Technique.

I planned to perform the Nine Transformations Demonic Art once before departing. To break through this place, I had used all my latent potential. In that process, I had gained enlightenment, and I wanted to verify that achievement.

Though there was no one here, the reason I deployed the Spacetime Displacement Technique was partly to protect this place's interior, but mainly because I needed to perform the Temporal Heaven Secret Art. To me, internal energy was time, and time was the lives of me and those around me.

Within the Spacetime Displacement Technique, I performed the Nine Transformations Demonic Art. I earnestly went through its forms from the beginning to those I had mastered.

First Form: Annihilation.

Saaaaaaahk!

To an enemy, it would be utterly horrifying and terrifying, but to me, it was an all too familiar sight—the illusory figures of four evil ghosts appearing simultaneously in the east, west, south, and north, swinging their swords.

Swhack! Swhack! Swhack! Swhack!

And today's Annihilation Form was different from before.

In my previous attainment, the four evil ghosts of the cardinal directions had grown faster and stronger; today, there was a change on a different level.

They did not vanish immediately. Instead, they looked at one another. The fearsome one gazed at the handsome one, and the mysterious one exchanged glances with the clever-looking one.

Until now, I had only sensed instinct from them, but today, for the first time, I felt reason and emotion.

Truly, it lasted only an instant, but it felt like the four of them were muttering among themselves!

That feeling was unmistakable.

Before vanishing, the clever-looking one among them glanced at me. I could clearly sense reluctance to part in its eyes.

Just before the four evil ghosts of the cardinal directions disappeared completely, I spoke to them.

"Greetings."

They vanished silently, so in the end it was just a soliloquy, but it was the first greeting I had ever offered them. It felt like taking yet another step closer to the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Next, I performed the Second Form: Grand Annihilation. One look at the Grand Annihilation Form made it clear that my realm in the Nine Transformations Demonic Art had risen.

Sssssssssss.

Originally, the fearsome evil ghost at the center of the four directions split into eight, but now two more had been added, splitting into ten.

Kwaaaaaaak!

The ten evil ghosts swept forward, obliterating everything before them. No matter when I performed it, Grand Annihilation was always spectacularly fierce.

Third Form: Great Demon Wall.

What else would it be but a wall of fierce energy? The Great Demon Wall had grown smaller yet again. The Great Demon Wall congratulated me on my accomplishment by reducing its size.

And then, the Fourth Form.

Dark Flash (暗黑一閃).

Huk!

Darkness descended. A darkness deeper and heavier than before.

Shwiiiing.

A single ray of light streaked across the darkness.

It was faster and more powerful than the previous flash. The darkness had deepened, and the light had brightened.

I had not yet attempted the Fifth Form. Instinctively, I felt that now was not the time to advance, but to consolidate what I had gained.

I considered this achievement a gift from the old man. A gift given to me for working hard and doing well even after my regression. Having broken through the wind to reach this place was also thanks to his guidance.

"I shall continue to do my best going forward."

Striving again and again, so that even if the result turned out wrong, I could still say these words.

Since this had been my best, I had no regrets.

Only at such times could I dream of revenge; yet paradoxically, that very attitude would prevent me from being consumed by vengeance and allow me to live my life fully.

And lastly, when leaving this place, I did not train arduously.

Having endured at the source of the wind with my internal energy pushed to the extreme.

"I'm leaving noooooow!"

I released my hold and let the mad wind carry my body, flying toward the entrance. I flew with sheer exhilaration.

If I ever missed the wind, I would return.

* * *

Hanseol fell into deep thought.

The moment the name Elder Xu came up, she grew grave.

He was the person her mother trusted most and the most respected figure in the Ice Palace.

The quick-witted Ian guessed where her quandary was ultimately pointing.

'She's afraid the Palace Lord might be involved.'

In other words, she couldn't trust her own mother.

At that thought, Ian remembered someone she had forgotten for a long while: a name that made things hard whether present or absent.

Ian controlled her emotions and spoke calmly.

"If he's an important figure in the Ice Palace, I think we need to approach this carefully."

"First, I should meet Elder Xu. I'll go there alone."

"Did you visit him often?"

Hanseol shook her head.

"Then he'll definitely be suspicious."

"What if we say we came out to investigate something specific?"

It would be fortunate if they learned something from that. This was what worried Ian.

"Can you tell if someone is lying? I'm not confident I can, especially when the opponent is an elder."

As if Hanseol would be any better at noticing?

"If Elder Xu is involved in this matter, he'll undoubtedly lie. If we can't detect that lie, we'll only reveal that we're suspicious of him, won't we? Our cards will be exposed before we've even begun to fight in earnest."

She certainly had a point.

"Then what should we do?"

Ian cautiously made a suggestion.

"Let's inform the Young Cult Leader."

Hanseol's expression stiffened slightly. They had found out about Elder Xu through their own efforts—were they giving up already? She wanted to uncover the full truth of this matter through her own strength.

Having guessed her feelings, Ian was all the more careful.

"I think you've already played a major role, Young Palace Lady, simply by finding evidence that Elder Xu is involved."

It was a proposal she would never have accepted under normal circumstances.

"Do you believe the Young Cult Leader will have a clever plan?"

"Yes, I do."

"How can you be so certain?"

"Because he has shown us plenty until now."

Hanseol pondered briefly.

"Fine, let's return for now."

Since it involved Elder Xu, she had to handle it with care. She also wanted to see firsthand what abilities the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult possessed.

They returned to their quarters in high spirits and asked Chwima where Geommugeuk was.

"The Young Cult Leader has gone out."

At a time like this, he could have stayed. They had come back thinking, "Just trust our Young Cult Leader," yet here he was, gone again at this crucial moment.

Ian hid those thoughts and smiled.

"He must have gone to take care of something important."

Then Chwima, in a playful mood, said something entirely unnecessary.

"He said he was going to prepare for the cold, didn't he?"

"Pardon?"

"That's all he said before leaving."

"Did he go to buy clothes or something?"

Even as she said it, Ian realized how flimsy her own response sounded.

Ian let out a sigh and looked at Hanseol.

"Shall we go meet Elder Xu right now?"

Hanseol laughed at Ian's joke.

"Huh? You just laughed."

Hanseol said with a straight face,

"I suppose scoffs count as laughter?"

Just then, someone spoke from behind them.

"A scoff is laughter too. Indeed, I know the most magnificent scoff in the world."

They turned to see Geommugeuk entering.

Ian shouted delightedly.

"Young Cult Leader! Where have you been?"

"I went out for some air."

He had truly gotten enough air for a lifetime.

* * *

Half a shichen later, visitors arrived at Elder Xu's residence.

"The Young Palace Lady has come with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult."

Despite the unannounced visit, Xu Luo was not surprised.

"Show them in at once."

Shortly after, Geommugeuk and Hanseol entered the main hall.

"This is our first meeting. I am Geommugeuk."

"So I finally meet the protagonist of the rumors that shook the Central Plains."

The two exchanged formal greetings.

Xu Luo possessed a benevolent smile, noble speech, and dignified bearing. He had such a warm air about him that it seemed he would forgive any wrongdoing with gentle words.

Indeed, he was a man widely respected not only within the Ice Palace but among the martial artists of the North Sea.

"I heard you are investigating the death of the Lord of Baekju Seolwon?"

"Yes, with the Palace Lord's permission. Since I am a junior of the martial world before I am a Young Cult Leader, please treat me comfortably."

Geommugeuk put on a relaxed smile, just like Xu Luo. The meeting was off to a good start.

"He was a man to be regretted."

"Have you ever had a drink with the Lord?"

At Geommugeuk's casually thrown question, Hanseol tensed instead. The testimonies from Baekju Seolwon had stated the two had shared drinks several times. Would he tell the truth?

"We've had a few drinks. But it was mostly talk of wine; I did not know the Lord well."

Xu Luo did not hide the facts.

"What do you think of the Lord's death?"

"I don't know what you mean."

"Do you think there is anyone who might hold a grudge against him?"

Xu Luo held his answer briefly.

"Could it be you've come to investigate me?"

"Is that not allowed?"

At the provocative retort, a momentary, taut tension filled the air. Soon, Geommugeuk smiled and eased the tension.

"A jest. Just a jest. I said I should investigate those around the Lord, and the Young Palace Lady here agreed. Among those around him, the most trustworthy is the Palace Lord, and the next is you, Elder Xu. So I came to see you first—to remove you from the list of suspects early on."

Xu Luo smiled kindly and looked at Hanseol.

"Thank you for thinking of me that way."

"Not at all."

Hanseol slightly bowed her head.

"By the way, you have decorated this main hall splendidly."

Geommugeuk walked over to one wall and examined the display cabinet set before it. All manner of ornaments were placed there.

Meanwhile, Xu Luo looked at Hanseol with his ever-unchanging, benevolent smile.

She had grown up thinking of him as an uncle from a young age. She had heard that her mother had received much help from Elder Xu when she rose to the position of Palace Lord. But now that she was here looking at his face, this thought came to her:

'This isn't the place for this.'

She had heard testimony that the Lord had shared drinks with Elder Xu several times, not that there was evidence Xu Luo had served him the wine.

Originally, she should have been standing by Xu Luo's side, watching Geommugeuk.

'What are Demonic Cult members doing here?' Yet now, alongside the Demonic Cult members, she was suspecting him. How had it come to this?

When she had thought of it vaguely, Elder Xu had seemed suspicious, but now that she saw him smiling at her, she felt sorry for no reason. How different a person's heart could be when seeing someone in person versus not.

"Have you heard the news of the Northern Blood Sect?"

Geommugeuk asked while still examining the items in the display cabinet. Hanseol tried to say something about his rude attitude, but Xu Luo raised his hand to stop her. He had not lost his composure in the slightest.

"What news do you mean?"

"Their second young master lured people into dangerous experiments."

"I haven't heard of it."

"I see."

After a brief pause, Geommugeuk glanced back and asked,

"You don't ask what kind of experiments?"

"Isn't it obvious? That second young master has always been an eccentric one."

"So he is."

Geommugeuk dropped the conversation and touched the ornaments in the cabinet. Hanseol could not sense any of the specialness Ian had spoken of in Geommugeuk. Transcendent? The only thing transcendent about him was his rudeness.

"Is there anything else?"

"No, I came simply to greet you today."

"As expected of the Demonic Cult. Even a simple greeting is enough to put people on edge."

"Why would you be nervous if you've done nothing wrong?"

Hanseol quickly bid farewell.

"We shall take our leave now."

"Come again if you have questions. I shall assist you anytime."

"Thank you."

The two paid their respects and turned to leave. As they walked toward the door with Hanseol, Geommugeuk turned as if he had just remembered something and asked abruptly,

"By the way, what did you use the Fragrant Snow Ice Bead for? Did you go somewhere cold?"

It was Hanseol who was surprised. She hadn't expected Geommugeuk to ask such a question.

Despite the sudden question, Xu Luo was not flustered.

He tilted his head as he answered.

“The Fragrant Snow Ice Pearl? Why ask me about that?”

“Did you not receive it from the Pavilion Master?”

“I never received it.”

His response was so natural that he did not seem to be lying.

“I see.”

After bowing politely, Geom Mugeuk was about to turn away when he looked back once more and asked,

“You’re not asking again? How I knew he gave you the Fragrant Snow Ice Pearl, I mean. Wouldn’t most people be curious? If someone said something about them that wasn’t true.”

“What does that matter? What matters is that I did not receive it.”

Seo Nak did not ask, but Geom Mugeuk told him anyway.

“In the letter the Pavilion Master sent to Lord Chwima, he wrote that he had given the Fragrant Snow Ice Pearl to Elder Seo.”

“I don’t know why the Pavilion Master wrote such a thing, but I have never received the Fragrant Snow Ice Pearl.”

“There must have been some misunderstanding.”

After offering their farewells, the two of them stepped out of the main hall.

Once they had fully left the place behind, Han Seol asked,

“Why did you do that?”

“I was probing him here and there to see if he was lying.”

“You were the one who lied.”

The Pavilion Master had never mentioned Elder Seo to Chwima.

“By lying yourself, did you manage to uncover Elder Seo’s lie?”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“I couldn’t tell.”

“Because Elder Seo wasn’t lying.”

“Perhaps not.”

Geom Mugeuk readily admitted it for the sake of what he would say next.

“However, there was one thing that was strange.”

“What was that?”

“After we met him today, there was exactly one moment when Elder Seo became angry. Do you know when?”

“You were so rude so many times that I can’t tell. So? When was it?”

Then an unexpected answer flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s lips.

“When I moved the objects on top of the display cabinet and threw them out of order.”

Han Seol had not noticed that such a thing had happened, nor that Seo Nak had grown angry. Only Geom Mugeuk, who had secretly released his qi throughout the conversation and carefully observed Seo Nak’s emotions, could have known.

“Did you see that display cabinet?”

“No.”

“There wasn’t a single speck of dust, and the objects placed on it were lined up with not an inch out of place. To the point that it felt compulsive.”

“The maidservants must clean it thoroughly.”

“That’s the bigger problem.”

“What do you mean?”

“How frightening must the master be for them to clean it that thoroughly?”

The problem was that it was not a matter of the maidservants.

“If Elder Seo truly had no interest in the display cabinet, he wouldn’t have reacted that way when I disturbed the ornaments.”

Through that incident, Geom Mugeuk had discovered an important fact.

“He has two contradictory sides. A benevolent side that smiles good-naturedly, and a neurotic side that does not permit even the slightest disorder.”

Han Seol was inwardly impressed. What she had failed to recognize despite seeing him for so many years, Geom Mugeuk had grasped in that brief span of time.

Only now did she begin to understand why Ian had said they should inform the Young Cult Leader. Even his rude behavior must have all been calculated.

“Even so, in the end, it’s only his personality, isn’t it?”

She was right. Though the contradictory nature of his personality was unexpected, it was not evidence proving he was behind this matter.

“That’s right. So let’s find out a little more.”

“How?”

That, too, was a method Han Seol had not imagined.

“This time, instead of objects, we’ll shake the people around him.”

Han Seol was seized by a strange tension. Because the first and closest person among those around Seo Nak was someone who, in all her life, had never once shown herself shaken.

‘Can you really shake my mother?’

Chapter 410: You Must Look Back More Often.
-------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk and Han Seol returned to their quarters and told the Drunken Demon and Ian what had happened when they met Seo Nak.

When Geom Mugeuk finished speaking, Han Seol said to Ian,

“It was a good judgment to suggest we leave it to the Young Cult Leader instead of handling it ourselves.”

She had thought Ian was being deceived by Geom Mugeuk, but this incident changed her mind a little. Even if he was not some transcendent being, there was certainly something extraordinary about him.

Geom Mugeuk’s mischievous gaze turned toward Ian.

“A subordinate who pushes work onto his leader?”

“At times like that, a good leader pretends not to notice and lets it pass.”

“It’s hard, living as a leader.”

“Young Cult Leader, you’re the one making it hard. Other leaders all live easily.”

Han Seol still found this kind of conversation unfamiliar. She was convinced that such an easy relationship with one’s subordinates was bound to bring serious side effects.

“Do you know who is closest to Elder Seo?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Han Seol answered honestly.

“The closest person to him is the Palace Lord.”

“Would you arrange a meeting with the Palace Lord for me tomorrow?”

“I will.”

“But I’ll go alone tomorrow.”

“Why?”

Geom Mugeuk gave an unexpected reason.

“Because when one’s child is present, a palace lord becomes a parent instead.”

Han Seol thought Geom Mugeuk still did not know about her mother.

“I don’t think you need to worry about that.”

“There are parental feelings that a child can never know.”

“How would the Young Cult Leader know a parent’s heart? You aren’t even married yet.”

Geom Mugeuk was not the sort to be at a loss for an answer to such a question.

“When you have someone like my father as your parent, you end up studying parents very diligently. What is a parent!”
