When Han Seol stared at her with wide eyes, Ian said with a smile,

“It’s a joke, a joke. I should take revenge first, then follow him in death.”

Ian smiled at Han Seol, who still said nothing.

“That was a joke too.”

“It isn’t funny.”

It was not a situation for jokes, but Ian was trying hard to keep her heart at ease.

On the outside, she seemed peaceful and carefree, but right now a storm was raging in her heart.

If she did not at least read a book, her heart would pound so hard she could not endure it, and in her mind, the sight of Geom Mugeuk being killed by the enemy kept surfacing.

A scene kept unfolding where if she simply threw herself in and helped for just one move, he could live.

‘Yes, this is a side effect of serving as a bodyguard for too long.’

In the past, when Geom Mugeuk had left the cult alone and returned, she had simply thought vaguely that he would surely come back safely. But being close by together made her worry a hundred times more.

‘Young Master has been fighting battles like this all along.’

Then Han Seol asked,

“Would you like some tea?”

“Yes. I’ll prepare it.”

“No, keep reading.”

Han Seol went and began brewing tea.

Feeling uncomfortable just sitting there, Ian walked over beside her. Just as she was looking around to see if there was anything she could help with, her gaze went to the tea set that had been placed there.

On the white teacups, two birds were drawn in flight, and their appearance was distinctive.

“Why are you looking at it like that?”

“The birds engraved on this teacup… I feel like I’ve seen them somewhere.”

Han Seol glanced at the Ice Palace Mistress standing by the window, then said to Ian again,

“That can’t be.”

She meant that it was an original pattern, but Ian still tilted her head.

“I’m sure I saw it.”

The Ice Palace Mistress turned her head toward Ian. A strange light passed through her eyes.

It was just then.

A subordinate hurried up and reported.

“The battle is over.”

* * *

When the three women, who had rushed over urgently, arrived at the residence where the fight had taken place, they were left speechless.

It was a sea of blood. Dozens of corpses were strewn everywhere, and the fishy smell of blood was so strong they wanted to block their noses.

At the center of that sea of blood were two people.

Chwima lay on the blood, and Geom Mugeuk was leaning against a broken stone lantern, looking up at the sky.

The moment Ian saw that sight, her heart dropped.

She thought Chwima, lying on the blood, was dead.

But the sound of snoring reached her ears. The resounding noise was saying, “I’m safe, so don’t worry.”

Seeing the three of them, Geom Mugeuk made a shushing gesture. It meant he did not want to wake Chwima, who was asleep.

Geom Mugeuk had also experienced the same condition as Chwima many times. When sleep poured over you after fighting with all your strength. At times like this, you had to sleep. Following what the body desperately wanted—that was the best treatment. Trust the body of a master.

Seeing this place in person, the Ice Palace Mistress once again felt just how grave this battle had been.

If a Demon Venerable had fought until he ended up in that state, she could understand how strong the enemy must have been.

She examined a corpse nearby.

These were the Blood King’s subordinates who had died by Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

Just from their trained bodies, she could guess the realm of their martial arts. They were masters. If they had learned the Self-Destruction Art as well, truly many warriors of the Ice Palace would have been sacrificed.

All their vital acupoints had been cut by a sword.

‘He severed all their blood vessels before they could use the Self-Destruction Art.’

Just by looking at the cause of death, she could tell how remarkable Geom Mugeuk’s martial realm was.

Lastly, the Ice Palace Mistress looked at Chwima again.

Was it because he was lying in the blood?

She felt even sorrier toward him. If not for him, countless warriors of the Ice Palace would have been submerged in that blood.

“Let us go back.”

Following the Ice Palace Mistress, Ian and Han Seol turned around.

Before Ian turned away, Geom Mugeuk waved at her first with a bright expression that said, I’m all right.

At that wave and smile, the storm that had been raging in Ian’s heart stopped.

She too smiled brightly, then turned around.

It is not difficult to like someone. What is truly difficult is making effort and struggling so as not to lose the person you like, or the feelings of affection you hold for them.

Because happiness must suffer the challenge of the anxiety that it may shatter at any time. Because the storm blowing in the heart does not cease even when morning comes the next day.

* * *

Chwima opened his eyes at the cold sensation he felt on his cheek.

What had fallen on his cheek was snow.

Snow was falling from the darkening sky.

When he turned his head and looked to the side, Geom Mugeuk was looking up at the snowy sky.

“You awake?”

“How long did I sleep?”

“About three sichen.”

“My mouth didn’t freeze crooked, did it? In this cold place…”

Chwima stopped speaking. It was not cold. The blood-soaked ground and the surroundings were warm.

Chwima could tell. Geom Mugeuk had warmed the surroundings with his yang heat qi.

Chwima tried to raise himself and screamed.

“Aigoo! It hurts. It hurts so much.”

“Want some liquor?”

“No. My head feels like it’s going to split.”

He had drunk so much throughout the fight that the hangover was tremendous.

“If I drink again…”

“You’re my little brother!”

“That’s right. If I drink again, I’m your little brother. Ah, my head hurts so much it feels like it’ll burst.”

Chwima, who had been clutching his head, happened to look down at the ground.

“Aaagh! What’s with all this blood? Is it that bastard’s blood? Don’t tell me you left me lying here?”

“You don’t remember a thing, do you?”

“Ugh, the smell of blood is awful.”

“You were lying there earlier and even smiling.”

“Are you drunk too?”

Then he saw the soles of his feet and was startled.

“My feet! What’s wrong with my feet now?”

Broken liquor bottles were embedded in the soles of his feet. He had fought drunk without even realizing his feet were being torn up.

Chwima pulled out the broken bottle shards stuck in his soles.

“Uuuuugh, it hurts. You could’ve pulled these out while I was drunk.”

“If I was going to do that, I would’ve pulled out the thing stuck in your shoulder first.”

At that, the startled Chwima looked at his own shoulder.

“Aaagh! What’s this now?”

Only then did he see the sharp fragment of a sword lodged in his shoulder.

“When did this get stuck in me?”

“There’s one in the other arm too.”

“Call a doctor! Hurry and tell a doctor to come! The Demon Venerable is in pain!”

Chwima shouted with exaggerated fuss.

As if Geom Mugeuk would have simply left him lying there for several sichen. He had confirmed that the external wounds had not reached the bone, and had secretly sent true qi into his body to examine the inside as well. After confirming, fortunately, that he had suffered no internal injury, he had left him to sleep.

“For a martial artist, wounds are something to boast of.”

“These wounds aren’t even worth boasting about.”

Chwima looked down at the blood still pooling thickly on the ground.

“On the outside, he was a handsome, impressive fellow, but inside, he was worthless. The bastard himself hadn’t actually learned the Self-Destruction Art.”

Geom Mugeuk was surprised by that fact as well.

Before his regression, he had not known much about the Blood King. He had only known the cruel deeds committed by his subordinates.

“If he weren’t worthless, he wouldn’t have implanted the Self-Destruction Art in his subordinates in the first place.”

As if he agreed, Chwima nodded.

The snow began falling heavier and heavier. For a moment, the two of them looked up at the snow falling from the sky that had begun to darken.

“Now the Manor Lord will be able to close his eyes in peace.”

What had begun with the Manor Lord not sending liquor had come this far. It had begun with Chwima and ended with Chwima.

And at the center of that incident had been included the most important matter Geom Mugeuk had to resolve.

Fate was flowing onward, entangled together with the people around him like this.

“Before we go back, let’s have a drink at the peak of the snowy mountain.”

At Chwima’s words, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“Sure thing, little brother.”

* * *

Chwima washed, ate, and fell asleep again.

The most skilled physician in the Northern Sea Ice Palace came and examined Chwima before leaving. He also prescribed medicine that would help his body recover quickly.

While Chwima was resting like that, Geom Mugeuk was meeting with the Ice Palace Mistress.

“You have suffered greatly.”

“Not at all. I am truly grateful that you yielded and allowed us to handle it.”

She could roughly guess, but this was what the Ice Palace Mistress was curious about.

“Did you learn what that man’s objective was?”

“The battle was too urgent, so I did not hear it directly, but I suspect he was trying to use the Extreme Cold Physique to neutralize Your Palace Mistress’s martial arts.”

Geom Mugeuk added one more conjecture.

“He searched for an Extreme Cold Physique for a long time. If his goal had merely been to harm Your Palace Mistress, he would have used the Self-Destruction Art far more actively. I expect there must have been another reason he needed an Extreme Cold Physique. Does anything come to mind?”

The moment she heard those words, the Ice Palace Mistress thought of one reason. Even so, she did not answer at once because it was not something to be revealed lightly to outsiders.

If it were anyone else, she would never have told them, but at least Geom Mugeuk should be made an exception.

“It seems he was aiming for the Ice Palace Treasury.”

The Ice Palace Treasury was a place one could enter only by mastering the secret martial arts passed down to the Palace Mistress of the Northern Sea Ice Palace, or by possessing the Extreme Cold Physique.

“An ordinary person could never withstand the cold there.”

“Then it seems certain he was aiming for the Ice Palace Treasury.”

Now that even his concrete objective had been revealed like this, the Ice Palace Mistress’s heart turned cold.

She deeply regretted not killing him herself.

At the same time, this thought occurred to her.

What if Geom Mugeuk and Chwima had not come to the Northern Sea? What if they had not killed Elder Seo and spirited away the Extreme Cold Physique? What if they had not uncovered the fact that the Northern Blood Sect had joined hands with them?

Would they truly have been able to withstand this conspiracy?

An answer filled with certainty that they could have stopped it did not come easily.

If they had not stopped it? She and Han Seol would have met death, and the Northern Sea Ice Palace would have been taken from them.

That was why there was nothing she would begrudge Geom Mugeuk.

“Is there anything you want? If there is, I will grant anything.”

After pondering for a moment, Geom Mugeuk said,

“There is one thing.”

“What is it?”

“Please allow me to choose one treasure from the Ice Palace Treasury and take it.”

It was an unexpected request.

“What is it you want? Tell me, and I will take it out for you. Whether it is a spiritual medicine or a treasured sword, tell me anything.”

“No. I want to go in and see it myself.”

It was because of the secret chest. At the Pungcheon Cult, he had obtained the black crystal, and at the Evil Dao Alliance, he had obtained the blue crystal. Perhaps he could obtain something related here as well—that was his expectation. If not now, it would be an opportunity difficult to gain again.

“Did I not say earlier? If you do not have the Extreme Cold Physique, you cannot enter.”

Geom Mugeuk said with an inscrutable smile,

“I am strong against the cold.”

Because she did not think Geom Mugeuk was someone who would joke about something like this, the Ice Palace Mistress asked cautiously,

“Are you perhaps also an Extreme Cold Physique?”

“I am not.”

“Then you cannot go there. Absolutely.”

Though she attached the word absolutely, Geom Mugeuk spoke confidently.

“Why not trust me once?”

Could there perhaps be a demonic art that warded off cold? Even so, it should not be able to block that cold.

“I am someone who even endured my father’s cold and frosty paternal affection.”

“This is not the time for jokes. You may die on the way.”

The Ice Palace Mistress was certain that Geom Mugeuk absolutely could not go there. Still, it was a request from the man who had saved the Ice Palace. Also, she was curious why he insisted on going there. And she wanted to coldly break this childish stubbornness as well.

“Let us go. I will hear your regret and reflection after I bring you back to life.”

Following after her, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Doesn’t everyone who comes to the Northern Sea experience their heart freezing solid and stopping at least once?”

Chapter 430: Three Reasons for Giving a Gift.
---------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk walked behind the Ice Palace Lord.

Naturally, he had assumed the Ice Palace Treasury would be outside the palace. Somewhere deep in the Northern Sea, somewhere amid snowstorm-lashed mountains, in a place so cold it would freeze the heart.

But the Ice Palace Lord led Geom Mugeuk deep into the interior of the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

“This is the first time I have shown it to an outsider.”

“It is an honor, Palace Lord.”

“It is my repayment for saving this palace.”

After passing through a series of gates guarded by the Ice Palace’s elite warriors, they came upon a frozen lake.

“From this point on, step exactly where I step and follow me.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. A formation had been laid over this lake that appeared ordinary at first glance.

The terrifying thing about this formation was its unexpectedness.

He had never once experienced a formation laid over a frozen lake. While everyone’s mind was occupied with the tension of whether the ice beneath their feet might break, the true danger would devour them from somewhere else entirely.

Geom Mugeuk stepped precisely where the Ice Palace Lord had stepped and followed behind her.

What would happen if he failed to find the Gate of Life? Would the ice break and send him sinking into the freezing lake? Or would he be forced to wander forever across the surface of this lake?

The Ice Palace Lord, who had been walking in silence, abruptly asked,

“That child named Ian—is he of a demon practitioner’s bloodline?”

“No. He was entrusted to our cult when he was very young.”

The Ice Palace Lord continued walking in silence. Somehow, her back looked as though there was something she wanted to say.

“But why do you ask?”

Whether in appearance or temperament, Ian was no ordinary person, but knowing the Ice Palace Lord’s nature, Geom Mugeuk had not expected her to take such an interest.

“No reason. The thought simply occurred to me. Watch your step.”

Once again, the Ice Palace Lord diligently stepped along the Gate of Life and crossed the lake.

When they passed through the formation, a valley appeared. It was a narrow path between cliffs, one that would be filled if three or four adults stood shoulder to shoulder.

Whoooosh.

The wind was cold from the very entrance. Not merely chilly—truly cold.

This was the true path to the Ice Palace Treasury.

“It suddenly grew cold.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, a strange smile formed at the corners of the Ice Palace Lord’s mouth.

“The cold has yet to even begin.”

The Ice Palace Lord was slightly excited at the thought of letting the young cult lord taste the cold. Saving the Ice Palace was one thing, but since he had stubbornly insisted on going somewhere no one without the Extreme Cold Physique could reach, she intended to show him the fierce cold winds of the Northern Sea.

“Now, let us go.”

This time, the Ice Palace Lord did not walk ahead. She walked side by side with Geom Mugeuk. She had to observe his condition as they went. Regardless of whether he was an expert or not, one wrong move could kill him.

Truly, it grew colder with every single step.

“Do not force yourself to endure too much.”

“It is still bearable.”

If ever there was a time to say one’s lungs were freezing solid, this was it. It was not cold because a biting wind was blowing. It felt as though they were approaching a place where ultimate cold existed. One step forward brought them into a colder space, and the next step into a space colder still. The change was immediate and drastic.

Shiiiiii.

There was no wind blowing, yet a strange sound arose.

“Are you all right?”

From the halfway point onward, the Ice Palace Lord checked Geom Mugeuk’s condition with every step.

But Geom Mugeuk was perfectly fine. He did feel the cold, but it did not affect his body or turn into pain.

The Ice Palace Lord worried that Geom Mugeuk might freeze while standing upright.

But Geom Mugeuk walked just fine. If anything, he strode more vigorously than she did.

Shiiiiiiii.

Though no wind blew, the farther they walked, the louder that strange sound became.

The farther they went, the more astonished the Ice Palace Lord became. If he did not possess the Extreme Cold Physique, then this meant there existed some other demonic art capable of withstanding this cold.

‘Could such a demonic art truly exist?’

The farther they went, the more severe the cold became.

This was not the sort of cold that instantly froze water exposed to the air. It was cold that surpassed common sense and the limits of the human body.

Neither Geom Mugeuk, who had consumed the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit, nor the Ice Palace Lord, who endured it with her unique internal energy, was in any state to hold a conversation. That was how tremendous the cold of that place was.

At last, the two arrived at the end of the path.

There stood a massive block of ice. No, it was a gigantic wall of ice.

It was not ordinary ice. As if it were the origin of all the cold in the world, its chill was beyond description.

In other words, the two of them had walked toward the source of the world’s cold.

And astonishingly, there was a door in that ice wall.

The fact that someone had thought to carve a hole into this place and create a space within it was astonishing in itself.

The Ice Palace Lord opened the door and went inside. Geom Mugeuk followed behind her.

The moment the Ice Palace Lord closed the door, silence descended.

The strange sound that had been ringing in their ears vanished completely, and at the same time, that insane cold disappeared as if it had been a lie.

Inside, it was not even that cold. It felt as though they had entered a house made of ice. He even had the premonition that the closer they moved toward the center, the more the cold would subside.

They could now converse, but the Ice Palace Lord stared at Geom Mugeuk in silence for quite some time. At last, she confirmed once more.

“Do you have the Extreme Cold Physique and have been deceiving me?”

“I will say it again: I do not have the Extreme Cold Physique.”

“Then do you possess a demonic art that allows you to withstand cold?”

“No. I doubt any demonic art of our cult could endure this cold.”

For a moment, she even had such a thought. Could the young cult lord know the secret martial art passed down only to the lord of the Ice Palace?

“There is one method by which one can endure any cold, is there not? The legend handed down in the Northern Sea.”

The Ice Palace Lord, who had been blank for a moment, recalled one thing.

“Surely not. You?”

Geom Mugeuk told her honestly. He did not want to make some vague excuse and leave an unpleasant doubt in her heart.

“Yes. I consumed the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit.”

Geom Mugeuk confirmed just how large the Ice Palace Lord’s eyes could become when she opened them as wide as possible.

The Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit was a legendary elixir handed down like a myth.

It was the pinnacle of all elixirs that strengthened one against cold, and it allowed one to comfortably accept not only cold itself, but any energy of an icy attribute.

“The Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit truly existed?”

Even the Ice Palace Lord had regarded it as a legendary elixir. A mystery that existed, yet did not exist.

“I was fortunate enough to encounter an opportunity.”

The Ice Palace Lord firmly shook her head.

“The Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit is not something one can obtain through luck alone.”

If it were, then that spiritual fruit would not have been passed down as a legend.

The person who obtained it was the young cult lord of the Demon Cult?

And that young cult lord had saved the Ice Palace. Was this a coincidence? The mastermind behind the incident had aimed for the Ice Palace Treasury, and now she had come here together with the young cult lord who had eliminated that mastermind. Was all of this merely coincidence?

Or were they standing face to face like this within the flow of inevitable fate?

“Let us go in.”

They slowly walked down a corridor made of ice. At the end of that corridor was a large space, and that was the Ice Palace Treasury.

The walls, the ceiling, the floor, and even the display cases—everything visible was made of ice.

This place was different from other treasuries. Astonishingly, all the treasures were inside the ice. As if packed within ice bricks, they were frozen in blocks of varying sizes.

The ice was incredibly transparent.

Because of that, the weapons piled within the ice looked as though they were floating in midair.

Geom Mugeuk had never seen such a mystical sight before.

“It is truly magnificent.”

The Ice Palace Lord smiled.

“It cannot be melted easily, even with considerable yang heat qi.”

Reaching this place was difficult enough, but even if one did, it would not be easy to extract the treasures from within that ice at the end.

She wondered what Geom Mugeuk would want from this place.

Geom Mugeuk pointed at one sword among the treasures in the ice stacked directly ahead and said,

“Could that sword be the Cold Heaven Divine Sword (寒天神劍)?”

“That is correct.”

It was a legendary sword known to double in power when wielded by someone who cultivated ice arts.

“And beside it, that is the Shattering Saber (破碎刀).”

“You truly have an excellent eye for weapons.”

Not only that, many other divine and wondrous weapons were stored there as well. They were things the Northern Sea Ice Palace had collected over many long years.

Even so, the Ice Palace Lord could sense it.

‘He will not choose a sword.’

He had not drawn the Black Demon Sword in front of her, but she could feel it. The sword Geom Mugeuk wore at his side was better than any sword in this place.

Indeed, though Geom Mugeuk was astonished and impressed by each and every weapon, he did not choose one.

Beside them, jewels floated in midair. The jewels enclosed within small pieces of ice looked even more mysterious.

Among the jewels, some were truly priceless. They were so exquisite that the top-grade Poison-Warding Pearl and Night-Luminous Pearl floating beside them looked shabby by comparison.

But Geom Mugeuk passed by those as well. Of course, he moved slowly, just in case Bigwe reacted.

The two beads Bigwe had obtained so far had not been among precious things. They had been abandoned and hidden.

So he had hoped that, as a twist, it might be boldly sitting among these precious jewels, but Bigwe did not react at all. How nice it would have been if it were proudly placed at the very top of these treasures.

Next came a place where elixirs were displayed.

Truly, they were elixirs that were difficult to obtain no matter how much money one had. Even the least effective among them were precious things that martial artists would wish to consume even in their dreams.

Geom Mugeuk passed that place as well. Instead, so as not to give the impression that he was searching for something special, he examined various things in detail.

“Is there nothing you want?”

“I intend to choose something if it draws my heart, but there is nothing yet.”

He looked through all the places where weapons, elixirs, and various artifacts were displayed, but Bigwe did not react.

‘Was there nothing in the Northern Sea Ice Palace for Bigwe to absorb?’

He thought perhaps it might not be reacting because everything was inside ice. But there was nothing he could do about that. Everything here was inside ice. He could not very well ask her to melt it all for him.

Just as he was about to give up, a large mural drawn on the distant wall caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye. It depicted a massive white dragon ascending to the heavens, large enough to be visible even from afar.

Geom Mugeuk walked toward it.

And when he arrived near the mural, Bigwe reacted from within his robe.

Geom Mugeuk was startled. He had not expected Bigwe to react to a mural.

Looking at it up close, he understood why. The mural was not merely painted.

In the place of the wish-fulfilling jewel held by the white dragon, a blue jade was embedded, and red jade was set into its eyes. That was not all. Jades of various colors were also embedded in its claws and across its body.

And among those many jades, there was a piece of white jade. Bigwe was reacting to it.

‘It is the White Eye!’

If he had not come this way to look at the mural, he would never have discovered it.

Who had hidden these here, and why?

That was something he could not know now. Since it had become connected to him in this way, he could only hope the day would come when he would learn.

Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“May I choose one of the jades embedded in that mural?”

Naturally, the Ice Palace Lord was surprised. She had surely never imagined he would ask for a jade embedded in the mural.

“Do you want the wish-fulfilling jewel?”

“No.”

What Geom Mugeuk pointed to was the white jade embedded at the tip of the tail.

“Please give me that tail.”

White jade was precious, but it could not compare to the treasures he had seen earlier.

“May I ask why, of all things, you want that?”

“In our cult, many have compared me to a latent dragon or a dragon ascending to the heavens. But I have always wished to live as the tail. I want that white jade so I do not forget that feeling.”

The Ice Palace Lord still looked as though she could not understand.

She lightly launched herself into the air, removed the white jade embedded in the wall, and descended.

No matter how closely she examined it, it was nothing more than white jade.

“Will this truly be enough?”

“Yes, it is enough. And simply being able to look around the Ice Palace Treasury has been an immensely valuable experience for me.”

Geom Mugeuk received the white jade and carefully placed it inside his robe.

Because she did not know what the white jade meant, the Ice Palace Lord misunderstood him in this way.

‘He does not wish to impose upon me.’

If that was the case, she could not send him away like this. The Ice Palace Lord said something unexpected.

“It is not enough for me.”

If he had not come at all, then so be it, but since he had come all the way here, she could not send away the man who had saved the Ice Palace with nothing more than a single piece of white jade.

The Ice Palace Lord went to where the spiritual medicines were kept. Then, with object retrieval through the air, she brought down the ice sitting at the highest point among the piled-up medicines.

“In any case, you are the only one who can take it.”

As the Ice Palace Lord’s inner power was infused into it, the ice melted, revealing a small bead. At a glance, it looked as if ice had simply been frozen into the shape of a bead, but this was the pinnacle of the North Sea’s spiritual medicines.

Man-geuk Ice Essence.

Among spiritual medicines with a cold attribute, it was the finest of all, but because it possessed such an extreme energy, it was known that only those who had consumed the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit could take it.

“May I truly accept something this precious?”

“If you do not eat it, it will remain frozen here forever.”

Even now, Geom Mugeuk possessed more inner power than anyone else in the murim, but he still thirsted for more.

Training in the Nine Flames martial arts required a great deal of inner power, and on top of that, he had to cultivate it within the Space-Time Transposition Art while opening even the Sicheon Secret Art. To Geom Mugeuk, inner power was time. To him, inner power was the strength to protect everyone.

“Take it.”

“Then I will no longer decline and will accept it gratefully.”

Geom Mugeuk immediately consumed the Man-geuk Ice Essence on the spot.

It was truly a freezing energy. If the effects of the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Fruit had not made his body capable of withstanding any chill, the moment he swallowed it, his entire body would have frozen solid and he would have died. No, he would not have been able to swallow it in the first place.

The immense energy of the Man-geuk Ice Essence surged through Geom Mugeuk’s meridians. It raged like a mad northern wind, but its opponent was already a body that had pushed through even mad winds.

After carefully regulating his true qi several times, the energy of the Man-geuk Ice Essence gathered in his dantian and became pure inner power.

Until now, he had vaguely thought that perhaps he possessed the greatest inner power in the murim, but now he could be certain. His inner power was likely the greatest of all. He was not happy simply because he had gained more inner power. He was happy because he could raise the realms of the Sicheon Secret Art and the Nine Flames Demonic Art even a little faster.

Today, the Ice Palace Lord was met with one surprise after another. She had not expected him to dissolve the Man-geuk Ice Essence so quickly.

Because of that, she could decide on the third favor even more easily.

“Choose one more.”

“This alone is more than enough.”

“There are three reasons I am giving you three gifts.”

“What reasons are those?”

“The first is repayment for uncovering Wonju’s death and eliminating those behind it.”

Yes, he understood that much. He was the benefactor who had saved the Ice Palace.

“The second is because you made me look back.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. It was because he had told her that her daughter was still standing behind her. Because of her gratitude for reconnecting her with the daughter she would have lost forever.

“The third is a gift given in advance.”

“What do you mean by that?”

“You will understand someday. What those words mean.”

It was an investment for her daughter.

When Han Seol became the Ice Palace Lord, this Geom Mugeuk would have become the Heavenly Demon. She was asking in advance. In the future, please take care of the Northern Sea Ice Palace, and my daughter as well. If that request cost her only one treasure, it was a bargain.

Geom Mugeuk guessed her feelings. And so he accepted the heart she held for her daughter.

“Then I will choose one more.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around the place once again. Since this gift was not for himself, he chose even more carefully.

“Then I will take this as the last one.”

What Geom Mugeuk chose was a wine cup.

Chapter 431: The Moment My Busy Life Stopped.
---------------------------------------------
It was a wine cup with a gorgeous yet mysterious air.

The pure-white cup was decorated like a moon rising over a snowy field. If one gazed at it quietly, it felt as though one were standing alone in a white snowfield beneath the moonlight.

The crescent moon floating on one side of the cup served as a handle, so it could even be worn at the waist.

But what made that cup special was not its appearance.

“Do you know what kind of cup that is?”

The Ice Palace Lord truly had not expected Geom Mugeuk to choose this cup.

“I have heard rumors that the North Sea Ice Palace possesses a sacred wine cup. Is that not it?”

“You have seen correctly.”

She explained the cup to him.

The Ice Palace Holy Grail.

At a glance, it appeared to be made of pure-white porcelain, but the cup was made of metal. This specially alloyed cup was said to be incredibly light, yet absolutely impossible to break or bend.

What mattered was the cup’s effect.

Because the cup itself possessed a cold nature, one could drink chilled wine even in the height of summer. It was also said that drinking wine from this cup increased one’s internal energy and nourished the body.

Of course, the amount of internal energy gained was not greater than what one would gain through breathing and energy circulation in the same amount of time, so drinking wine at the cost of one’s health for the sake of increasing internal energy could be called foolish.

With the exception of one person in this murim.

For the Drunken Demon, who lived with wine at every moment, it was undoubtedly a cup of great meaning.

Moreover, there was only one wine cup in the world with such an effect, and it could only be obtained here in the Ice Palace Treasury.

For someone who loved wine more than life itself and would spend his entire life drinking it, this cup would be the finest gift.

“Are you truly going to choose this?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“Lord Drunken Demon will like this cup more than the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.”

That was not why the Ice Palace Lord had asked. There were far too many precious items here for him to choose a gift for someone else. Looking at a splendid protective armor displayed in the air on the opposite side, she said,

“That Ice Guardian Armor could save your life. There are also elixirs that could grant you more internal energy than the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng, even if not as much as the Mangeuk Ice Crystal you consumed earlier.”

“I am fine.”

Seeing that Geom Mugeuk’s mind was set on the wine cup, the Ice Palace Lord no longer recommended anything else.

When she reached out her hand, the ice brick containing the cup flew toward her.

Using her unique internal energy, the Ice Palace Lord easily melted the ice. If an ordinary expert tried to melt this special ice with hot yang energy, they would have to expend an incomparable amount of internal energy.

Freed from the ice, the Ice Palace Holy Grail revealed its mysterious aura.

A pure-white energy shimmered around the cup, like snow-laden wind blowing across a snowy plain. Just looking at it made clear how precious it was, so it was not a cup an ordinary person could carry around even if they wanted to. Countless people would rush in to steal it every day.

“Here, take it.”

The Ice Palace Lord handed the cup to Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk carefully accepted it, placed it on his palm, and marveled.

“It truly is a splendid cup.”

Geom Mugeuk was genuinely delighted at the thought of giving it to the Drunken Demon.

The Ice Palace Lord felt it. At least in this moment, Geom Mugeuk was rejoicing with the pure innocence of a boy.

‘So all the rumors were true.’

When she first heard that Geom Mugeuk had brought the Eight Demon Venerables under his control, she had been half in doubt.

What sort of people were the Eight Demon Venerables, that he could control them all?

They surely did not follow him sincerely. Some of them must have joined hands with him only on the surface. The Eight Demon Venerables were not easy people for a young heir to handle.

But seeing this now, her doubts vanished from that half-belief.

If his relationship with them was such that he could joyfully give such a precious gift? The word “control” seemed insufficient.

“Thank you. I will never forget the great grace you have shown me today.”

Geom Mugeuk was sincerely grateful to the Ice Palace Lord.

No matter how much she had told him to choose anything, it was not easy to so readily give away a cup bearing the name of a holy grail.

“Compared to what you have done for our palace, this is a small thing. Now, let us return.”

Geom Mugeuk left the Ice Palace Treasury together with the Ice Palace Lord.

Shiiiiiiii.

When they opened the door and stepped out, a strange sound rang out, and the insane cold they had forgotten came rushing in.

Leaving behind that place where even conversation was difficult, the two quickly walked out.

As they retraced the path they had come and were about to leave the valley, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Sometimes, people around my age gather together.”

The Ice Palace Lord knew that as well. She also knew that at the recent tripartite meeting, the heirs and siblings of each faction had all gathered.

“When that happens, I will invite Miss Han as well.”

The Ice Palace Lord stopped walking.

Even without him saying it, she understood why he said he would invite her daughter.

Han Seol would only be able to grow further by leaving this North Sea and meeting many people.

But could she really send her daughter, whom she had never once sent to the Central Plains? Just thinking about it made her this worried.

When the Ice Palace Lord looked back at Geom Mugeuk, his eyes seemed to say this:

Do not worry. I will take good care of her and send her back safely.

It was an expression of gratitude toward her for giving him such a precious gift. Of course, whether she could trust him and send her daughter with him was another matter entirely.

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk returned, the Drunken Demon was lying on the bed.

“How is your body?”

“I feel like I’m going to die from the pain.”

Just a little while ago, when the physician had come and gone, he had received treatment with a dignified air. Not a single look in his eyes, not a single expression on his face had changed, showing the majesty of a Demon Venerable.

He had been perfectly fine like that, but the moment he saw Geom Mugeuk, he began exaggerating his pain.

“I can’t even lift my arms. It hurts too much.”

Still lying down, the Drunken Demon lifted the sole of his foot.

“Look here. If you had pulled out the pieces of bottle earlier, it wouldn’t hurt this much.”

“My persistent elder brother. That’s why I brought you a gift.”

“What gift, when I’m dying from pain?”

Geom Mugeuk placed a small box on the table.

“You really don’t need it?”

“I don’t need it.”

“You’ll regret it.”

“It’s wine, right? You bought wine, didn’t you? It’s obvious.”

The Drunken Demon pretended not to care while glancing sidelong at the box.

“It’ll be no use regretting it later.”

When Geom Mugeuk moved to take it back, the Drunken Demon casually asked,

“What is it? Let me at least see.”

“Then it’ll only make you feel worse for nothing.”

How could he not look after hearing that?

“It’s fine, so show me!”

“You can only look with your eyes. You have to give it back.”

Geom Mugeuk slowly opened the box.

The moment he saw what appeared inside, the Drunken Demon’s eyes went wide. Even if no one else in the world recognized it, the Drunken Demon would.

“Uwaaaah!”

With a shout, the Drunken Demon rushed to the table. Forgetting the pain in the sole of his foot, he ran.

“Don’t tell me this is? Is it really that? It is, right? It’s that, isn’t it?”

The Drunken Demon could not continue speaking. This was the very thing he wanted most.

“Why is this here?”

The Drunken Demon’s voice trembled.

“I picked it up on the way.”

“Don’t joke around!”

“I received it as a gift from the Ice Palace Lord.”

The Drunken Demon immediately understood what had happened. It seemed the Ice Palace Lord had given him a gift in return for this matter.

“You gave up your own thing and accepted a gift for me.”

“That would have been truly touching, but I received mine too. So don’t feel burdened and take it. Ah, no. You said you didn’t want to receive it, didn’t you?”

“What nonsense!”

The Drunken Demon spread both arms like wings and guarded the cup. The arms he had claimed hurt too much to lift had formed a defensive line, just like that.

“Take my life instead! It’s mine! Go away!”

How could the Drunken Demon not know? Geom Mugeuk could have taken something else instead of this cup. He had given up the value of the Ice Palace Holy Grail and gifted it to him.

Emotion surged through the Drunken Demon’s heart. It was the most valuable gift he had ever received, and more than anything, it was what he had wanted most.

Geom Mugeuk had known the Drunken Demon would like it, but he had not expected him to like it this much.

“Here, have a drink. I washed it clean before coming.”

Geom Mugeuk poured him wine.

Torrereung.

It was the moment wine was poured into the Ice Palace Holy Grail, which had been sealed away for so long. Was it just his imagination? The pure-white energy shimmering around the Ice Palace Holy Grail seemed to grow even stronger.

After taking a deep breath, the Drunken Demon drank the wine. The cold wine flowed down his throat in uninterrupted gulps.

“Kyaaa! It’s truly delicious!”

Geom Mugeuk shouted “Kyaa!” along with him. Just watching made it look incredibly delicious.

“Me too, one cup!”

This time, the Drunken Demon filled the cup for him.

When the Drunken Demon poured, the moonlight of the wine cup seemed to shine even more softly.

Geom Mugeuk drank the wine in the Ice Palace Holy Grail in one go. The wine was truly cold and delicious. The exclamation “Kyaa!” came out naturally.

“Outstanding!”

The taste of wine always depended on one’s mood. There would not often be days when wine tasted as good as it did in this moment.

“Now, one more for me.”

“You’re taking medicine. Stop!”

“Just one more cup!”

As Geom Mugeuk poured the wine, he said,

“I’m not giving you this cup so you can drink more. I’m giving it to you so you’ll drink less. They said the cup also has the effect of protecting the body. So think of the heart with which I gave you this gift, and every time you drink from it, drink one cup when you would have drunk two. Think of it as drinking one cup of delicious wine instead of two.”

Geom Mugeuk’s concern came through. He was always nagging him to eat food with his wine. Even after coming all the way here, he was still nagging.

“Absolutely do not overdrink. Understood?”

“I understand. But one last cup. I should at least drink three.”

“Fine, I’ll allow it today!”

The two looked at each other and laughed.

After drinking three cups of wine, the Drunken Demon fastened the Ice Palace Holy Grail on the side opposite the Blood Tear. The two artifacts suited each other strangely well. Of course, that was probably because the person wearing them was the Drunken Demon.

“Then rest.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to leave, but behind him, the Drunken Demon spoke.

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Because there was no playfulness in his words, Geom Mugeuk also answered respectfully.

“You accomplished something great this time, Lord Drunken Demon.”

* * *

After returning to my room, I used the Spacetime Transposition Art in order to absorb Baekjeong.

Snap! When I flicked my finger, I was standing in a new space.

I did not open the beach I always opened, but the second space: the place with a small hot spring in a pure-white snowy field.

It was the very place I had created before, inspired by my father’s hot spring. This space suited the North Sea perfectly.

There were only three things in this space.

Pure-white snow, stars in the night sky, and a warm hot spring.

First, I took the Secret Casket and Baekjeong out from my robe.

Wooooong.

At the sight of Baekjeong, the Secret Casket vibrated in response.

It was already the third time, but I could not help feeling tense.

I carefully placed Baekjeong on top of the Secret Casket.

Sssssss.

Then, a sight that amazed me every time unfolded. The hard Baekjeong seeped into the Secret Casket like liquid.

After the Secret Casket absorbed all of Baekjeong, it vibrated greatly.

Sssss!

Something tinged with white energy began to emerge from the Secret Casket.

As always, it was neither smoke, nor liquid, nor light. That indescribable thing passed through my hand and arm, then spread throughout my entire body.

The Heavenly Demon Protection Art, which I had mastered to the great success stage, still did not activate. That meant it was all right. Because I trusted the Heavenly Demon Protection Art, I was finding and absorbing this energy.

A refreshing sensation spread through my whole body.

The moment that energy spread throughout me, radiance burst from my body. It was a radiance so intense I could not open my eyes.

When I opened my eyes again after closing them, that white energy had all been absorbed into my body. Through the Secret Casket, I had already absorbed three energies.

As with the previous two times, there was no change in my body. The energies did not meet or mix. It meant they were not yet ready to bring about some great change. Or perhaps it meant I had to absorb more of the other energies.

Thud.

The Secret Casket spat out Baekjeong after absorbing all of its energy. By then, it had become an ordinary stone.

“Just who made you?”

As always, the Secret Casket only smiled.

After absorbing Baekjeong’s energy, I stripped off my clothes and immersed myself in the steaming hot spring.

“Ah, this is nice!”

Cold wind above, warmth below.

I enjoyed the hot spring with my eyes closed. This time alone was an incredibly precious moment to me.

After my regression, my life has flowed by with every day packed full. One full day after another.

But at least in this moment, alone within the Spacetime Transposition Art, my life that had been running diligently slowed its pace.

The more its pace slowed, the further I moved away from my life. I liked this distancing. Naked as I was now, I had cast off all responsibility as well.

“The murim is going to collapse? The demonic path is going to fall? What do you want me to do about it? Don’t disturb me while I’m resting!”

Such irresponsibility slowed the pace of life even further.

Thus, the pace slowed and slowed again, until at last it stopped completely. The moment my busy life came to a complete stop, I finally became free.

At the same time, I came to meet another me.

The me from before my regression, whose loneliness had piled up and piled up until he had become loneliness itself.

The me who had never rested, who had never once properly comforted himself, and who had run only for a single purpose.

With a huge, pitch-black hole in my heart between us, we stood facing each other.

Was the me on the other side lonelier?

Or was the me smiling among countless people lonelier?

I could not easily find an answer.

Still, I wanted to say this to him.

—It was all thanks to you that I was able to come this far.

I had said all sorts of kind words to the people around me, but this was the first comfort and gratitude I had ever offered myself.

—Thank you. Truly.

I gazed gently at the lonely me on the other side. I did not wish for him to disappear. The me on the other side was, in the end, still me.

—Just don’t fall into that pit.

At my words, the me on the other side sent me a look that said this: You be careful. The ones who always fall into pits are those trying to become perfect, and those who are smiling.

In the next moment, the pitch-black hole between us turned into a hot spring, and I was soaking in it.

I looked up at the countless stars for a long while, then came out of the hot spring.

Now it was time to return to reality.

“The murim can’t be ruined. My demonic path can’t collapse. What else is there to do? I have to go out and protect it.”

Snap!

Chapter 432: The Gates of the Ice Palace Are Open to You.
---------------------------------------------------------
The snow-covered courtyard was clean and peaceful.

The snow that had settled on the trees looked just like white blossoms blooming, and the icicles hanging from the eaves shone clear and transparent.

Entering this pure white space, Ian began to unfold the Bicheon Sword Technique upon the untouched white snow.

Since Geom Mugeuk and Chwima had returned safely.

'Now the only thing I need to worry about is my own life.'

A good personality or a pretty face, if not backed by strength, often did more harm than good to one's survival in the martial world.

She had trained for Geom Mugeuk.

She no longer harbored the kind of heart she had in her days as a guard.

She trained for herself. Instead, she thought that this effort would ultimately become a path for Geom Mugeuk.

She moved back and forth across the snow, swinging her sword. Unbearably beautiful, she unfolded the Bicheon Sword Technique upon the pure white snow—it was truly like a painting.

After performing a bout of the Bicheon Sword Technique's forms, she looked at the footprints stamped into the ground.

She had never before practiced forms upon snow that no one had trodden, so this was the first time she saw footprints left so vividly.

"This is the First Form, Gyuncheon. This is the Second Form, Byuncheon...."

Then her gaze suddenly fixed upon one spot.

"As expected! This part spread apart again. Just what is the problem? Please tell me!"

She had been looking up at the sky when she jumped in surprise. Somehow, Geom Mugeuk was floating above her head.

"Young Master, what are you doing up there?"

"What else? I was watching to see where the problem lies."

Geom Mugeuk, who had been looking down at her footprints from mid-air, descended.

She hadn't been able to receive Geom Mugeuk's teachings recently. So, not wanting to miss this opportunity, she quickly spoke.

"When I perform the Eighth Form, Hwangcheon, my back foot keeps splaying to the right. I've kept training to fix it, but I simply can't."

"Why can't you fix it, when you know the problem so clearly?"

"It's because my ability to interpret martial arts is lacking, I suppose."

"Aren't you overestimating the Bicheon Sword Technique?"

Ian turned to look at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk nodded with a serious expression, as if he hadn't spoken in jest.

Ian looked at the footprints again.

'The Young Master's words mean I might not be wrong, don't they? If that's the case...?'

Ian gazed down at the footprints, lost in thought.

How much time had passed?

"Could it be something like this?"

Ian, who had been raising her head, started in surprise. Before she knew it, the surroundings had grown dark.

Geom Mugeuk was looking at her from where he had originally been.

"Young Master? What happened?"

"You had fallen into a state of no-self."

At the words "state of no-self," she grew even more surprised.

"It felt like only a brief moment had passed?"

"That is the state of no-self."

"It wasn't a dilemma so great that I would fall into a state of no-self."

"Then what kind of dilemma is great?"

"How would I know?"

"It was something worth pondering sufficiently."

Then Ian asked in surprise.

"Surely not? You were standing there all this time?"

"Because your state of no-self must not be broken."

Geom Mugeuk knew well what an important moment it was for her. The vast majority of martial artists never experienced it even once.

"You stood there until your legs hurt for no reason because of me."

"Compared to the time you spent standing guard at my door, this hardly counts as standing."

"That was my duty."

She was grateful that Geom Mugeuk spoke this way. Had she ever expected any reward for guarding him? Yet even such a single word turned her past hardships into something worthwhile.

It was Geom Mugeuk, who always practiced the teaching to express what needed to be said.

"So? What conclusion did you reach?"

"I think it's fine even if it turns to the right. Even if I'm attacked, it's a foot stance that causes no hindrance, and it connects to other forms without any problem. No matter how much I think about it, there was no reason to keep it straight."

"Then why didn't you think so until now?"

"Because the Bicheon Sword Technique couldn't possibly be wrong."

To her, the Bicheon Sword Technique was a perfect martial art. It was the martial art learned by the successors of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult before they mastered the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

Geom Mugeuk didn't answer with words but showed her directly.

Right where she had performed the forms, he began to unfold the Bicheon Sword Technique.

Footprints began to stamp in similar places.

Ian watched with wide eyes, determined not to miss even a single movement as Geom Mugeuk performed the Bicheon Sword Technique.

It was the same form, yet different. Different while being the same, and the same while being different.

As they say, you only see what you know; watching a demonstration now felt different from before. It would likely look different again upon achieving mastery.

The footprints left behind in that manner.

What she focused on was that footprint she had agonized over earlier.

"Huh? Your foot splays to the right too, Young Master."

Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

"It means the answer you found is correct."

Ian felt joy, yet on the other hand, it was unexpected. But then why did the form say the feet should be in a straight line?

Geom Mugeuk resolved that doubt.

"Whether the founder of this martial art intended it or it happened by chance, this part serves as the greatest wall on the path to mastery of the Bicheon Sword Technique. It is also the point where tightly bound forms begin to gain freedom for the first time."

Those words also meant that she had taken one step closer to mastery of the Bicheon Sword Technique.

She had worked hard and Geom Mugeuk had given her many teachings, but she possessed an innate talent for martial arts.

"Thank you so much, Young Master."

How would she have found the answer if not for Geom Mugeuk? If he hadn't told her earlier to trust herself rather than the sword technique, she would never have experienced the state of no-self.

"Now let's return to the main cult soon, as well."

"Young Master. I was thinking of not returning and continuing the mission I took on instead."

She had left the cult originally to make Seojin, the younger sibling of the Gwiryeongja, a squad leader of the Gwiyeongdae.

But she had come all the way to the North Sea at a woman's request to save her husband.

"Shall I go with you?"

Ian shook her head.

"I want to handle this matter alone this time."

Her journey through the martial world had brought her all the way to the North Sea over a cup of water.

Whether she brought Seojin back or not, she had a feeling she would return having grown greatly after completing this mission. It would be even better if she achieved mastery of the Bicheon Sword Technique.

"Be careful when getting that cup of water."

Ian burst into a bright smile at Geom Mugeuk's joke.

Then a martial artist of the Ice Palace came and delivered the Ice Palace Lord's words.

"The Palace Lord requests an audience with you both."

Geom Mugeuk could sense it. That Binggungju had called them because of Ian.

* * *

Binggungju was waiting alone in the Palace Lord's hall.

After exchanging greetings, Binggungju asked what she was curious about first.

"Is Lady Chwima satisfied?"

She was curious about Chwima's reaction upon receiving the Ice Palace's sacred chalice.

Geom Mugeuk wanted to vividly convey how delighted and excited Chwima had been, but he considered her dignity.

"She liked it very much."

"That's a relief. Now, please sit."

After Geom Mugeuk and Ian sat, Binggungju brought out tea.

Ian looked at the pattern on the teacup again.

"You said before that you felt like you'd seen that pattern somewhere?"

"Looking again, I'm sure of it. I've definitely seen it before."

When Geom Mugeuk looked, it was a painting of two birds in flight, but it gave off an extremely unique feeling.

Binggungju watched Ian silently. For some reason, her gaze had kept drifting to her from the first time they met.

Binggungju, who had been quietly watching Ian, made an unexpected request.

"Might I check your pulse just once? I'll tell you the reason after I do."

Ian was bewildered by the unexpected proposal. For a martial artist to offer their pulse was tantamount to giving their life; unless one was injured or in a special situation, they would never allow their pulse to be checked. Of course, she didn't think Binggungju would harm her, but hesitation was only natural.

She stole a glance at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk said nothing.

If he had judged it a situation to stop, he would have stepped in. With this in mind, Ian readily consented.

"Yes, very well."

When Ian obediently extended her arm, Binggungju took her pulse.

After examining her blood meridians for a moment, Binggungju's eyes opened wide. What flashed across her face was an intense emotion she had kept buried in her heart for a very long time.

Both Geom Mugeuk and Ian felt that her emotions were different from usual.

After quelling her surging emotions for a moment by drinking tea, Binggungju spoke calmly. Her gaze remained fixed on Ian.

"The pattern drawn on this teacup is one my older sister drew before she left the Ice Palace long ago."

Still, Ian couldn't guess the identity of this unfamiliar emotion that Binggungju was revealing.

"Might I have seen your older sister's pattern at some point?"

"Perhaps. One thing is certain: this pattern is unique in the world. There would be no one else who draws such a pattern on teacups."

Binggungju's voice was trembling. Even when the Ice Palace had been in danger, she had shown composure, but now she was trembling.

"Elder Seo mentioned my older sister before he died. At that time, I sensed it. That bastard had killed my sister."

Seonak had been utterly loyal to her at the time. He had exercised a misplaced loyalty to prevent her sister from threatening the position of Palace Lord.

"Before he died, Elder Seo told me. That my sister had a daughter. He spoke ambiguously to torment me, but it was definitely the word 'daughter.'"

Only now could Ian understand. Why she was checking her pulse, and why she was telling this story.

Ian's heart began to pound like mad. She had never dreamed that she would have such a conversation in the Palace Lord's hall of the Ice Palace.

The decisive words flowed from Binggungju's mouth.

"The women of our family are born with special blood meridians suited for learning the Ice Arts."

They could already tell the result from her trembling voice.

"You are my niece."

Had she grown dazed from too much shock? Even though the secret of her birth had been revealed, Ian was calm.

She didn't know how to react or what to say.

She had lived all this time thinking she was abandoned by her parents as a child. But her mother was Binggungju's older sister?

She looked at Geom Mugeuk. She had really wanted to ask like this.

Young Master, I'm of Binggungju's bloodline. How should I react at a time like this?

Geom Mugeuk, reading her heart, asked.

"How do you feel?"

"Just...."

She had been about to say she was bewildered when suddenly her heart surged with emotion. The image of her mother, who had lost her life to a villain, suddenly came to mind. All this time she had thought her parents abandoned her. She had resented them so much.

"I...."

Ian couldn't continue speaking, her throat choked with tears.

Both Geom Mugeuk and Binggungju waited for her.

Only after a long while did Ian compose herself. After taking a deep breath, she revealed her heart.

"Nothing will change, though."

She conveyed the conclusion she had reached in a calm tone.

"In the future too, I am the daughter of Lady Gwonma, a former guard of the Young Cult Lord, and the Commander of the Gwiyeongdae. I am satisfied with my life. I don't want to change this life moving forward."

They were sorry words to Binggungju, who was becoming her aunt, but they were her honest feelings. With the thought that she had to say this first, no other words came to mind.

As if she had expected this reaction, Binggungju was also calm.

"Yes, do as you wish."

She felt sorry for Ian. Her sister's tragedy had ultimately been because of her.

Was it the trick of fate? Ian, who had become a sacrifice in the succession struggle, trusted and followed the one who ended the succession struggle without bloodshed the most.

"Thank you for growing up so well."

Binggungju was so proud of Ian. She was truly grateful that she had grown up so excellently.

"I heard the story of how you came here to save the husband of a woman who gave you a cup of water. I might not have come, but your mother would have. You look exactly like your mother."

Perhaps it was an unavoidable fate that had led her all the way to the North Sea.

A fate imbued with her sister's earnestness.

"From now on, the gates of the Ice Palace are open to you. Come whenever you wish."

"Thank you."

Binggungju wanted to embrace Ian. But she couldn't bring herself to suggest it.

Confused, awkward, and with a complicated heart, Ian politely bowed first.

"Then I shall take my leave first."

"Yes, go and rest well."

The words aunt and niece still did not come easily.

Once Ian left the Palace Lord’s Hall, only Geom Mugeuk and the Ice Palace Lord remained there.

“She’ll need a little more time.”

“I suppose she will.”

The Ice Palace Lord was overjoyed at the thought that she had found her sister’s daughter. But Geom Mugeuk put a brake on her soaring spirits.

“I think it would be best not to announce this officially.”

“Why is that?”

“Was it not because she wished to avoid a succession struggle that your sister left? If it becomes known that Ian is of your blood, someday someone like Elder Seo may appear and try to use that fact to plot against you. In the end, your niece may be forced to bear the same fate.”

“!”

She had not thought of that. The thought of having found her sister’s blood had come first, making her want to reveal it to everyone as soon as possible.

‘Truly, the more I experience with him, the more astonishing he is.’

Since the moment they met, Geom Mugeuk had truly left no openings.

“We will leave at dawn tomorrow.”

“Already?”

She felt regret. To think she would feel saddened by the departure of the young cult leader of the Demon Cult? Before Geom Mugeuk came, she would have thought it an utterly absurd emotion.

But he had come and restored her relationship with her daughter, found her niece, and saved the Ice Palace from crisis. Yes, this much was enough reason to feel sad at his departure.

“Then please take care until we meet again.”

“May you achieve your great undertaking.”

Geom Mugeuk was about to turn and leave when the Ice Palace Lord spoke from behind him.

“When there is a gathering of the rising talents, please invite Seol as well.”

It was an expression of her willingness to send her daughter to the Central Plains. Yes, if Geom Mugeuk was waiting in the Central Plains, she could trust him and send her.

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and left behind a joke whose meaning was unclear.

“Of course. I’ll have to invite our young palace lord, if only because I’m curious about her dancing skills.”

* * *

Before leaving, Geom Mugeuk stood side by side with Chwima atop the snowy mountain.

“I was able to do it because you came with me.”

It was an honest statement. Had he come alone, he would not have been able to handle such a great conspiracy. He had killed the Blood King, but when he thought of the process that had led up to it, it had not been something he could have endured by himself.

“If you’d been alone, you wouldn’t have gotten this wine cup either, and whenever you returned after leaving the cult, you’d always have come to me last.”

He was subtly pressuring him to come to him first from now on, but it did not work on Geom Mugeuk.

“I’m still going to come to you last, hyung.”

“Why on earth?”

“Because it’s comfortable.”

“You mean because I’m easy!”

“I’m saying I’ll meet everyone else first, then come drink comfortably at the end.”

“What a fine excuse.”

Even if he did not come at all, let alone last, how could Chwima ever feel hurt by Geom Mugeuk?

Through this journey, not only had he grown closer to Geom Mugeuk, he felt he had come to understand a little more what kind of person he was.

The two silently enjoyed the majestic snowscape that could only be seen from the peak of the snowy mountain.

Before descending the mountain, they shared a cup of wine each.

And the final cup they scattered into the air from the mountain’s peak.

“Wonju, drink well before you go!”

Chapter 433: Calling It a Heart and Treating It Like One.
---------------------------------------------------------
At dawn the next day, the carriage carrying Geom Mugeuk and his party departed from the Ice Palace.

Ian, who had said she would continue the mission she had put on hold rather than returning to the main cult, had decided to travel with them at least until they left the North Sea.

As the carriage passed through the entrance of the Ice Palace, one person was waiting.

It was Han Seol, the Young Palace Lady.

“I came out because I wanted to say goodbye.”

Geom Mugeuk and Chwima exchanged light glances from the carriage, and Ian stepped down on their behalf. The two of them had things they needed to say.

“I heard from Mother.”

Han Seol called her not Palace Lord but Mother. It was partly because she had grown closer to the Ice Palace Lord recently, but also because what they were discussing now was family matters.

Ian quietly gazed at Han Seol. They were now cousins.

“Were you surprised? I was surprised to hear it from the Palace Lord yesterday as well.”

“You are my elder sister, so please speak comfortably.”

It was the first time in her life Han Seol had ever called someone “sister.” In the past, those words would not have come out so easily.

Recently, as her relationship with her mother had changed, there had been some inexplicable shift in her feelings. That was likely why. Why this meeting was not as uncomfortable as she had expected.

“I thought I was the younger one.”

“…….”

“I’m joking.”

“It’s still not funny.”

It was the same Han Seol who had said Ian’s joke was not funny the other day. But today, contrary to her words, a smile hovered at her lips.

“And this.”

Han Seol held out a small box.

“Mother told me to give this to you. She said to open it later.”

The box was tightly sealed so it could not be opened right away. She did not know what was inside, but she should still express her gratitude.

“Please tell my aunt that I am grateful.”

Just as it was Han Seol’s first time calling someone “sister,” it was Ian’s first time saying “aunt.” It was very awkward, but she had done so out of courtesy, thinking of the Ice Palace Lord who had been so delighted to learn that Ian was her niece.

“Then safe travels.”

Ian, who had been about to turn away, looked back at Han Seol and spoke.

“Seol, take care.”

When Han Seol’s expression turned surprised, Ian quickly said.

“Is it too soon?”

“No. A sister is a sister.”

Ian was the type to be bold at times like this.

“If you come to the Central Plains next time, I’ll show you around.”

Han Seol nodded. It was an awkward, unfamiliar feeling. But this feeling was not so bad, either.

Ian climbed back into the carriage.

Geom Mugeuk poked his face out the window and bade farewell to Han Seol.

“When all the junior disciples have gathered, I shall invite you. Let us meet then.”

Han Seol did not yet know that this very gathering was such a special occasion where the status of the Young Palace Lady of the North Sea Ice Palace would be nothing special at all.

And so the carriage carrying Geom Mugeuk, Chwima, and Ian departed from that place.

Han Seol stood in place, watching the carriage leave. The three of them had truly come like the wind and left like the wind.

The Ice Palace Lord approached her side. She had been watching from afar and now revealed herself.

“Mother, have you ever been to the Central Plains?”

The Ice Palace Lord was startled by the word “Mother.” When had Han Seol ever called her that? Since Han Seol was still looking at the carriage disappearing in the distance, she could not tell whether it had been intentional or had slipped out unintentionally.

“A few times.”

“What kind of place is the Central Plains?”

Han Seol knew of the Central Plains only from the stories of others and from books. She wanted to hear what the Central Plains meant to her mother.

The carriage was growing distant, but it was the moment when curiosity about the Central Plains sprouted in her heart for the first time.

The Ice Palace Lord walked slowly and brushed a snow-covered tree. The snow on the branches fell away as if fluttering.

“In my youth, the Central Plains was such a place to me. A place you want to return from when you go, yet want to go back to as soon as you return.”

And as the years passed, those feelings gradually faded. Now the longing for the Central Plains was no longer as great as it had been in her youth.

She loved the North Sea.

Neither the scattering spring blossoms, the scorching heat of summer days, nor the beautifully embroidered autumn leaves could surpass the beauty of this snowy landscape.

But that was merely her own life.

The Ice Palace Lord’s gentle gaze, clearly changed from before, turned toward her daughter.

“Your Central Plains will be different.”

* * *

The carriage sped southward.

Since it was a carriage dispatched by the North Sea Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, the martial artist in the driver’s seat was extremely nervous.

To think he would be driving a carriage carrying the Young Cult Leader and Chwima. Moreover, they were traveling without a single escort.

So perhaps they could avoid ambushes? No one would believe the Young Cult Leader was in this carriage.

Contrary to the anxiety of the driver, inside the carriage was peaceful.

Chwima, who had not yet fully recovered from his injuries, occupied one side and lay down. Of course, he did not forget to exaggerate his condition.

“What’s the rush, dragging a dying brother along like this?”

“When work is done, we have to go. I want to see Father, and the Demon Lords too. I want to see you too, brother, and Daeryong and Lord Jang.”

“And me, again……”

He had been about to say that he would be treated like cold rice, but stopped. With Ian riding along, he could not easily make such a joke.

Of course, he probably could have. Ian had been staring blankly out the window all this time.

Chwima signaled with his eyes for someone to look after Ian, then closed his eyes to sleep.

Geom Mugeuk looked out at the same window Ian was watching.

Feeling that Geom Mugeuk was concerned about her, Ian brought up what she had wanted to say.

“Actually, I could not sleep a wink last night.”

Geom Mugeuk had thought that she probably had not.

“The Palace Lord likely did not either.”

Then Ian turned to Geom Mugeuk and asked with a worried expression.

“Should I have gone to see her at dawn and bid her a separate farewell?”

She had agonized over whether to do so many times, but in the end had not.

“Earlier, the Palace Lord was watching us from afar.”

“Really?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

He knew well what it meant to watch from afar because you did not want to burden someone.

Hearing that the Ice Palace Lord had watched over her, Ian felt even more apologetic.

Her gaze turned to the box given by the Ice Palace Lord.

“Open it.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Ian carefully broke the seal and opened the box.

Inside was another small box. She saw a book underneath as well, but she opened the inner box first.

When she opened it, a unique fragrance spread through the carriage.

It was so intense that Chwima, who had been trying to sleep, opened his eyes wide.

Inside was a pure white fruit. Just by looking at it, one could tell it was extraordinary from the spiritual energy emanating from it.

“What is this?”

Geom Mugeuk recognized it at once.

“It is a Bingseolcho fruit.”

Geom Mugeuk had once joked with Seo Daeryong that if they went to the North Sea, the Snow People cultivated Bingseolcho. To think he would see it here.

“Is it precious?”

Earlier, when the Ice Palace Lord had asked if she would truly choose a wine cup when there were spiritual medicines even better than ten-thousand-year snow ginseng, she had been referring to this very Bingseolcho.

Geom Mugeuk immediately spoke to the driver.

“Stop the carriage for a moment.”

The carriage came to a halt.

Geom Mugeuk took the box containing the Bingseolcho fruit and had Ian get out as well.

He had her sit beside the carriage and held out the Bingseolcho fruit.

“You want me to consume it here?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Receiving a spiritual medicine as a gift while I am with you must also be your good fortune.”

When consuming a spiritual medicine, someone trustworthy must watch over you. Even setting aside the fact that one could not respond to external attacks, one might suffer severe internal injuries or fall into qi deviation if unable to withstand the medicinal effects.

Where could one find someone as reliable as Geom Mugeuk when considering such dangers?

“Is it acceptable for me to eat something so precious? You should take it instead, Young Master.”

“Are you trying to start a war between the main cult and the Ice Palace?”

Ian smiled faintly at Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

Once her life had changed, everything was changing in a dizzying whirl. She felt swept away by the fierce currents of fate regardless of her own will. Yet the reason she could remain brave and steadfast was surely because when she turned her head, there was always someone watching over her.

“Then I will take it with gratitude.”

Ian carefully consumed the Bingseolcho fruit.

The fruit melted in her mouth instantly and spread through her body.

In that instant, a shudder ran through Ian’s body. The energy spreading through her meridians was cold. So cold that she wondered if it was allowed to be this cold.

But the cold was momentary. Like someone who catches a cold from the chill and develops a fever, her body became burning hot.

Passing between cold and heat, the energy of the Bingseolcho fruit rampaged wildly through her body.

Ian was bewildered by this intense energy. She had prepared herself mentally, but she had not known it would be this strong.

‘Can I refine this?’

She circulated her true qi through her circuit, trying somehow to make the energy her own.

Thanks to her efforts, the energy rampaging like a wild horse was somewhat subdued, but the energy began to scatter and leak out.

‘No!’

However, Ian’s current internal energy and skill were insufficient to absorb all of the medicinal effects.

It was then.

Geom Mugeuk’s hand touched her back. A stream of internal force entered her body through his palm. Geom Mugeuk’s internal force helped her, beginning to pull the scattered energy back into her meridians. Though it was but a single thread of energy, it helped more than a mighty army.

Even though he was helping, Geom Mugeuk had not assisted from the beginning.

He had let her gain sufficient experience in refining the spiritual medicine before helping her at the last moment. This was the good fortune Geom Mugeuk had spoken of earlier.

Ian persevered until the very end. She repeatedly controlled her true qi and refined the medicinal energy.

In the end, the energy of the Bingseolcho fruit transformed into vast internal energy and gathered in Ian’s dantian. The feeling of her dantian filling to the brim was truly an unforgettable pleasure. Especially since this spiritual medicine suited her constitution so well, her internal energy became even more pure and tranquil.

As a result, Ian’s eyes grew clearer and more intense.

“The energy felt from my dantian is completely different. Young Cult Leader, have you been living in such a wonderful world all this time?”

Then Chwima said, poking his head through the carriage window.

“Is the world of your Young Cult Leader truly only to that extent?”

“Right? If I am a puddle, the Young Master is the ocean, is he not?”

At her words implying the difference in internal energy was that great, Geom Mugeuk waved his hand.

“The difference is not that great.”

As if practicing modesty.

“If it were a stream and the ocean, perhaps.”

The two smiled at each other.

Ian bowed her head respectfully.

“Thank you for helping me become a stream.”

“Save that greeting for when you meet the Palace Lord later.”

He spoke those words, but Ian knew. She knew how great Geom Mugeuk’s help had been in refining the Bingseolcho. If not for him, she would have lost at least a third of the energy.

The two climbed back into the carriage.

Removing the small box that had contained the Bingseolcho fruit from inside the box, they found a letter and a secret manual underneath.

Ice Condensation Divine Art.

It was not the Palace Lord’s personal martial art, but it was a family martial art of the Ice Palace, ranking among the finest ice arts. The relationship was comparable to that between Geom Mugeuk’s Bicheon Swordsmanship and Guhwa Demonic Art.

“The secret manual is a copy, so she said to memorize it and burn it.”

No other words were written in the letter. She had not written anything at all, worried that Ian might feel burdened.

“Is it truly acceptable to receive all of this? Merely because we are blood relatives?”

Geom Mugeuk gave a ready answer.

“You can tell just from this gift how much the Palace Lord missed your mother. I believe accepting it gladly is the right heart for both of you.”

Ian said nothing more and began memorizing the secret manual.

Chwima, who had been watching her, sent a telepathic message to Geom Mugeuk.

—Call someone your heart and treat them like your heart, and they truly become your heart.

This was Chwima’s impression upon seeing Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

—If you can feel that, then it is time for you to take care of your own heart too, Hyung.

He said this while thinking of Yeobin, who had feelings for Chwima, but Chwima spoke evasively.

—When we get back, I should start by checking the wine stores. Let’s see if anything’s gone rotten somewhere!

Geom Mugeuk smiled and spoke loudly to the driver.

“Let us depart again!”

* * *

Of course, no matter how much stronger the heart had grown, it was still a baby’s heart left by the water’s edge.

When they reached the point where they had to leave the North Sea and part with Ian, Geom Mugeuk spoke to Chwima.

“Keep going straight like this. I’ll see Ian off for a moment and come back.”

Though Ian said there was no need, Geom Mugeuk sent the carriage ahead.

“Get on.”

“Why?”

“Hurry.”

Without explanation, Geom Mugeuk carried Ian on his back.

And he began to run. The swift movement technique that had reached the Twelve Constellations truly split the air like light.

The fact that it was comfortable despite running so fast—Geom Mugeuk’s true skill and realm lay not in speed, but in that comfort.

‘But where is he going?’

Ian was puzzled the entire time she was on his back.

After running without rest for nearly two hours, Geom Mugeuk finally stopped.

“Where is this?”

“I do not know, either.”

Ian made an expression asking what kind of joke this was, but Geom Mugeuk had truly run wherever his feet took him. And the reason was something Ian had never expected.

“Just in case someone was watching us.”

Ian asked in surprise.

“Was there someone watching?”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head. He had sensed that there were no watchers around, but just in case, a master of concealment could have been watching. Therefore, that person might have pursued Ian with malicious intent.

But if they ran like this with a swift movement technique for two hours, no watcher would be able to track Ian’s whereabouts.

“Are you not being too careful?”

“I am merely preparing for the slightest possibility. No excuse about letting your guard down just once will work for something that cannot be undone.”
