Ian was always moved by and grateful to Geom Mugeuk, but never more so than at this moment. Hiding her emotion, she asked playfully.

“If you worry about me this much, how did you send me away before?”

The situation was different now. Because this was after he had just killed the Blood King.

“If something happens despite all this effort, then it cannot be helped.”

She could understand that this meant he would not regret the outcome once he had done his best.

Ian engraved Geom Mugeuk’s teaching into her heart. She too would live this way from now on.

“This narrow stream shall return as a river.”

Chapter 434: The Cult Leader Woke Up.
-------------------------------------
Upon returning to the cult, I headed to the Cult Leader’s Hall with the Drunken Demon.

The thought of meeting Father had the Drunken Demon slightly tense.

Once before, the Drunken Demon had gotten drunk and caused a scene in front of Father, begging him not to hate him too much. The misunderstanding had been cleared up then, but that did not seem to mean Father had become easy for him to face.

“You can’t leave first! I’m going to pay my respects and then go.”

He wanted to avoid, at all costs, being left alone to face Father one-on-one.

“Where did that mysterious and frightening Lord Drunken Demon in the mist go?”

“He’s probably drunk and asleep after crawling under some carriage somewhere.”

Even so, he had brought a gift for Father. Before leaving the North Sea, he had stopped by a famous brewery and bought liquor to offer him.

When you looked closely, the Drunken Demon was someone who very much wanted to make a good impression on Father.

And so, together with the Drunken Demon, I entered the Cult Leader’s Hall.

As always, Father was seated in the grand preceptor’s chair. Whenever I entered this place, I was moved by the sight of Father unfailingly guarding that seat.

“Father! Your son has returned!”

At the same time, I also felt fear.

What if that grand preceptor’s chair were empty? What if I had to guard that chair? Would I truly be able to give others the same unwavering faith that Father did?

I was not afraid because I lacked the confidence to give that faith. I was afraid because I could.

Because my past life had confirmed that I, too, was someone who could silently hold that seat as Father did. Because in this life, I wanted to live brightly and freely.

“I have returned after finishing matters in the North Sea!”

I greeted Father in a loud voice.

Beside me, the Drunken Demon paid his respects with solemn courtesy.

“I have returned, Cult Leader.”

Father, looking down at the Drunken Demon, noticed his condition at once.

“Were you injured?”

“I was hurt a little, but I’m fine now.”

The Drunken Demon was already excited simply because Father had asked about his physical state. Though he had not drunk, I could feel heat rising from his face.

“I heard about the North Sea from the Strategist.”

I did not miss that moment and stepped in.

“Lord Drunken Demon personally eliminated the leader of the hidden forces that were attempting to swallow the Ice Palace.”

I had resolved to properly praise the Drunken Demon in front of Father today.

“Of course.”

At the word of course, the Drunken Demon looked up at Father with a startled expression.

Father looked not at the Drunken Demon, but at me, and said,

“He is not the sort of man who would go somewhere, lose, and come back, is he?”

I could tell. Father had already known how strong the Drunken Demon was.

Even without looking to the side, I could feel the Drunken Demon’s emotion. Perhaps this was the first time he had ever been acknowledged by Father like this. And in front of someone else, at that.

“Thanks to him, I was safe. This was the first time I saw Lord Drunken Demon fight, and it was truly magnificent. And more than anything.”

My gaze, which had been on Father, turned toward the Drunken Demon. The Drunken Demon, too, looked tense, wondering what I was about to say.

“He was admirable. Lord Drunken Demon, avenging his friend.”

I was saying it to raise him up in front of Father, but it was also the truth.

I gave Father a detailed account of what had happened in the North Sea. Of course, Father must already have received reports on the process and the result through Tongcheon Pavilion, but he naturally would not know the small incidents that had occurred in between.

It was something I always did when I returned after leaving the cult. When I told him what had happened like this, chattering away for a while, I felt my bond with Father deepen.

Today, I centered the story mainly on the Drunken Demon’s exploits. I spoke so entertainingly that it would not have bored him. I made it feel even more vivid than reality.

“…And so, as a token of gratitude to Lord Drunken Demon, the Ice Palace Master even bestowed upon him the Ice Palace Holy Grail.”

Even if I had not said so, Father had surely already recognized the Ice Palace Holy Grail. A white energy rippled around the cup like a halo, so it could not possibly escape notice.

He could have simply let it pass as something received in gratitude, as I had said, but the Drunken Demon did not let it slide.

“The Ice Palace Holy Grail was given to me because the Young Cult Leader gave up his own gift and accepted this instead.”

It would not have been strange for Father to look at me with eyes that said, He is not the sort of fellow who would give up his own thing for someone else. Instead, Father said something unexpected.

“May I have a cup from that goblet as well?”

“Of course. As it happens, I bought a famous liquor from the North Sea to offer to you, Cult Leader.”

With measured steps, the Drunken Demon walked up to where the grand preceptor’s chair stood.

Then he poured the liquor he had brought into the Ice Palace Holy Grail and offered it to Father. Each of his movements was careful and composed. Until just before entering the Cult Leader’s Hall, he had trembled whenever Father was mentioned, but at times like this, a Demon Supreme was still a Demon Supreme.

Father emptied the cup in one gulp.

“The liquor is good, and the cup is good as well. It makes the taste excellent.”

When Father was pleased, the Drunken Demon was delighted.

“Would you like another cup?”

“Rather than that, have a drink with me. It has been a while.”

For an instant, the startled Drunken Demon’s eyes trembled. This, too, seemed to be a first. Father asking him to drink together.

“I would be honored.”

After answering with a joyful expression, the Drunken Demon glanced toward me and signaled with his eyes for me to hurry up and leave.

Of course, there was no way I would miss such a fine chance to tease him.

—Didn’t you say I couldn’t leave first?

—Go already! I said go!

—Let’s drink as three. No, I’ll call all the other Demon Supremes too!

—No! Do you know how rare this chance is?

Only after teasing the Drunken Demon like that did I respectfully bow to Father.

“Then I will take my leave. I may have spoken ill of Elder Brother a great deal when he wasn’t here, but that doesn’t mean the two of you are allowed to speak ill of me just because I’m not here!”

I was about to turn and leave when Father abruptly spoke.

“Are you all right?”

For a moment, I flinched and turned back toward Father.

What did he mean by that?

My eyes met Father’s. Looking at those eyes silently gazing down at me, I understood why Father had asked. If he had included the words left unsaid, it would have been this.

Lately, you have stained your hands with too much blood. Are you all right?

I could feel what Father’s worry was.

No matter how deserving of death the villains were, continually staining one’s hands with blood was something that wounded a person’s heart and soul.

No matter how outstanding I appeared, to Father, I was still the immature youngest son in his twenties.

“That is why I am not being greedy and trying to do everything alone. I am asking the Demon Supremes for help. To protect myself.”

The Drunken Demon, too, listened to my words with a gaze as deep as Father’s.

“I am still all right. If I ever feel it becoming difficult, I will come to you.”

Father silently nodded.

“Then I will take my leave.”

I slowly moved my feet and left the Cult Leader’s Hall.

Father, please do not worry. Even when I stained myself with dozens of times more blood than this, I stood firm and came all the way here.

And more than anything.

“Young Cult Leader!”

From over there, Seo Daeryong was running toward me with a bright smile. He was so glad to see me that I could feel his heart even from this distance.

With people like this by my side, if I still were not all right, would that not make me far too sorry toward my own life?

* * *

Seo Daeryong greeted me as if a dead man had returned alive.

“I truly wanted to see you.”

He had been worried, and he had missed me as well.

He had become the Master of Hwangcheon Pavilion, and he had become the disciple of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Even though he was in a situation where he had to fully live his own life, with Geom Mugeuk gone, it felt as though the joy had vanished from his life.

Then he had received word just a little while ago that Geom Mugeuk had returned, so how happy must he have been? He had simply run straight to the Cult Leader’s Hall in a single breath.

“How have you been?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Seo Daeryong answered.

“The same as always. Train at dawn, eat, handle Hwangcheon Pavilion work, go to Master and train, eat, sleep. Every single day is so identical that even if you mixed them all up, there would be no problem at all. Is it all right for a person’s life to be this monotonous? I’ll say this in advance, but I refuse to hear anything like, ‘Ordinariness is happiness.’”

For a guy this talkative, how hard must it have been for him to play the dignified Master of Hwangcheon Pavilion?

“Isn’t it just that you’re the only one who doesn’t know?”

“What?”

“That every day is different. I’m asking if you’re the only one who thinks they’re the same.”

For a moment, Seo Daeryong could not answer. Strictly speaking, they were the same, but they were also days that were not entirely the same. Of course, he did not obediently admit it.

“The cases I handle are the same every day.”

“The prisoners are all different, aren’t they? Their personalities are different, and the reasons they committed their crimes are different.”

“I see the same subordinates every day.”

“Those people’s moods are different each day, their thoughts are different, the dreams they had the night before are different, and what they ate is different too.”

“When work ends, I meet Master and do nothing but train every day.”

“Your Ren and Du meridians were opened, and your martial skill has improved a great deal, hasn’t it? Naturally, your training must have changed as well.”

Geom Mugeuk was right. Recently, the techniques he was learning from his master had changed. As he had grown stronger, the training had become even harder.

“They’re different, aren’t they? Your days.”

“They’re the same, aren’t they? The fact that I can never win against anything the Young Cult Leader says.”

“That alone will never change for the rest of your life.”

“Nor will the Young Cult Leader’s smugness!”

We looked at each other and laughed.

“Now it finally feels like the Young Cult Leader has returned. No one says these kinds of things to me, you know. I have to hear words like this so that I can go, Ah! I see. And nod along as I live. Starting today, every one of my days will be different. I’ve just decided it will be so! Now I’m going to ask my subordinates. What did you eat for breakfast? Did you take a shit before coming? What dream did you have yesterday? What thoughts are you living with these days?”

Together with Seo Daeryong, I looked up at the sky. Beautiful flower clouds in many hues were drifting leisurely by. Seeing Daeryong made me truly feel that I had returned to the cult.

“Go and do your work.”

“Where are you going now?”

“I saw your face, so now I have to make a round to the Demon Supremes.”

“The Young Cult Leader really never gets tired.”

Daeryong, it isn’t a matter of numbers. One loathsome person alone is enough to wear someone out.

“Really, what would you have done if our cult had sixteen Demon Supremes?”

At Seo Daeryong’s joke, Geom Mugeuk answered as he strode away.

“If that were the case, our brother Drunken Demon’s sense of neglect would have doubled.”

* * *

“Elder!”

I ran toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon with both arms spread wide.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had been reading a book in his room, raised the blade standing beside him in front of himself like a shield.

“How cruel of you!”

From beyond the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade blocking the space between us, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s voice came.

“You should rest at home. What’s so good about seeing a face you see all the time that you come looking for me like this?”

He always says things he doesn’t mean like that. I moved past the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade and lightly sat at the edge of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s bed.

“If you put it that way, what’s so interesting about books you see all the time that you keep reading them like that?”

“This is different.”

As he said that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon warned me with his eyes, lest I lie down on the bed.

“I returned safely.”

Though the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had not asked, I first told him about my trip.

If I had made the Drunken Demon the protagonist when speaking to Father earlier, this time I spoke mainly about myself.

“And so, right now Father and the Drunken Demon are drinking together, just the two of them.”

“The Cult Leader is drinking with that drunkard?”

Was he perhaps jealous? A slight strange light passed through the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s eyes.

But soon, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon lowered his gaze back to the book he had been reading.

“Is reading books that fun?”

“It is.”

“You’ve read so many, and yet there are still things you don’t know?”

“That’s why it’s fun. The more I read, the more it lets me know how much I don’t know.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon lifted his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“When you have children later, have them read plenty of books. If they take after you anyway, they’ll be born with swordsmanship.”

Will I truly live a life in this lifetime where I have children? Right now, I had no such thought.

“No, Elder, are you telling me to live a life you yourself have never lived?”

With an expression that seemed to say, That is true, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon smiled faintly.

After watching for a moment as the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned the pages, I suddenly asked him the same thing Father had asked me.

“Do I look all right to you?”

Then, with his eyes still on the book, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered.

“You look like you aren’t all right.”

“Why?”

“Because people who are all right don’t ask that.”

Was that why? Unexpected words flowed from him.

“Sleep here tonight.”

He was always chasing me away, telling me to go home and sleep. He was the sort who would not even let me lie on the bed unless I washed first.

“What’s gotten into you?”

“You look like you aren’t all right.”

I smiled broadly and flung myself onto the bed.

“Sounds good!”

I thought he would catch me in midair with Void-Seizing Object, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not.

“Ah, this is comfortable!”

Whatever anyone said, lying on this bed was the most comfortable. It must have been because of the specialness of the person called the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

To think that this stiff, fussy old man would become the most comfortable person to me. To think he would become the person I wanted to run to first and see.

Lying on the bed, I gave an answer to a question he had not asked.

“I’m all right, Elder.”

“I know.”

And then came one shocking sentence.

“The one who is not all right is the Cult Leader.”

“!”

I sprang upright and stared at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. I still could not guess what he meant.

“I wondered whether the Cult Leader has awakened from his sleep and begun to stretch.”

I had already understood what those words meant, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon explained it more plainly.

“I wondered whether the Cult Leader has begun moving in earnest to realize his dream.”

Father’s dream was the unification of Murim.

“He has also made you his successor, so perhaps he thinks the time has come to move.”

“How long has it been since the tripartite meeting ended?”

That wonderful atmosphere was still vivid. Even now, just hearing the music from beyond the wall made my body sway.

“If he were the sort of person to care about that, he would not have dreamed such a dream in the first place. Rather, he may have been confident he could kill both of them that day.”

“Are you thinking that because he’s drinking with Lord Drunken Demon right now? Couldn’t they simply be drinking together?”

If it was because of that, it seemed like a wildly exaggerated misunderstanding. But that was not the only reason.

“Not long ago, the Cult Leader met privately with the masked fellow.”

“!”

Father met the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon?

If he had met the Fist Demon instead, perhaps I would not have had such suspicions. He was someone who would absolutely obey Father’s orders.

But the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon was someone who would absolutely follow me. Even if it meant defying Father’s command.

And yet Father had met him, of all people? On top of that, he was now drinking one-on-one with the Drunken Demon for the first time in his life? It was only natural that one could make this assumption.

“Father has begun managing the Demon Supremes in order to wage war.”

Chapter 435: The One Who Awakened the Cult Leader’s Dream.
----------------------------------------------------------
I had long known that Father dreamed of the unification of Murim.

The crucial question was when he would draw his blade—a matter of timing, nothing more.

If Hyeolcheon Doma's speculation was correct, that time was coming sooner than I thought.

"We haven't even completely uprooted the hidden forces lurking in the various sects lately, have we?"

To think of war in this situation?

I knew well that Father was not someone who handled such great matters emotionally. Nor was he someone who would make a new enemy while already facing one.

How could Hyeolcheon Doma not know that?

Though they had grown distant now, Hyeolcheon Doma had been closer to Father in his youth than anyone. That was precisely why he knew Father better than anyone else. And yet, the reason he made such a speculation was exactly this:

"You know as well as I do that Murim has been peaceful for a very long time. There were no real fights. But this time, while facing those hidden forces, the Majon were awakened."

He was right. All the Majon had taken turns participating in the battle. They had experienced true combat against formidable enemies. For master-level experts like the Majon, an opportunity to face opponents of similar strength was a blessing one might hardly encounter a handful of times in a lifetime.

"By facing them, we naturally began full-scale war preparations."

Hyeolcheon Doma's astonishing words came now.

"And above all, the cult has gained a combat strength more powerful than anything else."

"What do you mean?"

Hyeolcheon Doma looked at me in silence.

"Surely you don't mean me?"

Hyeolcheon Doma nodded.

He was right. My martial arts had grown the most while facing the hidden forces.

"Thanks to you, the cult's chances of achieving the unification of Murim have grown much greater."

That was also correct. If I achieved the great completion of Guhwamagong and combined my strength with Father's, we could accomplish the unification of Murim far more easily than if he attempted it alone.

Finally, Hyeolcheon Doma added one more astonishing thing.

"Perhaps you are the one who awakened the Cult Leader's dream."

"!"

I could not refute those words. Whether it was true or not, Hyeolcheon Doma's argument was persuasive.

Because recently, I was the one who had influenced Father the most.

"Of course, my speculation could be wrong."

Father had changed a lot lately as well. Perhaps he had simply been influenced by how I treated the Majon and thought he should become closer to them. If that were the case, it would truly be a good thing.

If not, he might simply be placating the Majon in order to uproot the hidden forces. How wonderful would it be if that were his mindset?

Truly, nothing was as difficult as reading Father's inner thoughts.

Hyeolcheon Doma's final reason for this speculation lay in the distant past.

"The Cult Leader has dreamed of unifying Murim since he was young."

"Did you ever speak of it?"

The light in Hyeolcheon Doma's eyes deepened as he recalled the past.

"It was before he ascended to the position of Heavenly Demon. At the time, he roamed the Central Plains with Gwonma, fighting wherever they went. Just like you now, the two of them traveled around without any guards. Then one day, the Cult Leader returned to the cult with an excited face and spoke to me."

In that time when everyone was young, what had Father said to Hyeolcheon Doma?

Hyeolcheon Doma conveyed Father's words exactly.

"I shall have this Murim."

The moment I heard those words, the image of Father in his youth came to mind.

The appearance of him at the very first moment he held the dream of unifying Murim. What had been the catalyst for Father's dream? Had he seen a good side of Murim then? Or an ugly one?

"After that, the Cult Leader never brought it up again."

"It was likely the first and last time. Father is not someone who goes around revealing such dreams to just anyone."

When he had wanted to speak of the passion boiling in his heart, Hyeolcheon Doma had been that sole recipient.

Because of that shared past, Hyeolcheon Doma had once been able to say with conviction that while he could give up on children, he could not give up on him.

I grew heavy of heart. Could I make Father give up on a dream he had held since then?

"Elder."

"What is it?"

"If Father orders you to strike the Murim Alliance, what will you do?"

Hyeolcheon Doma lowered his gaze to his book. He was clearly troubled.

"Please speak comfortably. I am ready to feel hurt!"

I said it like a joke, but Hyeolcheon Doma did not take it as one.

"I am not ready to hurt you yet. Nor your father."

From those words, I could understand his heart. No matter how close we had become, no matter how distant he had grown from Father, defying Father's order was never going to be easy. Leaving aside all personal matters, it was the relationship between the Cult Leader and a Majon.

The mere fact that he agonized like this showed how much he cherished me.

"I will not let you face a moment of choice."

It was not only Hyeolcheon Doma but the other Majon as well. I would not let them fall into such anguish, forced to disobey Father's command for my sake. This matter was between Father and me alone.

"How do you plan to do that?"

"Do you not know what kind of demonic path I dream of? I must persuade Father somehow."

Hyeolcheon Doma's gaze returned to his book. After staring blankly at it for a moment, he said quietly,

"One cannot live as everyone wishes."

Whether he was speaking to me, to Father, or to himself, I could not tell.

* * *

I told him I'd bring pajamas and sleep another time, then left Hyeolcheon Doma's quarters. I felt he would want to be alone.

I decided to think about this matter positively.

This opportunity marked the moment to advance my relationship with Father, and naturally with the Majon as well, to the next level. Becoming close is easy. The problem always comes after that.

Now we had stepped onto a new proving ground for our relationships.

When I returned to my quarters, someone was training in martial arts in the courtyard.

It was Jeogyeon, the leader of the guards. The news of my return must have already reached him, so it seemed he had been waiting at my quarters until I arrived.

I watched his training without interrupting. His skill had visibly improved compared to when I had last seen him. It meant he had been training relentlessly while I was in the Northern Sea.

When his training round ended, I revealed myself.

"You've improved a lot."

At my appearance, Jeogyeon greeted me formally.

"Young Lord! I have eagerly awaited your safe return."

His face was filled with joy.

"I was waiting after hearing word of your return. I sent back the other guards who wanted to stay, telling them to train."

Just hearing that, I could guess how diligently they had been training. They did not want to miss even a day of their scheduled practice.

"Take off your eyepatch first."

"I'm fine."

"I always say this, but I'm not fine with it."

When I see his eyes, I am reminded of Ian's Jeonsinseokhwagong. Feeling the hearts of guards who bear such pain to protect someone, I want to free them from their agony as soon as possible.

The moment the eyepatch was removed, I was startled.

His eyes had returned to normal.

"You've achieved great completion!"

As if he had intended to surprise me, Jeogyeon finally smiled.

"It is thanks to the Young Lord. Perhaps because you tended to my eyes from time to time, I made rapid progress in training the Ghost Eye Technique."

I knew it wasn't just that. He must have endured great pain and trained fiercely.

"But why are you still wearing the eyepatch?"

"It feels awkward to suddenly take it off."

"Take it off. If you do, the heroines will follow you around."

"Then I must wear it all the more. I plan to follow the Young Lord around, after all."

Perhaps because the pain in his eyes was gone? He felt much brighter than before.

Maybe because joking was unfamiliar to him, he hastily made his farewell.

"Then please rest."

"Jeogyeon."

"Yes, Young Lord."

I couldn't send him away like this when he had waited until late just to see me.

"You should at least have a celebratory drink."

I drank with Jeogyeon and we talked about this and that.

Just as I reported on what happened with Father, Jeogyeon told me everything that had occurred within the cult. He had gathered information one by one, hoping it might help me.

He also told me that the guards' skills had greatly improved. He spoke of how hard they were working.

"Perhaps because they all yearn to serve and accompany the Young Lord, everyone is training with all their might."

I always told them: to walk with me, they must become stronger.

Hyeolcheon Doma's words were right. The cult's strength was growing everywhere.

Especially everyone connected to me was growing stronger. Even Ilhwa Geomjon, who had not fought the hidden forces, had grown stronger through sparring with me.

Perhaps those words were really true.

That it was really I who had awakened Father's dream.

* * *

The next day, I went to Akingok to meet Geukak Soma.

My sleeping mat and his were still well-placed in Geukak Soma's quarters.

There were even newly added desks and chairs that hadn't been there before.

Geukak Soma was looking at something at the desk.

"I have returned safely."

The two eyes behind Geukak Soma's mask smiled brightly at me.

"You have come?"

Geukak Soma welcomed me with his usual unchanged demeanor.

"You've gotten a desk."

Of course, the desk and chairs were painted pure white.

"It is comfortable to have."

He then picked up what he had been looking at and showed it. It was a ledger with numbers written on it.

"These days, I am also studying something new."

Seeing him like that reminded me of when I went to the Central Plains with him in the past. Back then, Cheongmyeon had told him the contents through voice transmission, but now he was looking at it himself. He must have learned how to read a ledger in the meantime.

I thought it was a good sign. When master-level experts like the Majon ascended to the next realm, they sometimes drew inspiration from completely unexpected places. That was what made them fearsome. Hyeolcheon Doma reading books, Geukak Soma reading ledgers.

These were changes that had occurred since I brought the sleeping mat. I could feel what Geukak Soma's heart was in placing the desk.

My heart had simply been to lie down and sleep comfortably; he was answering with an even greater change. As though saying that thanks to me, he had changed this much.

"How was the Northern Sea?"

"It was a magnificent and beautiful place. You must have gone often."

Since Geukak Soma liked the color white, I had thought he surely went often.

"I went exactly once. I simply detest the cold."

I laughed loudly at the unexpected reason.

This time, Geukak Soma asked.

"Then you must have visited the Northern Sea Ice Palace as well."

"Visited? I stayed there for quite some time."

"It is known to not accept outsiders. As expected of the Young Lord."

I sat at the edge of my sleeping mat and told him in detail what had happened in the Northern Sea. I told him everything about Hyeolwang and his subordinates without omission.

Repeating the same story was sometimes tedious, but I did not mind. I believed that even a small piece of information could later become precious enough to save a life.

After delivering the story of the Northern Sea, I brought up the most important business of today's visit.

"I heard you met Father some time ago."

"Yes, I did."

"I am curious what conversation you had with Father that day."

A flicker of puzzlement crossed Geukak Soma's eyes behind the mask. After all, I was asking about a conversation between the Heavenly Demon and a Majon. No matter how close we were, it was not something to ask lightly.

"I know it is a rude question."

"You must have a reason, of course."

I did not hide the matter from him.

"I suspect Father has begun moving to unify Murim."

At the words "unification of Murim," Geukak Soma's eyes blazed intensely.

"I thought so after seeing him spar with Chwima-nim yesterday. I wondered if Father is finally drawing his blade in earnest."

Only then did Geukak Soma's eyes show that he understood why I had asked such a question.

"When he met me, he said nothing at all regarding the unification of Murim. It was merely matters within the cult and casual conversation. He spoke mostly of the Young Lord."

"Of me?"

"Yes. He asked me to look after you well in the future."

Father met Geukak Soma and asked him to look after me?

With my relationship with Father having improved lately, that was possible.

Yet my unease did not fade.

"Are you worried?"

"Honestly, I am worried. Worried enough to come visit you and be this rude."

Leaving aside my relationship with them, what thoughts did the Majon hold?

Since I regressed, they had truly changed a great deal from before. Their relationships with me had changed as well.

But had their perspectives and hearts regarding Murim changed as well? If I were to ask something like this:

Would Geukak Soma truly like a peaceful Murim? Or would he want to race across battlefields, unleashing demonic energy?

Could I be certain that he liked peace? Was the Geukak Soma before me such a person?

Then Geukak Soma spoke. It was as though he had read my mind.

"I can endure it."

I knew what heart spoke those words. This was Geukak Soma's heart for me.

"I do not wish for Soma-nim to live a life of endurance."

His eyes behind the mask asked: Is there a way?

"We must find it from now on. I will make it so that Father and the Majon say something other than 'I can endure it.'"

What words should replace "endurance," I did not yet know.

"Young Lord."

"Yes, Soma-nim."

Geukak Soma spoke quietly with clear, deep eyes.

"No matter what order is given, I will follow the Young Lord. The Young Lord's demonic path is my demonic path."

Chapter 436: Even Wounds Are Split Eight Ways.
----------------------------------------------
Behind the mask, Extreme Evil Soma’s two eyes held sincerity.

When he said he would follow me no matter what order came down, it meant he would support me even if it meant defying the Heavenly Demon’s command.

The mere fact that he had said those words could cost him his life. To defy the Heavenly Demon’s orders meant death, regardless of rank or status.

Just now, Extreme Evil Soma had risked his life for me.

That was why my gaze and my voice trembled as I looked at him.

“This must be why. Why Father met you first among the Demon Venerables, Soma-nim.”

Extreme Evil Soma quietly gazed at me, then said something unexpected.

“I hope you do not make this matter one between father and son.”

“What do you mean?”

“I mean that you must not let this drive a wedge between the two of you. Instead, make it a matter between the Cult Leader and the Demon Venerables.”

Extreme Evil Soma was worried about the relationship between me and my father.

“Young Cult Leader, you must make active use of the Demon Venerables. I do not know about other matters, but if it is war, the will of the Demon Venerables is absolutely important.”

Because no matter how much of a Heavenly Demon he was, when it came to a war in which all our lives were at stake, he could not make the decision on his own.

“If more than half of the Demon Venerables oppose it, even the Cult Leader will have no choice but to withdraw his will.”

But would the other Demon Venerables really stand on my side as willingly as Extreme Evil Soma had? They would not. Even Blood Heaven Blade Demon had agonized over it and failed to reach a decision.

“I may have grown closer to the Demon Venerables, but making them go against Father’s will will be difficult.”

“Of course it will be difficult. Even so, if you are to fulfill your demonic path, Young Cult Leader, you must do it.”

I silently nodded and revealed my honest feelings.

“Because we’ve grown closer than before, it feels even harder. In the past, I would unleash my Divine Art of Flattery and practically bewitch them with all sorts of words, wouldn’t I? Now that feels a little difficult. Has the time finally come to return to the beginner’s mind of flattery?”

Even at my joke, the two eyes behind the mask remained serious.

“Do you think the Demon Venerables fell for that Divine Art of Flattery?”

“Did they not?”

Extreme Evil Soma spoke firmly.

“They fell for your sincerity, Young Cult Leader. The sincerity that was always there beneath that flattery and brilliance you speak of. Your demonic path, which tries to protect even a mere tavern owner. I believe they all fell for that.”

The reason he was speaking more than usual was surely because he was truly thinking of me.

“So you may be even more unrestrained. Whether it is flattery or coercion. Shout at them to follow you because your demonic path is thus and so. Do not worry about the Demon Venerables. Do not worry that you might burden them. If those people have made a decision, then they chose it because it was worth doing, and they must have made that decision because they could bear it.”

After a brief pause, Extreme Evil Soma added,

“There are eight of us. Even if we are wounded, the eight of us share the wounds. So do not hesitate. Draw that sword.”

The Extreme Evil Soma before my regression had not been this kind of person. And now I could see that such heat existed deep within his heart.

The heart that had wished to become my friend even until the very moment of his death must have stemmed from this very heat.

Soma, in this life, let us go together until the very end. Let us remain friends to the very end.

This was my answer to Soma’s heat.

I cupped my fist politely and conveyed my gratitude.

“Yes, I will keep it in mind. Thank you for your advice.”

Extreme Evil Soma also cupped his fist politely to me.

“I should be the one thanking you.”

“What do you mean?”

“Thanks to the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng you gave me before, my martial arts have risen by one level.”

“Ah, congratulations!”

I rejoiced as if it were my own achievement. No, I was happier than if I had achieved it myself.

And I could not accept even this gratitude.

“That achievement is probably not because of the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.”

After all, simply increasing one’s internal energy did not mean one’s martial arts would move on to the next level.

Many factors must have played a part. The Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng would have had an influence, but so would everything from the life-and-death battle he fought with me to the change in his heart that led him to bring a bed into his room and study the ledgers.

“My martial arts had been stagnant for a long time. It was extremely frustrating, but this time I broke through and rose. Thanks to that, there has been a great change in my martial arts as well.”

He was happy. Reaching the next stage from the level of a Demon Venerable was truly a difficult thing.

Perhaps this was what he most wanted to hear from me right now.

“I look forward to our next fight.”

As expected, Extreme Evil Soma’s eyes smiled brightly.

“Then I will take my leave.”

Before I left, Extreme Evil Soma emphasized one fact.

“If you wish to stop the war, you must absolutely make one of the Eight Demon Venerables take your side.”

“Who is that?”

The person Extreme Evil Soma had in mind was none other than—

“The Poison King.”

In truth, I had been thinking the same thing.

Because in war, the person who would inflict the greatest fear upon the enemy was the Poison King.

The moment war broke out, he would become the number one assassination target of the Martial Alliance and the Heretical Alliance. In reality, countless assassins and assassination squads would be mobilized to kill him.

Perhaps he would even be classified as an enemy who had to be killed before Father or me. That was how terrifying a being capable of mass slaughter like the Poison King would be.

I thought of the Poison King alone in the forest. Of him lost in his own world no matter what anyone around him said, staring at poisonous plants and venomous insects.

“He is the most difficult person to persuade among the Demon Venerables.”

Extreme Evil Soma’s eyes gleamed meaningfully as he said,

“That will likely be true for the Cult Leader as well.”

* * *

After leaving Extreme Evil Soma’s residence, I went straight to Drunken Dream Pavilion.

Meeting the other Demon Venerables was important, but more urgent than that was finding out Father’s true intentions.

Just what conversation had he had with the Drunken Demon?

Yeobin, whom I had not seen in a long time, greeted me warmly and guided me to Drunken Dream Pavilion.

“I am glad you returned safely, Young Cult Leader.”

“It was thanks to the Drunken Demon-nim.”

“The Drunken Demon-nim said so. That he gained much from this journey.”

“On this journey, I learned for the first time just how wonderful the Drunken Demon-nim is.”

When I praised the Drunken Demon, Yeobin smiled faintly.

I rode the boat she rowed and crossed to Drunken Dream Pavilion in the middle of the lake.

The Drunken Demon was sitting on the railing of Drunken Dream Pavilion, gazing at the lake. At times like this, I was reminded of Extreme Evil Soma, who had sat alone staring at a white wall.

Even from afar, I could tell he was drunk.

“He has been drinking continuously ever since he returned from seeing the Cult Leader.”

“Did he come back in a bad mood?”

“No, he is drinking because he is in such a good mood.”

The ferryboat reached the island like that.

When he saw me, the Drunken Demon waved his hand and shouted,

“You’re here? Come on up! My little brother!”

He shouted excitedly in a slurred voice.

When I went up to the pavilion, empty wine bottles were scattered everywhere. Even now, he was pouring wine into the Ice Palace Holy Cup and drinking from it.

“What is this rampage? Didn’t you promise that when you drank with that cup, you’d drink only one cup where you would have drunk two?”

“It’s a special day, so overlook it just this once. I feel so good that I can’t not drink.”

Had I ever seen the Drunken Demon in such a good mood before?

“Looks like your drinking session with Father was pleasant. Did you drink a lot?”

“Don’t even ask. We emptied all the wine in the Cult Leader’s Hall. I didn’t realize it while we were drinking, but once I came out of the hall, the intoxication hit me all at once. When I was with the Cult Leader, I was so nervous I didn’t even know I was drunk.”

I could imagine how nervous he must have been without even seeing it.

“It was your first time drinking one-on-one with Father, wasn’t it?”

“I’ve drunk with him a few times. I drank with him when I first became a Demon Venerable too. But back then, it was just a light cup or two. Or there were other people with us. This was the first time I properly drank with the Cult Leader.”

“How was it?”

“It was good. It was really good.”

“What did you talk about?”

“All sorts of things. The Cult Leader even remembered everything from when I was young.”

As if the memory alone pleased him, the Drunken Demon drank happily.

“The words that made me happiest were these. The Cult Leader said he felt at ease sending you out with me. Ah, I never knew the Cult Leader trusted me this much.”

The Drunken Demon truly looked moved. Had Father really begun managing the Demon Venerables? Or had he simply wished to spend time with the Drunken Demon?

“This is all thanks to you.”

“Why?”

“If you hadn’t accepted this Ice Palace Holy Cup, the Cult Leader would never have suggested we drink, and he suggested it because the wine tasted so good in this cup.”

“It might not be because of the Holy Cup.”

“No?”

“He might be trying to use this opportunity to make you Father’s right arm, you know?”

At that, the Drunken Demon shook his head firmly.

“That cannot be.”

“You never know.”

After a brief pause, the Drunken Demon said,

“I am not the kind of person the Cult Leader would like. The Cult Leader likes people like the Fist Demon or Blood Heaven Blade Demon.”

“Seeing how clearly you’re judging things, you’re not drunk at all.”

Because no matter what order Father gave, the Fist Demon was a person who would obey it without the slightest hesitation. That was the deep trust possessed by the man known as the Fist Demon.

The Drunken Demon stared at the lake for a moment, then abruptly asked,

“Why did you come here?”

He had realized that if I had come to Drunken Dream Pavilion when there were many Demon Venerables I needed to seek out, there had to be a reason.

Now it was time to throw a stone into this calm lake.

If I honestly stated my business, I would pour cold water over the Drunken Demon’s good mood. And it was not even something that had been confirmed yet. Should I just let it pass pleasantly?

There are times when, under the pretext of being considerate of the other person, one wants to avoid troublesome and uncomfortable situations. This was exactly one of those times. If I simply said nothing, we could pass it over with a smile. If I brought it up, things would become serious and faces would redden.

But it was precisely at such times that one had to think from the other person’s perspective. How would I feel if someone failed to speak honestly to me, claiming it was for my sake? Would that consideration not become a wound?

Putting myself in his position, I thought I would feel bad.

Setting everything else aside, the decisive reason for not telling him would be that I did not want to ruin your mood because you were so happy? That was not right.

And one more thing. If it were a secret no one knew, that would be one thing, but this was something Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew, and Extreme Evil Soma knew as well.

“Did you enjoy it enough?”

“What do you mean?”

“I’m asking if you sufficiently savored Father’s affection.”

The Drunken Demon’s expression hardened slightly. He had realized that what I was about to say was related to his drinking session with Father.

“I’m sorry to spoil your good mood. But it’s something I have to tell you honestly.”

I conveyed to the Drunken Demon the essence of the conversations I had shared with the two Demon Venerables earlier.

“Nothing is certain yet. We’re only guessing at Father’s true intentions.”

After listening to the whole story, the Drunken Demon sank into thought for a moment.

“I thought carefully back on yesterday’s drinking session, but he did not mention a single thing about the part you are worried about. Nor did he ever hint at it.”

Then, as if he suddenly craved alcohol, the Drunken Demon downed several cups in succession. His expression said exactly this: Of course, there’s no way the Cult Leader would like me!

“We still don’t know the exact circumstances. He may have wanted to resolve the distance that had existed between him and you until now. Who can know what Father is thinking?”

As he filled his cup again, the Drunken Demon pointed out one fact.

“Still, one thing is certain.”

“What?”

“He told me to take care of you too. You said he said something like that to Soma as well.”

“He said it to you too?”

The Drunken Demon nodded. Father had asked someone to take care of me again?

“Could he have given up on Soma-nim and you entirely and acknowledged you as being on my side?”

At that, the Drunken Demon looked aggrieved.

“Why would he give up on me?”

“What? Hyung, weren’t you on my side?”

At that, the Drunken Demon’s expression turned serious as he said,

“Young Cult Leader, you must distinguish between public and private matters.”

It was half joke, half sincerity, but it felt closer to sincerity.

Yes, and how could it be only the Drunken Demon? No matter how close they had grown to me, how could they refuse Father’s command? There was absolutely no need for me to feel disappointed about this point.

However, I did want to know what the Drunken Demon thought on this occasion.

“Even if that order is an order to start a war?”

The Drunken Demon gave no answer. Did the Drunken Demon want to roam through the fog of the battlefield? Or did he want to drink here, gazing at that beautiful lake?

The Drunken Demon did not give a direct answer to my question.

“When I was dealing with those bastards who used the Self-Destruction Art this time, I had this thought. These pieces of trash are running wild without knowing how terrifying our cult is.”

I could tell that the Drunken Demon was speaking indirectly.

I want to sweep away everything that ought to be swept away.

Of course, that did not necessarily mean he wanted to start a war, but I could tell it also did not mean he wished to live his life gazing at this lake.

“So in the end, you’re on Father’s side.”

“What sides are there, yours and mine?”

“This time, I think it can’t help but become a fight between sides.”

Chwima drank without answering.

“When I see Father, I’ll be sure to tell him about your loyalty. I’ll say, Your loyalty to Father was stronger than our bond as brothers!”

At that, Chwima said in a dispirited voice,

“Do you think he’ll really be pleased?”

“What do you mean?”

“I think he might dislike it instead.”

“Why would Father dislike you saying you’ll follow him?”

Chwima drank again.

“The Cult Leader told me to take care of you, didn’t he? For all I know, he might be expecting me to side with you.”

Chwima looked at me and held out his cup. After drinking the wine he poured for me, I poured him another cup in return.

“If you know that so well, why are you doing this?”

“Why do you think? Because I’m a drunkard.”

Chwima had said there was no such thing as your side or my side, but I could feel it. He was someone who truly wanted to be on Father’s side.

Deep in Chwima’s heart, there was surely a desire to look good in Father’s eyes.

Father was that kind of person to Chwima—the one person before whom he wanted to appear better, the one person from whom he wanted to be acknowledged more.

I could not know where the source of that longing came from. Whether it was because of the misunderstanding he had held for so long, believing Father hated him. Or whether there was some other reason.

“Young Cult Leader.”

Since the playfulness had vanished from Chwima, I answered respectfully as well.

“Yes, Lord Chwima.”

“You must persuade the other Demon Venerables.”

“I will.”

After bowing politely, I added with a shout.

“I’ll do what! Brother, you’ve abandoned me too, so who’s going to take my side?”

“I’m sorry. I have no choice. He remembered a version of me even I barely remember myself.”

“Next time I leave the cult and come back, I’m coming to see you last again!”

As I grumbled like that and was about to rise, Chwima said,

“Thank you for telling me honestly.”

I looked into his eyes and answered calmly.

“Of course I should. You’re my brother.”

At my words, Chwima smiled in deep relief.

Yes, this was the heart of our relationship today. I had been honest with him, and he had been honest as well.

Honesty sometimes becomes a double-edged sword and hurts us, but we were people who handled blades better than anyone.

That was why, even if Brother said he would side with Father, we could still grow closer.

“Who’s next?”

Someone who might perhaps know Father’s true thoughts.

As I slowly walked down from Chwimongru, I clenched my fist.

“I’m going to try crashing into it from the opposite direction. I’m heading for the biggest, hardest cliff.”

Chapter 437: Do You Like Father? Or Me?
---------------------------------------
A faint thunderclap echoed through the clear sky.

Cheon Sohui was the one staring with wide eyes at the punch she had just thrown.

“Did you hear it?”

When the Gwonma standing before her nodded, Cheon Sohui shouted, overcome with emotion.

“Master! Your disciple has done it!”

At last, the sound of thunder had erupted from her own fist.

Following Geom Mugeuk, she had become the Gwonma’s second disciple and had been designated as the next Gwonma.

From that day, she had trained tirelessly in the Thunderbolt Asura Fist she learned from the Gwonma, and today was the first time she had produced the sound of thunder.

Knowing better than anyone how hard Cheon Sohui had worked, the Gwonma smiled. It was a precious smile rarely seen from him.

“This is only the beginning.”

“Yes, Master.”

She was so overjoyed she felt as though she could fly.

She knew there was gossip and whispering behind her back. The envy and jealousy were unavoidable.

She also knew there were words of concern about a woman having been internally designated as the next Gwonma.

After all, the current generation’s Gwonma was symbolized by that fearsome face, massive frame, and above all, those huge fists.

So she understood. But she would show them clearly. She would show them with her skill what this small fist of the next Gwonma could shatter.

Then someone spoke from behind.

“Congratulations, Junior Sister.”

She turned in surprise to see Geom Mugeuk entering the training grounds.

“Senior Brother! You’ve returned!”

Having been completely immersed in her training of late, she hadn’t even known that Geom Mugeuk had returned.

Geom Mugeuk answered her with a smile and greeted the Gwonma respectfully.

“Your disciple has returned from completing the Bukhae journey.”

“Welcome back.”

The Gwonma welcomed him with the same bearing as always. His fearsome face was unchanged, and his muscular body shone even more brilliantly.

“There were scoundrels on the Binggung side plotting something as well. They all collapsed dead drunk in the end, though.”

“I heard. Chwima-nim said you had a difficult time.”

He must have spoken with Father about the situation in Bukhae.

*Master, do you know what Father is thinking?*

Long ago, when Father was young, he had spoken of his dreams to Hyeolcheon Doma with an impassioned face. By now, wouldn’t he have spoken of such things to the Gwonma as well?

After exchanging greetings with the Gwonma, he turned his attention to Cheon Sohui.

“To already produce the sound of thunder. Truly remarkable.”

“It is all thanks to Master’s guidance.”

“I’ll treat you to celebratory drinks soon.”

“Yes, Senior Brother.”

Cheon Sohui was deeply respectful to Geom Mugeuk. Not only because he was her senior brother, but because he was the precious one destined to become the next Cheonma.

But there was a greater reason than that.

It was thanks to Geom Mugeuk that she had become the next Gwonma. Her fate had changed at the late-night training gathering she attended with him.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking some distance away, turned back to her.

“Junior Sister, you will become a truly magnificent Gwonma.”

In that moment, in Cheon Sohui’s heart, she did not call him Senior Brother. Nor did she call him Young Cult Leader.

*Cult Leader, I shall serve you with all my loyalty.*

* * *

The place I arrived at with the Gwonma was the cliff within the Donggwonmun.

To destroy this cliff in a single blow was the Gwonma’s dream. Just how much had that dream grown?

I stood shoulder-to-shoulder with the Gwonma and looked up at the cliff. It was still overwhelming. And the cliff resembled the Gwonma who gazed up at it.

Could this cliff truly be toppled?

Could this Gwonma truly be persuaded?

After looking up for a long while, I began with a story to gently loosen his heart.

“Ian has left to carry out his mission.”

“I know.”

“How?”

“He sent me a letter.”

It seemed that boy was certainly looking after his father.

“Then you must have heard the news that he found his family in Bukhae.”

The Gwonma silently nodded. Instead of asking if he was surprised, I said this.

“The Binggungju was greatly astonished by Ian’s identity. The niece he found had become Master’s adopted daughter.”

The one who should be surprised was not us, but them. I said this to elevate the Gwonma even above the Binggungju.

“Did you write a reply as well?”

Surprisingly, the Gwonma nodded. As if to say, *of course*.

I tried to imagine the Gwonma holding a brush—so minuscule in that massive hand—to write a reply. He wasn’t the type to write letters, so wouldn’t he have agonized over what to say?

But for that moment alone, he must have been a father who worried for and missed his daughter.

“I heard that if you had not gone to Bukhae this time, An would have been in danger.”

“I would like to say she would have handled it well even without me, but it likely would have been dangerous.”

The Gwonma’s gaze softened slightly. For now, he was not merely a disciple and the Young Cult Leader, but a disciple and Young Cult Leader who had also saved his daughter from crisis.

Now was my chance.

“Master. May I ask one question that is difficult to answer?”

He must never have imagined he would hear such a question from me.

“Do you like Father, or do you like me?”

The Gwonma looked at me with a bewildered expression.

“Too difficult?”

“No. I was surprised because it is far too easy.”

The Gwonma looked back up at the cliff, and I looked at the Gwonma.

Could I truly learn Father’s intentions from him, a man whose loyalty was higher and harder than this cliff?

If I had the Geukak Soma, then Father had the Gwonma.

If only I could make the Gwonma my ally, stopping Father’s ambition of unifying the martial world would be far easier.

But toppling this cliff before my eyes felt harder than toppling that great cliff.

“Father has become strange.”

“The Cult Leader?”

“He met separately with the Geukak Soma, and schemed with the Chwima.”

Whether he was feigning ignorance or truly thought nothing of it, the Gwonma asked bluntly.

“What is strange about the Cult Leader meeting a Majon?”

“If it were something he did all the time, it would not be strange.”

The Gwonma’s gaze shifted from the cliff to me.

“What are you trying to say?”

“I want to know Father’s plans.”

“If you are curious, ask the Cult Leader yourself.”

That was a poor move. Father had openly decided on a scheme with the Chwima while I was present. He must know that word of his separate meeting with the Geukak Soma would reach my ears.

Even when I first regressed and stood upon the dueling stage, Father knew I had been struck by sangongdok. Yet he did not intervene; he only watched. Watching to see how I would find my way out.

It is the same now. Father is watching me. It is like a game of Go. I have made this move. What move will you make now?

I intended to show him. That more than half the Majon oppose the war.

Even if Father’s recent overtures to the Geukak Soma and the Chwima were not for that reason, it did not matter.

Since Father harbors the ambition of unifying the martial world, this was a confrontation that would come someday.

“If he were someone who would tell me simply because I asked, I would not have agonized. You know what kind of person Father is.”

On that point, at least, the Gwonma did not deny it.

“To be honest, I was on high alert the moment I returned. Why is he suddenly managing the Majon when he never did before? Has he begun to move in earnest for the unification of the martial world?”

Though the words “unification of the martial world” were spoken, the Gwonma did not waver in the slightest.

“Did you know? Even you, Master, are acting strangely. You are too calm. As if you already know everything. Please tell me what it is you know.”

The Gwonma’s lips were heavy. To open those heavy lips, I honestly laid bare my situation.

“Should my suspicions prove correct, I am currently trying to win the Majon to my side. Of the three I have met so far, one is with me, one is with Father, and one remains undecided.”

What drew the Gwonma’s attention was the single undecided vote.

“Which Majon is undecided?”

“The venerable Doma.”

The Gwonma’s face showed a flicker of surprise before he nodded as if he understood.

“Doma truly cherishes you. For him to be unable to decide with that temperament of his.”

It was true. It should be one or the other. For Hyeolcheon Doma, being neither was intolerable.

But Hyeolcheon Doma was conflicted. Enduring the gaze that called him spineless, solely for my sake.

“Master is obviously on my side, is he not? After all, whose side would you take if not your disciple’s?”

“There are two disciples, and one Cult Leader.”

“I am your disciple, and also the Young Cult Leader.”

“Sohee will side with you later, no doubt.”

I knew his heart could not be turned with a few words.

So I asked him directly. The Gwonma was not one to speak in circles.

“If Father starts a war, will you accept that command?”

The Gwonma’s eyes flashed with intensity.

“If it is the Cult Leader’s command, of course I must accept it.”

I met that fearsome face and spoke.

“That is precisely why you, of all people, must oppose it.”

“What do you mean?”

“Because Father cannot ignore your words above all others.”

I knew these were burdensome words for the Gwonma. To him, who lived as a Majon, I was forcing my will upon him.

But like the Geukak Soma had said, I was shouting to the Majon. To walk my demonic path with me. Yes, I believe. My attack will come divided into eight.

“War is the one thing that cannot happen, Master.”

The Gwonma silently stared at me, then began removing his upper garment.

“It has been a while since we trained.”

“That war—are you declaring it on me?”

Even at my jest, the Gwonma did not stop undressing.

The Gwonma’s upper body was revealed.

His physique was perfect no matter when one saw it. The large, thick muscles forged through grueling training showed no sign of sluggishness. His body shone like adamantine.

A single sword scar divided the Gwonma’s chest. I had heard long ago how he received that wound. It was from the days when he fought in an underground arena before entering the main cult.

It was a wound received after refusing the threats of the underworld criminals who rigged matches—wounds left by men he could now defeat with a single finger. He was ashamed of that scar, but I had told him it was magnificent.

Because it was a scar born from the precious courage of his youth, a dignity that did not falter even in the face of death.

At first, that was all I felt, but seeing it again today, the scar seemed like a symbol that spoke of the man called Gwonma. *I am one who does not fear death.*

I, too, removed my shirt and stepped forward.

“Ah! When will I ever have a body like Master’s?”

“Would you trade your body for mine if you could?”

“…….”

*Rumble.*

The Gwonma’s tightly clenched fist rumbled low.

“I will confess in advance. Lately, I have neglected fist training to focus on my Guhwamagong.”

Having been pushed down the priority list every time, I felt sorry toward the Gwonma. But he understood my situation well.

“There is no need to rush. Master the Guhwamagong first; the fist technique can come later.”

I had expected him to suggest a spar, but he gave the same instruction as always.

We stood shoulder-to-shoulder and began performing the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. Setting aside all debate of war, we immersed ourselves in training.

*Boom!*

The Gwonma’s fists began tearing through the air. I began executing the forms with movements identical to his.

When I performed the First Stance, Black Cloud Asura, the sounds of thunder erupted simultaneously from the Gwonma and from me.

The Second Stance, Thunderbolt Asura, sent fists so fast they were invisible tearing through the air; the Heavenly Thunder Asura descended with crushing weight. The Iron Horn Asura was magnificent, and the Adamantine Asura was unyielding.

Truly, we moved like a body and its shadow.

But the martial intent contained within the movements was entirely different. If the Gwonma was a deep ocean, I was still but a stream.

We finished the final round with the sixth stance, Flame Thunder Asura—the lightning strike of the Flame King.

Having concentrated so fiercely, sweat cascaded from my entire body like rain. Training bare-chested with the Gwonma was more exhilarating than any other training.

“The power in your fists has changed.”

Even though I used the same force as before, he had noticed that my internal energy had increased.

It was no longer easy to perceive the change in my realm now that I could manifest the perfect Banbakgwijin, but the power contained in these fists was something I could not hide from him no matter how I tried.

“As a reward for this matter, the Binggungju bestowed the Mangeuk Bingjeong.”

The Gwonma looked at me with a surprised face. He, too, knew what had to precede taking the Mangeuk Bingjeong.

“I was fortunate enough to also consume the Mannyeon Seolgwa.”

The Gwonma’s expression seemed to ask how one could consume the Mannyeon Seolgwa and obtain the Mangeuk Bingjeong by mere luck.

“Can you generate cold energy from your internal energy?”

The energy of the Mannyeon Seolgwa had melted into my blood vessels and organs.

“Yes, I can.”

“Then perform the Thunderbolt Asura Fist with the coldest energy you can.”

I performed the Thunderbolt Asura Fist alone again. As the Gwonma instructed, I executed it while filling my internal energy with cold.

The sensation was completely different from before. Even with the same internal energy, the power was far greater.

“The Thunderbolt Asura Fist was originally a fist art created to shine brightest when internal energy is pushed to the extreme. I believe the fact that you, my disciple, obtained the Mannyeon Seolgwa and the Mangeuk Bingjeong means you share a deep fate with the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. Therefore, do not neglect it, and make certain you attain great mastery.”

“I shall engrave it in my heart, Master.”

The Gwonma once again imparted his realizations of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. It was a different lesson from the one taught on the snowy mountain in the past. The details omitted back then because they were unnecessary, and the new insights gained since then.

The Gwonma taught without the slightest reservation. A man who becomes a true teacher when he is a teacher, and a true father when he is a father—that is the kind of man the Gwonma is. This Gwonma is Father’s Majon.

When the Gwonma’s instruction ended, I prostrated myself before him.

“Master bestows such great teachings upon me, yet your disciple has been showing you disrespect.”

The Gwonma raised me up. In those blunt eyes of his, that day from long ago flashed past.

“You know how I met the Cult Leader, don’t you?”

When he had nearly died at the hands of the underworld thugs in the underground arena, Father saved him. The Gwonma followed Father to the main cult and entered the Donggwonmun. He said Father came to him again when he became a Heukgwon there. On that day, Father had said this:

*‘I will become the Cheonma, so you become my Gwonma.’*

That day, the Gwonma became Father’s man.

“I am a man who already died long ago. The life I live now is but an extra granted by the Cult Leader.”

The Gwonma’s gaze upon me deepened.

“I respect your demonic path.”

Those fierce eyes held sincerity.

But that respect could not change his heart toward Father.

“But I will follow the Cult Leader’s demonic path.”

Chapter 438: The Cult Leader's War Has Begun.
---------------------------------------------
“So even if this murim becomes a sea of blood, I will follow the Cult Leader’s Demonic Path.”

I could feel that the Fist Demon was speaking from the heart.

Yes, this was the kind of man the Fist Demon was.

His loyalty could not be swayed by persuasion or conciliation. No threat worked on him, and no human relationship could worm its way in.

Once he gave his loyalty, he was a man who went with it to the end.

Of course, that did not mean I believed those words were the Fist Demon’s true feelings.

How could he possibly desire to turn the murim into a sea of blood and kill people?

If anything, such a longing might be hidden in the blood of the Extreme Evil Young Demon, but I knew well that the Fist Demon was not that sort of man.

Suddenly, an image rose in my mind of the Extreme Evil Young Demon standing side by side with the Fist Demon. The two of them faced each other.

Both of them were showing a will that ran contrary to their own desires.

Out of loyalty to me.

Out of loyalty to my father.

If their ties had been reversed, they would have been saying the opposite things. Was that because they had no principles? Of course not! It was because they were passionate men who could bend their own desires for the one they were loyal to.

That was why I felt newly grateful to the Extreme Evil Young Demon. Through the Fist Demon, I realized anew just how great a source of strength he had been to me.

And I was grateful to the Fist Demon as well.

Because he was protecting my father with loyalty to this extent.

“I understand, Master.”

I answered respectfully. There was no need to speak of what was right or wrong.

My father, the Fist Demon, and I.

All of us held wishes and values forged over long years.

And the words he had spoken.

I respect your Demonic Path.

Those words were enough.

“Thank you, Master.”

Was it because I withdrew cleanly, and because I was sincerely grateful?

After silently gazing at me, he suddenly spoke.

“Do you think the Cult Leader is preparing for war?”

It was a question that made me flinch for an instant. A question that seemed as though something immense would follow.

“Is he not?”

Then, from the Fist Demon’s mouth came the most shocking words he had spoken since we met today.

“The Cult Leader has already begun the war.”

“!”

I looked at the Fist Demon with a startled expression, but he left only those words behind and strode away.

As I watched his broad back, I felt my heart begin to beat faster.

My father’s war had already begun?

* * *

After leaving the Eastern Fist Gate, Geom Mugeuk headed for Maga Village.

He felt like drinking, and he also wanted to see Jo Chunbae.

“Young Cult Leader!”

Truly, when it came to the joy with which Jo Chunbae welcomed Geom Mugeuk, he was second to no Demon Supreme.

“Have you been well, innkeeper?”

“Thanks to you, I have been doing very well.”

The customers drinking on the first floor of the tavern all rose at once and greeted Geom Mugeuk.

The people of Maga Village liked Geom Mugeuk. Because Geom Mugeuk had established a branch of the Yellow Springs Pavilion in this marketplace and frequently came and went from the Pungryu Tavern, this place had become safer than any village in the murim.

No one would believe it. That the tyranny and crimes committed by martial artists in front of the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were fewer than those committed in front of the main headquarters of the Murim Alliance.

Geom Mugeuk sat at his usual seat on the second floor. Jo Chunbae first followed him up with only wine and simple snacks.

“How is business these days? Haven’t customers been swarming in?”

After the three-party talks ended, the Pungryu Tavern became the most famous tavern in the murim.

It had become famous just from the fact that the talks had been held there, but it became even more famous because of the rumor that the Heavenly Demon, the Murim Alliance Leader, and the Unorthodox Alliance Leader had left writings there.

So I had expected that crowds of customers might be packed in front of the Pungryu Tavern.

“Customers do come from time to time to look at the wall, but not to the point that it interferes with business.”

It seemed that the irrepressible curiosity of martial artists had not managed to overcome the high wall of being right before the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

There were no martial artists from the righteous or unorthodox factions at all, and even the occasional visitors were mostly demonic practitioners or their families.

Then again, perhaps because the people who left the writings were too grand, it might actually make them uneasy.

If they came to look for no reason, what would stop them from worrying that a demonic practitioner who had been sitting there watching might leap up and say, “How dare you look at it with such irreverent eyes? I’ll gouge them out!”?

“Rather, people may line up for it later.”

Once all the people who left the writings were dead, only then would this place become a famous attraction. Just like how a painting becomes a masterpiece after the painter dies.

“By then, I won’t be here either.”

Jo Chunbae let out a deliberate sigh, but his expression was bright. He was a man who lived joyfully, making sure to boast at least once a day to drunkards about the writings left on the wall and the talks that had taken place there. Because he was happy, he felt younger. Jo Chunbae was, quite literally, being rejuvenated by a wall.

Jo Chunbae went downstairs to prepare food, and Geom Mugeuk drank alone.

His gaze naturally turned toward the writings left on the wall.

Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon saw a new Demonic Path here.

He recalled the Alliance Leader’s appearance when he left those words. Then, the words written beneath them.

Unorthodox Alliance Leader Baek Jagang saw his disciple’s dance at the Pungryu Tavern.

He also recalled Baek Jagang.

He remembered the moment he had danced with him that day.

But if war broke out, what happened that day would become a cunning scheme of the Demonic Cult. They would think the three-party talks had been held to put them at ease.

At that moment, the words the Fist Demon had spoken rose in his mind.

—The Cult Leader has already begun the war.

What did he mean, that he had begun a war? Against the righteous or unorthodox factions? Or did he mean the force behind this recent affair?

Both were possible. One was Father’s dream, and the other had touched Father’s pride.

But if such movements had been taking place, Gowol should have noticed.

Did it mean they were moving so secretly that even Gowol’s eyes were being deceived? If not? Then who on earth was the target?

This was not something he could ask Father about either. Because the Fist Demon had told him specially for his sake.

As he drank several cups of wine, lost in such thoughts, Jo Chunbae brought up the dishes.

“Innkeeper, it has been a while. Receive a cup from me.”

“Oh my, I receive a precious cup.”

Jo Chunbae accepted Geom Mugeuk’s wine with an awed expression and gesture.

One of Jo Chunbae’s virtues was that he did not act carelessly just because they had grown close. He knew. Mistakes were always made when one let one’s guard down because they had become close. Even slips of the tongue caused by clumsy jokes ultimately came from the thought that one had become close.

Because he knew that, he always showed a consistent demeanor. Even if he received a cup of wine at the moment of death, he was the sort of person who would receive it apologetically. That was why he was someone one wanted to treat even better.

“Drinking wine given by the Young Cult Leader after so long makes it taste especially good.”

“Pour me a cup as well.”

As Jo Chunbae filled Geom Mugeuk’s cup, he asked cautiously.

“Do you perhaps have something troubling you?”

“Do I look troubled?”

Jo Chunbae gave a slight nod. Then again, how many years had he been in the wine trade? How could he not know? Just as we could tell a person’s skill by the way they held a weapon, he could probably discern a person’s emotional state just by the way they held a cup.

“I have many worries because I am opposing my father.”

At that, a low exclamation of “Good heavens” burst from Jo Chunbae’s mouth.

“I should not have asked, should I?”

“I heard nothing at all.”

As he shook his head and waved his hands, Geom Mugeuk poured him another cup.

“Still, I trust that our innkeeper is on my side.”

Jo Chunbae again let out a pained groan as he accepted the wine. Having grown close to Geom Mugeuk, he was now even experiencing what it was like to stand on the side opposing the Heavenly Demon.

Normally, he would drink exactly one cup and rise, but today he accepted one more.

“Do you perhaps remember what happened back then?”

“What happened?”

“When those esteemed people left their writings on that wall, I had many worries.”

What if the wall was damaged? What if someone stole it? It was truly a worry that kept him from sleeping even when he lay down.

“At that time, thanks to the advice the Young Cult Leader gave me, I have been able to live at ease.”

To the anxious Jo Chunbae, Geom Mugeuk had given this advice.

—If, at some particular time and day, someone damages the wall for one reason or another and disappears, that too will become a moment in history. Perhaps it may even become more interesting history.

That had been a great help to Jo Chunbae. If he had not said those words, he might have collapsed from nervous exhaustion.

What makes people anxious, and what puts them at ease, does not happen because something grand is at work.

A single casually thrown word can keep someone up all night. That one word can remain as a wound in the heart and torment a person for a long time.

But this time, he learned that the opposite was also true.

If the wall disappears, that would be even more interesting!

Amazingly, those words fought his anxiety and won. Because humans are hurt by a single word, they can also be healed by a single word. He had experienced that this time.

“Those words erased my worries as if by magic. Sometimes I even deliberately think extreme thoughts, like, why doesn’t someone steal that wall already?”

What Jo Chunbae wanted to say was this.

“The things I worried about did not happen easily. To the point that I wondered why I had worried so much. So please do not worry too much. Everything will go well, Young Cult Leader.”

And to that, he added his own method.

“Please wait a moment. I will make a special dish for the Young Cult Leader!”

Watching him go down to the first floor, Geom Mugeuk felt his heart grow at ease.

There was a sense of reassurance given by the Pungryu Tavern, by that Jo Chunbae. That was why this place was also a world within the real-life art of shifting time and space.

As he was drinking, someone entered the Pungryu Tavern.

The person who climbed to the second floor with a great saber on his back was none other than Seo Daeryong.

“For some reason, I strangely felt you would be here.”

“What brings you here at this hour? What about evening training?”

“We decided to rest for today.”

“Because of me?”

“Pardon? What do you mean?”

He wondered if Seo Daeryong had heard about this matter from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, but that was not the case.

“Master said he was going to Daecheon Mountain and told me to rest today.”

“Why Daecheon Mountain?”

“He did not tell me the reason.”

Seo Daeryong added playfully.

“I am sure the Young Cult Leader, who knows all things in the universe, already knows the reason.”

“Of course I know.”

He had said it as a joke, but when Geom Mugeuk said he knew, Seo Daeryong’s eyes widened.

“You know? Why did he go to Daecheon Mountain?”

“To settle his troubled mind.”

If one looked at the night sky from the summit of Daecheon Mountain, the showering stars were truly a magnificent sight. He was probably standing there, sorting out his thoughts about Father and me.

“Why is his mind troubled?”

“Because I made it troubled.”

Since this involved the Young Cult Leader and his master, the two people closest to him, Seo Daeryong naturally became cautious.

“What on earth happened?”

“Yes, you are my right arm, so you should know the situation too.”

Geom Mugeuk told Seo Daeryong about the current situation. After hearing the whole story, Seo Daeryong put strength into his eyes and said,

“I wish I had the right to choose as well.”

“Then it would have been one against three instead of one against two, wouldn’t it?”

“How terribly quick-witted you are.”

He said it that way, but Seo Daeryong knew for certain.

If such a situation truly came, he would support the Young Cult Leader. Even knowing that one had to stake one’s life to defy the Cult Leader, he felt that he would support Geom Mugeuk.

Rather than being surprised at himself, he felt relieved.

The relationship between Geom Mugeuk and the Demon Supremes was one of equals, but he was different.

Geom Mugeuk had made a mere investigator into the Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion, and had made him the disciple of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. No, leaving all that aside, he had led his gloomy, discontented self into a world of light.

And this was Geom Mugeuk.

“No matter what, we cannot defy the Cult Leader, can we?”

He felt relieved that he did not seem like someone who would say such cowardly words.

“With a matter this important, it should be done by secret ballot!”

He was grateful for himself, and proud of himself, for being able to make such a joke without hesitation.

“So who will you visit now?”

The Demon Supremes remaining now were the One-Flame Sword Supreme, the Demon Buddha, the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme, and the Poison King. Seo Daeryong could not tell who would stand on the Young Cult Leader’s side.

“I tried to bring down a cliff, but it did not budge.”

Geom Mugeuk said as he drank.

“So while climbing the cliff, I suppose I should at least gather some poisonous herbs.”

* * *

There are many ways to make someone take your side. You can persuade them with sound reasoning, move their heart, or simply subdue them by force. Or you can draw them in through negotiation, giving what must be given and receiving what must be received.

And now, I had come to meet someone on whom none of those methods would easily work.

When I arrived at the Thousand Poisons Forest, it was not Sangseon, who always greeted me, but another poison person who received me.

“Young Cult Leader, Sangseon has entrusted me with guiding you. This way, please.”
