I followed him through the Thousand Poisons Forest. Venomous snakes flicked their tongues atop the branches, and beneath them, poisonous insects rustled through the heaps of fallen leaves, making bizarre sounds.

“It has been a long time since I came here too.”

I asked the poison person guiding me.

“Does the Demon Buddha still come often these days?”

“Yes, I understand he comes often to gather medicinal herbs.”

The Poison King and the Demon Buddha. Whether in appearance or personality, they were truly two people who did not suit each other. And yet, the connection formed when the three of us gathered medicinal herbs together had led to a friendship between those two.

Between the Poison King and the Demon Buddha, the easier one to predict was the Demon Buddha.

He was my elder brother’s man from beginning to end. Above all, my brother’s opinion would influence him, and unfortunately, my brother would never defy Father’s will. I could not know for certain, but there was a high chance the Demon Buddha would also follow Father.

Even if not for my brother, the Demon Buddha was someone with great ambition for power. If one side called for war, he would be on the side that called for war; he was not someone who would raise his hand for peace.

Then I had to make at least the Poison King my ally no matter what.

After walking for quite some time, I arrived at the Poison King’s residence. Sangseon was standing in front of it.

“If he was here doing nothing, why did he send a subordinate?”

That question was soon answered.

“It seems you will have to wait a little.”

“Is the Poison King perhaps making an important poison?”

“No.”

An answer I had not expected at all flowed from Sangseon.

“The Cult Leader is here right now.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 439

Chapter 439: Could Father Be a Gatherer Too?
--------------------------------------------
Father is here?

The Poison King, the one who played the most important role in Father’s unification of the murim.

Father had come one step ahead of me to meet him.

Even so, what I felt was not worry, but a pleasant tension.

It felt different from before. If until now Father had been testing me, this time I could sense that he meant to have a contest with me. It must mean he acknowledged me to that extent.

Then I should enjoy this match against Father.

All of this process was a memory with Father, and it was my life. As I always resolved, I would not live a life meant only for revenge. One such life was enough. In this life, I would enjoy myself. Even a contest like this with Father.

I spoke loudly so my voice would carry inside the building.

“Father, your son is here! Please let me in as well.”

The Chief Steward looked flustered. After all, I was probably the only person in the world who would shout like this at the Heavenly Demon. I was looking at his expression—truly, the Young Cult Leader is impossible—when Father’s voice came from within.

“Come in.”

It was a strange situation.

Originally, the Poison King should have spoken. This was a situation where he should have conveyed Father’s will and said, “Escort the Young Cult Leader inside.” Yet Father himself was telling me to come in?

When I opened the door and entered, an unexpected sight unfolded before me. Seeing it, I understood why Father had answered in the Poison King’s place.

The Poison King was manufacturing poison, and Father was helping him. It was such an unimaginable scene that I stood there blankly, watching.

Before the Poison King was the vat he used when making poison. Around it floated a dozen or so bottles.

The one keeping those bottles suspended in midair was none other than Father. From a spot far beyond the poison’s range, Father was assisting the poison-making with object manipulation through empty air. He was as far away as the entrance where I stood.

“Black, a little more, please!”

Drip, drip, drip.

“Green, stop!”

At his command, liquid poured from the bottle marked with black paper, floating in the air, while the bottle marked with green paper stopped pouring its contents.

“This time, pour Blue, Red, and Purple at the same time.”

Then the blue, red, and purple bottles floating in the air tilted at once and poured out the liquid inside.

Hissss.

Smoke rose from the mixed poison liquid.

Even as he spoke, the Poison King’s hands moved busily. In the rising poisonous fumes, he was stirring the poison liquid with a wooden rod while adding other poisonous substances.

It was truly a magnificent sight. At a glance, it might not have seemed like much, but both Father and the Poison King were displaying the highest level of skill and concentration.

“Blue, stop for a moment! Brown instead.”

Once again, at the Poison King’s command, the blue bottle stopped and the brown bottle moved.

The level at which Father handled objects through empty air was exquisitely precise and accurate. It felt as though he had granted the bottles life and will.

The Poison King watched the falling liquid intently and mixed in powder to match it.

The only person who could withstand the poison generated during this process was the Poison King. That was why he had originally done this work alone.

Pouring this in, mixing that in. A truly dangerous and complicated process was underway when—

“Stop everything!”

At the Poison King’s shout, the bottles floating in the air all stopped at once.

A single drop that should not have entered splashed and fell inward.

The next moment.

That single drop stopped in midair.

Father had blocked that one drop with object manipulation through empty air.

Silence descended.

With that one drop of liquid hanging in the air, it felt as though time itself had stopped.

Then it flew back through the air and returned to the bottle it had come from.

Only then did the Poison King’s poison-making finally come to an end.

The Poison King’s face was flushed. He had not even realized I had entered. He had been focused solely on the poison.

Rather than the fact that he was making poison together with Father, what came first for him was his desire to complete the poison. I knew very well that he had never once had any thought like, “Out of courtesy, because I am with the Cult Leader.”

The Poison King took out the compounded poison, smelled it, and performed several tests.

Was the Poison King in his own world right now, or in reality? I could always roughly guess where the Poison King was standing, but right now, I felt I could not tell.

After completing several checks, the Poison King’s expression brightened radiantly, and he spoke with a face full of joy.

“It is a success.”

The Poison King was genuinely delighted.

“This time, it was compounded properly. I kept failing when I did it alone, but thanks to the Cult Leader’s assistance, I succeeded.”

This, I thought, was one of the Poison King’s greatest strengths. For the sake of poison, he readily accepted help from others. Whether it was asking the Demon Buddha to gather poisonous herbs, or receiving Father’s help today.

If someone was prone to falling into a world of his own, one might expect him to put forward needless pride and stubbornness. Yet unexpectedly, he had no such obstinacy.

That was why I thought the Poison King was frightening. Because he was a man who could not be defined.

“Huh? When did you get here?”

Only belatedly did the Poison King notice me. He was not pretending not to have known. He truly had been focused on manufacturing poison to a frightening degree.

“I came a little while ago.”

“I succeeded in making Mansajeoldok (萬死絶毒).”

“Congratulations.”

Mansajeoldok was a new poison the Poison King had created, one he had failed to manufacture dozens of times until now. The Chief Steward had helped as well, but as I had just seen, the process of compounding the poison was extremely delicate and difficult, so it had not been easy to make. And now he had succeeded together with Father.

“What a shame! It should have succeeded because I helped you.”

“Then it would have failed again today.”

This was the Poison King’s way of expressing his thanks to Father.

I belatedly greeted Father.

“It seemed you were doing something important, so my greeting was late.”

I must not forget. The one who had allowed me to enter this place was Father. Father had meant to show me himself and the Poison King compounding poison together.

“I did not know you were here.”

“I stopped by while passing.”

Father, do you know that every time you “stop by” like that, everyone’s fate changes dramatically? Whatever you do, please do not say you should stop by the Martial Alliance!

I imagined myself standing with Father atop a huge go board.

In the situation with the Poison King, the move Father had played this time was the move of poison-making.

Just what conversation had Father and the Poison King had before I arrived? Had he asked him to take care of me again? Or had he tried to firmly nail down at least the Poison King as one of his people?

“I returned safely from the North Sea.”

“Is that so?”

Even to my greeting, the Poison King answered perfunctorily. He was gazing in wonder at the poison he had completed. He was intoxicated by the poison. It was only because Father was present that he answered and even asked a question.

“Did you obtain the poisonous herbs that grow only in the North Sea?”

“I did try, but…”

In truth, on the way back, I had looked for poisonous herbs whenever I had time. But I had not found any poisonous herb worth bringing as a gift.

“Whenever they see me, the poisonous herbs must all get shy and hide. This is all Father’s fault. I take after Father, so I’m far too handsome.”

A sneer formed at the corners of Father’s mouth. Seeing that sneer for the first time in a while made me happy. Father, please laugh like that more often. For now, please give me tests rather than contests.

“Father, next time, go have an outing with the Poison King and gather poisonous herbs together. Surely he would not dare use even you as a gatherer.”

I had said it intending to continue with a joke about how he had turned the Demon Buddha into a gatherer through a bet, but Father asked the Poison King an unexpected question.

“When was it?”

At the sudden question, the Poison King thought back for a moment.

“Four years and seven months ago.”

“Has it already been that long?”

With a no-way feeling, I looked back and forth between the two of them and asked,

“Surely that ‘four years and seven months ago’ is not the day the two of you gathered poisonous herbs together?”

From the expressions of the two as they faced each other, I could tell. That “surely not” was the truth.

Father often surprised me, but this time, I was truly astonished. No matter how much I tried to picture it, I could not easily imagine Father and the Poison King gathering poisonous herbs together.

Moreover, the Poison King remembered that last day with exact precision.

It meant it had been that precious a moment for him as well. It was also the moment I realized that making the Poison King my ally would not be easy at all.

Suddenly, I remembered what the Extreme Evil Young Demon had said.

—The Cult Leader will be just as difficult to persuade.

For some reason, I think it will be harder for me.

At that moment, Father took his leave.

“I shall be going now.”

Then the Poison King bowed with proper courtesy. He did not again say thank you for helping him compound the poison.

But I had the thought that someday, if Father asked, “When was that again?” the Poison King might remember this very moment with exact precision and answer.

Before leaving, Father looked at me. His eyes were saying this.

My move is over, so now it is your turn.

I could feel Father’s confidence.

From Father’s own method, which involved neither restraint nor coercion, I realized once again that composure came from strength.

As soon as Father left the residence, I asked the Poison King,

“Did you ask him first to help you manufacture poison? Is that why Father came?”

“How could I dare do that? I was just about to make poison when the Cult Leader came without notice. I was going to postpone the process, but he said he would help.”

He surely had not displayed the kind of concentration from earlier right from the start. During the preparation process, they must have exchanged this and that.

“May I ask what you spoke about with Father today?”

Then an astonishing answer flowed out.

“He said nothing.”

“Surely you do not mean he did not say a single word?”

The Poison King nodded. Father had come, helped with the poison-making, and then left just like that.

I could feel that it was a blow far stronger than persuading the Poison King with every possible word.

You do all that and then just leave? How am I supposed to win?

If I tried to say anything to the Poison King now that went against Father’s will, it would only backfire.

After all, my hundred words of flattery could not defeat this heavy blow from Father, who had helped make poison without saying a single word and left.

“I will take my leave today as well. I am in the middle of a tactical retreat.”

Still, I could not simply leave, so I let slip that the reason I had come today had not been merely to greet him.

It must have been a somewhat sudden remark, but the Poison King did not ask what I meant. Father had come, and I had come. He must have sensed that something was definitely happening. For some reason, the Poison King stopped me.

“Since you came all this way, help me with my work before you go.”

“Just tell me what to do.”

“Let us bottle this poison first.”

The Poison King brought over a box. Inside were a dozen or so bottles, and the box was structured to store the bottles without them breaking.

These specially made bottles had been crafted so they could be perfectly sealed, preventing the poison from leaking out.

“I will bottle it. Please rest.”

The Poison King sat perched on a desk a little ways away and silently watched me.

I divided the Mansajeoldok into the bottles. The poison filled exactly ten bottles. I could tell that from the start, he had manufactured precisely one box’s worth of poison. If the poison-making had not succeeded, it would have come up short or overflowed.

How many people could be killed with this poison? Hundreds? Thousands?

“Take that and follow me.”

I put the poison-filled bottles back into the box. Carrying it, I followed the Poison King out.

The Poison King headed deep into the Thousand Poison Forest.

There had been a time when I searched for poisonous herbs with him here. But the place he brought me today was somewhere I had never been before.

The Poison King pushed through the forest, creating a path where there was none, and went deep inside.

Was there such a place in the Thousand Poison Forest?

As if we had entered a new district within the Thousand Poison Forest, all kinds of poisonous creatures and substances were there, each possessing poison more vicious and dangerous than what lay outside.

Moreover, the area we were walking through now was a swamp. It was not an ordinary swamp. It was a swamp of death from which a person who fell in could never escape.

Even the mist spread over the swamp like clouds had poison mixed into it.

Truly, this was a place that needed no guards and no formations.

A place no one could enter unless they possessed invulnerability to all poisons or were the Poison King.

I stepped on the stones and branches the Poison King stepped on as we crossed the swamp.

Tsssss!

Strange sounds came from all directions, and as we continued, the Poison King would sometimes stop to examine the condition of the poisonous creatures. With a boyish face, he explained to me what each poisonous creature was and what state it was in. That one was angry because it had failed to mate, or that one was sick somewhere. He could see things an ordinary person could never recognize.

Past the swamp, there was a cave.

The cave, too, was filled with poisonous creatures. If not for the two of us, even an ordinary master would not have been able to take a single step inside this cave.

At the end of the cave stood a heavy iron door. The Poison King unlocked the door and entered, and beyond it was a huge storehouse.

The moment I stepped inside, I was overwhelmed by the sight spread out before me.

That vast space was filled with bottles. There were hundreds of display shelves, and bottles were placed upon them. I could tell. Everything contained in these bottles was poison. An enormous quantity, numbering in the thousands.

“All of our cult’s poisons are stored here.”

This was the secret storehouse where the extreme poisons of the Thousand Poison Forest were kept. If war broke out, these were the poisons that would be released into the world.

Why had the Poison King brought me here?

“Put the poison you brought over there at the end.”

I lined up the Mansajeoldok in the place the Poison King indicated and stored it there. The Poison King wrote the name of the poison on the bottles. The bottles standing beside them each had names written on them as well.

Snake Poison (蛇毒), Madness Poison (狂毒), Blood Poison (血毒), Gut-Severing Poison (斷腸毒), Severing Poison (斬毒), Soul-Severing Poison (斷魂毒), Annihilation Poison (滅毒), Yin-Yang Poison (陰陽毒), Shadowless Poison (無影毒), Corpse Poison (屍毒), Gu Poison (蠱毒), Lost-Soul Poison (失魂毒)…

There were truly all kinds of poisons, too many to count. Every terrifying poison that existed in the world was here.

Strangely, the arrangement of the poisons was completely different from what I had imagined. I had thought the more powerful poisons would be placed farther inside, but the bottles containing formless poison were displayed right by the entrance. It was an arrangement that gave the impression that the stronger something was, the sooner it should be taken out and used.

“What happens if all the poisons here are released into the world?”

The Poison King answered from behind me.

“Everyone dies.”

It was not merely an exaggeration.

I was curious about what the Poison King thought. So before questioning whether he was on Father’s side or mine, I asked this first. It was a question I had never asked since meeting him.

“Poison King, why do you make poison?”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 440

Chapter 440: The Poison King’s Seat Father Chose.
-------------------------------------------------
Could there be any question more provocative and rude than asking the Poison King why he made poison?

If our relationship had not been as good as it was now, those words might have come out at once.

What kind of damned question is that to ask the Poison King?

Or he might have asked in return:

Then why do you learn swordsmanship?

Fortunately, the Poison King knew my question had not been tossed out thoughtlessly.

Instead of answering, the Poison King quietly stared at me.

I slowly moved my steps and walked between the display cases.

“There is already so much poison.”

Even just the poisons in this passage I was looking down were staggering in number.

They were probably poisons passed down through the generations in the Thousand Poisons Forest, but there would also be many poisons the Poison King had collected himself. There would be many newly created poisons too, like the one from earlier.

“Why, then, do you make poison with such passion, as you did earlier?”

This question was both fundamental and deeply personal. He could answer as a Demon Venerable, or he could answer with a personal reason. It was a question whose answer would change depending on how he took it.

I looked back at the Poison King and added,

“You have devoted your entire life to poison, haven’t you? What does poison mean to you, Poison King?”

At that moment, I saw a small flash of emotion in the Poison King’s eyes, something I had never seen before.

He must have been told countless times that he was strong, and countless times that he was frightening.

But had he ever been told that he had devoted himself to poison?

Was that statement burdensome to him? The Poison King’s gaze turned toward the poisons on the opposite side from where I was looking.

A brief silence flowed between us.

Still, the Poison King did not answer the question of why he made poison.

I threw out a new question. This, too, was something I was asking for the first time since meeting him.

“Or is it that someday you wish to dye the world in blood with poison?”

That had been his life before my regression.

This answer came quickly. Without turning back, the Poison King replied,

“If I must.”

Rather, I felt relieved. Compared to an answer like, “That will never happen,” this answer felt farther from a sea of blood. After all, the person who takes his own life is not the one who was crying yesterday, but the one who was smiling.

Was I not worried that he might commit such an act again? Not at all. I knew better than anyone how even the same person could change depending on their circumstances, environment, and the people around them.

The Poison King before my regression and the Poison King now were completely different people. No, they were the same person, but living different lives.

Just like me now.

“It seems I asked an unnecessary question. Please forgive my rudeness.”

At that, the Poison King, who had been staring blankly at the poison bottles, slowly shook his head.

“No one has ever asked me such a question before.”

Then again, who would dare ask the Poison King why he made poison?

And the reason he could not answer was not because of my question.

“I have never thought seriously about it, either.”

If I had been my younger self, I would not have believed him. I would have thought he was merely trying to sound impressive.

But now I knew. The higher one’s realm became, the less one ended up thinking about such things.

“You must have. You simply can’t remember now.”

“Yes, I suppose I did at some point. But I cannot remember when that ‘some point’ was.”

“It seems even you, Poison King, cannot avoid being swept along by life.”

I said it playfully, but the Poison King’s expression remained serious.

He moved his steps to the display stand and picked up the bottle containing the Mansa Severing Poison he had made today. The look in his eyes as he stared at it seemed to ask:

—Why did I make this? Why did I not give up, even after repeated failures?

Before his thoughts deepened and he entered a world of his own, I answered in his stead.

“To protect our cult.”

To the Poison King, it was an obvious wrong answer.

“With the Cult Leader here, why would I protect it?”

Now the time had come to properly explain to him why I had sought him out.

“The orthodox and unorthodox factions will fear you, Poison King, more than my father.”

At the root of that fear lay this terror:

The fear of dying without even getting to fight properly.

If one died while fighting, no matter the opponent, it would not feel unjust. But in a fight against the Poison King, it was over if you simply took one wrong breath, drank one wrong mouthful of water.

Perhaps the reason the Poison King was the most powerful deterrent to war was not the fear of mass slaughter, but the fear of dying a dog’s death.

“There is a reason I asked such a question earlier.”

I told the Poison King exactly what had happened after I returned. I even told him that I was worried my father might be dreaming of unifying murim.

Even after hearing the whole story, the Poison King showed no change in expression. From the beginning, he must have known that there had been another reason I had come to the Thousand Poisons Forest today, and that was why he had brought me here.

“Even if everyone opposes the Cult Leader’s dream, shouldn’t you, at least, support it?”

“It seems he didn’t only give me my good looks. He passed down his stubbornness as well.”

“You intend to block the Cult Leader’s path?”

Though I felt terribly sorry toward my father, I quietly nodded and began persuading the Poison King.

“I wish for these poisons to remain here forever.”

“You want to make me into a useless person?”

“It is the opposite.”

“The opposite?”

I slowly touched the poison bottle in front of me. On the paper attached to the bottle were the characters Yeombaek Life-Severing Poison. It was a terrifying poison that, with only a few drops, spread through the surroundings like flames and took every life nearby.

“When these poisons remain here, your fear and dignity, Poison King, will continue to be maintained. The moment you release poison upon murim, fear will become anger. Dignity will be buried beneath resentment.”

The Poison King said nothing.

“The essence of poison arts is not poisoning, but detoxification.”

They were words I had once said to him. He, too, had taught his successor this way, so he must certainly have held such thoughts.

“When you neutralized the Poison Tyrant’s poison last time and saved everyone, Poison King, you were truly magnificent.”

I could not know what he was thinking at that moment, but I conveyed my sincerity as it was.

“Please live not as the Poison King who poisons in my father’s demonic path, but as the Poison King who detoxifies in mine.”

Because I sensed that I had lost to my father, who had come today and silently helped with the making of poison before leaving, I said everything I wanted to say.

“I apologize for such a rude request.”

I bowed my head politely. After all, I was forcing this upon him without truly knowing his heart.

The Poison King, who had remained silent until then, suddenly spoke.

“You are mistaken about one thing.”

That one thing was truly something I had never expected.

“Do you think the Cult Leader intends to put me at the front and unify murim?”

“Does he not?”

“Four years and seven months ago, when I went to gather poisonous herbs with the Cult Leader, he said this to me.”

And then, an astonishing truth was revealed.

The Poison King relayed exactly what my father had said to him that day.

“I will not unify murim with poison. My murim has no need for poison arts.”

I was startled. I had never imagined my father had said such a thing. I knew that my father disliked poison arts, but to think he had said it directly to the Poison King.

“Were you not disappointed?”

“I was. But I did not feel offended. It was far better than him disliking poison arts while pretending outwardly that he did not.”

This was precisely how my father treated the Demon Venerables.

And those two men had made poison together today without exchanging a single word.

Perhaps, though I could not know for certain, there was a kind of understanding that existed only between the two of them.

The Poison King then relayed the last thing my father had said that day.

“To me, you are not someone who attacks murim, but someone who protects our cult. Even if my unification of murim fails, because of you, our cult will not collapse.”

That was the place my father had set for the Poison King: the final bastion.

“I promised that day to follow the Cult Leader’s will.”

Only now could I understand the Poison King’s end in my life before regression.

When the Cult Leader’s Hall was swept away and my father died, the Eight Demon Venerables gave up on revenge.

Until now, I had thought it was because they had been suppressed by Hwa Mugi’s overwhelming martial prowess. I had believed they chose life over death. That was why I considered it betrayal, and I also understood it as a fight they were destined to lose anyway.

But today, for the first time, this thought occurred to me. Perhaps that day, another situation I did not know about had unfolded. A situation in which everyone had no choice but to give up on revenge.

Because it did not seem like the Poison King, at least, would have simply stayed still. Yet he did not take revenge, and afterward, he caused a tragedy and met a wretched end.

Perhaps we were both thinking of the same person, but the Poison King broke that reverie.

“Because I like poison.”

It was the answer to the question I had asked earlier. He had been unable to give this simple answer for so long. It was likely not because the answer had not occurred to him.

Do I still truly like poison? Have I perhaps forgotten that feeling and simply been making poison out of habit?

He must have been unable to answer because he had been searching for the answers to the many questions that followed.

“Of course it is because you like poison.”

When I spoke as if I had already known the answer, the Poison King looked at me.

“A person who does not like poison could not crouch for hours watching poisonous insects crawl, could they? Such a person would not give names to venomous snakes. You truly like poison, Poison King. Please be sure to show me what lies at the end of that love and devotion to poison. Please be sure to let me know.”

Because he was the Poison King, these were likely words he had never heard from anyone. The Poison King, my father, and I, who had regressed. No, even the old man who had sent me back in time—surely there were words he wanted to hear, words he needed to hear. Words that anyone, in any life, must hear at least once. I now said those words to the Poison King.

The Poison King looked at me and smiled. Joy and gratitude dwelled in his eyes as he looked at me.

“We can’t let such a good atmosphere go to waste. Now then, Poison King, are you on my father’s side? Or mine?”

The answer to this question came quickly. As the Poison King walked out of the storage room, he said calmly,

“I am on no one’s side.”

* * *

The one who gave me a clear answer was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“I will follow the Cult Leader’s will.”

She made her decision without even pretending to deliberate.

She had listened to my explanation of the situation while practicing swordsmanship in the training ground, then said those words as she unleashed her final technique.

After finishing a round of training, she gulped down the water that had been set at the edge of the training ground.

There had been a change in her since I had last seen her. She had stopped wearing makeup at some point, and now her hair was white.

“This is my hair as it is, without adornment.”

The red camellia engraved on the scabbard of the Ilhwa Sword contrasted with her hair, creating an elegant impression.

“You grow more impressive by the day, Sword Venerable!”

“As expected! I knew you would say that.”

But how could appearance be the only thing that had changed? I could feel that her martial realm, which had already risen by one stage, was now advancing toward yet another realm.

“I am grateful for your praise, but still, I will follow the Cult Leader’s will.”

I had expected as much. When it came to reverence for my father, there was no one who could claim to surpass her without making her resent it.

What if I could not persuade her?

The remaining Demon Venerables were the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable and the Demon Buddha. Even if Seophon's, the young Demon Venerable, took my side, if the Demon Buddha followed my father, then half would already be following him. Since the Poison King had said he was on neither side, even if the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon sided with me, it would be four to three.

“This is too much! Have you forgotten? I am your sparring friend, Sword Venerable.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled apologetically.

“You and I have become quite close, haven’t we?”

“Yes.”

“And you know how grateful I am to you, don’t you?”

“I do.”

“No, you likely do not. How grateful I truly am. Even so, I must say this.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable revealed her honest feelings about this matter. Truly unexpected words came from her.

“I am worried that the Cult Leader may not start a war.”

Becoming close to me was one thing; her convictions were another.

“I believe that if it is the Cult Leader, he can accomplish the great undertaking of unifying murim. As you know, the Cult Leader is truly an exceptional man. The chance has come to fulfill our cult’s long-cherished desire. If this chance is missed, the descendants of our cult will regret this moment for generations.”

Perhaps the greatest reason she trained so desperately was so that, someday, she could realize the dream of unifying murim together with my father.

“In a way, the birth of a son like you could be considered a trial for the Cult Leader. Great endeavors are always accompanied by trials. That destiny has applied itself here.”

The words could have wounded me, but she looked at me and continued calmly. By not deceiving me and speaking honestly, she was making up for her apology.

“I hope the Cult Leader will overcome the trial that is you. And I sincerely hope he accomplishes the great undertaking.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable asked with gentle eyes,

“I am sorry if I have offended you.”

I answered with a smile.

“Not at all. Rather, I am grateful that you spoke honestly.”

“Thank you for thinking so.”

“The next time I come to see you, I will come as your sparring friend.”

I bowed to her politely and turned around.

At that moment, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable said something unexpected from behind me.

“Do not be too disappointed. From what I can see, you are winning this contest right now.”

At her incomprehensible words, I turned back to her.

But she drew her sword and resumed her training.

“That is why I am unconditionally on the Cult Leader’s side.”

Absolute Regression Chapter 441

Chapter 441: All with a Single Drawn Sword
------------------------------------------
I visited Seohwanjin for the first time in a long while.

Not only the towers and grotesque ornaments placed on every side, but even the flowers in the garden swaying in the wind exuded a ghostly aura that unsettled the heart.

The ghostly aura was unchanged, but the ghost sorcerers I encountered in passing were no longer hostile as they had been before. They greeted me courteously, with the proper respect due the Young Cult Leader.

Following the ghost sorcerer who guided me, I walked between houses built like snail shells.

They were so bewitching and dazzling that anyone who was not a reasonably accomplished master would have had to close their eyes and walk just to avoid dizziness.

At the end of that path stood a cone-shaped building. This was the residence of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

Walls, ceiling, even the floor. In a room filled with swirling patterns, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable received me.

“Young Cult Leader, you have come?”

The ghostly aura flowing in her two eyes had grown even more intense than when I had seen her before.

“I believe this is the first time I have met you here.”

“Are you all right? If you are uncomfortable, we can go outside and speak.”

Just being in this place, because of the dizzying patterns that covered the room, was enough to disturb one’s state of mind.

She was being considerate in case I found it uncomfortable. Of course, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, having reached great completion, was guarding me, so her concern was needless.

“When the previous Demon Venerable was here, this place was nauseating and dizzying. But now, it is beautiful and mysterious.”

I knew that she was doing well. Though she had ascended to the position of Demon Venerable at a young age, she had already taken complete control of Seohwanjin and was being acknowledged by the ghost sorcerers as their new Demon Venerable. What remained was for her to bring her unique martial art to great completion, and to accumulate experience as a Demon Venerable.

“What brings you here?”

She was different from the other Demon Venerables. She was still so young that she felt less like one of my father’s Demon Venerables and more like one of mine.

Thus, this very meeting was also the moment when the Heavenly Demon and Demon Venerable of the next generation stood face to face.

I told her the current situation exactly as it was. I neither omitted nor embellished anything because she was young. I respected her as one Demon Venerable.

“To state the conclusion first, there is only one Demon Venerable who stands on my side.”

I had not revealed who that was, but to my surprise, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable guessed that person correctly.

“Could it be Lord Extreme Evil Young Demon?”

“How did you know?”

She told me that during the three-party meeting, when she had seen the Extreme Evil Young Demon standing apart from the Demon Venerables, alone and lonely, she had felt a certain kinship.

“During the last three-party meeting, Lord Young Demon said he envied me. He said that because I was young, I could serve you for a long time. Because of the way he thought of you then, I guessed it might be him.”

She still remembered vividly the conversation they had exchanged that day.

“Then do you know what I said to Lord Young Demon?”

“What did you say?”

“Lord Young Demon, you truly do enjoy difficult things.”

I burst out laughing. She, who had always seemed somewhat stiff when dealing with me, was now speaking comfortably. It meant her position had become that much more secure, and she had gained confidence.

Like the other Demon Venerables—no, she still had a long road ahead of her, and for that very reason she was growing faster than anyone.

“If not for you, Young Cult Leader, I would never have ascended to the position of Demon Venerable.”

“You need not make your decision this time for that reason. Decide as you wish, Demon Venerable.”

At that, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable asked with a peculiar look in her eyes.

“Do you mean that?”

There was mischief in her gaze, as if asking, Do you really not need my help? Of course that was not true. If it were, I would not have come.

“No. I desperately need your help, Demon Venerable.”

At my honest answer, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable smiled. Her silent smile now suited her ghostly aura rather well.

“As I said, the situation is unfavorable. I may even fall out of my father’s favor.”

That was the most important point to the other Demon Venerables, but not to her.

“You need not worry about that. I already stand outside the Cult Leader’s sight.”

She did not mean that my father hated her. She meant that it would require more time and effort for her to enter his sight. To my father, she would still be regarded as an incomplete Demon Venerable.

She understood precisely where she stood, and precisely what sort of man my father was.

“Will someone like me still be acceptable?”

I cupped my fist to her respectfully and said,

“You may stand outside my father’s sight, but to me, you stand before me.”

Those words were enough. The ghostly aura flowing from the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s eyes grew even more intense.

“All the ghosts of our cult will follow you, Young Cult Leader.”

* * *

The Demon Buddha was making a stone statue in a small clearing some distance from the Golden Great Buddha.

The sight of the Demon Buddha carving away stone with a chisel and hammer suited him, and yet also felt unfamiliar.

The surrounding buildings, stone statues, and towers were all gilded in gold, but the stone statue the Demon Buddha was making now was ordinary stone.

“I am winning, three to two.”

I tried bluffing first with the Demon Buddha. Of course, it did not work.

“In the time I have not seen you, only your lies have improved.”

“And you remain as sharp as ever, Demon Buddha.”

Those words, in fact, were the true lie. The Demon Buddha felt softer than before. Had he gained some realization of the heart?

I stood for a moment and watched the Demon Buddha who had become a stonemason. He was not only good at digging up medicinal herbs. His craftsmanship in carving stone statues was excellent as well.

“But how did you know? That there were two on my side.”

“Because even two is many.”

At those words, I could not help but laugh. How could I deny it? My opponent was my father.

“There is a good side to this matter as well. I have been able to meet all the Demon Venerables like this and confirm what they are thinking. Perhaps Father intended this. Know your place and face reality!”

Clang! Clang!

The hammering stopped for a moment. Beyond the hammer, the Demon Buddha glanced at me.

“Do you truly think the Cult Leader made this stage to show you that many Demon Venerables support him?”

The Demon Buddha used the word stage. In other words, he believed my father had created this situation deliberately.

“Was it because he wanted me to grow closer to the Demon Venerables?”

Clang!

As though telling me I was wrong, the Demon Buddha’s hammer rang out briskly.

“Or is Father trying to look back on his relationship with the Demon Venerables through this opportunity?”

Again, as though that too was the wrong answer, only the sound of hammering rang out, clang, clang.

How did the Demon Buddha view this matter? Though he was now acting as if he had taken a step back from the world and attained enlightenment, at one time he had been the most political and realistic of the Eight Demon Venerables.

“Please tell me. Why did Father create this stage?”

The Demon Buddha stopped hammering and examined the stone statue. Perhaps its balance was off, for he frowned and shook his head.

Of course. How could he concentrate on his work while having this sort of conversation?

The Demon Buddha set down the hammer and turned toward me.

“To seat us as the audience for that stage.”

It was not what I had expected. If he had seated the Demon Venerables as the audience?

“Surely not? Do you mean he intends to fight me on that stage?”

The Demon Buddha looked at me as if it were only natural.

At that moment, I recalled what the Fist Demon had said.

—The Cult Leader has already started the war.

Had the war my father spoken of through the Fist Demon meant a war against me?

“Isn’t it said that no parent can defeat their child?”

Then came an unexpected line.

“The Cult Leader will not lose to you on purpose.”

I could feel that the Demon Buddha, too, saw my father in the same way the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did.

“Ordinary people think it foolish to create another enemy when they already have one, do they not?”

“Is it not?”

“For ordinary people, that may be true.”

“Then what about Father?”

The Demon Buddha raised his head and looked toward the Heavenly Demon Hall in the distance.

“With a sword drawn once, the Cult Leader intends to cut down everything.”

“!”

“Before the watching eyes of the Demon Venerables, he will persuade you, then with the power united as one, he will punish those behind the scenes. And further still, with that sword dripping blood, he intends to cut down even the Martial Alliance and the Evil Dao Alliance.”

The words “That cannot be” did not come out of my mouth.

Instead, a single scene came to mind.

An empty taishi chair.

It was the image of my father, who had always guarded that place, absent from it. That was a sight more terrifying than the image of my father holding the Heavenly Demon Sword dripping blood.

“Is there a reason you are so certain?”

“There is.”

A golden radiance blazed fiercely from the Demon Buddha’s body.

“Because our cult has never been this strong before.”

What if the Demon Buddha’s thoughts were true?

In the end, the one who had moved my father was me.

* * *

“Young Cult Leader!”

The person looking at me with startled eyes was Gowol.

After leaving the Demon Buddha, I ran straight to where Gowol was with Swift Steps. Truly, I ran like the wind.

Even while I had been in the North Sea, Gowol had been traveling between the main cult and the outside world, further strengthening Silver Moon.

The branch I arrived at today was at a distance that could be reached after half a day of running with Swift Steps.

“You must be busy. What brings you all the way here?”

The desk where he had been sitting was still piled high with documents.

“How can there be so much work? I told you to have your subordinates do it all.”

“I do have my subordinates do it.”

“You should just drink tea and nag them.”

Because I knew very well that was not in his nature, I added one more thing today.

“Strategist Go. You know how important you are to me, do you not?”

“I know.”

“Then do not make me worry about your health too.”

I was not worried about his guards, since I knew the experts sent by the Cult Leader Pungcheon were protecting him. How thoroughly would Cult Leader Pungcheon take care of Gowol?

But as for his health, I had to nag him.

“You must be busy meeting the Demon Venerables. How did you come all the way here?”

As expected, Gowol knew the circumstances within the main cult in detail.

“I came because I wanted to see you. Come, let us get some air, even if only for a moment.”

I left the office together with Gowol. When we stepped out into the backyard, it led directly to the marketplace. We walked side by side through the place where people came and went.

“Do not sit in one place for too long. They say your lifespan shortens by as much as you sit, do they not? Get up and walk at least once every half hour.”

“I understand.”

“It has been a long time since we last met, and I am nagging too much, am I not?”

Gowol smiled without denying it. I knew. I knew just how well Gowol teased Cult Leader Pungcheon. If Cult Leader Pungcheon had been the one to nag him like this, his reaction would have been different.

“Does the Cult Leader contact you often?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon had remained at Pungcheon Cult for the sake of his disciple, the current Cult Leader Pungcheon. I had thought he would return after a month or two, but he had stayed quite a long time. Since his relationship with his disciple had recovered, it seemed there was much he wanted to teach.

“Letters come from time to time. Half of them are complaints about his disciple.”

“And the other half are insults about me?”

“How did you know?”

We laughed together.

“Seeing that the interval between letters is growing shorter and shorter, it seems the Cult Leader is feeling frustrated as well. I believe he will return before long.”

“I miss Cult Leader Pungcheon too.”

After walking and talking about this and that, I revealed the main reason I had come.

“Throughout the time I met the Demon Venerables, one thought kept occurring to me.”

“What thought was that?”

“Do I have the right to break Father’s dream?”

The Demon Venerables all understood my father’s desire. Though they did not reveal it openly like the One Flower Sword Venerable, their feelings were likely similar. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s conflict must lie there as well, and would the Extreme Evil Young Demon be any different?

“Even so, what is the reason you intend to stop him?”

Because too many people will die.

But that answer did not come easily. Just as it had been difficult for the Poison King to answer, “Because I like poison,” I too could not say those words easily.

Are you truly someone who worries for the safety of all martial artists?

That question was the first to rise in my heart.

Originally, I should have said this:

The purpose of my regression is to save those who must be saved, and kill those who must die.

But if war breaks out, people who should not die will die. I do not know who that will be.

It could be one of the Demon Venerables. It could be Ian, or Seo Daeryong. It could be Gowol standing before me, or Cult Leader Pungcheon. It could be Jo Chunbae, or the patrons of the Pungryu Tavern. This matter will not end with a table being broken.

Beyond that, there is also my feeling for my father.

In my life before regression, I experienced truly many things. I saw many lives, and felt extreme loneliness. I endured many hardships difficult to bear, and crossed the threshold of death many times.

I endured all of it for revenge.

That is what revenge is.

To kill a person is to create this kind of vengeance. Even a few hurtful words can make a human being remember the other party for a lifetime; this matter would take the lives of countless people.

The friends, comrades, and families of the dead.

Through war, thousands, tens of thousands of vengeful hearts will be born.

Tens of thousands of me.

Tens of thousands of Hwa Mugi.

That burden will come to rest on the shoulders of my father, the one who started the war. I do not want my father to bear the weight of that resentment. I do not want him to stand at the tips of countless swords of vengeance.

You say it is Father’s dream? His long-cherished wish?

I believe that my father was the same as I was before regression.

We were never given the chance to dream a different dream.

My father lived as the Heavenly Demon, unable to open his heart to anyone, and I had lived as the blood kin who had lost that Heavenly Demon.

We were stronger than anyone, but we were people who had never been able to dream properly.

In addition to that feeling, I had one more belief.

The heavens had not given me the chance to regress so that I could drive countless people to their deaths.

After many thoughts circled round and round, I returned to the starting point.

“Because too many people will die in that war.”

For a moment, Gowol silently watched the people passing by.

Then he looked at me and said quietly,

“Is that reason not enough to risk everything and stop it?”

Yes, it seemed I had wanted to hear those words from Gowol. That must have been why I had run such a long road like the wind. Because I wanted to hear those words from my strategist.

Now the moment had come for me to return and meet my father.

“I think I am ready to step onto the stage as well.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 442

Chapter 442: Father's Demon Venerables Are So Cool
--------------------------------------------------
Was it because my heart had grown lighter?

The Swift Step was faster on the way back.

After returning to the cult, I ate and rested well. Then I bathed thoroughly and changed into a new martial uniform.

In the best of moods, I was about to head to the Heavenly Demon Hall to see Father when I found two people waiting outside my door, having heard that I had returned.

They were Seo Daeryong and Jang Ho. Jang Ho’s massive frame seemed to have grown even larger in the time I had not seen him.

Seo Daeryong approached me and, lowering his voice, said slyly,

“I know I shouldn’t make this kind of joke when Master is here, but if you put the two of us together, wouldn’t we count as one Demon Supreme?”

With those words, I knew at once what feelings had brought the two of them here to wait for me. If they could, they wanted to step forward themselves and become my strength.

“The two of you are worth three Demon Supremes.”

“Am I supposed to handle two Demon Supremes?”

“Lord Jang is two and a half. You’re half.”

“I’m the right arm! Me!”

Jang Ho, who had always been eyeing that right-arm position, bowed to me politely. No matter when I saw him, he was reliable.

“Young Cult Leader, you have always shown me more than I could imagine, so I am not worried. Still, please judge carefully and with caution.”

He was worried about me because my opponent was Father.

Before I knew it, the playfulness had vanished from Seo Daeryong’s face as well, and he was looking at me.

These were two men who would gladly lay down their lives if I ordered it. My people, who must never die. That was why today’s fight was also a fight for them.

“I’ll reserve the Pungryu Tavern. And if you’re thinking of making a joke about drinking in defeat, don’t.”

“I wasn’t planning to make that joke, but you just did.”

Seo Daeryong smiled brightly and gave me a respectful cupped-fist salute. Jang Ho also saluted and bowed his head. It was a moment that gave me more strength than any encouragement in the world.

“Reserve the entire Pungryu Tavern. Let’s get drunk all night.”

Leaving their encouragement behind, I departed from my residence.

When I arrived at the entrance of the Heavenly Demon Hall, this time my elder brother, Geom Muyang, was waiting for me. I could tell just how important this matter was to everyone.

“I heard everything from the Demon Buddha.”

It seemed the Demon Buddha had told my brother all about this matter.

“So? Did you come to help your cornered younger brother?”

My brother gave an unexpected answer.

“I came to save our cornered father.”

“Right. In this murim, only you and I could make a joke like that.”

“It is not a joke.”

There was reproach in my brother’s gaze as he looked at me.

“He trusted you and made you Young Cult Leader, and now you defy Father’s will?”

“If I fall out of Father’s favor, wouldn’t that be better for you?”

“I thought it would be.”

Apparently not. Not when he had come out like this to stop me.

We stood side by side without looking at each other, gazing together at the view from the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Now it was time to have a conversation we should have had at least once. A conversation I could not have with anyone else, only with my brother.

“Do you know this? No one can speak about Father’s life.”

My brother looked at me with an expression that asked what I meant.

“That means Father has never once heard it. Someone speaking about his life, about the path he has lived.”

“Isn’t the Chief Strategist there?”

“Yes, he is. Uncle Hwi is there too. They may be able to speak frankly. They may be able to discuss matters within the cult comfortably. But Brother, do you really think they would dare speak about Father’s life? About Father’s convictions?”

“!”

My brother had probably never thought about this before.

“Are you doubting their loyalty?”

“I’m saying this because I know that loyalty is real. Because they are truly loyal, they cannot do it. They will not do it. Because they will try to accept every side of Father. Because they will try to bear it all. You know how it is, don’t you? If you’re too close, you can’t say it because you’re close. If you’re distant, you can’t say it because you’re distant.”

After a brief pause, my brother asked,

“So? Are you saying you will do it?”

“Yes. I will.”

My brother shouted with an angry face.

“How insolent!”

“Even so, we have to do it. Because it is something even you think must not be done. That is why Father has lived a life in which he can never receive advice or admonishment. We have to tell him. Father, I believe you are mistaken about this. Someone has to say it.”

My brother tried to say something, but in the end he let out a sigh. How could he not understand what I meant?

“And what if my choice isn’t right? Father will correct me. Let’s use our faith in Father for things like this.”

My brother said nothing more.

“Go in.”

“Still, thank you. For coming here because you were worried.”

“I told you I did not come because I was worried about you.”

My brother, who had walked some distance away, looked back at me. Even then, I was still looking at him.

Until the very end, his thoughts did not change.

“Even so, I am against it.”

“That is why I am going to do it.”

I watched my brother turn away again and grow distant, then entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Still, please know this. Among the people I do not want to kill now, you are included too, Brother. That is why I am fighting this fight.

* * *

I entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Father was seated in the grand chair as always. Today, that sight felt more special than on other days. Perhaps it was because I had truly felt how terrifying it was to see that chair empty.

“Father, I have come.”

I greeted Father in a voice brighter than usual.

I walked slowly along the Path of Blood laid out in red carpet until I reached the foot of the grand chair. Today, that path felt especially long.

At the end of the Path of Blood, I looked up at Father.

Do you know this, Father? When I regressed, that day as well, I stood in the dueling ground and looked up at you like this. Since that day, I have always been watching you.

After my regression, I worked hard for everyone, but at the center of it all, there was always Father.

“I have something to confess.”

Of course, Father must have already known what I was about to say. Even so, just as I had when I returned after being expelled from the cult, I told Father everything.

“For the past few days, I went around visiting the Demon Supremes and pestered them to take my side.”

As expected, there was no surprise or agitation on Father’s face.

“They are my Demon Supremes, so why did you ask them to take your side?”

“Perhaps because I was jealous?”

I felt the corners of Father’s mouth preparing to rise slightly.

“Father’s Demon Supremes are too magnificent. I think that is why I felt jealous.”

Father did not laugh at me. Rather, as if he sympathized with my words, he nodded once and said,

“It is not easy for such fine people to gather in one era.”

The moment I heard those words, I remembered what the Demon Buddha had said.

—Because our cult has never been this strong before.

Those eight fine Demon Supremes had changed in their own ways, and had grown stronger in their own ways. Even at this very moment, they were growing stronger. The stagnant Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had begun to surge forward without obstruction.

“Since we’re on the subject, why don’t we call them all and see their faces? Don’t they say that if you do not see one another often, hearts grow distant?”

It was a probe. If, as the Demon Buddha thought, this matter was a stage Father had created, and if Father’s goal was to persuade me and gather the Demon Supremes into one, then Father would call the Demon Supremes. If not, he would simply pass it over as though he had not heard.

Father, how will you receive this move?

Father did not withdraw his hand, but accepted my move.

Father said quietly,

“Call the Demon Supremes.”

Then Hwi’s answer came from the air.

“I receive your command.”

A soundless surge of emotion spread from deep within my heart. Father had truly called the Demon Supremes.

He was not someone who summoned the Demon Supremes unless the matter was important, so I began to feel strongly that the Demon Buddha’s words might be true.

Father, if that is so, what do you intend to do on this stage? With the Demon Supremes as your audience, do you truly mean to persuade me? Or do you mean to suppress me with force?

A brief silence flowed between us. But since that silence did not feel particularly awkward or uncomfortable, I did not force myself to continue the conversation. I held back my words. There would be many things to say today.

How much time passed like that?

At last, the door opened and someone entered.

The first to arrive was the Fist Demon. The sight of him striding across the red carpet with that enormous body was as if he were walking out through the gates of hell.

After walking to the foot of the grand chair, he greeted Father respectfully, then stood on the right side in the direction Father was looking.

“Welcome, Master.”

Because we were before Father, the Fist Demon merely greeted me with a light nod of the eyes.

I asked with my gaze.

Was the war with Father you spoke of a war against me?

The Fist Demon must have sensed the meaning in my eyes, but he simply stood there in silence.

Another person entered. Someone who followed Father as much as the Fist Demon did—the One Blossom Sword Venerable.

Her pure white martial robes and her white hair suited each other perfectly. It was a sight truly opposite to when the Fist Demon had walked in. Seeing her walk lightly along the Path of Blood, I had this thought.

When the female martial artists of our cult see her, do they not think this?

I want to age like that too.

That was the impression the One Blossom Sword Venerable gave these days. She was personally showing that age had nothing to do with a person’s change.

She too greeted Father respectfully, then stood beside the Fist Demon.

“You have come?”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable also greeted me with a faint smile.

The first two who had arrived were people who absolutely respected and liked Father. Without even the slightest hesitation, they had chosen Father.

The next person to enter was the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. Wearing his white mask, he walked in. Like the One Blossom Sword Venerable’s snow-white hair, his mask formed a striking contrast against the red carpet.

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon greeted Father respectfully, then stood on the opposite side of the carpet from the Fist Demon and the One Blossom Sword Venerable.

It was not that he had deliberately meant to confront them. Since the two were standing on one side, he had naturally stood across the red carpet.

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon looked at the Fist Demon and the One Blossom Sword Venerable before him with eyes that held no emotion. Then his gaze turned to me.

The two eyes inside the eyeholes said this.

Young Cult Leader, I have come.

I smiled at him with my eyes.

Smiling Demon, I have been waiting.

A courtesy understood only between him and me. I liked this feeling very much.

When I turned my head again, Father was silently watching us.

‘I am sorry, Father. Your Demon Supreme was too magnificent, so I brought him over as one of my people. It is only one person, so please understand.’

The Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme, who arrived next, felt different from the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. She was not only Father’s Demon Supreme, but also the future Demon Supreme.

After greeting Father, the place the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme chose was beside the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. There were two people across from her and one on this side, so she stood there, but as a result, the arrangement became one of confrontation.

Though she faced the Fist Demon and the One Blossom Sword Venerable, she was not intimidated in the slightest. Also, as she stood side by side with the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, the auras of extreme evil and soul-capturing, which stimulated a martial artist’s primal fear, mingled together and doubled the terror.

The next Demon Supreme to arrive was the Drunken Demon, who entered reeking of alcohol.

He naturally stood beside the One Blossom Sword Venerable.

Unlike the Fist Demon and the One Blossom Sword Venerable, his gaze toward me held apology. After telling me he would choose Father, he had probably drunk a great deal.

At times like this, he looked weaker than anyone, but had I not seen it? What kind of man the Drunken Demon was on the battlefield.

The next Demon Supreme to enter was the Demon Buddha.

Entering with a golden radiance, he stood beside the Drunken Demon without hesitation. Normally, the natural arrangement would have been for him to stand beside the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme, where two stood, and balance the sides, but for some reason, he stood on the opposite side.

I could tell he had done so intentionally. He was the one who had said that this matter was a stage Father had created. That must be why he was trying to heighten the tension of this stage as much as possible.

No matter how hard he hammered away at a stone statue, it seemed there was no helping his innate love for conflicts like this.

The seventh Demon Supreme was the Poison King.

As if to keep his word that he was on no one’s side, he stood alone, far apart from both sides.

The Poison King had made it perfectly clear before my father and the Demon Venerables where he stood.

In some ways, he was the bravest of all. In this case, choosing neither side was a more difficult choice than choosing one or the other.

But in the end, by standing at a distance, he was also keeping the promise he had once made to my father. Father had told him not to take part in the unification of murim.

The last person to enter was the Blood Heaven Saber Demon. He had been unable to decide until the very end. If he had made up his mind, he would have come to me and told me.

What choice would he make today?

As he walked the path of blood, he looked at the Demon Venerables standing on either side.

Since the balance was so uneven, I had briefly hoped that he might stand on my side.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon crossed over without hesitation and stood on the opposite side.

I put on a deliberately hurt expression and said to him,

“Elder, this is too much! I believed that at least you would support me.”

He did not take my words as a joke. Instead, he sharply rebuked me.

“The one who has gone too far is you, Young Cult Leader. How dare you oppose the Cult Leader’s will?”

That distinctly displeased look he had worn when I first met him. It was as though our relationship had been reset to the very beginning.

From that cold response, I could tell.

He had chosen me.

Though he stood over there, I felt that he had chosen me.

Why?

Because he was worried about the relationship between my father and me. Setting aside my father’s long-cherished wish and my demonic path, he had thought only of that.

The concern that the more I stood in firm opposition to my father, the more a crack might form between us.

He must have decided that standing on my father’s side like this and scolding me sharply was, in fact, the way to act for my sake.

Thank you, Elder.

But I did not send him a voice transmission of thanks, nor did I give him a look that said I understood everything in his heart. For now, following his intention would be the gratitude he deserved.

But what should I do, Elder? Today, I intend to defeat my father and leave.

And so the eight Demon Venerables gathered in one place.

My father, the eight Demon Venerables, and me.

With this force, even if we walked out as we were and set off to conquer murim, no one would find it strange.

At last, my father rose from the Grand Preceptor’s chair.

The Demon Venerables all straightened their postures at once, their nerves taut. Those who had each shone in their own colors until now turned black and white in unison, concealing themselves.

Within that black-and-white painting, only my father and I revealed our presences as they were—one red as blood, the other blue as the sky.

After gazing at me for a moment, my father began slowly walking down toward the stage packed with spectators.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 443

Chapter 443: Father's Battle Was Noble.
---------------------------------------
I silently watched Father descend the stairs.

‘Father. Do you know when I thought of you the most in my life before regression? When you died? When I went around gathering materials, dreaming of revenge? When my body and mind were exhausted? Or when I was lonely? None of those. It was when I reached the same age as you.’

I had never once had a proper, heart-to-heart conversation with Father, so I had thought there would be no longing for him. I thought it would be nothing more than a vague sort of yearning.

And yet, strangely enough, as I grew older, Father came to mind from time to time.

If it were now, I felt I could speak deeply with Father.

If it were now, I felt I could understand Father a little better. If it were now.

Father stopped a few steps in front of me.

Our gazes tangled in midair. I had faced Father many times, but never had there been a moment as tense as this one.

At last, Father asked.

“Did you say you were jealous because my Demon Venerables were impressive?”

His first words were for my sake. They were words that informed them that I had called the Demon Venerables impressive.

I looked around at the Demon Venerables standing on either side.

Briefly, my eyes met with each of theirs. The emotions in the gazes watching us were all different.

As always, the Fist Demon’s gaze was heavy. Beside the composed One-Flower Sword Venerable were the Drunken Demon’s half-lidded eyes, concealing his complicated feelings. The Demon Buddha’s eyes were filled with interest, the Poison King was indifferent, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was burning hot. The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was dignified, and the Extreme Evil Young Demon was smiling.

“Yes. I’m jealous enough to want to make every one of them my own Demon Venerables.”

The first to react was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Young Cult Leader, you should not say such things, even as a joke.”

Today, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had clearly taken it upon himself to play the role of scolding me. He knew. He knew that someone had to take on that role here today. I was grateful to him for it.

“What can I do when they’re so impressive?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sent me a strict glare. Is this how you’re going to be? Suppressing the urge to tease him, I bowed my head politely.

“I apologize. I will be more careful with my words and actions from now on.”

As I apologized, I saw the Demon Buddha looking at me and smiling. Among the audience today, he was enjoying himself the most.

These days, you’ve withdrawn and spend your time carving stone statues, so this sort of thing must be exciting, right?

As if answering my thoughts, the Demon Buddha’s radiance deepened.

Even so, I could not treat them carelessly. If I were alone with a Demon Venerable, or if it were only the Demon Venerables among themselves, it would be a joke; but a clumsy joke made in front of Father could become a grave discourtesy to the Demon Venerables.

With jokes, what matters more than making them is knowing how to hold back when you want to.

I looked at Father again.

“Father, there is one thing I would like to ask.”

Everyone’s attention focused on me. Amid the tension in the air, I finally asked Father this question.

“I would like to ask what your dream is, Father.”

I could feel everyone flinch. Their thoughts were probably, Surely he isn’t asking the Cult Leader that directly?

Naturally, everyone’s gaze shifted to Father. They were not waiting for the answer. There was no one here who did not know what Father’s dream was. They were curious how Father would react. Whether he would answer obediently, or become angry.

Father gave no reply.

Father’s silence heightened the tension. He did not release demonic energy, nor did his expression harden, and yet he made everyone tense.

The one who resolved that swelling, bursting tension was the Drunken Demon. He stepped forward in a slurred voice. Because the only person who could cut in at a time like this was a drunkard.

“Before asking about the Cult Leader’s dream, you should reveal your own first, Young Cult Leader.”

“Of course. My thinking was lacking. My dream changes often, so if you ask me later, I may give a different answer.”

I deliberately added those words first for Father to hear. Please do not dream only one dream. Dreams can change.

At that moment, the Drunken Demon thankfully asked this question.

“Does our Young Cult Leader’s dream change as often as you say?”

The quick-witted Drunken Demon understood my intention in bringing that up.

“Does a dream have to be just one? We only live once, so we can dream of this and then dream of that. And even if one has no dream, what of it?”

I spoke while looking at the Drunken Demon, but in truth, these words were meant for Father. Of course, Father would know that too.

I did not simply let the drunkard who had barged onto the stage leave.

“By any chance, Drunken Demon, do you have a dream?”

Normally, he would have laughed and said he would just spend his whole life drinking.

“To serve the Cult Leader and unify the murim.”

The Drunken Demon said the words “unify the murim” in his place. He knew Father would not say those words himself.

I will step forward in your place, so use me to say what you want to say to the Cult Leader.

He was repaying the guilt he felt for standing on Father’s side in this way.

“Do you think the world will become better if the murim is unified?”

Yes, this was something I could not ask Father directly.

“At the very least, it would be better than it is now.”

“No, I believe it will become worse.”

“What is your reason for thinking so?”

“Imagine a world where the demonic practitioners, the orthodox faction, and the unorthodox faction are all bound together as one. The demonic practitioners will think that since we unified the world, they should trample the orthodox and unorthodox factions beneath them. The unorthodox faction, soaked in defeat, will commit even more vicious atrocities. The orthodox faction will search for every possible method to reclaim the orthodox murim. In that process, there will be countless sacrifices again. In the end, it will become a world where everyone is dissatisfied.”

Father and the Demon Venerables quietly listened to me.

“Could we not strongly control that chaos?”

Speaking on their behalf, the Drunken Demon presented a solution.

“A murim ruled through oppression and control—is that the world you hope for through the unification of the murim?”

I said it while looking at the Drunken Demon, but these words, more than any others, were directed at Father.

I had actually experienced it. A murim where the balance among the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox factions had collapsed. I had also learned that the nature of power never allowed a vacancy.

Another power known as the Twelve Zodiac Kings had taken that place, and life became even harsher for the martial artists.

It would be the same for us. The Eight Demon Venerables would simply take the place of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

“I believe they are worlds that should never have been merged in the first place.”

Because the Drunken Demon stepped forward, I was able to say everything I wanted to say.

Now, sending the Drunken Demon down from the stage, I looked at Father. I could not tell what meaning my earlier words had carried to him.

Because Father was looking at me with an expression unchanged from the beginning.

“Father, setting everything else aside, I do not want to lose anyone here.”

“No one here fears death.”

“Yes, I know. I know that all of you are valiant people. I also know very well that you are people who would cast away your lives without hesitation at Father’s command. This is purely my personal feeling. It is my greed in not wanting to lose these people.”

With a serious gaze, I looked around at the Demon Venerables.

“I do not want to lose even a single person here. If one person dies, do seven not remain, you ask? No. For as long as I live, I will miss that one person.”

Perhaps Father could not understand. Why I wanted so desperately to keep them alive. Because he did not know the effort I had made before regression to save those who had to be saved.

My gaze, which had been directed toward the Demon Venerables, turned to Father.

“To confess honestly, this is an effort for the sake of my own life. That is right. It is my desperate struggle not to live a life spent longing for the dead.”

“Is that your dream?”

“No. This is my personal wish. It is merely my selfishness.”

“Then what is your dream?”

Father was allowing me to say everything on this stage. As if he were making me the protagonist of this stage.

Say everything you want to say before me and the Demon Venerables.

And so, perhaps, this would become an even more difficult battle.

“My dream is to live while pondering in that Taishi chair.”

Not only Father, but everyone revealed a puzzled look. With just those words, they would not be able to understand what I meant.

“I will sit in the Taishi chair and ponder. Which Demon Venerable should I visit today? What dish should I ask the Heavenly Demon Hall’s cook to make for me?”

“Does that seat look so leisurely to you?”

“I know that it is not. I know very well how much work Father handles from that seat.”

At his expression, which seemed to ask why I would say such a thing if I knew, I calmly asked Father in return.

“Then should you not ponder all the more? One cannot live working for an entire lifetime.”

“!”

Depending on how one took it, this was a clear provocation toward Father, and so the atmosphere sank again. Father himself remained still, but the reactions of the Demon Venerables shaped the mood.

“How should I handle work efficiently? How can I secure as much time for myself as possible? I will ponder that every day. Then, when I grow tired of it, I will go visit the Murim Alliance Leader. When I grow tired after teasing that straitlaced man, I will then seek out the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao and ask him whether his dancing skills are still as good as ever. Living like that is my dream.”

My true dream was not to be trapped in that Taishi chair. To roam the world and live freely—that was my true dream.

But I could not say such a thing in front of Father. I could not tell such a dream to Father, who had lived his entire life in that Taishi chair for the sake of our cult. Nor for the sake of my elder brother, who had been pushed away from that Taishi chair.

So what I was saying now was a compromise of my dream. Father, can dreams not be compromised like this? Is it not because one handles them too preciously that one cannot fulfill them in the end?

“What about you, Father? Have you never wanted to live such a life?”

My answer and my question were treading a dangerous line. But if I did not speak this forcefully, I could not face Father on this stage. I could not persuade Father.

But the reality Father believed in was cold.

“That straitlaced Murim Alliance Leader you spoke of will one day stab a blade into your back and say he had no choice. That because he is the Murim Alliance Leader, he had no choice. Then, as he looks down into your dying eyes, he will spout some worthless chivalrous righteousness.”

The Fist Demon, the One-Flower Sword Venerable, and the Demon Buddha nodded. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Drunken Demon did not react, but their expressions were not far from Father’s thoughts either.

Separate from their relationship with me, they did not trust the orthodox faction. And how could that distrust be limited only to the orthodox faction?

“How long do you think the Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao can keep dancing? At what point in those dance steps will a surprise attack emerge? Do you truly believe humans will not change? Do you believe they will remain steadfast in the face of circumstance or profit?”

I gave no answer. If I wanted to refute him, there were things I could say, but I did not. At least on this matter, it was a conclusion Father had reached through his long life and experience.

I let out a light sigh and spoke quietly.

“Perhaps that may be so.”

Then I looked around at the Demon Venerables.

“Is there no one who will step forward and help me at a time like this? Someone who will say, ‘Cult Leader, it will not necessarily be so!’ Anyone?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke with his eyes. You brat, stop fooling around. Be serious!

The Demon Buddha was simply smiling from pure amusement.

The One-Flower Sword Venerable’s eyes seemed to say, I am sorry, and the Drunken Demon pretended to be drunk.

And at this moment, someone truly unexpected stepped forward.

“The Young Cult Leader will not be defeated so easily.”

It was not the Extreme Evil Young Demon. The one who stepped forward was someone I thought would absolutely never do so—the Fist Demon.

“He may look that way on the outside, but the Young Cult Leader does not trust people easily.”

“As expected! Master knows his disciple well. But are those words praise? Or an insult?”

I spoke to Father again.

“Even if I am deceived by them, it will be all right. I am training in martial arts like a madman in preparation for that moment. A blade from behind will not pierce my skin, and in the end, those dance steps will remain only a dance.”

“Are you saying you will let the martial arts you have built with such difficulty go to waste?”

“I am saying I will enjoy life with that skill.”

“By worrying over something as trivial as what to eat and where to go play?”

“Yes, that is what I will do.”

Father, who had been gazing at me, slowly moved his feet.

The place Father headed toward was before the Extreme Evil Young Demon.
