The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon’s gaze was calm, but I was the one who tensed instead.

“Even so, will you still support the Young Cult Leader?”

It was a burdensome question. The question itself carried the demand that he answer no.

But like his pure white mask, the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon’s heart was of one mind.

“Yes. I support the Young Cult Leader’s will.”

Father asked him again.

“Even if you can never wear that Immortal Ghost Mask of yours for the rest of your life?”

The Immortal Ghost Mask was the mask the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon wore when he went to war. In the past, when the Demon Venerables had stepped forward before Father for my sake, the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon had worn the Immortal Ghost Mask and pleaded with Father.

Since it was the mask he cherished most, Father had struck where it would hurt the most.

But the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon spoke to Father more courteously than ever.

“Fortunately, I have many masks.”

The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon’s gaze, which had been directed at Father, turned to me.

“I will live the rest of my life wearing the Pleasure Ghost Mask with the Young Cult Leader.”

The Pleasure Ghost Mask was the mask worn for major events or banquets within the cult.

Though he had given up the Immortal Ghost Mask he loved most, the two eyes within his mask were clear and deep. That gaze he had when he revealed his true feelings.

Before none other than Father, he had shown his sincerity. There was only one way I could repay it.

I will make that banquet the grandest banquet of all.

All the Demon Venerables looked at the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon. Each of their gazes held different emotions.

Father said nothing more to the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon. Had Father truly asked without knowing he would react that way?

This time, Father stood before the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

She was dignified even before Father. After all, everyone who knew anything knew that Father did not favor the Poison King or the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. That was why she tried even harder to stand tall.

I thought he would ask why she had sided with the Young Cult Leader, but Father asked an unexpected question.

“Is your training going well?”

Though inwardly flustered by the gentle question, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable answered politely.

“I am training diligently.”

I thought he would simply encourage her and turn away.

“Do not forget. You are my Demon Venerable.”

This time, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was startled and moved. She had thought she stood outside Father’s favor.

“Will you still stand there?”

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was flustered. She looked at me.

There are many times when I do not know what Father is thinking, but at least I know this much for certain.

He is not the sort of person who would pressure a young Demon Venerable in a situation like this and bring her over to his side.

Which means? This was not Father pressuring me, but him looking after her.

He was telling her that it was not yet time for her to stand out. It was Father’s intention and consideration that she should simply grow quietly among the Demon Venerables.

I conveyed my thoughts to her with my eyes.

It is all right for you to go.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, understanding my meaning, cupped her fist toward me with great courtesy.

“I will serve you well later.”

She crossed the red carpet and stood on the other side.

Of course, what I said was completely different from the look I had given her.

“This is too much! Did you even think about who you’d be spending more years as a Demon Venerable with before deciding? Where in the world is there such a spineless ghost!”

The Demon Buddha, standing beside her, smiled brightly.

‘Not easy, is it?’

On this stage, Father was strong.

Me, the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, and even the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. He was leading this stage while looking after and showing consideration to all of us.

Father never became agitated. His battles had dignity, and they were elegant.

All the Demon Venerables’ gazes turned toward me. The question contained in their eyes was the same.

Young Cult Leader, what will you do now?

Absolute Regression, Chapter 444

Chapter 444: If Father's Dream Is Still the Same Then.
------------------------------------------------------
When everyone’s gaze focused on me, I felt as if I were standing alone on an empty stage.

But I was not lonely.

Behind me stood the Extreme Evil Soma, and the people looking at me were also my Demon Venerables. In truth, they were Father’s Demon Venerables and stood on Father’s side, but in my heart, they were also mine.

Of course, there was one fact I must not forget. My relationship with these Demon Venerables was possible in the first place because I was Father’s son.

That was why this stage was important. I had to settle this contest well upon this stage. Whether I won or lost, I, too, had to do so with dignity and grace.

Because this stage was Father’s and my first stage.

First of all, I must absolutely not damage Father’s majesty and authority in front of the Demon Venerables. That was the principle of this fight.

‘Father, do you know that I am even considering that? Do you know how difficult that makes this fight for me?’

Father’s gaze turned toward me. I could not tell how much of what I had said earlier had reached him.

The one who broke the silence was the Drunken Demon.

“Cult Leader, I would like to offer the Young Cult Leader a cup of wine. Would that be all right?”

At the Drunken Demon’s request, Father nodded.

The Drunken Demon poured wine into the Ice Palace Holy Grail.

“Young Cult Leader, accept a cup from me and change your mind.”

Though he was taking Father’s side, the Drunken Demon’s true feelings were different.

—I do not wish to see the murim become a sea of blood, so use that fine head of yours and find a way!

Between Father and me, he had no choice but to choose Father, but the Drunken Demon opposed war. This wine was a cup of encouragement.

“I will drink it gratefully.”

As I slowly drank, I exchanged sound transmissions with the Drunken Demon.

—As you can see, hyung, changing Father’s mind won’t be easy.

—Even so, the only person here who can do it is you.

“I drank it well.”

Just then, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon sent me a sound transmission.

—Give up. The Cult Leader’s dream and yours run along parallel lines. They can never meet.

From his perspective, he had judged that no matter how much I tried to persuade Father, I could not change Father’s long-cherished wish.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was right. How could a dream Father had held for so long be easily changed?

I calmly asked Father.

“There is something I would like to ask. Father, do you often think of that dream?”

Father looked at me with an expression asking what I meant by that.

“When we dream of something, at first it makes our hearts race and excites us, but as time passes, doesn’t that dream become something we take for granted?”

Father’s eyes wavered slightly. That long-cherished wish must have become a ‘given’ dream to him as well.

“Father, it is all right for you to dream the same dream. But could you not dream it again from the beginning? Dream it again from a state where you have no dream at all. Not as a dream you have had since so long ago you no longer remember when it began, but as a vivid dream of the present.”

Father gave no answer.

“All throughout the time I met the Demon Venerables this time, I thought about it. Is it truly all right for me to go against Father’s will? Do I have the right to do so? That was why I even went to meet my strategist. Even after meeting him and steadying my heart, when I saw Father, my heart wavered again.”

It was my true sincerity that I was conveying to Father.

“That is how much I do not wish to go against your will, Father. Because I respect you more than anyone else in the world.”

It was not flattery. It was my sincere feeling toward Father, revealed before all the Demon Venerables. Father still listened to me without any change in expression.

Today, Father was dealing with me not by speaking, but by listening. Thanks to that, today’s stage was truly my solo performance.

“I know very well how important Father’s dream, that long-cherished wish and ideal, is. The Demonic Way I spoke of is also part of my dream. But I believe there is something just as important as that.”

“What is that?”

“What we will eat for dinner later, whom we will meet tomorrow. I believe everyday life is just as important as a grand dream. I mean the scenery we look upon as we move toward that dream.”

It was also the reason I tried to live my life without being swallowed by the existence of a powerful enemy like Hwa Mugi.

It was a principle of life I had upheld since my regression, but I could not know how persuasive the words of someone as young as me would be to Father. Even so, I did my best.

“It is important to me to defeat you in baduk, Father, and it is also important to me to fulfill my wish of bathing in hot springs with you. Traveling through the Central Plains together with you is also my earnest desire. If I saw Father achieve the unification of murim without fulfilling any of these things and then sitting in the taishi chair, would I truly be happy?”

And how could that be all? My gaze turned toward the Demon Venerables.

“It is important to me to exchange pointers with the Sword Venerable, and it is important to me to train late into the night with Master. I want to read beside the Blade Demon while he reads. I will gather poisonous herbs with the Poison King, drink with the Drunken Demon, and watch the statue the Demon Buddha completes. I will also watch the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable grow. And there is still much furniture I want to place in Soma’s room. To me, these things are just as important as my dream. Because when I look back before I die someday, all these moments will be terribly precious and fill me with regret.”

Each and every moment had been so lonely and painful, yet when I looked back after it had passed, life had truly gone by in the blink of an eye. I had experienced it.

What I had to say from here on had to be said carefully.

“I believe the dream Father has held for so long was a dream with too few choices. To one who walks the path of the Heavenly Demon, many milestones would not have been necessary.”

But Father’s thoughts were firm.

“Even if there had been a hundred choices, I would have chosen the same one. Even if there had been dozens of crossroads, I would have continued walking the path I was on.”

“I am not telling you to take another path.”

“What do you mean by that?”

“I am telling you to look at the scenery around you as you walk.”

So that at times your steps may stop, at times another path may make you curious, and at times you may even doubt your own path.

For that, I, too, had to walk the road with Father. I had to walk with him, converse with him, and when scenery worth seeing appeared, tell him to look over there.

As I spoke with Father, I realized that this was the only way to make Father’s dream change. That realization immediately led to action.

“Father, will you make one promise with me?”

Not only Father, but the Demon Venerables as well looked at me with curious expressions. With everyone’s attention on me, I continued.

“Yes, a promise between martial artists. It is also a promise between the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader, and a promise between father and son.”

“What promise?”

“Please give me time.”

“Time?”

I thought about how many years I would need. I needed a span of time that was neither too short nor too long.

A time in which I could accomplish my goal exactly.

“Please give me exactly five years from now.”

“And if I do?”

“At that time, I will ask you again. If Father’s dream is still the unification of murim then, I will stand at the vanguard of that war.”

“!”

Not only Father, but all the Demon Venerables were startled. The Drunken Demon wore an expression as if he wanted to shout at once, “No! What are you doing?”

On the other side, both eyes of the Extreme Evil Soma were smiling. Was he imagining himself standing shoulder to shoulder with me at that vanguard?

That I would stand at the vanguard of the war—this was the decisive move I had thrown down.

Within five years, I would eliminate all of Hwa Mugi’s faction, and within five years, I would change Father’s mind. Within that time, I would make Father dream a new dream without fail.

Five years.

War was not something one could wage immediately just because one wished to.

Depending on what strategy one used to fight, preparations could take several months, or at length, two or three years.

In that case, to Father, five years was not such a long time.

If he won this wager, he could obtain me.

Me, who would have achieved the Twelfth-Star Great Completion of the Nine Flames Demonic Art through the Heaven-Defying Secret Art?

And would he obtain only me? He could also obtain everything the current me would accomplish by then as a bonus. If I took the lead and helped, Father’s dream of unifying murim would certainly draw closer.

This promise was an official promise made before the Demon Venerables.

“I am now staking my life to make this promise to you, Father.”

“Will you not regret it?”

“Father, are you the one who will not regret it?”

Our gazes intertwined in the air. Everyone here knew that we were two people who, once we made a promise, would keep it without fail. That was why both Father and I had to make our decision carefully.

“Why five years?”

“This is about changing the heart of none other than Father. If I followed my own desire, I would make it fifty years.”

“This is a fight in which I only need to carry this heart as it is. Five years will pass quickly. Even so, you will do it?”

“In that time, I will definitely change Father’s heart.”

That sneer formed at the corners of Father’s mouth. That smile, which gave a different impression each time, contained amusement today. It felt as if he were asking, “In truth, you want to fight too, don’t you?”

Perhaps that was why. Father readily nodded.

“Good. I accept that promise.”

I confirmed once more with the Demon Venerables this wager that would change all our fates.

“Five years from this day, I will invite all the Demon Venerables present here again, and I will ask Father about his dream. Let us settle the match that day.”

Five years from now, Father’s dream will have changed. You will speak of a dream that will startle every Demon Venerable here. I will make certain that happens.

“Thank you, Father.”

The Demon Venerables all looked at Father and me with expressions of surprise at this unexpected result.

Now, with a lighter heart, I asked Father.

“Since it is all over now, I would like to ask. Meeting the Demon Venerables and all that—was it to prepare for the unification of murim?”

But unexpectedly, Father shook his head.

“It was not?”

He was not someone who would answer such a thing with a lie. Startled, I asked again.

“Then why did you meet all the Demon Venerables?”

“All of them?”

“You met Soma, drank with the Drunken Demon, and helped the Poison King manufacture poison.”

“That is only three of them.”

He was right. The Demon Venerables he had met directly were three. Even for those, he offered other reasons.

“I heard that furniture had been placed in Soma’s room. So I went there once.”

If that was truly why, then Father must have had a change of heart recently as well.

“As for the Drunken Demon, the wine poured into the Ice Palace Holy Grail was so delicious that I simply drank with him.”

“What about the Poison King?”

“I was told that he needed precise object manipulation through the void. That he had failed several times in the manufacturing because he had no such assistant. So I merely went to help.”

I looked at Father. I could not tell whether he was speaking sincerely or not.

“I only met three of them, but you went around meeting them all.”

“!”

I flinched and asked Father with a startled expression.

“Then why did you gather everyone today? Was it not to persuade me and unite the Demon Venerables as one?”

“No.”

“No?”

“Are you the sort of child who would change his mind just because I persuaded you?”

Yes, there was no way Father did not know me.

“I received a report that you had gone around meeting all the Demon Venerables. Since you went around persuading them like that, I thought you should have a proper conclusion.”

In other words, this seat had been for me.

“Huh? Then why did I make that five-year promise?”

“You brought it up first. I did not say anything.”

In truth, Father had not even directly stated that his dream was this or that yet.

I shouted at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon as if accusing him.

“You said Father might have begun moving in earnest to fulfill his dream!”

“……I said he might have.”

Next, to the Fist Demon.

“You said Father was already in the middle of a war!”

“Would a father with a son like you not always be in the middle of a war?”

“That was what you meant?”

“What else would I have meant?”

And to the Demon Buddha as well.

“You said Father created this stage!”

The radiance that had been shining brightly from the Demon Buddha faded.

“Did I say that? My mind has been elsewhere lately. I should go finish the statue I was making.”

I looked back and forth between Father and the Demon Venerables.

“Right? I was had, wasn’t I? You all planned this from the beginning, didn’t you? That’s what happened, isn’t it?”

Just as I clutched my head with a pained expression, the Poison King, who had been standing apart at a distance, abruptly sent me a sound transmission.

—The one who was had is the Cult Leader.

He was reading my true thoughts accurately.

—If war breaks out, you will join it anyway. The you I know would ask the Cult Leader to let you stand at the vanguard. Because you would try to minimize the damage caused by the war.

I turned to him and smiled. He was right.

Would I enter secluded cultivation because I hated war, or would I side with the Murim Alliance? If I could not stop it beforehand, I would have to leap into the war anyway.

I had exaggerated as if I had been tricked, but this was not me being tricked. Rather, it was a contest advantageous to me.

Because at the very least, I would not have to worry about war for the next five years.

—Do you think the Cult Leader does not know your intentions?

—He must know.

I could tell just by the way Father looked at me. That did not mean he intended to go easy on me.

Father was giving me a chance, and at the same time, telling me to face him properly.

Not a contest of a single day, but a grand contest spanning a full five years.

The fate of murim was at stake in this contest.

By then, I would absolutely achieve the Twelfth-Star Great Completion of the Nine Flames Demonic Art and kill Hwa Mugi.

And I would also carry on a fight even more difficult than that one.

Showing Father that there was a better dream than the unification of murim. Walking together with Father, and making him see the scenery he had not seen all this time.

An empty taishi chair.

The empty taishi chair that now rose in my mind no longer carried the frightening feeling it had before. Because its absence would be for a different reason.

Father, from now on, you will have to go here and there with me. I have gained a justification.

Whether he knew my feelings or not, Father smiled faintly and said,

“If you become the vanguard commander, it will be reassuring.”

Absolute Regression Chapter 445

Chapter 445: It's All Because Father Is Picky.
----------------------------------------------
At Father’s words that I was dependable, I briefly imagined myself standing on a battlefield.

The countless fights before my regression, and the fights after I returned as well. Even if I added them all together, they were only fights, not war.

Once war began, battles that turned an individual life into hell would break out every day all across the Central Plains.

The cause, once as red and vivid as blood, would gradually fade, and people would die without even knowing why they had to fight.

What was more terrible was that it did not end easily. It could last months, years, even decades. War was easy to start, but hard to end.

What was gained through so many sacrifices—was it truly peace? Or was it greater conflict, wounds, and a thirst for revenge?

What if I faithfully served as the vanguard of that war?

Jin Hagun, the leader of the Demon-Slaying Unit, would die by my sword.

I could see Jin Haryeong wailing before his corpse. She would spit curses full of hatred at me and launch herself forward, thrusting her sword to kill me. In the end, she too would die by my hand.

Bisain would be no different. What could I say as I looked down at him while he died? Where would I throw away all the words I had once spoken to him?

This was the war for the unification of murim—a thing that made my chest feel tight just by imagining it. From this imagination alone, I could tell how I would have to live for the next five years.

‘Father, the war will not happen. And that is why I am so curious about what your new dream will be.’

I almost responded so honestly, but instead I acted as I usually would.

I put on an exaggeratedly pained expression and whined.

“This is too much! You conspired with the Demon Venerables to tie me down from the very beginning, didn’t you? Elder Doma laid the groundwork, Master and Demon Buddha gently wiggled the bait, and today Father yanked the hook all at once. Right? This was an operation planned by Tongcheon Pavilion, wasn’t it?”

At my joke, Father looked toward the entrance of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“Ask the strategist himself.”

Just then, Chief Strategist Sama Myeong was entering the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The fact that he had appeared here told me one thing. There had been another reason Father had gathered all the Demon Venerables today.

Of course. Father was not the sort of person to summon every Demon Venerable simply because he wanted to see them.

After paying his respects to Father, Sama Myeong greeted the Demon Venerables courteously, and the Demon Venerables also cupped their fists in return. Finally, Sama Myeong greeted me.

“Young Cult Leader, I am relieved you returned safely from the North Sea.”

“My greetings are late. I was rather busy after falling into the trap our strategist prepared.”

Though my words came out of nowhere, Sama Myeong smiled instead. He had guessed what I meant.

“The name of the trap I fell into is the Unification of Murim. Without even realizing I had fallen into it, I declared with my own mouth that I would become the vanguard.”

Since he knew well that I had been meeting the Demon Venerables one by one, he immediately understood what kind of promise this was.

Sama Myeong pinpointed the core of this promise.

“Of course, you must have set a time limit on becoming the vanguard. How long did you set?”

“I set it at five years.”

“Could the name of this trap perhaps be Five Years? From where I stand, it seems the Cult Leader has fallen into it.”

He had seen through the fact that I had bought five years of time. Just as Father had accepted the promise while knowing, Sama Myeong had already guessed my intention.

Indeed, the moment his words ended, I saw a faint smile appear at the corners of Father’s mouth. He was pleased that Sama Myeong was reading the situation accurately.

Sama Myeong had also realized that there was a clever move hidden in the five-year period.

“If it had been seven or ten years, I believe it would have been a trap he would not have fallen for. Five years is an exquisitely chosen length of time—one impossible not to fall for.”

The Demon Venerables must have understood what Sama Myeong meant as well.

Immediately, Chwima’s sound transmission flew to me.

—So this was all your plan. Not knowing that, I was worried you had made an unreasonable promise.

—It is an unreasonable promise. I’ll have to work myself to the bone for the next five years. You have to help too, hyung.

—Please don’t drag me into matters involving the Cult Leader!

In the meantime, Sama Myeong revealed to everyone the reason he had appeared here today.

“The reason I wished to meet the Demon Venerables today was to report in person on what Tongcheon Pavilion has investigated. After learning of the existence of the forces behind the scenes, our pavilion independently began tracking them. What we focused on was their source of funding. To mobilize experts and an organization of this level would require an enormous amount of money. Through discreet investigation, we found a clue that may allow us to trace those behind the scenes.”

He had come here to convey this and request the Demon Venerables’ help.

“Considering the strength they have shown thus far, I believe the Demon Venerables will need to step forward and deal with them personally.”

Sama Myeong must already have decided in his mind whom to entrust this matter to.

Before he could designate a Demon Venerable and make the request, I stepped forward first.

“Before that, may I say something?”

“Please do.”

Sama Myeong waited for my answer, but the person I wished to speak to was Father.

“Father, I have a request to make.”

“What is it?”

“Let you and I resolve this matter.”

Because they had never imagined I would say such a thing, not only Father but Sama Myeong and the Demon Venerables were all surprised. Their expressions seemed to say they had heard wrong. No, Sama Myeong looked as though he hoped he had heard wrong.

“Let us go out together. Father and I. Just the two of us.”

I had spoken several times about going to the Central Plains with Father, but we had never once put it into action. Now that an opportunity had come, I intended to spend time with him.

“Five years will pass in no time, won’t it?”

Those were the words Father had said to me earlier. Given the nature of this promise, in which I had to change Father’s mind, fairness required that I be given an opportunity as well.

The Demon Venerables watched with curious expressions, wondering what answer Father would give.

“Let’s go together, take care of it quickly, and come back.”

Before Father could make a decision, Sama Myeong stepped in.

“That cannot be allowed.”

Since he was not the sort to cut into my conversation with Father in this way, especially not like this, I could tell there was firm resolve in his refusal.

“Do you understand what you have just asked, Young Cult Leader?”

His tone was gentle, but his eyes were extremely serious.

“I do.”

Sama Myeong shook his head.

“No. It seems you do not. If you did, you could not have made such a request.”

“Why can it not be allowed?”

Amid the taut tension, I could feel everyone growing nervous. The contest between Father and me had now turned into a contest between Sama Myeong and me. The curtain had not yet fallen on today’s stage.

“By the cult’s rules, the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader do not move together except under special circumstances. You are even supposed to eat separately.”

It was to prepare against ambush or poisoning. If the two of us were together and died, there would be no Cult Leader.

“Surely you are not stopping this because you are worried Father may be in danger? Your concern is misplaced. You should not worry about Father, but about murim.”

At that, Sama Myeong answered as if he had been waiting.

“Correct. That is precisely the reason. If the Cult Leader moves, murim itself becomes endangered.”

That was what Sama Myeong was worried about.

“When the Cult Leader moves, murim becomes tense and rigid. The sense of crisis rises to the extreme, and a small matter may spread into a great one.”

I persuaded Sama Myeong in a pleasant tone.

“We can go secretly. We can say Father has entered seclusion for a while.”

“If it is discovered, murim will be even more shocked.”

The Heavenly Demon’s journey through the martial world was a weighty act that carried such immense responsibility.

Sama Myeong added a frightening statement.

“If matters go awry, the moment when the Young Cult Leader must stand at the vanguard may come not five years from now, but five days from now.”

I understood very well what Sama Myeong was worried about, but still.

“That does not mean Father must remain in the Heavenly Demon Hall for the rest of his life.”

At those words, even Sama Myeong could offer no rebuttal. Of everyone in our cult, he was likely the one who best understood Father’s suffocation.

My gaze turned to Father.

“Father, I respect the dream you have. But that dream is one directed toward the world, so I believe you must step out into the world to dream it.”

Father looked at me in silence.

I spoke with my eyes.

Father, it is time for you to go out with me now.

Father would know what feelings made me do this.

Because this was a matter between Father and me, the Demon Venerables did not step in. They simply watched us, each with their own gaze.

Sama Myeong let out a light sigh and said,

“I misunderstood the name of the trap. It was not Five Years, but Leaving the Cult.”

It was a joke that the one who had fallen into the trap was himself.

At last, Father spoke.

“We will deliberate on this matter and discuss it again later. Then today’s meeting ends here.”

Father had surely already made his decision. But he did not say it here, likely for the sake of preserving the dignity of the side that would not be chosen.

Thus, the Demon Venerables and I offered our respectful farewells and left the Heavenly Demon Hall.

At the entrance, Blood Heaven Blade Demon said to me,

“You truly never tire, do you?”

He meant that after making a five-year promise with Father, I was now creating another matter by raising the issue of leaving the cult with the Heavenly Demon.

“Five years will pass in no time, won’t it? I have to move diligently while I have the chance.”

At that, Chwima said what he had been holding back.

“No matter how I think about it, I don’t believe you can change the Cult Leader’s mind in just five years.”

It seemed Demon Buddha was of the same opinion on that point.

“Did I not say that this place today was a stage created by the Cult Leader?”

Light began to shine from his body again.

I looked at Fist Demon. He said nothing, but his eyes seemed to say this:

Did you not say the Cult Leader was at war with his son?

He would likely think that war had truly begun today.

Behind Fist Demon’s massive frame, I saw the Poison King. If all the Demon Venerables here went to war, he would be the one to protect the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. The one Father disliked, yet trusted most.

He was also the Demon Venerable who had grasped my intentions first. When I had whined that I had been had, he was the one who had sent a sound transmission saying that the one who had been had was Father.

“This time, make sure you dig it up. Don’t use the Cult Leader as an excuse.”

He was already certain that I would be able to go out with Father.

Standing beside him, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable respectfully bowed his head to me. It was an expression of apology for changing his mind and choosing Father. I answered with a smile. You are Father’s Demon Venerable, and also mine.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable gave me a word of encouragement.

“I believe that if it is you, Young Cult Leader, you will find a way.”

Apart from his support for Father, I could feel that those words too were sincere.

The Extreme Evil Small Demon was silently looking up at the sky. What dream was he dreaming right now?

I let out a sigh and lamented.

“I spoke a lot today and made some bold declarations, but it was truly difficult. As you know, who is my opponent? It is Father. I must change Father’s long-cherished wish. Would striking the Heavenly Demon Sword with an iron sword leave even a scratch? From now on, all of you must help me. Where are Father’s Demon Venerables and my Demon Venerables? You are our Demon Venerables. Now, in that spirit, since we have gathered after so long, why don’t we all share a drink togeth—huh? Where is everyone going? Elder! Master! Hyung!”

The Demon Venerables were already scattering in the eight directions where their residences lay.

“No, I said I’m exhausted! You should at least comfort me a little and help me devise a strategy. This is really too much!”

They all disappeared without even looking back.

Only, from far away, Blood Heaven Blade Demon sent me a sound transmission.

—There is someone else you should be drinking with right now, isn’t there?

* * *

“Young Cult Leader.”

Sama Myeong, who had been working in his office, wore an expression as if he had expected me to come.

“It is just about the time you would be hungry, isn’t it? As expected, you are working late.”

I had bought alcohol and dishes from the Pungryu Tavern and brought them in time for dinner. I had bought plenty, so he could share them with his subordinate strategists.

“I still have work left, so I will decline the alcohol. But I will enjoy the food with my subordinates.”

Seeing the pile of work, I was reminded of the work stacked up in Gowol’s room. Truly, the life of a strategist was a weary one.

“You must already know why I’ve come. What should I do?”

Sima Ming said nothing for a moment.

I understood him. No, I was grateful to him. Because he had been protecting Father like this.

“If you are truly worried, I will take the Demon Venerables with me.”

At that, Sima Ming asked with a peculiar smile.

“Which Demon Venerables?”

“First, they must not stand out, and Father must be comfortable with them.”

On the point that Father had to be comfortable, the Poison King and the Soul Seizer were the first to be eliminated.

I wanted to go with the Extreme Evil Young Demon, but his white mask was far too conspicuous. The same went for the Demon Buddha in that regard.

I had recently gone out of the cult and returned with the Drunken Demon. Even if that had not been the case, if I asked him to come along with Father, he would be the type to throw himself into the lake in front of the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

In truth, the person Father would be most comfortable with was the Fist Demon, but his build and appearance would draw attention wherever he went. Even if he hid his face, how could he hide those massive fists?

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, too, would attract every eye around him simply by wearing that huge Heaven-Destroying Great Saber. Ilhwa Sword Venerable would be the most unobtrusive, but these days she was racing toward the pinnacle of swordsmanship, so she would not wish to leave the cult.

“……There is no one to go with us. Even though there are eight of them.”

As if he had a rough idea of what I had been thinking, Sima Ming looked at me and laughed.

“This is all because my father is so particular. You have truly gone through much hardship attending to him.”

“You need not exert yourself so much.”

Then an astonishing fact flowed from his lips.

“The Cult Leader has already made his decision. He will go out with the Young Cult Leader.”

“Did he tell you that, Strategist?”

“Not yet.”

The reason he was certain nonetheless must have been because he had been with Father for so long.

“You will likely receive word within a few days, so understand it as such and rest well in the meantime. You returned from the North Sea and had to meet the Demon Venerables, so you have not even had a chance to recover from your travels, have you?”

“Thank you, Strategist.”

Once Father moved, everything would be handled as confidential. Our departure, and our return as well.

“Are you pleased that you will be going out with the Cult Leader?”

“My heart is already pounding.”

“I could have opposed it to the very end, but do you know why I changed my mind?”

“Why?”

“I liked what you said. That you would show the Cult Leader a different scenery.”

How could he not feel sorry for Father, who lived trapped within the name of Heavenly Demon? That must have been why my words moved his heart.

“Go and enjoy that scenery with the Cult Leader. I will guard the house well.”

He showed no worry whatsoever about the force behind the enemy we had to face. Well, with Father going out in person, what enemy was there to worry about?

“Because you are here, Strategist, I was able to act like a thoughtless child and pester him. Thank you, truly.”

I turned to leave like that, then suddenly asked him.

“Then what about your scenery, Strategist?”

Though the question came out of nowhere, he immediately understood what I meant.

“I quite like the scenery I see from this desk. So you need not worry about me.”

I answered that devoted heart with a clear and deep gaze, then left his office.

Hoping that someday, when he wished to find a scenery of his own, it would not be too late.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 446

Chapter 446: I’ll Help You Pack Your Bags
-----------------------------------------
When I returned to my quarters after meeting Sima Ming, someone was waiting for me in the courtyard.

The one who had planted a great blade in the courtyard and sat leaning against it, drinking, was none other than the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

I had suddenly thought on my way back that he might come. It seemed we had reached the point where such hunches came true.

“Have a drink.”

“Gladly.”

I accepted the drink from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and drained it in one cool gulp.

“It’s wonderful to drink with you, Elder, after so long.”

“Didn’t you drink your fill with that drunkard?”

“I could barely drink. He was abstaining until he cut down the masterminds behind it all.”

“Is that so?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon wore an expression of surprise.

“The incomprehensible world of a drunkard, isn’t it?”

At my jest, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon smiled faintly and drank.

After happily pouring for each other and sharing a few drinks, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon finally spoke what was on his mind.

“The Cult Leader will grant your request.”

“Why do you think so?”

Looking down at his cup, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered.

“Because if I were the Cult Leader, I would grant it.”

Was that the only reason? The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had a unique intuition for my father. He had also been the first to notice that Father had moved to unify the martial world this time.

“I just met with the Strategist. As you said, Elder, it seems Father will grant permission.”

Even though his hunch was correct, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon neither rejoiced nor acted smug. He seemed both pleased and worried about this departure from the cult.

“Are you happy to go out with the Cult Leader?”

“Yes, I am.”

Just thinking about leaving the cult with Father lifted my spirits. But I must not forget.

No matter that I was with Father, I must not forget our enemy was Hua Wuqi.

After sealing the Heavenly Demon Cult, the Martial Alliance, and the Heretical Path Alliance, Hua Wuqi had walked toward the pinnacle of martial arts through endless training.

Even though the Twelve Zodiac Kings who followed him committed all manner of outrages, he never officially appeared in the martial world.

In other words, he was not one to harbor worldly desires.

Why on earth had he killed the three leaders of the martial world? What kind of life had he led before, and what kind of life did he lead after?

I had been so focused on gathering materials for the grand ritual that I failed to learn more about him, which I now deeply regretted.

At the time, I had been afraid that approaching him unnecessarily would ruin everything. Instead, I tried to distance myself from him. I thought I could set everything right as long as I regressed.

Anyway, the certain fact was that he was by no means an ordinary person.

Therefore, he might not appear until all the Twelve Zodiac Kings were dead, or he might suddenly pop up out of nowhere.

Perhaps it was because of such a Hua Wuqi that my way of life had been decided as it was now.

Without minding him, I did my best to live my life.

Even if I had wanted to respond by matching him, I could not.

So, Hua Wuqi, you match yourself to my life!

Just as the me from before regression did, it would not be easy for you to respond by matching me either. Because I, too, was someone who defied common sense and expectations.

While I was lost in thought, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon abruptly said something unexpected.

“A Cult Leader with a son like you is truly blessed.”

It was the first time he had said such a thing since forming a bond with me. I had been about to joke that of course, Father would surely rank high on the list of fathers blessed with good fortune, but I asked seriously instead. Because it was the first time the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had ever said something like this.

“Do you truly think so?”

“Throughout the main cult, how many people would be this happy to go out with the Cult Leader?”

“Well, the same would hold true across the entire martial world.”

“That said, don’t act too cheeky toward the Cult Leader when you are out.”

“Are you worried about me?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head, then nodded, then shook his head again.

“You do everything better than I do, so one would think who is worrying about whom, but...”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at me.

“Seen this way, you are just a greenhorn, are you not?”

How could I not know his heart? To the elderly, the reason the young seemed so uneasy must be because of this experience. Because at that age, one was sure to make nonsensical mistakes.

That was why he worried. That I might make some mistake while out with Father. He worried that the reason I had not made a mistake until now was because I had been saving them all up for this moment.

“I am not a child.”

“In my eyes, you are a child.”

“And yet you wish you could return to my age.”

I often sensed that he longed for his youth. Though he always denied it.

“I wouldn’t go even if I could.”

“Why?”

“Because I do not want to live this life again.”

His longing for youth was sincere, but these words also rang true.

“You could live differently.”

“A person is the same; can they live differently?”

“You will. Because you have lived this life well.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at me silently, then emptied his last cup and rose. Pulling out the Heaven-Annihilating Great Blade planted in the ground, he bade farewell.

“Go and return safely.”

“Yes, Elder.”

I called out to him as he turned and walked away.

“This time, I will go out with Father and return having seen many things. So please, Elder, live on watching many of the sights you wish to see as well.”

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned back and said resolutely,

“I already live that way.”

In his gaze, I felt it. That I stood within that scenery.

* * *

After the Blood Heaven Blade Demon left, I entered my room and immediately unleashed the Space-Time Shift Technique.

Even for someone like me, the twelve-star grand perfection of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art could not be achieved in just five years. It required absolute time dedicated to martial training.

Yet the reason I had promised five years was because I believed in the Heavenly Time Secret Art.

The Heavenly Time Secret Art, which had previously granted three times as much time, had now increased to four times as much through steady training while cutting back on sleep. Meaning, while others trained for one shichen, I could train for four.

Moreover, my internal energy had grown greatly during my trip to the Northern Sea, making training even smoother.

I had to somehow increase the time gained from the Heavenly Time Secret Art to tenfold. But the higher the realm, the more difficult the training became and the slower the progress. Only effort was the path to survival.

I performed the Nine Transformations Demonic Art once, from the First Form, Annihilation Form, to the Fourth Form, Dark Flash.

Then I circulated my internal cultivation method to replenish my internal energy and composed my mind.

Now, for the first time, I would attempt the Fifth Form of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

If I went out with Father, there would surely come an opportunity to learn the essence of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art from him. I was preparing to leave having mastered up to the Fifth Form for that very moment.

The Fifth Form of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike.

The Fifth Form was dangerous from the training stage alone. It was a technique known to inflict fatal wounds or kill the practitioner if performed even slightly incorrectly. Therefore, I focused my mind more intensely than when performing any other technique.

I had to perform it with the mindset that a single mistake meant death.

I chanted the formula of the Fifth Form and slowly drew my sword.

At that moment,

the surroundings changed. The sky and the area around me darkened and grew dreary, as if a typhoon were about to strike.

A silence followed.

It was no ordinary silence. It was an absolute silence that swallowed all noise in the world. Even if one stood in the middle of a clamorous battlefield, all sounds would vanish.

The moment I swung my sword!

Swishswishswishswishswishswishswishswishswishswish!

Ten streaks of sword qi poured down vertically from the sky.

Thudthudthudthudthudthudthudthudthudthud!

Sword qi striking down around me!

The sword qi created thin yet deep holes in the ground. They were so deep the bottoms could not be seen.

It was truly an attack that struck down like lightning upon the crown of an enemy’s head.

Because it was my first time performing it, the sword qi did not land where I wanted. Two streaks even fell right beside me. I had nearly struck myself.

The crown of the head—the most difficult vital point on the human body to defend.

Even protective astral qi is hardest to maintain at the crown of the head. Imagine sword qi striking down there with lightning speed.

Moreover, this was no ordinary sword qi. The sword qi imbued with the essence of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art was a powerful attack that tore through the opponent’s protective astral qi and vertically split their head in two. Of course, the internal energy consumption was equally immense, making it a technique that could not be used carelessly.

Finally, the essence of the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike was that it was a technique for facing multiple enemies. Right now there were ten streaks, but as the realm of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art rose, the power of the descending sword qi would grow stronger and their numbers would gradually increase.

Imagine it.

Dozens of streaks of sword qi descending simultaneously like heavenly punishment, piercing through the heads of dozens of enemies.

Just how many enemies could Father strike with the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike at once?

Twenty? Fifty? A hundred?

I could not know what the Nine Transformations Demonic Art looked like at grand perfection, as I had never experienced it.

Once more, the second Soul-Severing Demonic Strike was performed.

Swishswishswishswishswishswishswishswishswishswish!

Again, sword qi lightning poured down vertically.

Thudthudthudthudthudthudthudthudthudthud!

The sword qi still deviated from my will. Overall, they landed a bit farther from me, but one streak fell even closer than before. It was truly a perilous technique.

I took a deep breath and composed my mind.

They will never fall on me. Believe in myself!

A deadly technique that could never be mastered without this belief.

I performed the Fifth Form again and again.

The sword qi still deviated from my will and threatened my life, but it was fine. Repeated training would keep me safer than any protective astral qi.

Because my nights were long.

* * *

Three days later, Sima Ming came to my quarters.

“Strategist, welcome!”

“Have you rested well during this time?”

“I’ve rested quite well.”

In truth, this was the response I ought to have given.

For a martial artist, is martial arts training not a form of rest?

Because up until just moments ago, I had been immersed in training within the Space-Time Shift Technique.

For the past three days, I had refined the Soul-Severing Demonic Strike, and now I could finally strike down more or less where I wanted. My accuracy was still lacking, but I had at least escaped the stage where my heart pounded with anxiety.

The reason Sima Ming had come was to deliver news.

“The Cult Leader has formally granted permission.”

As expected, Sima Ming’s prediction had been accurate.

“Thank you! It is all thanks to your efforts, Strategist.”

If he had opposed it vehemently, I would not have been able to go out this time. Even if Father wished it, he could not easily ignore strong opposition from the Chief Strategist. It was certainly thanks to Sima Ming’s compromise.

“During your departure, the Cult Leader will be officially recorded as being in closed-door training. We also deliberated on whether to inform the Martial Alliance and the Heretical Path Alliance, but concluded it best not to, considering the risk of information leaking.”

For someone who put Father’s safety above all else, it was a natural decision.

His caution added one more measure.

“And the Heavenly Demon Guard will secretly follow behind.”

They were led by Hwi. The greatest guards in the martial world, who always accompanied Father wherever he went.

I truly wanted to leave the cult with Father alone just this once, but I could not insist on that now. Since Sima Ming had compromised, I too had to compromise. Instead, I proposed a middle ground.

“Then let us have only Uncle Hwi follow us.”

I could guess why Sima Ming wanted to send them.

Protecting Father was important, but more than that, it was to maintain periodic contact with the main cult. They needed to know when, where, and how he was moving so that the main cult could prepare for any contingencies.

But if that was the case, Hwi alone was more than capable of handling it.

“Anyway, the guards will follow secretly from a distance, so you need not concern yourself with them.”

“That is not why. If those bastards are watching us, they will pay close attention to Father’s closed-door training. It is not yet the right time for such training. If they suspect it may be a ruse, they will undoubtedly observe the Heavenly Demon Guard. If they notice the Guard’s absence, they will realize that the Cult Leader has left the cult.”

Of course, I did not believe their spies had infiltrated as far as the Heavenly Demon Hall. I was simply trying to depart with as few people as possible.

I had expected to need more persuasion, but Sima Ming readily agreed.

“Very well. Let us have only Captain Hwi go.”

I put on a deliberately startled expression.

“Strategist, you intended to send only Uncle Hwi from the very beginning.”

“If I had said only Captain Hwi was going, the Young Cult Leader would have found some other reason to go out with just the Cult Leader, would you not?”

“Ah! You have read me completely, Strategist.”

Sima Ming smiled at my sigh and said,

“The destination is Shaanxi.”

Since Shaanxi was quite a distance from here, I would be able to spend ample time with Father.

“It is not an urgent matter, so you may travel slowly.”

He did not speak of the mission, as if implying I would learn of it upon arrival.

It was his consideration. Meaning, do not think about the mission until you arrive, and spend this time with Father.

“How do you wish to travel?”

“By carriage at times, walking at others. I wish to go as my heart leads me at each moment.”

“I shall prepare it so.”

Finally, he informed me of the time for this historic first departure.

“We depart tomorrow. You will leave mingled among the transport carriages heading out at dawn.”

* * *

After meeting with Sima Ming, I went to Father’s quarters.

“Father, I’m here.”

Then Father’s voice came from inside.

“What is it?”

“I came because I have something to tell you.”

A moment later, Father opened the door and came outside. He somehow seemed slightly flustered, unlike his usual self.

“Is this not the first time you have left the cult for such an extended period since becoming the Cult Leader? Let me help you pack. Am I not an expert at traveling?”

“Enough.”

Father refused with a sullen expression and turned his back. I felt he was treating me differently today than usual, but then through the gap of the closing door, I saw the inside of the room.

Clothes were spread out on the bed, and a large leather bag was visible too. Various other items were scattered about.

Father was packing!

I had thought he sent Sima Ming to my quarters instead of summoning me to the Heavenly Demon Hall to proceed in secret, but it turned out he had been busy preparing for the trip.

Father was looking forward to this journey as well.

I smiled brightly and said loudly from outside the door,

“Father, don’t forget to pack your sleepwear too!”

Absolute Regression, Episode 447.

Chapter 447: Sights Seen for the First Time.
--------------------------------------------
At dawn, the carriages left the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

They were carriages laden with supplies being sent from headquarters to the various branches.

Among those carriages was one carrying Father and me. The one driving it, dressed like an ordinary coachman, was Hwi.

Once we had put some distance between ourselves and headquarters, I asked Father,

“Father, how does it feel, your first trip with your son?”

At my excited question, Father, who had been silently looking out the window, asked in return,

“This is not a trip, is it?”

For that to be true, Father, you packed far too much. In the rear luggage space sat Father’s large leather bag.

“Of course, we’re going to carry out an important mission. Still, since it’s been so long since you left the cult, wouldn’t it be good to unburden everything that’s built up in your heart before we return? Leave all your worries back at headquarters.”

At that, Father, still looking out the window, said to Hwi,

“Stop the carriage.”

The carriage halted at once.

“What’s the matter?”

“Get off.”

“Me? Why me?”

“You told me to leave my worries behind, did you not?”

I burst out laughing. The fact that he was joking with me like this meant Father was in a very good mood.

“Isn’t it true that one needs worries in order to improve? Now, let’s go. Depart!”

When Father said “Let us go” again, the carriage set off.

I stuck my head out the window and said to Hwi,

“I look forward to being in your care, Uncle Hwi!”

Hwi did not drive the carriage in haste. Perhaps Sama Myeong had asked him.

That he hoped this departure from the cult would become a journey, not a mission.

Leaning out the window, I let the wind blow against me.

“The weather is truly wonderful!”

Father gave no answer, but I could feel it. Father was excited too.

“Isn’t it nice just to get a little ways away from the cult, Father? So even if it’s not this trip, let’s come out often from now on. Ah, if you cross that hill over there, there’s a beautiful lake. Have you been there? In winter, the lake freezes solid, and the pleasure of breaking the ice to fish is unmatched.”

Father already looked as if his ears hurt, but this would continue from here on out. If I wanted to make the journey with Father enjoyable, I would have to be the one chattering most of the time.

“Oh, and if you go over the mountain on the other side, there’s a tent that makes incredible noodles. Would you like to try it sometime?”

At that, an unexpected answer came from Father.

“In the old days, their dumplings were good.”

I pulled my head back inside the carriage.

“You’ve been there?”

“Long ago.”

Right. Father, too, had a time when he wandered the murim like I did. Was it when he went around fighting alongside the Fist Demon? Or when he was close to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable?

“Shall we stop by on the way back, then?”

Perhaps Father wanted to go there once more, for he nodded.

“People’s mouths are truly frightening, aren’t they? They know delicious things so well. Even when a place does business in such an out-of-the-way location, rumors spread like ghosts and people seek it out.”

“Let us go quietly.”

“Yes!”

Father, you don’t know this, do you? I lived as taciturnly as you. No, perhaps I spoke even less. All the words I couldn’t say then, I’m saying to my heart’s content in front of you.

After the carriage ran on for quite some time, it came to a stop.

While Hwi unhitched the horses to let them rest, I took a walk around the area with Father.

“Before we left, Elder Blade Demon came to see me.”

“What did he say?”

“He told me not to get cheeky with you, Father.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Father’s mouth.

“The Blade Demon cares for you a great deal.”

Of course. How could Father not know? That when the Blade Demon took it upon himself to scold me during the meeting to persuade Father, it had ultimately been for my sake.

“He likes you a great deal, Father. Elder Blade Demon does.”

I wasn’t simply saying that to please him. The Blade Demon’s feelings toward Father were truly deep.

“Do not get cheeky with the Blade Demon.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon told me not to get cheeky with Father, and Father told me not to get cheeky with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. How could there not be something special between the two of them?

I wanted to joke, Then who am I supposed to get cheeky with? But I couldn’t get cheeky after being told not to.

“I understand. I’ll always be careful.”

Just the fact that I was walking with Father and having this sort of conversation made me happy. Everything that happened from now on would be something neither Father nor I had experienced before.

As long as the words “leaving the cult together with Father” came first, anything we did would be a first.

Just then, Father suddenly asked,

“Do you feel it?”

“Wolves, right?”

When I answered immediately, Father looked at me in surprise.

First of all, he had not even said where the presence was coming from, yet I had answered. He was surprised that I had already sensed a presence more than twenty jang away.

“In unfamiliar places, I now naturally send out my qi and examine my surroundings.”

Around my body, countless strands of energy stretched out like a spiderweb.

“It’s thanks to what you taught me, Father.”

It was a level of skill on an entirely different dimension from the time I had gone hunting with Father and learned how to emit thin, long strands of qi.

“So you have not only been playing around.”

“They say martial training and love are both things one does where others cannot see.”

As much as his expression seemed to say he was dumbfounded, Father’s smile deepened.

After walking around like that, we returned to where the carriage was.

Just then, from afar beside the carriage, Hwi waved and called out,

“The meal is ready.”

Hwi had already lit a campfire and finished preparing the meal.

“Doesn’t Uncle Hwi seem somewhat excited too?”

Father did not say no. In Father’s eyes as well, there must have been an unmistakable liveliness different from usual.

I want Father to see many new sights.

Because Hwi waving at us like that must be a sight he has never seen before.

The meal Hwi prepared was something we had packed and brought when we departed.

“From here on, the meals will be poor.”

“That is fine.”

I cut in with a word.

“Don’t worry, Uncle. Father is here, isn’t he? I can’t forget the taste of the food Father cooked for me.”

The second time I went hunting with Father, he cooked for me himself.

That dish, stir-fried meat with vegetables and mushrooms, had been unexpectedly delicious. He even cooked soup with it, proving that he usually cooked.

Come to think of it, there are many sides of Father I do not know. His flower-patterned sleeping clothes, the way he packed his own things for this trip yesterday, and the fact that he cooks.

I hope I can see another side of Father on this journey.

“I prepared something too.”

From the leather bag loaded in the carriage, I took out some hides.

First, a tiger hide for Father.

“Now, please sit here.”

Father sat on it.

There were more things I had prepared. I handed one of them to Hwi.

“This one is yours, Uncle Hwi.”

Hwi looked slightly surprised that I had prepared something for him as well.

“I am all right.”

“I have one too, so please sit. Ours are wolf hides.”

Hwi’s and mine were identical hides.

As Hwi continued to decline, Father looked at him and nodded.

Only then did Hwi stop refusing and sit on the hide I had prepared.

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

“Don’t mention it.”

Through my past life, there was something I had come to deeply realize.

That a person’s seat is truly important.

Father’s grand chair.

The second-floor seat at the Pungryu Tavern.

And now, the hide seat Hwi sat on.

Because everyone lives in order to sit in their own place. Because we are the sort of beings who even start wars to claim a seat.

If one were to say, What does a seat matter? Isn’t the heart what matters? then that person must never have properly felt the awkwardness and distress of going somewhere and finding there was no place for them.

That is why those who have not experienced it would not know how much relief and gratitude a single small chair offered by someone can bring.

I had never once seen Hwi’s seat. He was always hidden in stealth. That was why that wolf hide was all the more important.

The three of us were just about to sit side by side and eat when a sudden shower began to fall.

Swaaaaaaa.

“Father, first go to the carriage and avoid the rain…”

I was about to tell him to take shelter, but stopped.

“Isn’t this too inhuman a sight?”

“What do you mean?”

“When a person is rained on, they ought to get wet.”

Around Father’s body, invisible qi had spread like a membrane, causing the raindrops to bounce away.

“No, and if you have a skill like that, you should keep us from getting wet too.”

Of course, Father was not the sort of person who would expand that qi just because I said such a thing.

Just then, Father extended his hand.

At once, the opening of Father’s leather bag inside the carriage came undone, and something emerged from it.

The thing that flew out of the carriage spread open above our heads with a snap. It was a wind-and-rain canopy cloth used to block wind and rain.

The cords attached to the cloth tied themselves to the carriage and a tree, fixing it in place. It was not being moved by simple object manipulation through empty space; its movements were as swift and precise as if a skilled person were tying them by hand.

To think Father had prepared even this!

“By nature, the flower of camping is watching the rain! It’s the height of elegance.”

When I said that cheerfully, Father even revived the campfire, which had been dying in the rain, with Scorching Yang energy.

Fwoooosh.

“Wonderful! Father!”

Father wore an expression that said, Well, this much is nothing.

If it rained during the journey, we could unfold it anytime, but in truth, this moment was truly nice. It had made the first meal on the first day of our journey unforgettable.

After we finished eating, Hwi brought over the tea he had prepared.

We watched the rain while drinking tea. Father sat in the center, and Hwi and I sat to his left and right.

“Uncle, isn’t it difficult serving Father?”

If such a question were asked in front of Father, one would expect him to be flustered.

“It is difficult.”

At the honest answer, Father and I looked at Hwi at the same time.

Hwi answered with a smile.

“I thought I should answer Young Cult Leader that way.”

He had been listening to every conversation I had with Father until now. He must also have heard all of Sama Myeong’s reports about me. He was a person who knew me as well as Father did.

“You struck at my blind spot properly. If you had answered that it wasn’t difficult, I could have turned the arrow toward Father.”

At that, Father looked over at me this time.

“If you answered that it wasn’t difficult at all because Father is so taciturn, I was going to say this to Father. That there must be parts that are difficult because he is taciturn. That it would actually be more comfortable if he simply said everything like I do, but because he does not speak, you have to discern his meaning. That for those below him, that is not an easy thing either.”

I had said it to Father on Hwi’s behalf. I did not know what sort of conversations the two of them usually had, but given their personalities, how much could they possibly share each other’s hardships?

“In the end, you say everything you wish to say.”

“I must. If I don’t say it, you won’t know, will you?”

At my words, Father looked straight ahead again.

Then he suddenly asked Hwi,

“So, was it difficult?”

This time, Hwi was truly flustered and surprised. Father was not someone who would ask such a question. If anything, he was the type who would have spoken even less in such a situation.

“No, it was not.”

The conversation did not continue any further. But I thought the mere fact that Father had asked whether it had been difficult held great meaning for Hwi.

In the meantime, the rain stopped.

* * *

That night, we camped outdoors.

If we had wanted to find an inn, we could have, but Father accepted my suggestion that we spend the first day quietly.

Lying side by side with Father while looking up at the shower of stars, I felt truly happy.

I told Hwi to lie down and sleep with us as well, but he firmly refused, saying he could not dare lie down together with the Heavenly Demon. Since there are things each person can accept and things they cannot, I did not force him further.

“Father, I’m sorry.”

“For what?”

“If I thought of you, Father, I should have asked Brother to come with us too. I’m this selfish. It seems I still want to have you all to myself.”

Father was silent for a moment.

“It is all right.”

At those words, the heaviness in my heart eased a little. Still, when I am alone with Father, I cannot help thinking of my brother.

“Father.”

“What is it?”

“I called you to see if you were asleep.”

“Then should you not have refrained from calling?”

I smiled and looked over at Father lying beside me. Father was gazing up at the night sky with an impassive expression.

I engraved the scene in my heart.

Father looking up at the night sky, me watching him, and even the stars in that sky.

And so the night of our first journey passed.

* * *

The next day, we stopped by a village.

While Hwi rented a stable to feed the horses and let them rest, Father and I stepped out for a short look around the marketplace.

We were walking together through a place bustling with people, but no one paid us any particular attention.

Both Father and I had reached the perfect realm of returning to simplicity, and Father, like my Black Demon Sword wrapped in the finest Heavenly Silkworm Thread, had wound an old cloth around the Heavenly Demon Sword to keep it from standing out.

“This is your first time visiting a marketplace outside of Maga Village, isn’t it?”

“I went often when I was young.”

“You went around to fight the Fist Demon, not to sightsee in marketplaces.”

Father nodded, acknowledging it.

“I wish I could have been Master Fist Demon back then.”

I answered the question in Father’s eyes.

“I want to see Father in his youth. I want to talk with that Father.”

“!”

I meant it sincerely. What would he have looked like? What kind of personality would he have had? Would he have resembled me?

“Perhaps you were as talkative as I am?”

After letting out a faint snort, Father said,

“I might be able to drink like the Drunken Demon. But talking as much as you do? I’m not confident.”

“A chatterbox who outdoes a drunkard? That’s too much!”

Chatting about this and that, I looked around the marketplace with Father.

When crowds came surging toward us, we stopped in place. Even this experience must have been unfamiliar to Father. He had likely never once stopped walking because someone was coming his way.

How does the scenery look when you stop like this?

People coming and going, people laughing and talking, people calling out to customers, people who were angry. All sorts of people were there.

Father watched them in silence. What thoughts was he having as he looked at them?

“Even with this many people, nothing happens, does it? The Chief Strategist was worried that if Father left the cult, all kinds of incidents would break out. That the fate of the murim wouldn’t leave Father alone. It would begin as some small incident, grow larger and larger, and spill over into another incident.”

I looked at Father and said as if declaring it.

“But nothing like that will ever happen.”

It was right then.

A man sidled up to us.

“Martial lords, how about a manly little contest?”

Where he pointed, a few people had gathered. On a makeshift table were three overturned bowls.

Ah! The Heavenly Demon and a swindler! How could I miss such a delightful opportunity?

“My father happens to be an unrivaled gambler. What do you say? At least enough for dinner……”

There was no need to provoke Father any further.

Before I could even finish speaking, Father walked toward the gamblers.

I smiled and asked the man who had called us over.

“Is that fellow’s hand fast? He’ll have to be truly fast if he wants to be quicker than my father’s eyes.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 448

Chapter 448: Why Do You Have That, Father?
------------------------------------------
They were all in it together.

The shills wedged in between the trickster gambler and the people placing bets to set the mood, and even the tout who had brought us here—they were all on the same side.

Everyone besides them were people who had already lost their money, or people who were about to.

“Now then, if you guess which bowl the die is in, you’ll receive three times your stake!”

The gambler was a small middle-aged man, but his eyes were as sharp as any martial artist’s.

Father asked me,

“Do you have money?”

“You don’t?”

Father nodded.

“I’ve had no reason to carry any.”

Well, of course. When would the Heavenly Demon ever have occasion to take out a coin pouch?

I could not let such a precious chance to tease Father pass me by.

“If you lose Uncle Hwi and me, you won’t even be able to buy yourself a meal. Then you’ll have to dine and dash, or leave that sword as collateral before you eat.”

Just imagining Father in such a state was amusing.

“If you’re done teasing me, money.”

Father held out his hand.

I took one nyang from the coin pouch inside my robes and handed it to him.

“Bet carefully! On this floor, that man is both the Sword God and the God of Gambling. That hand is faster than our eyes, I tell you.”

Of course, he could not evade Father’s eyes. I was only saying it for the swindlers to hear.

“Now then, I’ll mix them.”

The man opened each bowl one by one to show that there was nothing inside, then placed the die he was holding into one of them.

Open, close, this way, that way—his hands did not move too quickly, and that made it all the more confusing and suspicious.

At last, the man’s hands stopped.

“Now then, where will you place your bet?”

Father set one nyang down in front of the middle bowl.

Perhaps this was the first street gamble of Father’s life.

“Is there no one else? Then I’ll open it.”

When the man opened the middle bowl, the die was there.

The hand was not faster than the eye.

“You have good eyes.”

The gambler remained composed.

Was that composure because he had yet to properly display his skill? Or was it a strategy of letting Father win a little at first before eventually stripping him bare?

The man who had been touting came over as well and needlessly praised Father.

“Master martial artist, you truly have good eyes.”

Father did not even look at him. What held Father’s interest now were those three white bowls.

“Now, I’ll mix them again!”

The gambler mixed the die once more. The die darted dazzlingly between the bowls.

This time, Father chose the right.

“Is there no one else?”

Again, Father was the only one to bet.

When the bowl Father had chosen was opened, the die was there.

When he guessed correctly two times in a row, the onlookers let out low exclamations.

Father’s third choice.

This time, too, he chose the bowl with the die.

It was then that I saw it. The gambler had grown slightly flustered.

He had clearly induced his opponent to choose another side, but it had not worked.

At the same time, I saw this as well: the pleased smile resting at the corners of Father’s mouth. Father was enjoying playing around with these fellows right now.

“Father! You’ve won three times in a row.”

The people watching began to show genuine interest.

The gambler mixed the die again. This time, his movements were even more careful.

“You got it again!”

At that, those around us exclaimed in admiration and cheered. They were not shills, but true spectators.

From the tout to the shills, the signs of panic on their faces were plain. Because that gambler probably never allowed anyone to guess correctly four times in a row.

While swift glances passed among them, the die was mixed again.

On the fifth round, the other people also followed Father’s choice.

Father got it right again. Applause poured in from every direction. Now even passersby gathered to watch.

The gazes directed at Father. Until now, he had only ever received gazes filled with reverence and fear—had he ever received the expectant eyes of marketplace spectators like this? Of course not. These people would never have been able to come this close to him.

“Now, mix them again.”

Father was enjoying himself purely. If he had harbored ill will toward them, he would not have continued betting only one nyang at a time.

Or he could have made them suffer a much greater disgrace.

What if he had lost ten nyang, twenty nyang at first, over and over, and then later placed a draft worth a thousand nyang on the table? If they happily accepted it, and he guessed correctly then?

Squandering their money would not be the issue. They would try not to pay, and that would result in them losing their lives as well.

But Father did not do that. He simply kept betting one nyang at a time.

When he guessed correctly on the sixth round, the spectators cheered, and when he even got the seventh, the gambler’s expression hardened completely.

In the end, the tout came over and spoke obliquely.

“Um, how about stopping now?”

Father looked at him. Just what kind of feelings did Father hold as he looked at them?

I could not know that, but in any case, it was certain that he had not started this to torment them. As the words implied, it had simply looked amusing, and he had enjoyed it for a short while.

“Very well.”

Father handed me all the money he had won so far.

“Let’s eat dinner with this.”

“With this much, we could enjoy a banquet.”

Then the gambler spoke.

“Why not wager all the money you’ve won today and play one round?”

At that, the tout was startled and approached him, whispering something. It seemed to be telling him not to get worked up and to end it, but the gambler did not.

There was no way Father would turn away from such a situation.

“Mix them.”

Given the circumstances, would the gambler not use his trump card? A method by which he absolutely could not lose.

Three overturned bowls.

Father chose the middle. Since all the money he had won so far was at stake, the sum was fairly large.

Seeing the gambler’s supremely confident expression, it was certain the die was not in the middle.

“Now then, I’ll open it.”

The instant the man opened the middle bowl, his eyes went wide.

In the place that should have been empty, the die was there.

Even his final trick had not worked.

Not only the gambler, but also the tout and the onlookers were all shocked. They could not have imagined he would guess even this last one correctly. The spectators cheered.

“As expected! My father is the best!”

I said to the gambler,

“Now, pay up.”

For a moment, the gambler said nothing, as though his soul had left him.

“Please wait just a moment.”

The tout’s eyes rolled this way and that. The instant they thought the money was too precious to hand over, their fate would split apart.

At last, the gambler took money from the coin pouch inside his robes and handed it over.

“Here it is.”

This gambler had lost the match with money at stake, but he had won the match with his life at stake.

“How in the world did you do that?”

Father accepted the money and replied,

“Have you ever told anyone else your technique? I am the same.”

At Father’s answer, I laughed out loud. It was the first time I had seen Father say such a thing to a gambler.

But the surprise did not end there.

Father returned half of the money he had won to the gambler.

“Why are you giving half back?”

At the gambler’s question, Father answered quietly,

“There will be people whom, on occasion, you must let go by returning their money. When that time comes, return half and send them away.”

At that moment, Father’s eyes changed. They were no longer the soft eyes with which he had been playing along with swindlers.

It was a heavy gaze that a mere gambler could not bear.

The moment he saw those eyes, the gambler’s head lowered of its own accord.

“I will keep that promise without fail!”

Leaving him behind with his head deeply bowed, we resumed walking.

“Amazing.”

“Amazing, my foot. He won’t keep the promise anyway.”

Father did not believe that gambler’s promise.

“He will keep it.”

“Once or twice, perhaps. Even so, in the end, he will forget the fear of this moment.”

Father fundamentally did not believe in people. Much less would he believe a street gambler.

You are right, Father. Perhaps that may be so. But he might also keep that promise for the rest of his life. You only say that because you do not know how terrifying your eyes are.

“Then why did you give him a chance?”

After a brief pause, Father answered.

“Because I am in a good mood today.”

The moment I heard those words, I stopped walking.

Could his mood possibly be as good as mine right now?

As good as mine, when I was seeing Father look this happy for the first time? As good as mine, when I had heard him express that feeling to me?

“Since I won money, dinner is on me.”

“Why don’t we buy that dinner and eat it while riding in a carriage?”

Father looked at me, then soon nodded.

He had understood what I meant.

That gambler had acknowledged defeat obediently, but the tout might feel that money was too precious and release knife-wielders at night. On top of that, they had seen that our coin pouches were heavy.

And if we eliminated those men, then someone else behind them might come running. Then the thing the strategist had worried about could happen. Someone behind them, and someone behind that.

While traveling, we might inevitably get entangled in such things, but not now. Our journey was only on its second day.

Father, no matter how much fate tries to drag you in, it is enough if we avoid it first.

* * *

The next day, we were driving the carriage toward Shaanxi.

Father had quietly closed his eyes, and I was looking at the scenery outside.

As I gazed at the wildflowers blooming along the river, I suddenly asked Father,

“Father, are you good at fishing?”

Father nodded with his eyes closed. His expression was confident, as if to say, Is there anything I cannot do?
