“How about fish for dinner tonight? We’ll make a wager on who catches the bigger one, and the loser has to do everything from cleaning the fish to cooking it. Ah, and cleaning up afterward too. If you’re not confident, you can forfeit.”

With his eyes still closed, Father said to Hwi,

“Pull the carriage up by the river.”

Father had left things like hesitation back at headquarters.

Hwi brought the carriage to the riverside.

“Father, how big was the largest fish you’ve ever caught?”

At that, an astonishing fish was mentioned from Father’s mouth.

“Jinlin Xingyu.”

It was a spirit fish handed down in legend. It was probably larger than a person.

“That actually exists?”

“I caught it and even consumed its inner core.”

“Ah! I can’t beat Jinlin Xingyu.”

In terms of size, I would lose, but in terms of rarity, I would win.

The Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

The final overlord of all spirit fish.

Of course, I could not catch it now. The Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp could only be obtained later, at the place and time where I had once gained it.

“I happen to know a thing or two about fishing.”

I was not joking. I had truly roamed through the water in search of that damned Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp until my body was practically waterlogged.

“Now then, let’s make the fishing rods first.”

I circled the area with lightness skill and found bamboo. Among them, I cut down the finest-looking stalk. I cut two, including one for Father.

“Which bamboo will you choose?”

“You choose first.”

“How generous of you. I won’t go easy on you.”

But contrary to my words, I chose the less suitable of the two bamboos. No matter what, I could not choose the one better than Father’s.

I took out a dagger and shaved the bamboo to make a fishing rod.

“You must not blame the equipment later.”

“You are the one who should not.”

“Uncle Hwi, you heard that, right? You have to judge fairly. You can’t let Father stretch out the fish he catches later!”

At my words, Hwi laughed. If the fish Father caught were truly smaller than mine, he was the kind of man who would try to stretch it with martial arts.

“But Father, aren’t you going to make one?”

Father only watched me make the fishing rod.

“You aren’t planning to catch fish with martial arts, are you? That’s not allowed. We’re catching them fair and square with fishing rods.”

“I know.”

“Then why aren’t you making one? No matter how you look at me, I won’t make it for you. You have to make it with your own hands. Are you planning to blame the fishing rod later?”

Even so, Father did not budge.

In the end, I raised both hands in surrender.

“I lose. I lose, all right.”

I set down what I had been making and started on Father’s fishing rod first. Of course, I made it with even more care and skill than mine.

Father simply watched me in silence.

Once the rod was complete, I took fishing line and hooks from my luggage and finished the work.

And so, two fishing rods were made.

“Now, here is your weapon, Father. Choose first.”

When I solemnly held out the two fishing rods,

“I don’t need one.”

Father stretched a hand toward the carriage.

What an ominous gesture!

Then the large leather bag in the carriage came undone, and something slipped out from inside and flew toward us.

It was a bamboo pole about the length of an adult’s forearm.

Whoooosh.

As Father manipulated the bamboo, it lengthened like the Ruyi Jingu Bang.

Astonishingly, it was a fishing rod. It looked both flexible and sturdy, and at a glance it was clearly no ordinary object.

“What is that?”

Then came an astonishing name.

“It is the signature weapon of the Eastern Sea Fisherman.”

The Eastern Sea Fisherman was a martial arts master who had roamed the Central Plains with a single fishing rod. And his fishing rod was known as the perfect fishing rod. There was even a saying that if one could catch a blue whale with a fishing rod, it would have to be the Eastern Sea Fisherman’s rod.

“Why do you have it, Father?”

“Long ago, he lost to me in a martial duel, offered up his fishing rod, and left.”

“You were the one who made him retire?”

“It was his own pride that made him retire.”

I was deeply moved. Not because Father had defeated the Eastern Sea Fisherman in his youth. Not because I was seeing that perfect fishing rod with my own eyes.

I was moved by the fact that Father had brought it with him.

I imagined Father late at night, packing that fishing rod he had brought from the Heavenly Demon Treasury into his leather bag.

As he packed it, he must have thought that he might end up fishing with me.

“No, if you had a fishing rod, you should have said so. You just had to tease your son like this.”

Perhaps he had simply enjoyed watching his son make him a fishing rod.

“And the Eastern Sea Fisherman’s rod? This is cheating!”

“Who was it who said we shouldn’t blame our equipment?”

Father and I stood side by side on the riverbank.

Our fishing contest to catch the big one had begun.

“Now then, shall we begin?”

“You’ll stink of fish today, so sleep far away from me.”

“That is what I should be saying. I’ll endure the smell coming from you, Father, and sleep. I will prove that a true artisan does not blame his tools.”

I mean it. Even with that rod, it won’t be easy for you.

Our fishing lines flew over the river, sparkling in the sunlight.

The curves drawn by the fishing lines were more beautiful than any line created by martial arts. When the bait entered the river, gentle ripples spread out, then soon grew still.

The soft sound of water could be heard. The flapping of waterbirds came riding on the river breeze.

The surroundings were endlessly quiet and peaceful.

It was my first time fishing with Father.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 449

Chapter 449: One Boast Instead of Saying You Worked Hard.
---------------------------------------------------------
Father began preparing the fish.

With practiced hands, he removed the fish’s innards and scraped off its scales. Hwi watched from a short distance away, fidgeting anxiously.

“I’ll do it, Cult Leader.”

“It’s all right.”

At a time like this, I couldn’t not put in a word.

“Even your loyalty in trying to stretch the fish longer can’t do anything about my fishing skills, Uncle.”

I had won the contest against Father to catch the biggest fish.

“It was the natural result. Just because Father is holding a wooden sword doesn’t mean he can’t kill an opponent holding a treasured blade, does it?”

Father glanced at Hwi.

Can’t you do something about that mouth of his? At the look on his face, Hwi smiled awkwardly.

Father was serious about cooking.

As if to prove it, he reached out toward the carriage again.

“What is it this time?”

Then the leather pouch opened, and something else flew out from within. They were several small bottles. Amazingly, they were bottles filled with seasonings.

I was moved all over again. Father had even prepared seasonings in anticipation of cooking on this journey.

The dish Father made that way was truly excellent.

“It’s delicious. Truly, to my palate, it tastes better than anything the cooks make.”

A proud satisfaction could be felt in Father’s expression. Somehow, he looked as though praise for his cooking pleased him even more than praise for his martial arts.

That night, we camped outdoors.

We lit a bonfire and lay down, and of course, this little prank couldn’t be left out.

Unlike the first night, I spread out my bedding far away from Father.

Then Father glanced at me.

“Didn’t you say you’d endure the fishy smell and sleep?”

“Did I say that?”

“If your nose disappears, the smell should be a bit less noticeable, shouldn’t it?”

Father found and picked up the dagger he had used to prepare the fish.

When Father turned toward me, I had somehow already moved my bedding right beside him and was lying there.

“You’re not going to sleep?”

Father smiled faintly. In that fleeting smile, I could feel that Father was enjoying himself.

Father also lay down side by side with me. Compared to when we had gone hunting, the distance between Father and me had become much closer.

“Actually, there’s no smell at all.”

“I know.”

Just as he would dispel the effects of alcohol, Father had already released his qi and scattered the fishy smell into the air.

Lying down, I turned my head and looked at Father. Father was silently gazing up at the night sky. What was he thinking about right now?

What else would he be thinking about? A revenge match.

“Let’s have another round with night fishing.”

I laughed loudly. If Father had been defeated in martial arts, he would never have any lingering attachment. But fishing was different.

“Are you serious?”

“This time, we’ll compete by number caught. But we switch rods. That old East Sea Fisherman—I disliked him from that moment on.”

This was Father. Not someone who looked for better conditions because he had lost, but someone willing to accept a disadvantage when asking for another match despite having lost. I liked this side of Father so much.

“Very well.”

Father and I rose and gathered up the fishing rods. Father took the rod I had made, and I took the East Sea Fisherman’s rod. Light, yet fitting perfectly in my hand—an artifact was indeed an artifact.

“Uncle Hwi. You may be sick of it, but tomorrow morning’s breakfast will be fish cooked by Father again!”

* * *

The next morning, Father was preparing fish.

The night fishing had also been my victory. Truly, whenever I cast the line, I caught a fish. I caught so many that we kept only what we could eat and released the rest.

“The East Sea Fisherman’s rod in my hand is akin to the Heavenly Demon Sword in Father’s hand.”

I teased him on purpose. If I didn’t tease him like this, Father might genuinely have his pride wounded.

“I know you deliberately let me win. If you left the cooking to me, you would have had to eat a tasteless breakfast. Isn’t that right?”

Father’s dagger, which had been cleaning the fish, stopped. From far away, Hwi shook his head at me, telling me to stop. If that dagger came flying at me, it wouldn’t be carrying only the fishy smell.

When I shut my mouth, Father’s dagger began moving again.

“Today, it’s grilled fish.”

I said loudly,

“I’ll light the bonfire.”

And so, we ate fish Father had grilled himself for breakfast.

You might say grilled fish is grilled fish wherever you go, but if you have never tasted grilled fish made by the Heavenly Demon, don’t say such things. Father’s skill at grilling fish was also top-notch.

Just as we were nearly finished eating, our gazes turned in one direction.

We had sensed someone approaching us.

Father stopped Hwi from rising.

“Stay where you are.”

Father had already finished assessing an opponent who wasn’t even visible.

A short while later, someone appeared there.

The man who emerged, limping, looked as though he was on the verge of collapsing.

“Water… Please give me some water.”

Seeing us, his tension eased, and he sank to the ground as if collapsing. Three martial artists encountered in a remote place—if anything, it was a situation where he should have been more tense, yet he was in no state of mind to grasp the situation.

I gave him water first. Perhaps he had been wandering through the mountains, for his clothes were torn and his appearance was a mess. In particular, his injured ankle was swollen badly.

“How long have you gone without eating?”

“Three days? Four? I’m not sure.”

I shared rice and the leftover fish with him.

“Chew thoroughly as you eat.”

“Thank you.”

Driven by hunger, he ate without hesitation.

After he had eaten, I examined his body. Father silently watched me.

“You’ve injured your leg.”

Then the man said to me,

“I hurt it after misstepping in the mountains.”

“It seems it would be difficult to reach a village on that leg. Let me take a look for a moment.”

Fortunately, no bones were broken. I treated his sprained ankle and splinted it with tree branches. As much as my skill helped, his expression also became more at ease.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

Only then did the man feel relieved. He had finally escaped the fear that he might die.

“How did you come to lose your way?”

“It happened while I was searching for medicinal herbs.”

No matter how I looked at him, his appearance did not resemble that of an herb gatherer.

“Is someone in your family ill?”

“No.”

His circumstances were something I had never expected.

The man’s name was Im Gye. He said he ran a school in the village where he taught children letters.

“My father worked as a martial artist in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and retired this year.”

This place, not far from the main headquarters, was within the territory of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

His father had never learned proper martial arts, so he had spent his entire life as a low-ranking martial artist before retiring.

And this year happened to be his father’s sixtieth birthday.

Im Gye wanted to invite the leader of the branch his father had belonged to for his father’s birthday and make him happy. He thought his father would truly rejoice if the branch leader came to the sixtieth-birthday feast and congratulated him.

“But they said it would require fifty nyang to invite the branch leader.”

“Who said that?”

“There is someone in the village who has connections to the branch leader. That person told me so. He said even inviting a renowned guest to a banquet costs money. The amount differs according to the value of the person’s name, and according to how long they stay. He said it would cost fifty nyang for the branch leader to come briefly, offer congratulations, and leave.”

I glanced at Father and asked,

“How much do you receive, Father?”

At my question, full of mischief, a sneer formed at the corner of Father’s mouth.

Would Father move for a hundred thousand nyang? Would he move for a million nyang? He was someone you couldn’t summon even if you offered ten million nyang.

In that sense, if this journey were converted into money, the amount would truly be beyond imagination.

“Even if you gave that money, the branch leader probably wouldn’t come.”

At my words, Im Gye asked in surprise,

“What do you mean?”

“If it were discovered that a high-ranking figure of the Cult had moved privately after receiving money, not only would he be dismissed from his position, he might even be imprisoned in the dungeon. There’s no way he would move for fifty nyang.”

“Ah, I see. I have spent my whole life only studying letters, so I’m ignorant of the ways of the world.”

He lowered his head. Just seeing how he believed my words immediately made that clear. Did he even know who he was trusting?

“That person will pocket the fifty nyang and later say this: that the branch leader suddenly had an important matter on his schedule. Then what will you do? Go and protest? Even if you do protest, will he return the money?”

It was a world overflowing with people who swindled money from such innocent folk.

“When your own heart is good, you think others’ hearts must be good too. But reality isn’t like that, is it?”

Im Gye’s sigh deepened.

“My father lived diligently all his life for our family. I wanted to do something for such a father, but time only passes, and things are not as easy as one wishes.”

“Even so, is there a need to overdo it and invite the branch leader? Even if he came, it’s not as though he would sincerely congratulate him.”

“You’re right. The branch leader probably doesn’t even know my father’s name.”

That was reality. There were hundreds of martial artists in a branch, so how would he know a low-ranking martial artist at the very bottom?

“Then why?”

“Whenever my father had a cup of wine, he would say this like a habit: Though I live as a mere bottom-rank martial artist, though I am only at the bottom. Though my role is not a great one.”

From those words, I could feel the sorrow of a man who had wanted, at least once, to boast proudly before his child, but had ultimately never been able to.

“So I wanted to let a high-ranking person come so that Father could boast in front of me just once. I wanted him to be able to speak proudly in front of our family, relatives, and the villagers. More than words telling him he had worked hard, I simply thought that was the path for Father’s sake.”

How could I not understand his heart?

In wanting to do something for our fathers, he and I, who had come on this journey, were not so different.

“I heard that if I dug up a hundred-year snow ginseng, it would easily be worth over a hundred nyang. Even though I knew something so precious would never appear before my eyes, I couldn’t just stay at home.”

Tears gathered in his eyes.

“I will be going now. Today is my father’s sixtieth-birthday feast, and if I hadn’t met such honorable people, I might not have even been able to attend. You are the benefactors of my lifetime. Truly, thank you.”

He bowed his head several times in thanks, then left the place where we were.

* * *

Fortunately, Im Gye returned home before it was too late.

“Brother! What on earth happened?”

He had said he would be gone for a few days before leaving, but because it was behavior unlike his usual self, his younger sister had been worried all this time.

“I’m sorry. One thing led to another, and it turned out that way.”

“Are you all right?”

“I’m fine. The birthday preparations?”

“Our aunts came and helped.”

His father, Im Hak, looked at him and said bluntly,

“You’re here, so that’s enough.”

He had not known it in the past, but as he grew older, he could feel his father’s heart. His father’s worry and relief.

‘I’m sorry.’

He thought that perhaps wandering the mountains for several days had been not for his father’s sake, but for his own. To put his own heart at ease. To be able to say that he had tried this hard.

Family and relatives gathered, and the village people were invited as well.

But his father did not invite the people of the branch. Saying he would only burden his old colleagues for no reason, he did not invite them at all.

Im Gye could feel his father’s worry. Who would be interested in the sixtieth-birthday feast of a retired bottom-rank martial artist? If he invited them for no reason and no one came, it would be hard to endure that humiliation in front of his children.

And when many people gather, there is always someone like this.

“Looks like none of the Demonic Cult people came?”

He was the head of a martial hall in the village, and when his father had belonged to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, even if he was at the very bottom, this man had not dared treat him carelessly.

But once his father left the Cult, the man’s gaze changed, and his attitude changed.

“I deliberately didn’t call them. They’re busy working, so I’d only be causing them trouble for no reason.”

“It’s not that you called and they didn’t come?”

It was not so much that he bore malice toward his father; he was simply the sort of person who usually made such negative remarks. There are people like that, are there not? People who make even the same words sound unpleasant.

Still, he could have held back a little today. Even so, Im Gye could not show displeasure. The man was the head of a martial hall who had properly learned martial arts.

“It was a joke, a joke. Why so serious? As you get older, you’ve become timid.”

He made them feel unpleasant to the very end.

It was at that very moment.

The door opened, and a group of guests arrived. As martial artists wearing swords filed in all at once, the hall fell silent in unison.

Recognizing them, Im Hak was startled.

“Branch Leader!”

To his surprise, the Branch Leader and his fellow branch members had come to that very place. Im Gye and the villagers were all startled.

“Martial Officer Im, I heard it is your sixtieth birthday? We have come to congratulate you.”

At those words, Im Hak was so surprised and flustered that he did not know what to do.

The juniors he had worked with came to celebrate him. Among them were those he had been close to, but those he had not been particularly close with were there as well.

“Please, sit over here.”

“No, today you are the guest of honor, so take the seat of honor.”

The Branch Leader showed him great respect. While he wondered how this man had found out and why he was showing such courtesy, he also felt immense joy.

His son and daughter were watching. The villagers were watching as well. The Commander of the Martial Officers, who had run his mouth recklessly, watched this scene with a thoroughly cowed expression.

“If any problems arise in the future, contact me anytime.”

“Thank you, Branch Leader.”

Im Hak was delighted, yet at the same time, he could not understand the situation.

He had served as a low-ranking martial officer under many branch leaders, but he had never once exchanged a private conversation with them. It was the same with this current Branch Leader. This was a man who would not even have known his name.

That secret was now revealed. The Branch Leader too spoke cautiously with a puzzled expression.

“He somehow knew and sent this.”

The Branch Leader carefully took out a small box he had been keeping in his bosom.

Seeing his hands tremble, Im Hak grew nervous in turn. Just what in the world could it be?

“Here, take it and open it.”

Im Hak took it in a daze and opened the box.

To his astonishment, what lay inside was a hundred-year snow ginseng.

“To think you would give such a precious gift!”

The hands of Im Hak holding the box trembled violently. Im Gye and the villagers approached to get a look at the hundred-year snow ginseng.

But what would truly astonish Im Hak was only beginning now.

“It is not my gift.”

At the Branch Leader’s words, Im Hak unfolded the piece of paper that lay inside the box with the ginseng.

Im Hak’s eyes went wide as he read the contents. It was the first time Im Gye had ever seen his father so surprised. Im Gye, standing beside him, checked the contents and let out a cry. The villagers too gasped in shock.

Im Hak set down the box and performed a deep bow. Im Gye bowed together with him.

Tears streaming from Im Hak’s eyes fell onto the back of his wrinkled hand. Since joining the cult at seventeen, he had lived a hard life all his years.

To feed his children, he had endured no matter how much it hurt, living through suffering fierce enough to kill him. He had actually crossed the threshold of death several times.

And the brief words written on the slip washed away all the hardships of his past days.

To you who have devoted yourself to the main sect, I convey my heartfelt gratitude.

Geom Ujin, Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Absolute Regression – Chapter 450.

Chapter 450: The Father Who Was a Son.
--------------------------------------
The carriage was racing toward the place it needed to go.

Father spoke to me with his eyes firmly closed.

"Why do you look at me so?"

How was it that he sensed people's gazes so keenly even with his eyes closed?

"Because I like you."

Father cracked his eyes open slightly and looked at me.

"I am not joking. I am not teasing, either. I just like you too much, Father."

"Because I sent that person a gift?"

"No, because you wrote him a letter."

To Father, a single root of Hundred-Year Snow Ginseng was merely pocket change.

But the letter written by his own hand was beyond monetary value.

What moved me most was that he had not sent the gift because I told him to.

After Im Gye left, I had worried about how to persuade Father to help him. But before I could say anything, Father wrote a letter and handed it to Hwi, saying:

"Deliver this with one root of Hundred-Year Snow Ginseng, and have Branch Leader Ji hand it over personally."

How could I not have been moved?

"You are the best, Father."

Father remained silent, his eyes closed, saying nothing.

Not even taking credit for it—doesn't that make you even greater!

And I could feel it.

In this very moment, hearing his son call him the best, Father was surely happy.

There was a particular angle of Father's lips that only I could recognize now. That faint upward curve was proof that he was purely delighted.

Then Father suddenly spoke.

"It is because of those people that the main sect has endured."

Yes, Father too had his own demonic path.

I hoped Father's heart reached them well. I also hoped that Im Gye's father had become the best father, just like mine.

I poked my head out the window. Was it because the wind blowing in was refreshing? The sky was even bluer and clearer.

"The weather is truly fine."

When I glanced back, Father was looking at the sky through the opposite window.

Father, how is it? Does the sky here feel different from the one you saw at the Heavenly Demon Hall?

As the carriage, which had been running along the river, completely distanced itself from it, Father suddenly spoke.

"On the way back, let us have another fishing match at that river. This time, we will both use the fishing rod you made."

I laughed heartily. It seemed he wanted to beat me at fishing no matter what. But I could not let him win, either. With Father's perception, how could I lose on purpose? If I did, he would truly be furious.

"Very well. A match is always best of three, after all."

A certain resolve settled on Father's lips. It was the resolve to win at all costs.

* * *

"Uncle Hwi, please stop here for a moment."

When the running carriage stopped, I spoke to Father.

"Would you care to take a short walk?"

Without a second word, Father got down from the carriage.

"If we pass through that forest, there is a magnificent waterfall. Since we are passing by anyway, let us go see it."

Hwi waited to watch the carriage, and Father and I walked into the forest together.

"When did you come here?"

"I discovered it by chance a long time ago."

It was a place I had visited before my regression. I remembered the time I walked this path. I had walked that snow-covered road alone... but now I was walking that same road, lush with greenery, listening to the chirping of birds, together with Father.

Father walked without a word, but the expression on his face as he looked around seemed at peace. I, too, walked quietly in contemplation. The more time I spent with Father, the less awkward silence became.

At the end of that beautiful forest path was a waterfall. The waterfall, cascading down like strands of silver thread, refreshed the hearts of those who beheld it.

*Shwaaaaaa—*

Father gazed silently at the waterfall. When had he last left the sect to see a waterfall like this? It must have been truly long ago.

"It is good."

That single word was enough to know that Father was truly satisfied.

"Father."

"What is it?"

"What was it like with Grandfather?"

Perhaps because it was a sudden question, Father looked at me.

"I am curious what kind of person you were as a son."

Father's gaze turned back to the waterfall.

"I feel there is much I could learn from you, who were once a son."

I did not know well how Father had gotten along with Grandfather.

After a long silence, Father unexpectedly spoke.

"There are times I look at you and feel regret."

I looked at Father. Though he did not explain what he meant, I could tell it meant I was doing well.

We silently gazed at the waterfall for a long while, then walked back the way we came.

Thus I had stopped the carriage once, and after traveling for about half a day, this time it was Father who stopped the carriage.

"Stop for a moment if there is a secluded place."

Hwi stopped the carriage in a deserted place.

"Get down."

I got down following Father. On this trip, another thing I had been hoping to hear finally flowed from Father's lips.

"How far have you mastered the Guhwa Demonic Art?"

He meant to bestow teachings upon me regarding the Guhwa Demonic Art. I was so overjoyed I felt I might take flight.

Because a single word from Father would help me more than a hundred days of training.

The perceptive Hwi vanished into hiding the moment the Guhwa Demonic Art was mentioned. Since one had to step aside when the art was being passed on, he went far away to stand guard.

"I have mastered up to the Fifth Form, but the Fifth Form is still unstable."

"Show me up to the Fourth Form."

Before Father's eyes, I performed the Guhwa Demonic Art consecutively from the First Form through the Fourth Form.

A strange light flashed in Father's eyes as he watched.

He must not have expected my training through the Heaven-Piercing Secret Art to have deepened this much. At times like this, it was truly fortunate that I possessed the Heavenly Martial Constitution. Because it could explain the incomprehensibly rapid progress in my stead.

However, what Father was focusing on was surely not merely that my attainments were deep.

What he was paying greater attention to was likely the fact that it was different.

Father's Guhwa Demonic Art and my Guhwa Demonic Art were different.

Even looking at the demonic spirits made that clear. They were completely different from Father's. Not only in appearance, but also in the feeling and form when they manifested and vanished.

Of course, that did not mean I could guarantee my demonic spirits were stronger than Father's.

This difference was what maintained the tension between Father and me. It must have been the driving force that pulled the already-perfected Father back to the training ground.

"There are times when you must use the Fifth Form despite extreme inner power consumption. Do you know when that is?"

"I believe it is when allies and enemies are mixed together."

Father nodded.

"Yes, exactly. It is when you must pick out and kill only the enemies with precision. That is why the Fifth Form's accuracy is its lifeblood."

Because a Fifth Form with low accuracy was truly no different from a calamity falling from the heavens.

"Now, perform it once."

I drew the Black Demon Sword and stepped forward.

"Assume enemies are lined up every five steps, centered thirty paces straight ahead."

I pictured the enemies as Father described.

Ten masters stood lined up before me.

'I kill them all in a single move!'

After briefly composing my mind, I performed the Fifth Form, Soul-Annihilating Demon Strike.

*Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!*

*Thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud!*

They landed roughly around the spots I had pictured in my mind, but they were not precise.

Even from afar, Father accurately perceived the results.

"Your accuracy is still lacking, and the depth is inconsistent as well. The sixth and eighth are shallow, while the second and third are too deep."

Even up close, the ground only had black holes in it, yet Father had accurately discerned the result from a distance. Despite ten sword qi falling simultaneously in an instant, he had noticed each and every difference.

Father delivered a lecture on the Fifth Form.

In regard to the Guhwa Demonic Art, Father's lecture gave me absolute enlightenment and learning.

Father's teachings were concrete.

I knew better than anyone how difficult the Guhwa Demonic Art was to master.

Yet Father's explanation was easy. It was a concrete and easy explanation that only one who had reached a high realm could give.

Only a true expert could explain things simply.

Father had proven this eternal truth to me.

Of course, hidden within that simplicity were profound principles of martial arts. Those who did not know those principles would be inclined to doubt. 'Is this all there is? Is it this easy?' In the end, to them, that ease would become nothing but a mirage named simplicity.

Father explained simply and concretely, and I asked concrete questions.

Whenever I was curious about something, I asked right away. Question and answer.

That was precisely our realm.

"Now, perform it again!"

Once again, Father designated the spots where they should fall.

Ten strands of sword qi fell vertically once more.

"Better than before, but still far."

Father explained again.

It was not training where he threw out riddles to chew on and realize through deep thought. That deep thinking is for you to do on your own; what I teach is only ever the concrete parts!

The current training was just like a martial arts instructor teaching a trainee.

It was even different from when he had taught me before. It was easier and more concrete.

That was how I knew.

'In that time, Father has grown as well.'

Perhaps I felt it only because I had grown. The Father of now was definitely stronger than the Father of when I had first regressed.

"Now, perform it again."

This time, Father walked right into the spot where the sword qi would fall.

From there, he looked at me and asked,

"There is a hostage here. The enemies number five."

Father sent out a finger wind to mark their positions.

The hostage and the enemies were practically pressed together.

"Now, save them."

"I thought you would be standing in the hostage's place, Father."

"Why would I?"

"Is that not how it usually goes? 'I believe in you. Therefore, I will stand in the hostage's place. If you make a mistake, I die.'"

Father spoke decisively.

"How could I trust you?"

And with that, he stepped a few paces further away.

Knowing it was no joke but his true feelings, I burst into laughter.

I composed my mind and envisioned the situation. Those surrounding them were not mere martial artists. I pictured true masters surrounding a hostage.

Swords pointed at necks.

I had only one chance.

*Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!*

*Thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud!*

And Father announced the result.

"The enemies are all dead."

Just as I was about to let out a cheer!

"The hostage is dead too."

I ran to the spot where the hostage had stood. Sure enough, as Father said, a sword qi had fallen there as well. Not five strands, but six had fallen. It was a mistake born of still being unaccustomed to controlling the number of sword qi.

I looked down at the hole in the hostage's spot and mourned.

"Daeryong, I'm sorry."

I smiled, imagining Seo Daeryong arguing, "Why me? It's not like you can kill Ian either, right? Isn't there Left Arm Lord Jang? I'm the right arm!" Seo Daeryong's voice rang out in my mind.

"Still, it's better than before."

Just as I had felt, Father also clearly felt that this was different from when he had learned the Guhwa Demonic Art in the past. We were growing together.

"Of course. Who is teaching me, after all?"

We got back on the carriage.

"There is an inn in the village we are arriving at soon that has a skilled cook. It is a place I have wanted to take you to, Father. Since I failed to save the hostage, I'll treat!"

* * *

Zhou Xiangyue had been having nightmares for the past few days. What pressed down on her so heavily—she who usually did not dream often—was today's appointment.

"Young Lady, it is time to prepare."

Maidservants entered with clothes.

Though they were splendid and beautiful garments, Zhou Xiangyue took out a clean martial uniform from the wardrobe.

"I will wear this."

"The Family Head insisted you wear these clothes."

When Zhou Xiangyue looked at them coldly, the maidservants lowered their heads. It was because they were the sort who would not back down unless she did.

"Mother will know that I am going out in a martial uniform, too. So you need not worry about being scolded."

Then a woman's voice was heard from outside.

"That is why I came myself."

The woman who opened the door and entered was Zhou Xiangyue's mother and the Family Head of the Zhou Clan Sword Family, Lin Suhua.

"Go wearing those clothes."

"I do not want to."

"Why not?"

Zhou Xiangyue stared at her mother.

Did she ask because she did not know? Or was she pretending not to know?

"Do you not know how important today's meeting with Young Master Huang is?"

It is you, Mother, who does not know what you are saying, is it not? What relation does that splendid palace attire have to today's meeting? If you think there is a relation—

Those words had risen to Zhou Xiangyue's throat.

—Do you really want to sell your daughter to them?

But she could not bring herself to say them. They were words she would regret once spoken.

Five years ago, after her father passed away, the Zhou Clan Sword Family had rapidly declined. Though her mother had become the Family Head, she could not fill her father's absence. That choice had been a mistake. The position of Family Head should have been handed to someone more capable, and she should have stepped down.

In the end, for various reasons, the family's martial artists left one by one. Now, only those who had revered her father remained, struggling to carry on the family's legacy.

Amidst this, the recently prosperous Huangdao Merchant Group had proposed joining hands with the Zhou Clan Sword Family. Today was the very day of that negotiation.

And her mother knew.

She knew that Young Master Huang, who was coming to negotiate today, held favorable feelings toward her daughter. He had chosen the daughter as his negotiation counterpart. Her mother's choice had been that splendid palace attire.

'At a time like this, you should tell me to wear a martial uniform. That is how you get me to walk out in those palace clothes. Not even knowing that, why do you hurt me so clumsily!'

She was prepared to hurt her mother. Yet even though her mother would have brought the wound upon herself, she would be shocked, take offense, leap into a rage, and look for her dead father.

Those images kept coming to mind, so she could not open her mouth.

With such complicated feelings, when she arrived at the inn that was the meeting place, a scuffle was taking place at the entrance between the Huangdao Merchant Group's martial artists and a young man.

"Why on earth are you keeping me out? I absolutely must enter today!"

Absolute Regression: Chapter 451
